# Case 3 of the axisymmetric Navier-Stokes singularity analysis: the axis-escape planar ancient Euler branch

**Status: UNRESOLVED_NOT_ACCEPTED** (the case is unresolved, and nothing here is offered as an accepted answer to it). This report neither excludes Case 3 nor constructs a fixed-datum singular solution realizing it. The first missing implication is stated in Section 1.3. Section 4 supplies proofs of the numbered results, using the standard Navier-Stokes existence, continuation and blow-up facts identified there, published Liouville theorems (Lemma 2.2 and Remark 2.3), and classical linear parabolic theory, including the probabilistic representation of the Cauchy-Dirichlet problem for a parabolic operator with smooth coefficients (Lemma 7.10(a)), the Dirichlet Green function on a smooth bounded domain (Lemma 7.16), and solvability with Gaussian decay bounds for the backward adjoint problem (Lemma 7.28), together with Nash's inequality; this describes the proof dependencies and is not an independent certificate of completeness. The supplementary model calculations in Section 3 and in Section 6, items 2 and 7, are not used in any proof; where their full arguments are not reproduced here, they are reported findings, not established results of this report. The remark that exact nonconstant affine patches do not occur in bounded mild solutions is conditional on the positive-time spatial analyticity of such solutions, which is stated in [DZ20] with references to earlier work but was not checked here against a primary source; it is used in no proof.

**End of the run.** This is the final report of the second research round on this case. The round started at $T_0$ = 2026-10-17 19:11 UTC and was stopped at its time cap; $T_{\rm end}$ = 2026-10-19 19:15 UTC, so the elapsed time from $T_0$ is 48 hours and 4 minutes, and the prompt's minimum of 24 hours of research on this case was met in this round alone (an earlier round had already worked on the case for about one day). The work ran in consecutive sessions over the whole period. The last mathematical additions were entered at about 11:00 UTC on 2026-10-19; after that the report changed only in notation and wording, and the remaining time went to auditing, to checking results from parallel work on this case and on estimates shared by the four cases of the program, and to designing candidate routes, which were set aside before producing results because they repeated approaches that had already failed. The acceptance gate was not passed: neither a complete exclusion nor a fixed-datum counterexample was obtained. The status is therefore UNRESOLVED_NOT_ACCEPTED, and the first missing implication, stated in Section 1.3, is the one identified in the earlier round.

**What this round added.** The report published after the earlier round had the same status and the same first missing implication, with Theorem 1 items 1 to 34. This round added the following; items (1) to (4) are proved in Section 4 and audited as described in Section 5, and none of it closes the case. (1) Items 35 to 37 of Theorem 1: circulation absorption at the axis, which gives a bound for $\eta$ free of the swirl history and the inward-strain requirement $K_-(s_0-h,t_n)\ge\frac13\log(D_n/r_n)-C$ (Lemma 7.44, Corollary 7.45); the self-compression bound $|\Pi|\le\frac12\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2\|\eta\|_{L^1}$, with the resulting swirl clock and its localized family (Lemma 7.46 and its Remark (5)); and the comparison horizons $T''_\vartheta$, on which the net inner-boundary contribution must exceed $a_n/4$ at some age in either alternative of the dichotomy (Theorem 7.18, Remark (4)). (2) A logarithm-free lower bound for the perimeter and the isoperimetric ratio of the high set in alternative (I) (Lemma 7.40(c)), which yields the isoperimetric criterion of item 32, with a planar counterpart in Section 4.8, 8.3 (iii). (3) The Landau-type inequality $B^2\le\frac{4n}{\sqrt{3n+1}}AC$ for maps $\mathbb R^n\to\mathbb R^m$, sharp for $m\ge n$ (Remark (iii) after Lemma 4.1), from which a Case 3 sequence can exist only if $C_*\ge\sqrt7/8$, a threshold that cannot be raised using only the properties of the planar limit used there, together with a divergence-free three-dimensional variant. (4) For the planar model question of Section 1.3, a proved bound logarithmic in the Péclet number that needs no further assumption, and a bound in terms of the initial vorticity mass when the initial vorticity is in $L^1(\mathbb R^2)$ (Section 4.8, 8.3 (iii)). (5) Repairs from a block-by-block final audit of the whole report, a revised compatibility test (Section 1.2), further model and literature remarks, and numerical illustrations at Reynolds numbers 2000 and 4000 (Section 6, item 10 (e) and (j)), which prove nothing.

## 1. Result

### 1.1 The branch under study

Let $u_0\in H^\infty(\mathbb R^3)$ be divergence free and axisymmetric, with energy $E_0=\|u_0\|_2^2$ and circulation bound $G=\|ru_0^\theta\|_\infty<\infty$, and suppose the smooth Navier-Stokes solution $u$ (viscosity $1$) first becomes singular at $T_*<\infty$. With the historical suprema $M,S,D,H$ of $\|u\|_\infty,\|u^\theta\|_\infty,\|\nabla u\|_\infty,\|\nabla^2u\|_\infty$ over $[0,t]$ (pointwise Cartesian Frobenius norms, $|\nabla u|^2=\sum_{i,j}(\partial_iu_j)^2$, $|\nabla^2u|^2=\sum_{i,j,k}(\partial_i\partial_ju_k)^2$), assume the shape condition

(A) $M(t)H(t)\le C_*D(t)^2$ for all $t$ in some interval $[t_A,T_*)$.

A *Case 3 sequence* is a sequence of historical gradient first-crossing records $(t_n,x_n)$, $D_n=D(t_n)=|\nabla u(x_n,t_n)|$, $t_n=\min\{t:D(t)=D_n\}$, $t_n\uparrow T_*$, whose distance $r_n$ to the symmetry axis satisfies
$$\alpha_n=\frac{r_nD_n}{M_n}\to\infty,\qquad M_n=M(t_n).$$
By axisymmetry each record point may be chosen as $x_n=(r_n,0,z_n)$. The normalized fields are $U_n(y,\tau)=M_n^{-1}u(x_n+\ell_ny,t_n+\tau/D_n)$ with $\ell_n=M_n/D_n$ and normalized viscosity $\nu_n=D_n/M_n^2$. The research question is whether Case 3 can occur under (A), the no-swirl-extraction branch negation (NSE: there is no sequence of swirl first-crossing records whose swirl-scaled rescalings are defined on every fixed backward time interval for large $n$, are bounded on every fixed space-time cylinder by one constant independent of the cylinder, and have a vanishing weighted far-field tail; the precise definition is in Section 4.0), and one fixed finite-energy datum.

*Notation used in Theorem 1.* With $\omega=\nabla\times u$ and its angular component $\omega^\theta=\partial_zu^r-\partial_ru^z$, write $\eta=\omega^\theta/r$, $q=u^\theta/r$, $\Gamma=ru^\theta$ (so $|\Gamma|\le G$) and $P=(u^\theta)^2/r$; $S_n=S(t_n)$, $w=\omega^\theta$, and $b=u^re_r+u^ze_z$ is the meridional velocity. The constants $R_0=R_0(C_*)$ and $\kappa_0=\kappa_0(C_*)>0$ are those of Lemma 5.6; the point $x^0=(r^0,z^0)$ within distance $R_0\ell_n$ of the record point and the meridional disk $\mathcal D_n$ of radius $3\delta r_n$ about it are those of item 5. Put $a_n=|\eta(x^0,t_n)|$, so that, for large $n$, $a_n\ge c_aD_n/r_n$ with $c_a=\frac38\kappa_0$, and $v_n=\operatorname{sgn}(\eta(x^0,t_n))\,\eta$. Fix $\epsilon=3\kappa_0/128$ and $L=32/\kappa_0$ (in item 14, $L>1$ is arbitrary), and for $0<\vartheta\le\frac14$ put $T_\vartheta=\epsilon\vartheta^3r_n^2/(1+G)$. Lemma 7.10 represents $\eta(x^0,t_n)$ through the diffusion $X=(R,Z)$ with generator $\partial_{rr}+\frac3r\partial_r+\partial_{zz}-u^r\partial_r-u^z\partial_z$ run backward in time from $(x^0,t_n)$ and stopped at its exit time $\tau$ from the annulus $\{\vartheta r_n<r<Lr_n\}$: for each horizon $h$, $\eta(x^0,t_n)$ is the sum of the expected value of $\eta$ at time $t_n-h$ over the paths that have not exited, the expected value of $\eta$ at the exit point and exit time over the paths that exit by time $h$, and the expected integral of the swirl source $\partial_z(q^2)$ along the path up to time $\tau\wedge h$. The net inner-boundary contribution of Theorem 7.12 is $J_n(\vartheta)=\sup_{0<h\le T_\vartheta}\big(\mathbb E[v_n(X_\tau,t_n-\tau);\ \tau\le h,\ R_\tau=\vartheta r_n]\big)_+$; the expectation is not normalized by the exit probability, and the positive part is taken after it. The strain clocks are $K_\pm(s,t)=\int_s^t\|(u^r/r)_\pm\|_\infty$, and $\mathcal B(s_0,t)=\int_{s_0}^t\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}$ is the critical budget of the swirl source. The record disk of Lemma 7.8 is the meridional disk of radius $\frac{3\kappa_0}{8\sqrt2C_*}\ell_n$ about $x^0$, on which $|\omega^\theta(t_n)|\ge\frac38\kappa_0D_n$, and $\mathcal T_n$ is the solid torus it generates by rotation about the axis. The constants are $c_1=\frac{27\pi^2\kappa_0^3}{512C_*^2}$, $c_3=\frac{27\pi\kappa_0^3}{8192C_*^2}$, $c_4=\frac{81\pi^2\kappa_0^4}{2^{18}C_*^2}$, $C_I=\frac{16384e(1+G)}{3\kappa_0^3}$ and $C_f=4\sqrt2\epsilon G^{4/3}C_*^{2/3}/(1+G)$. The *long window* is the interval $[t_n-\delta r_n/M_n,t_n]$ of item 5 (the horizons $T''_\vartheta$ of item 37, which satisfy $T''_\vartheta\gg r_n^2$ for fixed $\vartheta$ but not necessarily at shrinking scales, are not long windows in this sense), and a *record window* is an interval $[t_n-A/D_n,t_n]$ with $A$ fixed, that is, a fixed number of record times $1/D_n$. A *formal scenario* assigns sizes (powers of a small parameter) to the quantities of a hypothetical configuration and checks them against the proved necessary conditions; it is not a solution, and no formal scenario in this report is shown to be realized. Some letters are given local meanings inside single items, which override this list there: $\epsilon$ is a free tolerance in item 26; $\mu$ is the measure $r\,dr\,dz$ in items 14 and 30 and a mass in item 27; $K$ is a smoothing constant in item 14, an integrated strain in items 17 and 20 and a dimensionless duration in item 34; $A$ is a matrix in items 33 and 34 and a cell amplitude in item 33; $T$ is a free horizon in items 17, 20 and 33 and equals $T_\vartheta$ in items 30 to 32; $h$ is a backward age or horizon except in item 29, where it is the homogeneous part of $\eta$; $s_n$ is the time $t_n-\delta r_n/M_n$ in item 7 and the vertical spread $s_n(\vartheta)$ in item 30; $\sigma$ is $u^r/r$ in item 27 ($\sigma_A$ the corresponding radial strain in item 29) and a side length in item 32; $U=u^\theta$ in item 27; and $S$ is a scalar curl in item 34.

### 1.2 What is proved

**Theorem 1 (necessary structure of a Case 3 sequence).** Let $(t_n,x_n)$ be a Case 3 sequence for a solution as in 1.1. Then, along a subsequence:

1. $\nu_n\to0$ (this uses the two-dimensional Liouville theorem of Koch, Nadirashvili, Seregin and Sverak for bounded ancient mild solutions, applied at positive viscosity), and $S_n/M_n\le(G^2C_*)^{1/3}\nu_n^{2/3}\to0$. Neither statement uses (NSE).
2. $U_n\to U$ and $\nabla_yU_n\to\nabla_yU$ uniformly on compact subsets of $\mathbb R^3\times(-\infty,0]$, where $U=(V_1,0,V_3)(y_1,y_3,\tau)$ is a bounded ancient planar Euler flow whose pressure gradient is the limit of the canonical one (Lemma 3.2; only its vanishing planar curl is used), $\|V\|_\infty\le1$, $\|\nabla V\|_\infty\le1$, $\nabla V(\cdot,\tau)$ $C_*$-Lipschitz and $|\nabla V(0,0)|=1$. Its vorticity $\zeta$ is nonzero, transported by the flow, and $\|\zeta(\cdot,-A)\|_\infty=\|\zeta(\cdot,0)\|_\infty>0$ for all $A\ge0$.
3. For every fixed $A\ge0$, $\liminf_nD(t_n-A/D_n)/D_n\ge\|\zeta(\cdot,0)\|_\infty/\sqrt2$ and $\liminf_nM(t_n-A/D_n)/M_n\ge\|\zeta(\cdot,0)\|_\infty^2/(16C_*)$.
4. (Energy) $r_nM_n^4/D_n^2\le(20736/\pi^2)C_*^4E_0$; hence $r_n\to0$ and $D_n^3/M_n^5\to\infty$.
5. (Long window) There are $\kappa_0=\kappa_0(C_*)>0$ and, for each $\delta\in(0,1/8]$, an index $N$ such that for $n\ge N$ and every $t\in[t_n-\delta r_n/M_n,t_n]$ the azimuthal vorticity satisfies $\sup|\omega^\theta(\cdot,t)|\ge(\kappa_0/4)D_n$ on the meridional disk $\mathcal D_n$ of radius $3\delta r_n$ centred at a point $(r^0,z^0)$ within distance $O(\ell_n)=o(r_n)$ of the record point (fixed by Lemma 5.6 in the preamble of Section 4.7, within distance $R_0\ell_n$ of the record point, and used in Lemma 7.1), and $\|u(t)\|_\infty\ge\kappa_0^2M_n/(1152C_*)$.
6. (Dissipation) $\int_{t_n-\delta r_n/M_n}^{t_n}\|\nabla u\|_2^2\,dt\ge c(C_*)\,\delta\,r_n^2M_n$, and therefore $r_n^2M_n\to0$.
7. (No Type I) $(T_*-s_n)\|\nabla u(s_n)\|_\infty\ge c\,\alpha_n\to\infty$ and $(T_*-s_n)\|u(s_n)\|_\infty^2\ge c\,r_nM_n\to\infty$ for $s_n=t_n-\delta r_n/M_n$.
8. (Source budget) For fixed $t_0\in(t_A,T_*)$: $\frac{3\kappa_0}8\frac{D_n}{r_n}\le\|\eta(t_0)\|_\infty+4G^{1/3}\int_{t_0}^{t_n}H(s)^{5/3}ds$, where $\eta=\omega^\theta/r$.
9. (Critical $L^1$ budget, under the data decay condition $\eta_0\in L^1(\mathbb R^3)$) $\|\eta(t_n)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}\ge c_1M_n^2/D_n$ and $c_1M_n^2/D_n\le\|\eta_0\|_{L^1}+\int_0^{t_n}\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}dt$, $q=u^\theta/r$; both quantities are invariant under the Navier-Stokes scaling, so both critical norms are unbounded. Without any decay condition on the data (item 22): for every fixed $s_0\in(0,T_*)$, $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}dt\ge c_1M_n^2/(2D_n)$ for large $n$.
10. (Improved dissipation) $\int_{t_n-\delta r_n/M_n}^{t_n}\|\nabla u\|_2^2\,dt\ge c\,\delta^2r_n^3D_n^2/M_n^2$, hence $r_n\alpha_n^2\to0$; up to fixed constants the ratio of this lower bound to that of item 6 is $\delta\alpha_n\nu_n$, so along subsequences with $\alpha_n\nu_n\to\infty$ it is larger by a diverging factor, along subsequences with $\alpha_n\nu_n$ bounded it is not larger in order, and an oscillating ratio has to be treated along subsequences (Corollary 7.11).
11. (Annular/axis dichotomy) Along a further subsequence, either (I) for a fixed $\vartheta\in(0,1/4]$ the record persists in the annulus $\{\vartheta r_n<r<Lr_n\}$ for a time $\asymp\vartheta^3r_n^2$, much longer than $r_n/M_n$, and then $r_n^5D_n^2\to0$; or (II) a net fraction of the record value of $\eta$ is carried, in a stopped backward stochastic representation, from the cylinder $r=\vartheta_nr_n$ with $\vartheta_n\to0$, and then over a horizon $o(r_n^2)$ the radial velocity satisfies $\int\|u^r_+\|_\infty dt\ge(1-o(1))r_n$ and $\int\|(u^r/r)_+\|_\infty dt\ge\log(1/\vartheta_n)-o(1)$. In (II) the inner radius satisfies $\vartheta_nr_n\gtrsim r_n^{2/3}M_n^{-1/3}$.
12. (Meridional circulation) $\iint_{\mathcal D_n}|\omega^\theta(r,z,t_n-h)|\,dr\,dz\ge c\,D_nh$ for $0<h\le\delta r_n/M_n$, where $\mathcal D_n$ is the disk of item 5; in particular the meridional circulation near the record at the start of the window is $\gtrsim\alpha_n$.
13. (Biot-Savart form of (II)) $|u^r/r|\le\frac3{4\pi}\int|\eta(y)||x-y|^{-2}dy$, so in alternative (II) $\int\min\{\|\nabla u\|_\infty,\ C_L\|\eta\|_{L^{3,1}}\}\,dt\ge\log(1/\vartheta_n)-o(1)$ over the horizon of (II), where $C_L=(3/(4\pi))^{1/3}$ and $L^{3,1}$ is the Lorentz space.
14. (Killed averages of $\eta$ are controlled by the meridional velocity) For the evolution of $\eta$ killed on the boundary of a bounded smooth domain $\mathcal O\subset\{\vartheta r_n\le r\le Lr_n\}$ of the meridional half-plane, the time average over $[h_1,h_2]$ of the killed value at a point is at most $\frac{V}{\vartheta r_n}\big(e\Lambda/(h_2-h_1)\big)^{1/2}$, where $V$ bounds the meridional velocity on $\mathcal O$ over the time interval, $0<h_1<h_2<t$, $\mu=r\,dr\,dz$, $\Lambda=\max\{1,\log(K\mu(\mathcal O)/h_1)\}$ and $K=C_NL^2/(\vartheta^3r_n)$ with an absolute constant $C_N$, whatever the size of the drift (Lemma 7.16).
15. (Logarithmic window) $\alpha_n\nu_n=r_nD_n^2/M_n^3\le C(\kappa_0,\delta)\log(r_nM_n)$: the transport window $r_n/M_n$ lasts at most logarithmically many viscous times $\ell_n^2$ of the core.
16. (Quantitative dichotomy) Alternative (I) of item 11 at scale $\vartheta$ forces $\alpha_n^2\le C\vartheta^{-5}\log(r_nM_n/\vartheta^3)$ with $C=C(G,\kappa_0)$. Hence (I) occurs only along subsequences with $\alpha_n^2=O(\log(r_nM_n))$, and if $\alpha_n^2/\log(r_nM_n)\to\infty$ then $J_n(\vartheta)>a_n/4$ for every $\vartheta\in[\vartheta_n^\sharp,\frac14]$, $\vartheta_n^\sharp=(C'\log(r_nM_n)/\alpha_n^2)^{1/5}\to0$, so alternative (II) holds with $\vartheta_n=\vartheta_n^\sharp$. An energy form of the same bound also holds: in (I) at a fixed $\vartheta$, $\alpha_n^2\le C\vartheta^{-5}\big(\log(CE_0/(\vartheta^{10}r_n^3M_n^2))+\vartheta^{-2}\big)$ with $C=C(G,\kappa_0,C_*)$, hence $r_n^3M_n^2\le C(\vartheta)E_0e^{-\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2/C}$ for all large $n$ (Lemma 7.40, Remark (4); the measure of the high set is bounded by the kinetic energy: each point of it carries a disk of radius $c\vartheta\ell_n$ on which the vorticity stays above half the threshold, a maximal disjoint family of such disks has doubles covering the set, and the solid torus of each disk carries energy $\gtrsim\vartheta^6RM_n^2\ell_n^2$, $R$ the distance of its centre to the axis). The two logarithms have no universal comparison; together they leave a nonempty range of logarithmic record-scale parameters, although the energy bound excludes some parameters that the first bound allows. A source form also holds (Lemma 7.40, Remark (5)): in (I) at a fixed $\vartheta$, for every fixed $s_0\in(0,T_*)$ and all sufficiently large $n$, the source integral $\int_{s_0}^{t_n-T_\vartheta/2}\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}dt$ is at least $c(\vartheta)r_n^2D_ne^{c\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2}$, where $r_n^2D_n=\alpha_n^2/\nu_n$ is much larger than the general lower bound $1/\nu_n$; hence $r_n^2D_ne^{c\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2}=o(D_n^{2/3}M_n^{-1/3})$, and the inward strain clock from $s_0$ satisfies $K_-(s_0,t_n)\ge\frac13(\log(r_n^2D_n)+c\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2)-C$. An upper bound $\log_+\big(\int_{s_0}^{t_n-T_\vartheta/2}\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}dt\,/\,(r_n^2D_n)\big)=o(\alpha_n^2)$ would exclude (I) at that scale; none is proved.
17. (Full-space representation without inner boundary) Lifting $\eta$ to an axially symmetric function on $\mathbb R^5$, for $0<T<t$ and $x$ at distance $r_0$ from the axis, $|\eta(x,t)|\le\frac{\sqrt2Ve^{K}}{r_0\sqrt T}\mathcal E_T^{1/2}+\frac{2Ve^{2K}}{r_0^2}+\frac{2GH_*Te^{2K}}{r_0^2}$, where $V$ and $H_*$ bound $|(u^r,u^z)|$ and $|\nabla^2u|$ on $[t-T,t]$, $K=\int_{t-T}^t\|(u^r/r)_+\|_\infty$, and $\mathcal E_T=C+2K+\frac52\log(2(1+V^2T))$. Consequently, along subsequences with $\alpha_n\ge L_n=1+\log(r_nM_n)$, $\int\|(u^r/r)_+\|_\infty dt\ge\frac14\log(\alpha_n/L_n)-C$ over the horizon $r_n^2L_n^{1/2}\alpha_n^{-3/2}$ before $t_n$.
18. (The logarithm of item 14 is structural) No logarithm-free version of item 14 holds for the class of meridional fields used there (bounded smooth meridional velocity, divergence free for $r\,dr\,dz$, data equal to its own curl divided by $r$); an explicit hyperbolic-strain construction attains the logarithm, and after a cutoff it is a smooth compactly supported finite-energy field whose strain is given by the instantaneous Biot-Savart law of its own vorticity. A logarithm-free bound for Navier-Stokes solutions, which would exclude alternative (I), must therefore use dynamical restrictions (the vorticity evolution, the energy-dissipation law); the construction is not a Navier-Stokes solution.
19. (Critical swirl energy) $Y=2\pi\iint(u^\theta)^2dr\,dz$ and $X=\int|\nabla(ru^\theta)|^2r^{-3}dx$ ($Y$ and $\int X\,dt$ are scale invariant) satisfy $Y'+\frac23X\le3\int(u^\theta)^2(u^r/r)_-r^{-1}dx$ and $\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}\le\frac23X$. Case 3 therefore forces $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\!\int(u^\theta)^2(u^r/r)_-r^{-1}dx\,dt\ge c/\nu_n\to\infty$ for every fixed $s_0$ (under the data decay condition of item 9 with $s_0=0$; without it by item 22): the critical swirl must accumulate a large inflow-weighted space-time integral (through amplitude, duration or concentration).
20. (Sharper full-space representation; strain forced by every Case 3 sequence) A radial occupation bound $\int_0^Te^{-K(h)}\mathbb ER_h^{-3}dh\le1/r_0$ for the lifted backward process started at $x$, with $R_h$ its distance to the axis at age $h$ and $K(h)=\int_{t-h}^t\|(u^r/r)_+\|_\infty ds$ (so $K=K(T)$ as in item 17), the swirl derivative bound $\sup|\partial_zu^\theta|\le2G^{1/3}H_*^{2/3}$, and a divergence primitive $F$ of $\eta$ on $\mathbb R^5$ with $|F|\le\sqrt5V/r$ improve item 17 to $|\eta(x,t)|\le\frac{e^{K}}{r_0}\big(\frac{\sqrt{10}V}{\sqrt T}\mathcal E_T^{1/2}+2GB\big)$, $B=\sup|\partial_zu^\theta|$. Consequently, for every Case 3 sequence, in either alternative of item 11, $\int_{t_n-T_n^*}^{t_n}\|(u^r/r)_+\|_\infty dt\ge\frac13\log(1/\nu_n)-C\to\infty$ with $T_n^*=\ell_n^2L_n\nu_n^{-2/3}\to0$, $\ell_n=M_n/D_n$; and $\int\|(u^r/r)_+\|_\infty\ge\frac13\log(\alpha_n/L_n)-C$ on the horizon $r_n^2L_n^{1/3}\alpha_n^{-4/3}$.
21. (Critical swirl inequality) $Y(t)+\sqrt3\int_0^t\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}ds\le Y(0)+\frac32\int_0^t\|u^\theta\|_\infty\|\nabla u\|_2^2ds\le Y(0)+\frac34E_0S(t)$, $S$ the historical maximum of $|u^\theta|$. Hence the critical $L^1_{t,x}$ norm of the swirl source is $o(S)$, and every Case 3 sequence has $S(t_n)\nu_n\to\infty$ (by item 22 no decay condition on the data is needed): the maximal swirl velocity must be much larger than the record circulation $M_n\ell_n=1/\nu_n$. This is compatible with $S_n\le(G^2C_*)^{1/3}M_n\nu_n^{2/3}$, so it is again a necessary condition; a bound $S(t_n)=O(1/\nu_n)$ would exclude Case 3.
22. (Budgets without data decay; inward strain; injection across a plane) For fixed $s_0\in(0,T_*)$ and $B_0=\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty$, $\int(|\eta(t)|-B_0)_+dx\le\int_{s_0}^t\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}ds$; at the records the left side is $\ge c_1/(2\nu_n)$, which removes the decay condition from items 9, 19 and 21. The integrating factor in item 21 gives $\sqrt3\int_{s_0}^t\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}\le Y(s_0)\exp\big(3\int_{s_0}^t\|(u^r/r)_-\|_\infty\big)$, so the full-space inward strain over the history must reach $\frac13\log(1/\nu_n)-C$ (a rate: by a regularity criterion on $u^\theta/r$ stated in the literature, whose proof was not rechecked here, and the maximum principle for $q$ this clock diverges at every first singular time, Lemma 7.26, Remark (5)); and Case 3 is impossible if $u^r\ge0$ wherever $u^\theta\ne0$ on some interval $[s_0,T_*)$. Finally, the signed circulation moved by the swirl source across a plane $\{z=z_0\}$ per unit time is $\int_0^\infty(u^\theta)^2r^{-1}dr\le2G^{4/3}H^{2/3}$, so during the transport window it is $o(1/\nu_n)$.
23. ($L^p$ budgets for $\eta$ and a sharper inward-strain condition) For every fixed $2\le p<\infty$ and fixed $s_0$, $\|\eta(t)\|_{L^p}^2\le\|\eta(s_0)\|_{L^p}^2+\frac{p-1}2\int_{s_0}^t\|u^\theta/r\|_{L^{2p}}^4ds$ (there is no stretching term), while at the records $\|\eta(t_n)\|_{L^p}^p\ge c_p(D_n/r_n)^{p-1}/\nu_n$; and $W_p=\int(u^\theta)^2|u^\theta/r|^{2p-2}dx$ satisfies $W_p'+\frac{4p-2}p\|u^\theta/r\|_{L^{2p}}^{2p}\le(4p-2)\|(u^r/r)_-\|_\infty W_p$. Consequently $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|u^\theta/r\|_{L^4}^4ds\ge cM_n^2/r_n$ for large $n$, and the inward strain over the history satisfies $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|(u^r/r)_-\|_\infty ds\ge(\frac14-o(1))\log(D_n/r_n)$, which is stronger than item 22.
24. (Adjoint representation of the record circulation) Let $\psi\ge0$ be $r^2$ times the solution of the backward adjoint of the five-dimensional $\eta$ operator with final datum $\phi/r^2$, where $\phi=\chi(|(r,z)-(r^0,z^0)|/\rho_1)$ is the radial cutoff of the Lemma 7.8 disk used in Lemma 7.28(d) ($\chi$ smooth and nonincreasing, $\chi=1$ on $[0,\frac12]$, $\chi=0$ on $[1,\infty)$), with the sign of the record vorticity normalized by the reflection described there; then $\iint\psi\,r\,dr\,dz$ is conserved and the record circulation equals the adjoint-weighted circulation at time $s_0$ minus $\int\!\iint(u^\theta)^2r^{-1}\partial_z\psi$. Hence $G\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|u^\theta/r\|_\infty V_\psi\,ds\ge c_3/\nu_n$ with $V_\psi=\frac12\sup_r\int|\partial_z\psi|dz$ and $V_\psi(t_n)=1$, and within the long window either the record circulation is inherited (already present, adjoint weighted, at $t_n-cr_n/M_n$, for a fixed $c>0$) or $\sup V_\psi\gtrsim(\alpha_n\nu_n^{4/3})^{-1}\to\infty$: the adjoint weight of the core must then have unboundedly large vertical variation.
25. (Energy of the adjoint weight; annular depletion of the source) The adjoint weight of item 24 satisfies $\|\psi(s)\|_2^2+2\int_s^t\|\nabla\psi\|_2^2=\|\phi\|_2^2$, so the source pairing over any set $E$ is at most $(\|\phi\|_2^2/8\pi^2)^{1/2}(\iint_Eq^4)^{1/2}$, and on every terminal interval on which the record circulation is not inherited $\iint_{\{\partial_z\psi\ne0\}}(u^\theta/r)^4dx\,ds\ge c_4M_n^2/r_n$. A weighted identity for $\iint\Gamma^2\psi\,g(r)r^{-1}dr\,dz$ (the swirl circulation run forward against the adjoint weight run backward) gives, with a cutoff $g$ vanishing for $r<\rho/2$, $|\int\!\iint gP\partial_z\psi|^2\le CG^4m_\phi^2\tau\rho^{-8}(1+\tau\rho^{-2}+M\tau\rho^{-1})$, $m_\phi=\iint\phi\,r\,dr\,dz$. Along Case 3, for fixed $\vartheta\in(0,\frac12]$ and every window of length $\tau_n\le\lambda r_n^2$ (indeed $\tau_n=o(r_n^{7/2}D_nM_n^{-1/2})$), the part of the source pairing from radii $\ge\vartheta r_n$ is $o(1/\nu_n)$: at the beginning of such a window either the record circulation is already present (adjoint weighted), or it is produced on the window at radii $<\vartheta r_n$, where the adjoint weight is at most $4\vartheta^2e^{2K_+}$.
26. (Decay of the adjoint weight; near-axis origin) Nash's inequality, the conserved mass $\int\psi\,dx=2\pi m_\phi$ and the energy identity of item 25 give $\|\psi(t-h)\|_2^2\le Cm_\phi^2h^{-3/2}$, hence $\int_{t-\tau}^{t-\tau/2}|\iint g\psi w|^2ds\le Cm_\phi^2\tau^{-3/2}\rho^{-2}\int_{t-\tau}^t\|\nabla u\|_2^2$. Along Case 3, if $\lambda_n=o((r_n^3D_n^2/M_n)^{1/2})$ and $\lambda_n^{5/2}r_n^5D_n^2/e_n\to\infty$, where $e_n$ is the dissipation on $[t_n-\lambda_nr_n^2,t_n]$ (for instance for every fixed $\lambda$ when $\liminf r_n^5D_n^2>0$, which excludes the persistence alternative (I) of item 11), then for every fixed $\vartheta\in(0,\frac12]$ and $0<\epsilon<2c_3$, for all $s_1\in[t_n-\lambda_nr_n^2,t_n-\frac12\lambda_nr_n^2]$ outside a set of relative measure $o(1)$,
$$\tfrac2\pi\vartheta^2e^{2K_+(s_1,t_n)}\big(\mathcal E_n(s_1)+\textstyle\int_{s_1}^{t_n}\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1(r<\vartheta r_n)}\big)\ge(2c_3-\epsilon)/\nu_n:$$
where $\mathcal E_n(s)=\int_{r<\vartheta r_n}(|\eta(s)|-\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty)_+dx$ is finite: the record circulation must be present near the axis as excess vorticity or produced there, and if that near-axis critical budget is $O(1/\nu_n)$, the outward strain must satisfy $K_+(s_1,t_n)\ge\log(1/\vartheta)-C$.
27. (Pointwise decay of the adjoint weight; costs of inheritance; the adjoint-weighted swirl identity) (a) $\|\psi(t-h)\|_\infty\le\min\{1,C_\psi\mu h^{-3/2}\}$, $\mu=2\pi m_\phi$, with an absolute constant (duality with the forward five-dimensional advection-diffusion evolution, whose axis terms have a favourable sign), so $|\iint\psi w(t-h)|\le\frac1{2\pi}(\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty\mu+C_\psi\mu h^{-3/2}\mathcal B(s_0,t-h))$; along Case 3, inheritance of the record circulation at age $\lambda r_n^2$ forces the earlier swirl source budget to be $\ge c\lambda^{3/2}r_n^2D_n=c\lambda^{3/2}\alpha_n^2/\nu_n$. (b) Inheritance at a fixed positive fraction of the times of $[t_n-\lambda r_n^2,t_n-\frac12\lambda r_n^2]$ is impossible if $\liminf r_n^3D_n>0$ (it would force $\int_0^{t_n}\|\nabla\omega\|_2^2\gtrsim D_n$, while this integral is $o(D_n)$), and in any case forces $\int_{t_n-\lambda r_n^2}^{t_n}\|(u^r/r)_+\|_\infty\ge\log\alpha_n-\frac12\log(1+\log(r_nM_n))-C$ (Fisher information, entropy and inverse radial moment of the normalized adjoint density). (c) With $U=u^\theta$ and $\sigma=u^r/r$, $J=\iint\psi U^2$ satisfies $J'+\iint\psi(2U_r^2+2U_z^2+U^2/r^2)=-3\iint\sigma\psi U^2$; since $\iint P\psi_z=-2\iint\psi UU_z/r$, this gives $\sqrt2\int_{s_1}^t|\iint P\psi_z|\le J(s_1)+3\int_{s_1}^t\iint\psi\sigma_-U^2$, and a localized version with error $CG^2m_\phi\tau(\rho^{-5}+M\rho^{-4})$. Consequently every Case 3 sequence satisfies $F_n=\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\iint\psi_n\sigma_-(u^\theta)^2dr\,dz\,ds\ge(\frac{2\sqrt2}3c_3-o(1))/\nu_n$: an upper bound $\limsup\nu_nF_n<\frac{2\sqrt2}3c_3$ for the inward strain acting on the swirl seen by the adjoint density would exclude Case 3. No estimate proved here gives such a bound.
28. (An inflow clock) Integrating the identity of item 27(c) against the linear integrating factor $e^{-3K_-(s_0,s)}$, $K_-(s_0,s)=\int_{s_0}^s\|(u^r/r)_-\|_\infty$, gives $J(t)e^{-3K_-(s_0,t)}+\int_{s_0}^te^{-3K_-(s_0,s)}\iint\psi(2U_r^2+2U_z^2+U^2/r^2)\,ds\le J(s_0)$, hence $\sqrt2\int_{s_0}^{t'}|\iint P\psi_z|\le e^{3K_-(s_0,t')}J(s_0)$. Along Case 3, $J(s_0)=O(r_n/(D_n\nu_n))$, so $K_-(s_0,t_n)\ge\frac13\log(D_n/r_n)-C$ (sharper than item 23, since $D_n/r_n\gg1/\nu_n$), and the record circulation is produced, adjoint-weighted, only after $3K_-(s_0,\cdot)$ has reached $\log(D_n/r_n)-\omega_n$, for every $\omega_n\to\infty$ with $\omega_n\le\frac12\log(D_n/r_n)$ (the adjoint-weighted circulation present at that time is $o(1/\nu_n)$). In the formal scenario of Section 4.8, 8.3 (vi), $\frac13\log(D_n/r_n)$ is exactly the strain that compresses a ring from radius one to the cell radius, so this is a necessary condition, not an exclusion. The same argument applied to an arbitrary terminal cutoff gives the a priori bound $\|\eta(t)\|_\infty\le\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty+2^{-1/2}G^{1/2}\|q(s_0)\|_\infty^{3/2}e^{3K_-(s_0,t)}$ for all $t\ge s_0$ (Lemma 7.36(d)).
29. (The source-generated component) Fix $s_0\in(0,T_*)$ and split $\eta=\eta_A+h$, where $\eta_A$ solves the $\eta$ equation with the swirl source and zero data at $s_0$ and $h$ is the homogeneous evolution of $\eta(s_0)$. Then $|h|\le\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty$, $\|h\|_2\le\|\eta(s_0)\|_2$, and the radial strain of the Biot-Savart field of $rh\,e_\theta$ is at most $C_{\rm init}=(3+\frac3{2\sqrt\pi})\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty^{1/3}\|\eta(s_0)\|_2^{2/3}$ up to $T_*$; so the inward clock of item 28 and the outward clock of item 20 hold for the radial strain $\sigma_A$ of the source-generated meridional field $v_A$, up to $C_{\rm init}$ times the length of the window. The energy $E_A=\frac12\|v_A\|_2^2$ obeys $E_A'=W_A-\|\nabla v_A\|_2^2+\int\sigma_A(u^\theta)^2dx$, where $W_A$ is the compressive work of the complementary field $b-v_A$ on $v_A$, and at the record, for large $n$, $\frac12\int_{\mathcal T_n}|v_A(t_n)|^2dx\ge c_{\rm rec}r_nM_n^4/D_n^2$ with $c_{\rm rec}=729\pi^2\kappa_0^6/(2^{26}C_*^4)$, which gives $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}W_A^+ds+\frac{G^2}2\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|\nabla u\|_2^2ds\ge c_{\rm rec}r_nM_n^4/D_n^2$ (Lemma 7.37). In the formal scenario of 8.3 (vi), with $N=1/\nu_n$ and $a=\ell_n$, one has $r_nM_n^4/D_n^2=N^3a\to0$ (it equals $s^{7/20}$, $s=T_*-t$, on the time history of 8.2), so the energy statement (which drops the nonnegative component dissipation of (7.37.1)) gives no divergent energy obstruction there; compatibility with the full inequality of Lemma 7.37 is not checked; the strain statement says only that both diverging clocks are carried by azimuthal vorticity generated after $s_0$.
30. (A vertical-dispersion refinement of the logarithm) Let $p_h$ be the killed density, with respect to $\mu=r\,dr\,dz$, of the backward process of the record point on the annulus $\mathcal A_\vartheta=\{\vartheta r_n<r<Lr_n\}$, and let $s_n(\vartheta)=\sup_{T/2\le h\le T}\inf_c(\int p_h(z-c)^2d\mu)^{1/2}$ be its centred vertical spread over the second half of the persistence time $T=T_\vartheta$. In Lemma 7.16 the measure of the domain can be replaced by a second moment, and then alternative (I) at a scale $\vartheta\in(0,\frac14]$ forces $\alpha_n^2\le C_D\vartheta^{-5}\max\{2,\log(\pi C_NL^4(1+G)s_n(\vartheta)/(\epsilon\vartheta^6r_n))\}$ with $C_D=\frac89C_I$, that is, $s_n(\vartheta)\ge c(\vartheta)r_ne^{\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2/C_D}$ once $\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2>2C_D$. Bounding the spread by $\sqrt{2h}+V_hh$, with $V_h$ the maximum of $|u^z|$ on the annulus over the preceding time $h$, recovers Theorem 7.18 for every $\vartheta\le\frac14$ without its lower bound on $\vartheta^3r_nM_n$; bounding it by the energy $E_\vartheta$, the maximum over $[t_n-T_\vartheta,t_n]$ of $\int|u|^2dx$ over the annulus, gives a logarithm of $1+E_\vartheta/(\vartheta^3r_n)$, which along Case 3 is at least $\log(1/\nu_n)-C(\vartheta)$ and so cannot improve the velocity bound at leading logarithmic order in alternative (I) (Lemma 7.38). Hence $\log^+(s_n(\vartheta)/r_n)=o(\alpha_n^2)$ at one fixed $\vartheta$ would exclude (I) at that $\vartheta$; no such bound is proved.
31. (The dispersed part carries high vorticity) In alternative (I) at a fixed $\vartheta$, with $k_n=r_n\exp(\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2/(64C_D))$ and any measurable centre $c(h)$, the time average over $[T/2,T]$ of the killed mass on $\{v_n(t_n-h)\ge a_n/4\}\cap\{|z-c(h)|>k_n\}$ is at least $3\kappa_0\vartheta/(64\sqrt2)$ for large $n$ (Lemma 7.39). So, in time average, the backward density keeps a fixed fraction of its mass on the high set at vertical distances that are exponentially large in $\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2$ compared with $r_n$. This does not identify the spread of item 30 with the high-vorticity part: low-vorticity mass farther away could still dominate the centred second moment. Tightness of this high-vorticity part at heights $k$ with $\log(k/r_n)=o(\alpha_n^2)$ would exclude (I) at that $\vartheta$; it is not proved.
32. (Occupation and thinness) With $Y_h$ the value of $v_n$ at the stopped backward process, $\mathbb E[Y_h^2]-a_n^2$ equals twice the killed occupation of $|\nabla v_n|^2$ up to a source term, which (A) makes negligible; consequently the time-averaged killed occupation of $\ell_n^2|\nabla v_n|^2/a_n^2$ over $[T/2,T]$ is at most $(1+C_f\vartheta\nu_n^{1/3})C_D/(8e\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2)$, for every $\vartheta\in(0,\frac14]$ (Lemma 7.40(a)). Since the integral of $\omega^\theta$ over a square of side $\sigma$ in the meridional plane (up to sign the circulation of $(u^r,u^z)$ around its boundary) is at most $4\sigma M_n$ in absolute value, at no time before $t_n$ does $\{v_n\ge a_n/4\}\cap\mathcal A_\vartheta$ contain a square of side larger than $128\ell_n/(3\kappa_0\vartheta)$ (Lemma 7.40(b)); in the other direction, (A) and axis regularity give only that $\{v_n\ge a_n/8\}$ contains the disk of radius $c\vartheta\ell_n$ about each point of $\{v_n\ge a_n/4\}$ at least that far from $\partial\mathcal A_\vartheta$ (Remark (2) there), not a lower width for the high set itself. In alternative (I), moreover, at each age $h\le T_\vartheta$ the set $\{v_n(t_n-h)>a_n/4\}\cap\mathcal A_\vartheta$ has perimeter in the meridional plane at least $c\vartheta^3h/\ell_n$ and isoperimetric ratio at least $c\vartheta^2h^{1/2}/\ell_n$, with $c=c(\kappa_0,C_N)>0$ and no logarithm (Lemma 7.40(c): the duality bound for the killed density and the Gauss-Green formula); a bounded ratio at ages $h_n$ with $h_n/\ell_n^2\to\infty$ would exclude (I) at that $\vartheta$. In alternative (I) the backward density therefore keeps, in time average, mass $\gtrsim\vartheta$ on a high set that contains no large square and extends vertically far beyond $r_n$, while it rarely visits regions where $\ell_n|\nabla v_n|\gtrsim a_n$. A lower bound, by a fixed fraction of $a_n^2$, for the averaged occupation of $\ell_n^2|\nabla v_n|^2$ would exclude (I) at that $\vartheta$; a weighted coercivity bound comparing it with the mass on the high set would give one, and a Harnack-type estimate for the killed density at the scale $\ell_n/\vartheta$ with constants uniform in $n$ might give that, but none is proved, and the Péclet number of the drift at the core scale is $1/\nu_n\to\infty$. Items 30-32 are necessary conditions for alternative (I), not exclusions.
33. (Passive persistence: logarithm removed for affine drifts; a logarithm-free bound fails in general) In a planar whole-space model of alternative (I) without killing, inner boundary or weight: if $v$ is a bounded classical solution of $\partial_sv+b\cdot\nabla v=\Delta v+f$ with an affine drift $b=A(s)x+c(s)$ (continuous coefficients) and a bounded continuous source $f$, and satisfies the circulation bound $|\int mv(s)|\le V\|\nabla m\|_{L^1}$ for all smooth probability densities $m$ with $\nabla m\in L^1$ at every time $s\in[0,T]$, then $\|v(T)\|_{L^\infty(\mathbb R^d)}\le\frac{3\sqrt3}2d^{3/4}V/\sqrt T+2\int_0^T\|f\|_\infty$, and the source-free scale $V/\sqrt T$ is sharp up to a dimensional constant; for $d=2$, $f=0$ and $v(y,T)\ge a/2>0$, the substitution $V=M_n/(\vartheta r_n)$, $a=a_n$, $T=T_\vartheta$ suggested by alternative (I) would give $\alpha_n^2\le54\sqrt2(1+G)/(\epsilon c_a^2\vartheta^5)$, the bound of item 16 without the logarithm; this has not been transferred to alternative (I) (Proposition 7.41; the proof tests the circulation bound on the backward evolutions of a needle aligned with the forward noise covariance, which are fat on average by the exact identity $\int_0^T\operatorname{tr}E(s)^{-1}ds=d/2$, $E(s)$ being the covariance of the forward noise transported to time $s$). For nonlinear drifts this is false even with the amplitude ratios of Case 3 and the Hessian ratio bounded: the steady cellular drift $b_A$ with stream function $\psi_A=A\sin(2\pi x)z\chi(z/R_A)$, where $A\ge256$, $\chi$ is a fixed even smooth cutoff with $0\le\chi\le1$, equal to $1$ on $[-1,1]$ and supported in $(-2,2)$, and $R_A=10\sqrt{T_A}\,e^{2\pi AT_A}$ with $T_A=A^{-1}e^{A/32}$, and the antiperiodic scalar with initial value $\cos(\pi x)$ satisfy the circulation bound with $V=\frac12$ at every time, $M_AH_A\le(4+o(1))D_A^2$ for the suprema $M_A,D_A,H_A$ of $|b_A|,|\nabla b_A|,|\nabla^2b_A|$, and $v(0,0,T_A)>\frac12$ at the time $T_A$, with $T_A/\ell_A^2\ge c\log\mathrm{Pe}_A/\log\log\mathrm{Pe}_A\to\infty$, $\ell_A=M_A/D_A$, $\mathrm{Pe}_A=M_A\ell_A$ (Proposition 7.42); the amplitude comparison with the drift gradient holds for the rescaled scalar $D_Av$, which satisfies $|D_Av|\le D_A$, $D_Av(0,0,T_A)>D_A/2$ and the circulation bound with constant $D_A/2\le(2\pi+o(1))M_A$. In the reduced model $u=(F,-F_xz+V)$ with the prescribed sinusoidal cell $F=A(t)\sin(2\pi x)$ and $V$ the $2$-periodic solution of $V_t=V_{xx}-FV_x+F_xV$ with $V(x,0)=\sin(\pi x)/\pi$, the shear vorticity $w=V_x$ (initial value $\cos(\pi x)$) satisfies $0\le w(0,t)\le e^{-\pi^2t}$ for every measurable, locally bounded nonnegative amplitude history, so $w(0,t)\ge\frac12$ is possible only for $t\le(\log2)/\pi^2$, a fixed multiple of the viscous diffusion time of the cell, independent of the amplitude history; the velocity of this class is unbounded in $z$, and it gives exact unforced Navier-Stokes solutions only when $A'=-4\pi^2A$. For a prescribed frozen cell $F=Af$ with $f$ smooth, odd and $1$-periodic, $f>0$ on $(0,\frac12)$ and $f'(0)>0>f'(\frac12)$, the principal eigenvalue of the frozen shear-mode operator $V\mapsto V''-FV'+F'V$ on $(0,\frac12)$ with $V(0)=0$, $V'(\frac12)=0$ (the antiperiodic shear class) tends to $f'''(0)/f'(0)$ as $A\to\infty$, with a proved asymptotic expansion in powers of $1/A$; so whether frozen shear modes decay uniformly in the amplitude depends on the shape of the cell, and some smooth prescribed cells have growing shear modes at every sufficiently large amplitude. When the cell evolves by its own Proudman-Johnson equation (the coupled class), the computed evolutions (numerical observations, not proved) from $F(\cdot,0)=A_0f$, $f=\sin(2\pi x)+\frac16\sin^3(2\pi x)+\sin^5(2\pi x)$, $w(\cdot,0)=\cos(\pi x)$, show a decaying cell and a transient amplification of $w(0,t)$ by factors of about $2.1$, $28.7$ and $5.4\cdot10^4$ at $A_0=32,64,128$ before viscous decay; the comparison bound, which holds for every prescribed smooth odd $1$-periodic cell with locally bounded $F_x$ and $F/\sin(\pi x)$ nonincreasing on $(0,\frac12]$ at every time, does not pass to the coupled class through this condition, since the Proudman-Johnson evolution does not preserve it (proved); whether a bound uniform in the initial amplitude holds in the coupled class is not decided. These are model statements and exclude nothing about (I) (Proposition 7.42, Remark (5)). So a logarithm-free exclusion of (I) must use structure not captured by these passive estimates; possible sources include the exact coupling $r\eta=\omega^\theta=\partial_zu^r-\partial_ru^z$ of $\eta$ to its own drift, energy constraints, and the weighted annular geometry (no sufficiency claim for any one of them is proved).
34. (Active model: fixed affine drift, logarithm-free survival) If a smooth planar drift with $|b|\le M$ agrees on a deterministic relatively open spacetime region with one fixed field $Ax+a$, $\operatorname{tr}A=0$, $S=A_{21}-A_{12}\ne0$, then the backward diffusion started in that region survives in it up to time $t$ with probability at most $4M/(|S|\sqrt{\pi t})$; hence if $|S|\ge\kappa D$ with $\kappa>0$ and survival has probability at least $p>0$, then $t\le16\ell^2/(\pi\kappa^2p^2)$, $\ell=M/D$, uniformly in $\mathrm{Pe}=M\ell$ for fixed $\kappa,p>0$ (Proposition 7.43; the velocity of the unrestricted affine comparison process, which agrees with the actual drift along the path before exit, is Gaussian with covariance trace at least $S^2t/4$). This is a model in which the drift curl plays the role of the persistent vorticity: it does not exclude alternative (I) and does not resolve the planar question of Section 4.8 (subsection 8.3, item (iii)). If the bounded mild solution is spatially real analytic at positive times, exact agreement with a nonconstant affine field on an open set is impossible by analytic continuation and boundedness; the exclusion results here do not depend on this supplementary observation. Hypothesis (A) supplies no approximation by one fixed affine field on the sampled region; a uniform drift error $\varepsilon M$ over $t=K\ell^2$ gives, by a stopped Girsanov-Pinsker estimate, an error of at most $\varepsilon\,\mathrm{Pe}\sqrt{K/8}$ in the survival probability.
35. (Circulation absorption at the axis; a swirl bound free of the swirl history) For $0<h<t<T_*$ and all $(r,z)$, $|\Gamma(r,z,t)|\le G\min\{1,r^2e^{2K_-(t-h,t)}/(4h)\}$ with $K_-(t-h,t)=\int_{t-h}^t\|(u^r/r)_-\|_\infty$, hence $\|u^\theta/r\|_\infty\le Ge^{2K_-(t-h,t)}/(4h)$; for the infinite-energy Burgers vortex, optimizing this bound for $\|u^\theta/r\|_\infty$ over $h$ loses exactly the factor $e$. Consequently $\|\eta(t)\|_\infty\le\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty+G^2e^{3K_-(s_0-h,t)}/(8\sqrt2h^{3/2})$ for $0<h<s_0<t$ (with $16$ in place of $8\sqrt2$ by the Remark after Corollary 7.45), and along a Case 3 sequence the inward strain must satisfy $K_-(s_0-h,t_n)\ge\frac13\log(D_n/r_n)-C$. These are tools: they introduce no contradiction among the scalar size constraints of Section 4.8 (the inward clock allowed by the gradient bound, $\int D\,dt$, is polynomially larger than the required logarithm on every power-law history with $D=(T_*-t)^{-b}$, $b>1$), although no joint swirl, vorticity and strain history satisfying them is constructed (Lemma 7.44, Corollary 7.45).
36. (Self-compression against the swirl maximum; a swirl clock) With $m=\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2$ and $\Pi=-\int\frac{u^r}rq^2r\,dx$ the compression term of the critical swirl identity, $|\Pi|\le\frac12m\|\eta\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}$ whenever $\eta\in L^1$, and the same bound holds for the compression by the source-generated part, whose $L^1$ norm is at most the source budget $\mathcal B(s_0,t)$. Consequently $Y(t)+\sqrt3\mathcal B(s_0,t)\le Y(s_0)\exp\big(3C_{\rm init}(t-s_0)+\frac{\sqrt3}2\int_{s_0}^tm\big)$, and along a Case 3 sequence $\frac{\sqrt3}2\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2\ge\log(1/\nu_n)-C(s_0)$, while this swirl clock is at most $AG^{4/3}C_*^{2/3}\nu_n^{1/3}$ on every window $[t_n-A/D_n,t_n]$; in alternative (I) at a fixed $\vartheta$ the source lower bound of Lemma 7.40, Remark (5), raises the requirement to $\log(r_n^2D_n)+c\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2-C$. This is a tool, not an exclusion: a power-law history satisfies it with polynomial room when its swirl exponent exceeds $\frac12$, which the swirl bound $\|u^\theta\|_\infty\le(G^2C_*)^{1/3}M\nu^{2/3}$, $\nu=D/M^2$ (Lemma 1.5 with (A)), allows except on a thin set of exponents next to the self-similar ones, where the condition fails; on its boundary $4b=2a+3$ (exponents $M=s^{-a}$, $D=s^{-b}$, $s=T_*-t$) the available clock is only logarithmic and compatibility depends on its coefficient (Lemma 7.46, Remark (2)).
37. (The comparison horizons $T''_\vartheta$) Assume $G>0$. A one-sided comparison using $\pm\eta+c\Gamma^2$ bounds the signed stopped-source contribution in the direction of the record by $(\frac1{24}+o(1))a_n$ on the horizons $T''_\vartheta=\kappa_0\vartheta^5r_n^4D_n/(256G^2)$, uniformly for $\vartheta\in[\vartheta_n^\flat,\frac14]$ with $\vartheta_n^\flat\to0$. For every such $\vartheta$ the net inner-boundary contribution exceeds $a_n/4$ at some age $0<h\le T''_\vartheta$, in either alternative of item 11, and the outward strain on $\{\vartheta r_n<r<Lr_n\}$ integrates over $T''_\vartheta$ to at least $\max\{\frac12\log(1/\vartheta),\log(1/\vartheta)-\frac12\log\alpha_n\}-C$. Equivalently, a bound by $a_n/4$ for that contribution at every age $0<h\le T''_\vartheta$, at one such $\vartheta$ (along infinitely many $n$), would exclude Case 3 without any persistence bound; a bound at a single chosen age would not suffice. No such bound is proved. These horizons satisfy $T''_\vartheta\gg r_n^2$ for fixed $\vartheta$, but not necessarily at shrinking scales. These are necessary conditions only; they give no lower bound on the shorter window of alternative (I) (Theorem 7.18, Remark (4)).
**Theorem 2 (the sufficient target is the whole problem).** Define $d(A)=\limsup_nD(t_n-A/D_n)/D_n$. The statement "every Case 3 sequence satisfies $\lim_{A\to\infty}d(A)=0$" (TARGET-3) is equivalent to "no Case 3 sequence exists".

**Compatibility.** Assume $G>0$ (for $G=0$ the test fails, since Corollary 7.6 then contradicts the Case 3 assumptions, and under the data decay condition $\eta_0\in L^1(\mathbb R^3)$ so does Corollary 7.9; Section 8.2). For $M=s^{-3/4}$, $D=s^{-7/5}$, $r=s^{11/20}$ and $H=D^2/M$ ($s=T_*-t$), the comparisons in Section 4.8 involving these four scalar functions (the scalar restrictions 1, 3-10 and 15) pass, including the comparisons between the required budgets and their scalar upper bounds; no actual dissipation, swirl, source or transport history is assigned. For this law $r^5D^2\to\infty$, so a Case 3 sequence with these rates could only satisfy alternative (II) of item 11. For the same $M,D,H$ and $r=s^{5/8}$, the listed scalar comparisons and the pre-logarithmic necessary conditions for alternative (I) pass, but $\alpha^2=s^{-1/20}$ violates the logarithmic necessary condition for (I) of item 16 (Theorem 7.18); a Case 3 sequence with these rates, if one existed, would therefore have to satisfy alternative (II). In exponent form ($M=s^{-a}$, $D=s^{-b}$, $r=s^c$) the restrictions are linear inequalities; an exact rational vertex enumeration shows that the region allowed for alternative (II) is nonempty (it contains $(a,b,c)=(\frac85,\frac{14}5,1)$ with margin $\frac15$ in every inequality, and has the self-similar point $(\frac12,1,\frac12)$ as a vertex of its closure), while in exponent form alternative (I) is empty. The point $(\frac85,\frac{14}5,1)$ violates the condition $1+c-2a>0$ that finite dissipation imposes on continuous histories (at a gradient record the shape bound (A) gives $\|\nabla u\|_2^2\ge\pi^2rM^2/(8C_*^2)$, and for strictly increasing $D$ every time is a record), which the listed restrictions do not include (it implies $a<1$ and $b<2$, which is also what the classical time integrability of finite-energy solutions gives); with this condition and the swirl-clock condition $4b\ge2a+3$ of item 36 (Lemma 7.46) added the region left for (II) by these scalar comparisons is still nonempty (it contains $(\frac23,\frac76,\frac5{12})$ with margin $\frac1{12}$), the two scalar laws above satisfy it, and along a sequence it gives no new condition on the long windows (Section 8.2). These are consistency tests only; they are not Navier-Stokes solutions and are not evidence that Case 3 occurs.

### 1.3 First missing implication

The first missing implication is TARGET-3 itself: an estimate forcing $\lim_{A\to\infty}\limsup_nD(t_n-A/D_n)/D_n=0$, with $n\to\infty$ first and then $A\to\infty$, or the localized substitute stated in Section 4.8, which asks for one fixed $A$ with a uniform strict gap, $\limsup_n\sup_{|x-x_n|\le(R_0+A)\ell_n}|\omega(x,t_n-A/D_n)|/D_n<\kappa_0$ (a bound below $\kappa_0$ for each $n$ separately, or vanishing on a fixed disk, would not suffice). By Theorem 2 this is not an intermediate step but the entire remaining problem. Since $A$ is fixed while $n\to\infty$, these are normalized times $A\ll\alpha_n$, where the persistence of Lemma 7.1 already holds for all sufficiently large $n$; a decay estimate proving TARGET-3 would therefore contradict that persistence and exclude Case 3. The order of limits matters (decay of $D(t_n-A_n/D_n)/D_n$ along normalized times $A_n\gg\alpha_n$ that vary with $n$ does not suffice), and a localized version must control the vorticity on the ball of radius $(R_0+A)\ell_n$ that contains the backward characteristics, not on a fixed disk. The principal proved estimates are local in time (valid on the transport window of physical length $\delta r_n/M_n$, normalized length $\delta\alpha_n$), integral budgets (energy, dissipation, $\eta$ source, critical $L^1$ norm), full-space representations and adjoint identities on longer horizons with their strain, occupation and dispersion consequences, and the dichotomy of items 11 and 16; none of them closes the argument. The remaining problem therefore splits into two concrete parts, neither of which is settled here: exclude the persistent killed homogeneous contribution of alternative (I) (a killed average at least $a_n/2$ at every age $0<h\le T_\vartheta$, $T_\vartheta\asymp r_n^2$), at its fixed scale $\vartheta$, in the remaining regime $\alpha_n^2=O(\log(r_nM_n))$ and $r_n^5D_n^2\to0$; and exclude alternative (II), in which a net signed fraction of the stopped representation of the record comes from the inner boundary $r=\vartheta_nr_n$, $\vartheta_n\to0$, together with the integrated bounds on the spatial supremum of the outward strain $(u^r/r)_+$ stated in item 11; that is, bound this net inner-boundary contribution, which the velocity bound of item 14 does not control. Precisely, to exclude alternative (II) it would suffice to prove that for every presumed Case 3 sequence and every sequence $\vartheta_n\to0$ the net contribution $J_n(\vartheta_n)$ of Theorem 7.12, which retains the supremum over all ages $0<h\le T_{\vartheta_n}$, is at most $a_n/4$ for all sufficiently large $n$; a bound $\limsup_nJ_n(\vartheta_n)/a_n\le\frac14$ would not suffice. Neither proposed exclusion is proved in this report; the scalar and model tests identify limitations of the arguments examined, but they do not establish logical independence from the full Navier-Stokes hypotheses or certify the absence of a proof elsewhere. Section 6, item 2, writes the absolute value of this contribution exactly as the unsigned contribution times a sign-mixing factor, and shows with a prescribed-drift scalar model that expansion, incompressibility and scalar diffusion alone do not make that factor small. (The theorem does not identify a material core stretched by a definite factor.) For the first part, item 18 shows that the logarithm in item 14 cannot be removed using only the structural properties of item 14, even together with finite energy and the instantaneous Biot-Savart law; a proof must use the Navier-Stokes dynamics. Items 30-32 refine the logarithm: it can be replaced by the logarithm of the centred vertical spread of the killed backward density of the record point in the annulus, measured in units of $r_n$ (a refinement, not an identification of the two logarithms). So, at a fixed $\vartheta$, any one of the following would exclude (I) at that scale, and to exclude the first part it is needed at every fixed $\vartheta\in(0,\frac14]$ (or at the scale furnished by a presumed occurrence of (I)): a bound $\log^+(s_n(\vartheta)/r_n)=o(\alpha_n^2)$ for that spread; tightness of the part of the density that lies on the high set $\{v_n\ge a_n/4\}$ at heights $k$ with $\log(k/r_n)=o(\alpha_n^2)$; or a lower bound, by a fixed fraction of $a_n^2$, for the time-averaged occupation of $\ell_n^2|\nabla v_n|^2$ (gradient-weighted; a lower bound for the unweighted time spent between the levels $a_n/8$ and $a_n/4$ would not suffice without a coercivity estimate); or a bounded isoperimetric ratio of $\{v_n(t_n-h_n)>a_n/4\}\cap\mathcal A_\vartheta$ in the meridional plane at some ages $h_n\le T_\vartheta$ with $h_n/\ell_n^2\to\infty$ (item 32, a logarithm-free criterion: in (I) the ratio at age $h$ is at least $c\vartheta^2h^{1/2}/\ell_n$). Bounds from velocity or energy alone give none of these at leading logarithmic order. Item 33 shows that for source-free passive data with the circulation bound at every time the logarithm is removable for affine drifts but not in general, even with the displayed passive amplitude ratios and the Hessian ratio bounded; so the first part needs structure not captured by passive estimates, for instance the exact coupling $r\eta=\partial_zu^r-\partial_ru^z$ of $\eta$ to its drift, energy constraints, or the geometry. A corresponding planar model question is a Reynolds-number-uniform persistence bound for two-dimensional Navier-Stokes vorticity: for constants $M,D,c_0>0$, $\ell=M/D$, and a smooth unforced solution on $\mathbb R^2\times[0,T]$ (viscosity $1$) with $|u|\le M$, $|\nabla u|\le D$, $|\nabla^2u|\le H$ and $MH\le C_*D^2$ throughout, should $\omega(0,T)\ge c_0D$ force $T\le C(c_0,C_*)\ell^2$? The arguments here do not resolve it. For every fixed $P_0$, compactness and Theorem 5.1 of Koch, Nadirashvili, Seregin and Sverak give $T\le C(c_0,C_*,P_0)\ell^2$ when the Reynolds number $M\ell$ is at most $P_0$, with no constant uniform as $P_0\to\infty$. Without any further assumption, a curl-structure spectral gap on the set where $\omega>c_0D/2$, a martingale survival estimate along backward stochastic trajectories and a Nash inequality give $T/\ell^2\le(32/c_0^2)\log((256\,\mathrm{Pe}^2/c_0^2+64\log(2/p))/p^2)$ with $p=c_0/(4-c_0)$, and, along every family with the same $c_0$ and $\mathrm{Pe}\to\infty$, $\limsup T/(\ell^2\log\mathrm{Pe})\le2/c_0^2$ (8.3 (iii)); the question is whether this logarithm, qualitatively analogous to the one in Theorem 7.18, can be removed. The proof uses only bounded incompressible drift-diffusion and the circulation inequality $|\int m\,\omega\,dx|\le V\|\nabla m\|_{L^1}$ at every time, for smooth probability densities $m$ with $\nabla m\in L^1$ (inequality (C) of Proposition 7.41) with $V=M$, and by Proposition 7.42 no argument using only these properties (with $V=O(M)$, comparable amplitudes and $MH=O(D^2)$) can remove it: for such passive scalars the bound is sharp up to a factor $\log\log\mathrm{Pe}$. A Fisher-information argument (Section 8.3 (iii)) removes the logarithm, for either sign of $\omega$, under an additional uniform bound on $\int_{T/2}^T\sup_x\|S\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,dt$, $S=\frac12(\nabla u+\nabla u^{\mathsf T})$, which the hypotheses do not provide, and we found no way to use nonnegativity of $\omega$ to remove it. For initial vorticity in $L^1(\mathbb R^2)$, one partial answer needs neither the shape condition nor compactness: the vorticity solves a drift-diffusion equation with divergence-free drift, so Nash's argument gives $\|\omega(T)\|_\infty\le\min\{\|\omega(0)\|_\infty,C\|\omega(0)\|_{L^1}/T\}$ with an absolute constant, and $\omega(0,T)\ge c_0D$ then forces $T/\ell^2\le C\|\omega(0)\|_{L^1}\|\omega(0)\|_\infty/(c_0^2\|u(0)\|_\infty^2)$ (Section 4.8, 8.3 (iii)). This ratio is bounded for Lamb-Oseen vortices and, in terms of $N$, for $N$ disjoint one-signed vortex cores with uniformly bounded normalized profiles and comparable radii; the same argument on a period cylinder gives $T/\ell^2\le C/c_0^2$ for the Kelvin-Stuart cat's-eye data uniformly in their parameters, and shears, planar circular flows $u=v(r,t)e_\theta$ and Taylor-Green cells obey the bound by direct computation. A family violating a uniform bound must therefore have this ratio (or its per-period analogue) unbounded, or infinite absolute vorticity mass; this does not determine the signs or the geometry of the vorticity, and no counterexample was found. A numerical illustration tested one family that the $L^1$ criterion does not cover: pseudo-spectral unforced two-dimensional Navier-Stokes with viscosity $\nu$ on periodic anisotropic cells, with an initial vorticity strip $D_0\cos(\pi x)$ superposed on bounded tall extensional rolls, dimensionless strain coefficients $1,2,4$ and strip Péclet numbers $50$ to $200$. Over the saved times and grid points, the maximum of the dimensionless quotient $\sqrt{\nu t}\,\|\omega(t)\|_\infty/\sup_{s\le t}\|u(s)\|_\infty$ lay between about $0.38$ and $0.43$, and the vorticity at the centre of the strip on the inflow line stayed below $D_0e^{-\pi^2\nu t}$, the heat decay of the strip profile (the maximum along the inflow line did not). Runs at Péclet number $400$ increased the grid maximum of $|\omega|$ by up to about $60$ percent between saved times, against the maximum principle, and are discarded as unresolved; no run had an independent convergence check. These sampled observations of one family prove nothing about the uniform bound. The closest quantitative results we found (Gallay and Slijepcevic, arXiv:1402.6563, for the periodic strip) bound the lifetime of large vorticity by a power of the Reynolds number, and Gallay's lecture notes (arXiv:1411.5156, Section 1, Theorems 1.2 and 1.3) state that on the whole plane the best available bound is $\|u(t)\|_\infty\le K\|u_0\|_\infty(1+\|\omega_0\|_\infty t)$ (after Zelik), a time-independent bound in the general case being described there as not available, while in the periodic strip of width $L$ (viscosity 1) $\|u(t)\|_\infty+t^{1/2}\|\omega(t)\|_\infty\le K\|u_0\|_\infty(1+R_u^5)$ with $R_u=L\|u_0\|_\infty$ the initial Reynolds number. A search of the papers citing these two (titles, and abstracts of the closest ones) found no Reynolds-uniform estimate; this is not a complete survey. A further, model-assisted literature consultation (Section 5) also found neither a proof nor a counterexample; two of the items it reported were read here. For planar vorticity satisfying a Gaussian localization condition, Gallay and Rodrigues (Ann. Fac. Sci. Toulouse Math. 17 (2008), Theorem 1.2 and Remark 4) give an upper bound on the time needed to approach the Oseen vortex in $L^1$ that grows like $\exp(\exp(CR^2))$ in the Reynolds number $R=\|\omega_0\|_{L^1}/\nu$ of the data (absolute vorticity; the bound also depends on the Gaussian moment and the tolerance). Theorem 1.3 of Seregin, Silvestre, Sverak and Zlatos (arXiv:1010.6025) gives a bounded smooth divergence-free planar drift with bounded derivatives of every order and a nonconstant bounded stationary passive scalar, so no bound $\|\nabla\theta(T)\|_\infty\le C\|\theta\|_\infty/\sqrt T$ with $C$ independent of the drift holds for passive scalars. This example does not by itself settle the circulation form (a scalar $\operatorname{curl}U$ with $U$ bounded), and the construction does not impose the Navier-Stokes coupling. For the second part, items 11, 16, 17 and 20 give the necessary outward strain; no upper bound for the time-integrated near-axis strain on horizons $o(r_n^2)$ beyond $\int\|\nabla u\|_\infty$ is proved here or was identified in the inspected sources.

## 2. Method

The argument proceeds on the physical solution and on its normalized blow-ups:

1. elementary axisymmetric facts (circulation maximum principle, swirl record identity, swirl-Hessian inequality, parabolic upper bound $D\lesssim M^2$, $\eta$ maximum principle with source);
2. the branch reduction, by positive-viscosity rescalings and published Liouville theorems;
3. compactness of the gradient-record rescaling, with an explicit near/far split of the canonical pressure that rules out a parasitic harmonic pressure term in the limit;
4. planarity of the limit from the rotation generator of the axisymmetry;
5. backward rigidity of planar vorticity via a mollified commutator estimate and the bi-Lipschitz flow;
6. an energy bound by copying a velocity ball around the symmetry torus;
7. a localized comparison argument for $\eta$ in the meridional half plane, with an exponential barrier, giving persistence on the transport window; then dissipation and source-budget consequences;
8. a weighted $L^1$ estimate for $\eta$ (critical norm), and a drift-independent smoothing estimate for the killed $\eta$ evolution: with the measure $r\,dr\,dz$ the modified drift $(2/r-u^r,-u^z)$ is divergence free, so a Nash inequality on annuli gives an $L^2\to L^\infty$ bound independent of the velocity; combined with a stopped backward stochastic representation of $\eta$ this yields the improved dissipation bound and the annular/axis dichotomy.
9. an $L^q$ dissipation inequality for the Dirichlet Green function of the killed $\eta$ evolution, with $q-1$ the reciprocal of a logarithm, in which the drift drops out because it is divergence free for $r\,dr\,dz$; combined with the identity $\eta\,r\,dr\,dz=\omega^\theta\,dr\,dz=(\partial_zu^r-\partial_ru^z)\,dr\,dz$ it bounds killed averages of $\eta$ by the meridional velocity (item 14), and applied on the record disk and on truncated annuli it gives items 15 and 16.
10. a backward stochastic representation of $\eta$ lifted to $\mathbb R^5$ (where the inner boundary disappears), with an inverse-square moment bound by a supermartingale, a Nash bound and an entropy bound with explicit dependence on the integrated radial strain, and velocity averaging by integration by parts (item 17); an explicit Gaussian construction in a hyperbolic strain for item 18; for item 20, an occupation estimate for the radial component of the lifted process from the supermartingale $(r^2+\varepsilon)^{-1/2}$ and a divergence primitive of $\eta$ that moves the derivative onto the transition density;
11. a weighted energy identity for $\Gamma=ru^\theta$ with the critical weight $r^{-3}$ and the one-dimensional Hardy inequality with constant $\frac49$ (item 19); splitting the dissipation into its radial and axial parts gives the constant $\sqrt3$ of item 21, and the inflow term is bounded by the swirl velocity times the enstrophy.
12. for item 33, an explicit Gaussian computation for affine drifts, in which the circulation bound is tested on the backward evolutions of a needle at the record time and a dyadic iteration removes the dependence on the numerical $L^\infty$ bound (qualitative boundedness is used in the limit), and an explicit steady cellular drift with an antiperiodic scalar, controlled by two exit estimates for the backward diffusion.
13. for item 34, the observation that in a fixed trace-free affine drift the velocity along the backward path is a Gaussian process whose covariance trace grows at least like $S^2t/4$ (Cayley-Hamilton for $e^{-At}$), so that the bound $|b|\le M$ on the survival event is controlled by a one-dimensional Gaussian interval estimate.
14. for items 24-28, the adjoint weight of the record circulation ($r^2$ times the solution of the backward adjoint of the five-dimensional $\eta$ operator whose final datum is a cutoff of the record disk divided by $r^2$), with its conserved mass, its energy identity, an $L^2$ decay bound from Nash's inequality, a pointwise decay bound by duality with the forward five-dimensional evolution, weighted energy identities that pair the squared swirl circulation with the adjoint weight (also with radial cutoffs), and, for item 28, an exponential integrating factor of the inward strain; for item 27(b), the vorticity energy inequality and an entropy-dissipation argument for the normalized adjoint density (Fisher information, an inverse radial moment controlled by the outward strain, a second-moment bound and the Gaussian entropy inequality); for item 29, the split of $\eta$ into its source-generated part and the homogeneous evolution of earlier data, whose radial strain is bounded by a constant times $\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty^{1/3}\|\eta(s_0)\|_2^{2/3}$, and an energy identity for the source-generated meridional field; for item 35, a maximum-principle comparison of the circulation with an explicit exponential barrier that uses its quadratic vanishing at the axis (the barrier expresses the absorption at the axis of the backward circulation process, whose squared radius has the diffusion part of a squared Bessel process of dimension $0$); for item 36, an exact five-dimensional kernel integral, evaluated by composing two order-one Riesz potentials in $\mathbb R^4$, which together with the oddness of the kernel and the positivity of $q^2/r$ bounds the compression term by one half of the squared swirl maximum times the $L^1$ norm of $\eta$ (or of its source-generated part), followed by a differential inequality for the swirl energy; for item 37, a one-sided comparison of the signed stopped source with $\pm\eta+c\Gamma^2$, combined with the stopped representation and an inverse-square hitting estimate for the radial process; for item 32 (Lemma 7.40(c)), the duality bound for the killed annular density, and for the isoperimetric criterion of Section 8.3 (iii) the corresponding bound for the whole-plane evolution, both followed by truncation of the positive excess and the Gauss-Green formula for sets of finite perimeter; for the logarithmic planar bound of Section 8.3 (iii), a coercivity inequality derived from the circulation bound and applied to diffusion killed by a smooth bounded potential at low vorticity values, a martingale survival estimate along backward trajectories, a Nash inequality, a Brownian displacement bound and an area comparison; for Section 6 item 6, an application of Theorem 1.2 of [LNZ16] at the record points, using the energy bound and the absolute continuity of the dissipation integral.

Verification. The arguments presented as proofs in Section 4 were checked by hand and the stated algebraic identities, including those of the axisymmetric system and the swirl-Hessian identity, with a computer-algebra system; the manuscript was then reviewed adversarially, lemma by lemma, by an independent automated reviewer. These reviews found no fatal error; the fixable gaps they found (an undefined branch hypothesis, a missing cutoff Hessian bound and a missing uniform-convergence argument in the pressure lemma, a quantifier in the equivalence statement, the threshold dependence in the long-window lemma, sup attainment in the dissipation lemma) and overstatements in an earlier version of the gap discussion were repaired in the text. Supplementary model claims whose proofs are omitted have the more limited status described in Sections 3 and 6. Section 5 describes the verification in detail.

## 3. Findings in brief

* The axis-escape limit is a bounded ancient planar Euler flow with nonzero, rigidly transported vorticity. Generic bounded 2D Euler flows of this kind exist (shear flows, steady vortices), so its qualitative properties give no contradiction by themselves; its quantitative derivative bounds exclude Case 3 only when $C_*<\sqrt7/8$ (Remark (iii) after Lemma 4.1).
* Because the planar vorticity is rigid, the sufficient target TARGET-3 is equivalent to the emptiness of the case.
* On the physical solution the record vorticity persists for a physical time $\delta r_n/M_n$, which is the time for meridional transport at the maximal speed to cover a fixed fraction of the distance to the axis; this costs dissipation $\gtrsim r_n^2M_n$, which must tend to zero.
* A Case 3 solution is never of Type I. This excludes any argument relying on a global Type I bound, and any argument relying on a local Type I bound valid uniformly on a neighbourhood that contains the long-window vorticity points. The receding-axis mechanism of Constantin, Ignatova and Vicol (arXiv:2609.20762) assumes local bounds on a fixed ball that imply a local gradient Type I bound there; it is excluded for Case 3 only when that ball contains these points uniformly in $n$ (in particular when the bounds hold globally).
* The swirl amplitude is small at the records ($S_n/M_n\to0$). For each fixed $\vartheta>0$, over windows of length $O(r_n^2)$, the adjoint-weighted source pairing from radii $\ge\vartheta r_n$ is $o(1/\nu_n)$ (item 25), so the adjoint-weighted record circulation is inherited from the beginning of the window or produced at smaller radii. Pointwise, the source satisfies $|\partial_z(q^2)|\le4G^{4/3}C_*^{2/3}D_n\nu_n^{1/3}/(\vartheta^3r_n^3)$ at radii $\ge\vartheta r_n$ up to $t_n$ (since $|q|\le G/r^2$, $|\partial_zu^\theta|\le2G^{1/3}H^{2/3}$ and (A)), so in the full-space representation of $\eta$ at the record its contribution from those radii over a terminal window of length $\lambda r_n^2$ is at most $4\lambda G^{4/3}C_*^{2/3}\vartheta^{-3}(D_n/r_n)\nu_n^{1/3}=o(D_n/r_n)$; the $\eta$ at the record (of size $D_n/r_n\to\infty$) therefore comes from the beginning of the window or from paths at smaller radii. The global source budgets ($L^1_tL^\infty_x$, and the critical $L^1_{t,x}$ norm from any fixed positive time, which must reach the core Reynolds number $1/\nu_n$) are necessary comparisons, not exclusions. Their projected scalar comparisons are compatible with the tests of Section 4.8; those tests assign no source or swirl history, so they do not verify any actual source budget.
* The branch hypothesis (NSE) has a quantitative local content (Lemma 2.4): at every sufficiently high swirl first crossing the meridional velocity exceeds any fixed multiple of the swirl level within a distance $\delta\to0$ of the axis projection of the record and within a time $\delta^2$ before it, where $\delta$ is much larger than the distance of the record to the axis. This is only a necessary condition, and it is not tied to the gradient records. A near-axis stagnation point flow of the size used in the formal scenarios below makes the swirl-scaled weighted tail diverge, so (NSE) would hold if such a flow were present, with uniform constants, at every sufficiently high swirl first crossing and every maximizing point within a fixed number of swirl time units before it; this universal occurrence has not been constructed or verified, and the stagnation ball needs kinetic energy of order $N^5a$, with $N=1/\nu_n$ and $a=\ell_n$ as in the formal scenario of 8.3 (vi), which restricts the parameters (for example $a=N^{-6}$); (NSE) gives no upper bound for the swirl source and no contradiction with Case 3.
* The circulation bound keeps the swirl source small away from the axis for times $\ll r_n^2$, much longer than the transport window. What limits the persistence argument beyond the window is $\eta$ entering from radii $o(r_n)$. Theorem 7.12 (item 11) gives, on a subsequence, either a killed homogeneous contribution at least $a_n/2$ for every age $0<h\le T_\vartheta$ at one fixed annular scale $\vartheta$, which forces $r_n^5D_n^2\to0$, or a net signed inner-boundary contribution greater than $a_n/4$ at scales $\vartheta_n\to0$, which forces the integrated outward strain bounds of alternative (II); neither alternative identifies a single material core.
* In the meridional variables $\eta$ solves a forced advection-diffusion equation for a drift that is divergence free for $r\,dr\,dz$ (the forcing is the swirl source; the killed average is its homogeneous contribution), and $\eta\,r\,dr\,dz$ is the curl of the meridional velocity. On a bounded smooth domain $\mathcal O$ with $\overline{\mathcal O}\subset\{\vartheta r_n\le r\le Lr_n\}$, if the absolute killed average is at least $a$ for every age in $[h_1,h_2]$, Lemma 7.16 gives $a\le V(\vartheta r_n)^{-1}\sqrt{e\Lambda/(h_2-h_1)}$, where $V$ bounds the meridional velocity on $\mathcal O$ over the time interval, $\Lambda=\max\{1,\log(K\mu(\mathcal O)/h_1)\}$, $K=C_NL^2/(\vartheta^3r_n)$, and $d\mu=r\,dr\,dz$. This bounds the transport window by logarithmically many viscous core times and confines the persistent killed homogeneous contribution of alternative (I) to $\alpha_n^2=O(\log(r_nM_n))$; for faster growth of $\alpha_n$ one has $J_n(\vartheta)>a_n/4$ throughout $[\vartheta_n^\sharp,\frac14]$, so only the axis alternative remains, with $\vartheta_n=\vartheta_n^\sharp$.
* The logarithm in that bound is not an artefact: a Gaussian density in a hyperbolic strain, with a tangent perturbation of the stream function, attains it within the structure used. Since the construction has finite energy and its strain is the Biot-Savart strain of its own vorticity, the remaining logarithmic regime of alternative (I) requires an argument using the Navier-Stokes dynamics (the evolution of the vorticity or the energy-dissipation law), not only these instantaneous properties.
* In alternative (I) the logarithm has a concrete meaning: the backward density of the record point must spread vertically, over a distance exponentially large in $\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2$ compared with $r_n$, while keeping, in time average, a fixed fraction of its mass on the high-vorticity set, which at every time contains no square larger than a multiple of $\ell_n/\vartheta$ and whose strict version $\{v_n>a_n/4\}\cap\mathcal A_\vartheta$ has, at each age $0<h\le T_\vartheta$ and for all large $n$, full planar perimeter (portions on the boundary of the annulus included) at least $c\vartheta^3h/\ell_n$ and isoperimetric ratio at least $c\vartheta^2h^{1/2}/\ell_n$, with $c$ depending only on $\kappa_0$ and $C_N$ and no logarithm, so that a bounded ratio at ages $h_n\le T_\vartheta$ with $h_n/\ell_n^2\to\infty$, at that fixed $\vartheta$, would exclude (I), and while rarely visiting regions where the gradient of $v_n$ is of order $a_n/\ell_n$. Excluding (I) is thus reduced to a statement about the backward density in a strongly advective flow (Péclet number $1/\nu_n$ at the core scale), for which no Harnack-type or coercivity estimate with constants uniform in $n$ is proved here or was identified in the inspected sources (items 30-32).
* For a source-free bounded passive scalar that satisfies the circulation bound at every time, the logarithm disappears when the drift is affine: testing the circulation bound on the backward images of a needle with covariance proportional to the forward noise covariance, instead of on the backward density of the record point, gives the heat-equation scale $V/\sqrt T$ with no dependence on the drift. It does not disappear for nonlinear drifts: an explicit steady cellular drift with alternating expansion directions keeps an antiperiodic scalar above half its initial maximum for a number of core times of order $\log\mathrm{Pe}/\log\log\mathrm{Pe}$, with the passive amplitude ratios and the Hessian ratio bounded. Excluding alternative (I) therefore needs structure that these passive estimates do not capture, for instance that $\eta$ generates its own drift, energy constraints, or the geometry of the annulus; the corresponding planar model question (Reynolds-number-uniform persistence of two-dimensional Navier-Stokes vorticity at the core scale) is not resolved by the arguments here, and compactness with the Liouville theorem of Koch, Nadirashvili, Seregin and Sverak settles it only for bounded Reynolds number.
* In an active model the logarithm also disappears, for a different reason: if the drift coincides on the sampled spacetime region with one trace-free affine field whose curl satisfies $|S|\ge\kappa D$, the affine velocity along the unrestricted comparison path, which agrees with the drift seen by the backward path before exit, is Gaussian with covariance trace at least $S^2t/4$, so bounded velocity limits a survival horizon with survival probability $p$ to $16\ell^2/(\pi\kappa^2p^2)$ (item 34, Proposition 7.43). The passive counterexample of Proposition 7.42 evades this because its confining line carries zero drift vorticity. If the bounded mild solution is spatially real analytic at positive times, exact agreement with a nonconstant affine field on an open set is impossible by analytic continuation and boundedness; the exclusion results here do not depend on this supplementary observation. So this is a model statement; transferring it needs an approximation by one fixed affine field that (A) does not provide.
* Supplementary planar model findings. The following are reported here without complete proofs (of the items in this list, Section 4.8, 8.3 (iii), proves only the shear and transported-vorticity bounds of (iii), the finite-area bound of a different form stated there, the isoperimetric bound, and the anisotropic Taylor-Green example at the end of (iii)) and are used in no proof or theorem of this report; the computations were checked as stated, but where the full arguments are not reproduced here, they are reported findings, not established results. (i) In the exact stagnation-point class $u=(F,U-Gz)$, $G=F_x$, with $F,U$ odd in $x$, the backward path satisfies two martingale identities which, for the exact time-dependent affine flow $u=(g(t)x,\,wx-g(t)z)$ with $w\ne0$ and $|w|L+R\sup|g|\le M$, give $T\le M^2/(2pw^2)$ whenever the backward survival probability $p$ in the rectangle $[-L,L]\times[-R,R]$ up to time $T$ is positive. For general planar flows the corresponding identities reduce the Pe-uniform planar question to a uniform absorbable bound on the signed pressure work along the backward path; this is a sufficient target, not an established estimate. (ii) An exact affine Euler and Navier-Stokes field with constant vorticity $S$ and strain rate $\gamma$, $Q=S/\gamma\ge4$, holds the backward diffusion in a family of ellipses (semi-axis $a$) for a time $T$ with $T/\ell_{\rm loc}^2\asymp\log Q/\log\log Q$, where $\ell_{\rm loc}$ is the ratio of the local velocity and gradient scales; its velocity is unbounded on $\mathbb R^2$, and a smooth, bounded, uniformly Lipschitz Euler solution agreeing with it on the ellipses must have $\sup|u|\ge\frac S2(1-b_*)aQ^{1/4}$, $b_*=(Q+1)/(2Q^{3/2})$. This does not decide whether a bounded viscous flow with an approximately affine core can trap comparably.
