(iii) For smooth planar Navier-Stokes solutions on $\mathbb R^2\times[0,T]$ with viscosity $1$, $|u|\le M$, $|\nabla u|\le D$ (Frobenius norm), $M|\nabla^2u|\le C_*D^2$, $\omega(0,T)\ge c_0D$ with $c_0>0$, and $\ell=M/D$: bounded shear flows satisfy $T\le\ell^2/(\pi c_0^2)$; if the initial set where $\omega>c_0D/2$ has finite area $A_0$, then $T\le(8\ell^2/c_0^2)\log(CC_*^2A_0/(c_0^4\ell^2))$ with a universal constant $C$ (the area estimate of Section 4.8, 8.3 (iii), with $\tau=T$ proves the different bound $T/\ell^2\le(16/c_0^2)\log(4A_0/(\pi p^2T))$, $p=c_0/(4-c_0)$, which uses no bound on $\nabla^2u$); and if $u\cdot\nabla\omega=0$ on the whole window, then $\omega(t)=\operatorname{curl}(G_t*u(0))$ with $G_t$ the heat kernel, so $\|\omega(t)\|_\infty\le\sqrt\pi M/(2\sqrt t)$; finally, if at some time $s\in[0,T)$ the set $E$ where $\omega(\cdot,s)>c_0D/2$ is bounded and has perimeter at most $Q|E|^{1/2}$, then $T-s\le CQ^2\ell^2/c_0^2$ with an absolute constant $C$, with no logarithm (the Gauss-Green formula for sets of finite perimeter bounds the excess of $\omega(s)$ over $c_0D/2$ in $L^1$ by $M^2Q^2/(2c_0D)$), so in a family with $c_0$ fixed and $(T-s)/\ell^2\to\infty$, along the members whose high set at time $s$ is bounded the isoperimetric ratio $\mathrm{Per}(E)/|E|^{1/2}$ tends to infinity (with the value $+\infty$ for infinite perimeter); large ratios of individual high components do not imply long normalized persistence: an exact anisotropic Taylor-Green flow with $MH\le2D^2$ has a convex high component through the origin with ratio at least $\sqrt L$, for every $L\ge1$, throughout a window whose length divided by $\ell^2$ lies in $[\frac14,\frac12]$; its entire high set is unbounded, so the example does not refute a converse restricted to bounded entire high sets (8.3 (iii)). (iv) For a compactly supported Lipschitz weight $0\le f_0\le1$ with support of area $V$, transported as $f_t=f_0\circ\Phi_t^{-1}$, $\Phi_t$ the flow map of $u$ ($\partial_t\Phi_t=u(\Phi_t,t)$, $\Phi_0=\mathrm{id}$), and $\mathcal E=\int_0^T\|\nabla f_t\|_2^2\,dt$, one has $|\int\omega(T)f_T-\int\omega(0)f_0|\le2D\sqrt{V\mathcal E}$, and a meridional version holds for the ring when the adjoint is killed below the transported support; such estimates constrain persistence only together with an initial weighted plateau and a uniform distortion bound, which neither the planar hypotheses nor alternative (I) have been shown to provide. Explicit cellular arrays satisfy a persistence bound; a strained Gaussian sheet, a vortex pair and the Stuart cat's-eye family were also examined, and no counterexample to the Pe-uniform bound was found. These investigations leave open whether the actual Navier-Stokes evolution supplies a uniform pressure-work estimate, a suitable transported circulation test, or another estimate that excludes the persistent annular contribution.
* Lifted to $\mathbb R^5$, the representation of $\eta$ has no inner boundary; the price is a factor $e^{K}$ or $e^{2K}$ in the integrated outward strain $K$. On subsequences with $\alpha_n\ge L_n=1+\log(r_nM_n)$ the record forces $\int_{t_n-\widehat T_n}^{t_n}\|(u^r/r)_+\|_\infty\ge\frac14\log(\alpha_n/L_n)-C$ over the horizon $\widehat T_n=r_n^2L_n^{1/2}\alpha_n^{-3/2}$ (Corollary 7.20); for every Case 3 sequence Corollary 7.24 gives the horizons of item 20, of which $T_n^*$ tends to zero but need not be short compared with $r_n^2$, and item 37 gives a strain lower bound on the annulus $\{\vartheta r_n<r<Lr_n\}$ over the longer horizons $T''_\vartheta$. These necessary strain lower bounds do not contradict the available upper bound given by the time integral of $\|\nabla u\|_\infty$.
* A sharper version (item 20) applies to every Case 3 sequence: the full-space outward strain must accumulate $\frac13\log(1/\nu_n)$ over a horizon that tends to zero. This is still only a necessary condition. A formal scenario in which a pair of opposite-sign cores is formed near the axis and expands outward, the inner core thinning and intensifying by stretching, has been proposed. The proposed expanding pair passes the scalar comparisons that were performed; no velocity, swirl, source, stopped-probability or companion-flow history completing that proposal has been constructed or checked. Possible exclusion arguments could obstruct its preparation or outward transport, but the scalar proposal does not identify an exhaustive list of mechanisms that could rule it out.
* The critical swirl energy $\iint(u^\theta)^2dr\,dz$ controls the critical $L^1$ norm of the swirl source up to an inflow term. Case 3 therefore needs critically weighted swirl in radial inflow, integrated over time, of the order of the core Reynolds number, and full-space inward strain of order $\frac13\log(1/\nu_n)$ over the history; none of this needs a decay condition on the data, because the integral of the excess of $|\eta|$ over $\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty$, for a fixed earlier time $s_0$, is controlled by the source alone (item 22). This is the classical concentration mechanism, and no upper bound for it is proved. Bounding the inflow term by the swirl velocity times the enstrophy shows that the maximal swirl velocity must exceed the core Reynolds number (item 21); the available upper bound for it leaves room. If the source is a single dipole split by a plane (positive and negative parts on opposite sides of it) during the generating interval, that interval must be much longer than the transport window; without this sign-separation hypothesis no conclusion about the preparation time follows, and configurations with several source lobes are not excluded.
* Item 23 bounds the growth of the $L^p$ norms of $\eta$ through swirl quantities whose estimates involve inward strain; item 24 decomposes the record circulation into an inherited term and a source pairing with the backward adjoint, and item 25 shows that over windows of length $O(r_n^2)$ the contribution from source radii at least $\vartheta r_n$, for fixed $\vartheta>0$, is negligible compared with the required record circulation. A record circulation whose inherited contribution is negligible must therefore receive its required source contribution, for every fixed $\vartheta>0$, from radii below $\vartheta r_n$. These are again necessary conditions. A formal scenario with $1/\nu_n$ swirl cells of size $\ell_n$ near the axis, whose like-signed products are collected into one core that then expands as a meridional dipole, has also been proposed (its cells sit at radii $\asymp r_n\nu_n$, consistent with item 25). The formal multi-cell proposal matches the projected parameter sizes checked against the estimates of items 19 and 21-25; it does not supply the source, sign separation, retention, adjoint correlation, merging or energy-supply histories needed to verify the corresponding integral budgets (its core energy would have to grow by a factor $\nu_n^{-2/3}$). These parameter comparisons produce no contradiction, but they do not establish compatibility of a completed configuration with the full necessary conditions.
* Splitting $\eta$ at any fixed time into a part generated by the swirl source with zero data and the homogeneous evolution of the data (item 29), the homogeneous part has radial strain bounded up to $T_*$, so both diverging strain clocks are carried by azimuthal vorticity generated after that time. An attempted regress (each generated structure needs a companion reservoir of the same strain on a larger scale, which needs its own source, and so on) fails: the transverse diffusion clock changes at the next scale, no lifetime bound for a coherent strain is known, and the interaction energies of the components do not add.

* Further results. Item (b), the weighted criterion in (f) and the pairing bound and swirl clock in (h)(1) are proved in Section 4 (Lemmas 7.44 and 7.46, items 35 and 36); the other assertions below are supplementary findings whose complete arguments are not reproduced in this report (words such as "proved" or "checked" below describe how they were obtained, not their status here), and none of them is used in any proof or theorem. (a) A frequency-function approach to alternative (I) (Almgren-Poon type monotonicity with the killed backward density as weight) fails at its first step. The exact second-order identity has the quadratic form $Q_h=\mathrm{Id}/(2h)+S_b+\nabla^2\log p_h$, where $S_b$ is the symmetric part of the gradient of the backward drift and $p_h$ the killed density. Under pure strain $\gamma$ this form has an eigenvalue tending to $-\gamma$. A locally exact Navier-Stokes shear-in-strain flow has persistence while its frequency tends to zero, and it is excluded only by the velocity bound. (b) Item 35, the circulation absorption bound, was proved and checked; it uses the absorption of the backward circulation process at the axis (the squared radius $Z$ of the backward process has the diffusion part of a squared Bessel process of dimension $0$, together with the drift $-2(u^r/r)Z$; the bound is proved by a barrier). (c) Numerical findings for swirl-free axisymmetric flows, reported and used in no proof. The head-on collision of two opposite-sign thin rings violates the shape condition (A): $MH/D^2$ grows with the resolution as the cores flatten against the collision plane, consistently with the thin-ring scalings $dd/dR=-Ld/R$ for the core separation $d$. A thin ring stretched in the outflow of a large ring shows an inner entry by a fixed factor while (A) holds in the computations: $MH/D^2$ decreases, $\eta$ is reported to be approximately conserved along the record material, and the record material comes from radius about $0.17$ times the record radius. These exploratory computations do not establish alternative (II) of item 11, which concerns inner radii $\vartheta_nr_n$ with $\vartheta_n\to0$, and they do not prove that arguments using only (A), energy, the circulation bound and the dynamics on a window around the record cannot exclude it; they suggest that an exclusion of (II) will need the swirl production history. (d) The instantaneous self-strain pairing of the swirl source is nonnegative in the weights $r^0,r^1,r^2$ (an exact Fourier-Bessel identity, checked symbolically): the strain generated at an instant by the source alone is net expulsive in these weights. It does not bound the compression produced by earlier vorticity, so it does not close the inflow budget of item 19. (e) A model result for alternative (I); its complete proof is not reproduced here, so it is a reported finding. In the planar stagnation-point class $u=(F(x,t),U(x,t)-G(x,t)z)$, $G=F_x$, $x$-periodic, the Navier-Stokes equations force $-\partial_z p=\lambda(t)z+\mu(t)$ with $\lambda=2\langle G^2\rangle\ge0$: the stagnation line is a pressure ridge. For the backward stochastic path $Y$ (unit viscosity) killed at exit from the band $|z|\le R$ at time $\tau$, if $\mu=0$, $\partial_t\langle F\rangle=0$ and $|F|,|u_z|\le M$ on the band over $[t-T,t]$, then $\mathbb E\int_0^{\tau\wedge T}\omega(Y_s)^2ds\le(\tfrac12+\sqrt2)M^2+M^2T/(2R^2)$. The proof combines the Ito identities $d(u_zZ)=-(u_z^2+\lambda Z^2+2G)ds+dN$ and $d(u_z^2)=(2\omega^2+2G^2-2\lambda u_zZ)ds+dN'$ ($N,N'$ martingales); the coercive term $\lambda Z^2$ absorbs the pressure work. Consequently a killed persistence $\mathbb E[\omega(Y_h);h<\tau]\ge c_0D$ for $h\le T$ with $R\ge\ell/c_0$ forces $T\le(1+2\sqrt2)\ell^2/c_0^2$, with no dependence on the Peclet number. This answers inside the class the question why an active flow cannot trap. It does not transfer: for a general planar flow the same computation leaves the unsigned pressure work, and the ridge sign holds only in strain-dominated regions, while trapping needs vorticity-dominated regions. (f) A reduction of the swirl budget to the sign of a self-compression, proved and checked by hand. Split the radial strain $\sigma=u^r/r=\sigma_s+\sigma_B$ at a fixed time $s_0$ as in item 29, with $\|\sigma_B\|_\infty\le C_{\rm init}$, and let $\Lambda(t)=\int_{s_0}^t\Pi_s$, $\Pi_s=-\int\sigma_s(u^\theta)^2r^{-1}dx$, be the accumulated compression of the swirl by the strain of the source-generated vorticity. The weighted swirl identity of item 19, Hardy's inequality and Gronwall's inequality for the $\sigma_B$ part give, for the swirl source budget $\mathcal B=\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}$, the bound $\mathcal B\le C(s_0)\big(Y(s_0)+\sup_{[s_0,t_n]}\Lambda_+\big)$ with $C(s_0)=3(1+3C_{\rm init}T_*e^{3C_{\rm init}T_*})$, while Case 3 forces $\mathcal B\ge c_1/(2\nu_n)$. Hence Case 3 is excluded by any relative bound $\Lambda\le\varepsilon\mathcal B+C$ with $\varepsilon<1/C(s_0)$ and $C$ independent of $n$. A weighted form has a threshold that does not depend on $s_0$: with the weight $w=e^{-3C_{\rm init}(t-s_0)}$ on both the compression and the source budget, Case 3 is excluded by $3\Lambda_w\le(1-\delta)\sqrt3\,\mathcal B_w+C$ for any fixed $\delta>0$ (Lemma 7.46, Remark (6)). This hypothesis implies a bound on the critical swirl dissipation $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\!\int|\nabla\Gamma|^2r^{-3}dx\,dt$, and, given the swirl regularity criterion of Chen, Fang and Zhang, it is equivalent to that bound (the bound on the critical swirl energy $Y$ needed for the converse follows from the regularity statement of the same Remark). That bound excludes Case 3 by itself and, through the same criterion, every singular time (same Remark), so a pairing estimate that proves the hypothesis must carry a critical input on the swirl. The instantaneous sign $\Pi_s\le0$ is false (an explicit Gaussian two-ring interaction, two independent numerical evaluations agreeing to five digits), so only accumulated or relative forms can hold. The instantaneous self-pairing of item (d) has a one-line proof valid for every decaying $\eta$: for $\phi=(-\Delta_5)^{-1}\eta$ on $\mathbb R^5$ and $w=|y|^{-p}$, $y\in\mathbb R^4$, $0\le p\le2$, one integration by parts gives $\langle w\phi,\eta\rangle=\int w|\nabla\phi|^2+\frac12\int\phi^2(-\Delta w)\ge0$, since $-\Delta_4|y|^{-p}=p(2-p)|y|^{-p-2}\ge0$. (g) Model results on the accumulated sign, used in no proof. In the drift-free weak-swirl model ($q$ solves the five-dimensional heat equation, the source-generated $\eta$ solves the forced heat equation with zero data, and the strain is given by the instantaneous Biot-Savart law) the accumulated self-compression satisfies $\Lambda\le0$ in the planar thin-ring limit for every source, by an energy identity in which the radial strain operator commutes with the heat flow and is nonnegative. In the axisymmetric model it was nonpositive in every configuration computed (single, stacked, radially opposite and odd two-lobe rings, axis blobs, flat and tall profiles, and the full quartic pair interaction of two rings of either relative sign), but later computations, item (h)(3), found configurations with two concentrated radial edges and different time histories in which it is positive; the planar proof does not extend because the weight $r^{-1}$ does not commute with the five-dimensional Laplacian, and the resulting diffusion term is not signed near the axis. In the prescribed-swirl drift-free model it was also proved that a radially nonincreasing source profile gives $\Lambda\le0$ for every time dependence, and found a two-ring configuration with $\Lambda>0$ when radial monotonicity fails. With transport the leading term is the pairing of the potential with the advection of $\eta$; its self-interaction part is not a conserved quantity of the Euler dynamics, and coupling the swirl energy with the critical meridional energy $\int|(u^r,u^z)|^2r^{-1}dx$ produces indefinite cubic and pressure fluxes, so these model results do not transfer as they stand. The self-interaction part of that advection pairing has no sign even for drift-free produced vorticity $\eta_s=\partial_zG$, $G\ge0$: in the thin-ring limit (support within $O(a)$ of the radius $R$, $a\ll R$) it equals $\pi^2R^2\int A(A^2+B^2)\,dr\,dz\,(1+O(a/R))$ with the planar Riesz transforms $A=R_z^2G$, $B=R_rR_zG$; for $G=g(r)(1+\varepsilon h(z))$ the $z$-independent part contributes nothing, so the form equals $\varepsilon^3$ times its value at $gh$, which for $g=e^{-r^2}$ and $h=\cos z+\cos2z$ (period $2\pi$) is positive (relative size $0.43$, computed numerically); $\varepsilon=-0.495$ then gives $G>0$ with a negative value, and a localized envelope gives the same sign change. Hence a favourable transport term cannot come from a kernel inequality. Lemma 1.5 (i)-(ii) gives $\|q(t)\|_\infty\le2G^{1/3}H(t)^{2/3}\le2(GC_*^2\nu_n)^{1/3}D_n$ for $t\le t_n$ under (A) (a sharper axis constant, $|q(0,z,t)|\le(27/16)^{1/3}(GH^2)^{1/3}$, was also obtained and is not proved here), so the swirl rate $u^\theta/r$ is uniformly small compared with $D_n$ along Case 3; pointwise budgets built from this bound do not reach the scale $1/\nu_n$ of the record circulation. (h) Further results on the self-compression, proved unless marked otherwise. (1) A pairing bound without the meridional strain (Lemma 7.46): writing $\Pi=-\int\eta\,r^2\Phi\,dx$ with $\Phi=\partial_z(-\Delta_5)^{-1}(q^2/r)$, the five-dimensional Green function $G_5=1/(8\pi^2|x|^3)$ and the exact integral $\int_{\mathbb R^5}|\partial_zG_5(x-y)|\,|y'|^{-3}dy=r(x)^{-2}$ (integrate in $y_5$ first, then use the composition rule for Riesz potentials in $\mathbb R^4$), half of it from each sign of the odd kernel, give $|r^2\Phi|\le\frac12\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2$, hence $|\Pi(t)|\le\frac12\|u^\theta(t)\|_\infty^2\|\eta(t)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}$ whenever $\eta(t)\in L^1(\mathbb R^3)$; the same holds for the source-generated part $\eta_s$ and its compression $\Pi_s$. The $L^1$ budget of the source-generated part, $\|\eta_s(t)\|_{L^1}\le\mathcal B(s_0,t)$, holds by the argument of the critical $L^1$ budget for $\eta$, with no decay assumption on the data since $\eta_s$ vanishes at $s_0$. With the reduction of item (f) and the Hardy form $Y'+\sqrt3\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}\le3\Pi$ of the swirl identity this gives $Y(t)+\sqrt3\mathcal B(s_0,t)\le Y(s_0)\exp\big(3C_{\rm init}(t-s_0)+\frac{\sqrt3}2\int_{s_0}^t\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2ds\big)$ (viscosity $1$): no norm of the meridional velocity gradient enters. If $\eta_0\in L^1$, the same argument applied to the whole field gives, for all $0\le s\le t<T_*$, $Y(t)+\sqrt3\|\eta(t)\|_{L^1}\le\big(Y(s)+\sqrt3\|\eta(s)\|_{L^1}\big)\exp\big(\frac{\sqrt3}2\int_s^t\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2\big)$, with no constant depending on the starting time. Along a Case 3 sequence, therefore, $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2ds\ge\frac2{\sqrt3}\log(1/\nu_n)-C(s_0)\to\infty$. On every window $[t_n-A/D_n,t_n]$ the bound $\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2\le S_n^2\le G^{4/3}H(t_n)^{2/3}\le G^{4/3}C_*^{2/3}\nu_n^{1/3}D_n$ (Lemma 1.5 (i) and (A)) makes this swirl clock at most $AG^{4/3}C_*^{2/3}\nu_n^{1/3}\to0$, so inside such a record window the compression by vorticity produced in that window is a vanishing fraction of the window production; vorticity older than many record times $1/D_n$ is not covered. This is not a contradiction: across many windows the swirl clock can grow like $\nu_n^{1/3}D_n(t_n-s_0)$, and its finiteness is a critical Prodi-Serrin type condition on the swirl that is not available (in fact a regularity criterion: the stated range in the arXiv version cited in Lemma 7.46, Remark (3), includes the endpoint $L^2_tL^\infty_x$, so this clock diverges at every first singular time); the new content is the explicit rate $\frac2{\sqrt3}\log(1/\nu_n)$. A localized form is also reported, without proof here: for an axial centre $a$, a radius $L$ and $R\ge2$, $|\Pi_s|\le\frac m2N_{\rm near}+\frac{6\pi m}{R^2}N_{\rm far}+\frac{m_{\rm out}}2(N_{\rm near}+N_{\rm far})$, where $N_{\rm near}$ and $N_{\rm far}$ are the $L^1$ masses of $\eta_s$ inside and outside the ball of radius $RL$ about $a$ and $m_{\rm out}$ is the supremum of $(u^\theta)^2$ outside the ball of radius $L$; it does not close the argument, since a second, weaker swirl cell can pair with nearby vorticity far from the swirl maximum. The pairing bound is Lemma 7.46 of Section 4.7. Its necessary condition holds with polynomial room on the scalar history of Section 4.8 augmented in Remark (2) after Lemma 7.46 and on every power-law history with swirl exponent above $\frac12$; in exponent form it removes only a thin region next to the self-similar exponents, where the swirl bound of Lemma 1.5 with (A) forces the swirl exponent below $\frac12$ (Remark (2) there). A localized form, in which vorticity at distance $\rho$ from the axis is paired with the rate $\frac{c_\theta}2G\|q\|_\infty(r_c/\rho)^{4\theta-2}$, $r_c=(G/\|q\|_\infty)^{1/2}$, $\frac12<\theta<\frac34$, with a constant $c_\theta$ depending only on $\theta$ and tending to infinity as $\theta\to\frac34$, valid when $\int|\eta|r^{2-4\theta}dx<\infty$ (a hypothesis), does not close it either: for the particular history of Remark (2) there, the available bound for that rate at the record radius has a time integral that diverges like a power of $1/(T_*-t)$ (Remark (5) there); on some other admissible power-law histories the bound at the record radius is integrable, so this is a statement about one history, not about all. (2) A reported construction, not proved here, would show that the relative bound $\Lambda\le\delta\nu\mathcal B+C$ fails as a law for single production episodes: smooth compactly supported data consisting of a bounded-strain stagnation flow and a thin tilted annular swirl sheet, with $\Lambda(T)/(\nu\mathcal B(0,T))\to\infty$ in the ordered limit described next. If correct, this is consistent with (1), whose ratio bound $(1/(2\nu))\int\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2$ is unbounded in that limit, and item (f) could then only be used along the history of one fixed datum; this report establishes neither conclusion. The construction is an ordered asymptotic statement (episode time, then sheet height, then amplitude, then viscosity tend to zero). Its mechanism needs a sheet height much smaller than the square of the episode time and a thickness equal to the $3/2$ power of the height; otherwise the source from the axial envelope of the sheet dominates and acts as an ordinary expulsion. Numerical simulations at accessible parameters, including the exact background flow of the construction, showed no sign change of the instantaneous compression. The construction is therefore unconfirmed and is used in no proof. (3) Model results, used in no proof: in the drift-free coupled model (swirl and produced vorticity coupled through the strain, no transport) a hollow large-amplitude column with two interfering radial oscillation modes gains in the critical weight. In the drift-free weak-swirl model (in computations later than those listed in (g)) two concentrated radial edges with different time histories reverse the sign of the accumulated self-compression, while one common Gaussian radial factor with any axial profile keeps it nonpositive. These are examples; no criterion for the sign in terms of the radial structure is claimed, and the model computations indicate that a relative bound of the type of item (f) would have to allow a constant depending on $G$. No sign law holds without transport either, so a bound must be quantitative. (4) A production-site analysis, not reproduced here, gives no new scale restriction: the sup-norm source bounds were found to give only $\vartheta_n\ge c\nu_n^{1/3}\alpha_n^{-2/3}$ for the inner radius of alternative (II), and the production over a window is bounded by the critical swirl energy at its start plus the accumulated compression over the window, which is again the relative bound of item (f).
* The constant of (A). Remark (iii) after Lemma 4.1 proves the Landau-type inequality $B^2\le\frac{4n}{\sqrt{3n+1}}AC$ for bounded maps $\mathbb R^n\to\mathbb R^m$ with Lipschitz gradient (Frobenius norms), whose constant is optimal for $m\ge n$. Consequently (A) at a time with $D(t)>0$ forces $C_*\ge\sqrt{10}/12\approx0.264$, improved to $C_*\ge1/c_{\mathrm{div}}\approx0.2701$, with $c_{\mathrm{div}}$ given explicitly there, by a variant of the argument that uses the divergence condition, and a Case 3 sequence can exist only if $C_*\ge\sqrt7/8\approx0.331$. These are necessary conditions on $C_*$: they exclude Case 3 only for $C_*<\sqrt7/8$ and give nothing for larger $C_*$. The planar threshold cannot be raised using only the properties of the planar limit used there (a rescaled steady circular vortex has all of them with $C_*=\sqrt7/8$); whether the further structure of a Case 3 limit raises it is not settled.

## 4. Detailed statements and proofs

In this section a label such as Lemma 3.2 or display (0.1) refers to subsection 4.3 or 4.0 respectively.

### 4.0 Setting and conventions

**Data and solution.** $u_0\in H^\infty(\mathbb R^3)=\bigcap_k H^k$ is divergence free and axisymmetric, $E_0=\|u_0\|_2^2$, $G=\|ru_0^\theta\|_\infty<\infty$. Let $u$ be the unique smooth solution on $[0,T_*)$, $u\in C([0,T_*);H^k)$ for every $k$, smooth on $\mathbb R^3\times(0,T_*)$, with canonical pressure
$$p(t)=R_iR_j(u_iu_j)(t)=(-\Delta)^{-1}\partial_i\partial_j(u_iu_j)\in H^k\quad(\forall k),$$
which is the only pressure in $L^2$ for which the NS system holds. For each $t<T_*$ and $j\ge0$, $\nabla^ju(t)$ is continuous and tends to $0$ at infinity (Sobolev embedding of $H^2$), so all spatial suprema below are attained when positive, and $t\mapsto\|\nabla^ju(t)\|_\infty$ is continuous on $[0,T_*)$. Axisymmetry is preserved (uniqueness), i.e. $u(Qx,t)=Qu(x,t)$ for every rotation $Q$ about the $x_3$ axis. $T_*<\infty$ is the first singular time, so $M(t)\to\infty$ (Leray: $\|u(t)\|_\infty\ge c(T_*-t)^{-1/2}$), and then also $D(t)\to\infty$ (otherwise Beale-Kato-Majda would continue the solution).

**Historical quantities and standing hypotheses.** $M(t)=\sup_{[0,t]}\|u\|_\infty$, $S(t)=\sup_{[0,t]}\|u^\theta\|_\infty$, $D(t)=\sup_{[0,t]}\|\nabla u\|_\infty$, $H(t)=\sup_{[0,t]}\|\nabla^2u\|_\infty$. (A) means: $M(t)H(t)\le C_*D(t)^2$ for all $t$ in some interval $[t_A,T_*)$. The *no-swirl-extraction branch negation* (NSE) means: there is no sequence of swirl first-crossing records $(s_n,x_n)$ ($s_n=\min\{t:S(t)=S(s_n)\}$, $|u^\theta(x_n,s_n)|=S(s_n)=:S_n>0$, $r_n=r(x_n)$, $s_n\uparrow T_*$, $S_n\to\infty$), with $W_n$ defined on $[-A,0]$ for all $n\ge n(A)$ for every $A<\infty$ (that is, $s_nS_n/r_n\to\infty$; the cylinders are not silently intersected with the available time interval), such that $W_n(y,\tau)=S_n^{-1}u(r_ny',z_n+r_ny_3,s_n+(r_n/S_n)\tau)$ satisfies, with one constant $K$, $\limsup_n\sup_{B_R\times[-A,0]}|W_n|\le K$ for all $R,A$ and $\lim_{R\to\infty}\limsup_n\sup_{[-A,0]}\int_{|y|>R}|W_n|^2|y|^{-4}dy=0$ for all $A$. A *Case 3 sequence* is a sequence of historical gradient first-crossing records $(t_n,x_n)$ (defined in Section 4.3) with $\alpha_n=r_nD_n/M_n\to\infty$, $t_n\uparrow T_*$; (A) is a standing hypothesis on the whole interval $[t_A,T_*)$, not only at the records (Corollary 7.6 uses it at all integration times). Only Lemmas 2.1, 2.2(i) and 2.4 use (NSE); nothing in Sections 4.3-4.8 uses it. The data decay condition (L1$\eta$) of Lemma 7.7 is used in Lemma 7.7, Corollary 7.9, Corollary 7.13(c), Lemma 7.22(d) and Lemma 7.25(c) and in the remarks that refer to these; Lemma 7.26(c) gives decay-free replacements for the conclusions of Lemmas 7.22(d) and 7.25(c).

**Norms.** $|\nabla u|^2=\sum_{i,j}(\partial_iu_j)^2$ and $|\nabla^2u|^2=\sum_{i,j,k}(\partial_i\partial_ju_k)^2$ (Cartesian Frobenius norms). The operator norm is $\le$ the Frobenius norm; $|\omega|\le\sqrt2|\nabla u|$.

**Cylindrical components at $\theta=0$.** At a point $(r,0,z)$ one has $e_r=e_1$, $e_\theta=e_2$ and
$\partial_{x_2}u_1=-u^\theta/r$, $\partial_{x_2}u_2=u^r/r$. Hence $|u^\theta|/r\le|\nabla u|$, $|u^r|/r\le|\nabla u|$, $|\partial_zu^\theta|\le|\nabla u|$ pointwise, and by axisymmetry everywhere.

**Equations (checked symbolically).** With $\Gamma=ru^\theta$, $q=u^\theta/r$, $\eta=\omega^\theta/r$, $b=u^re_r+u^ze_z$:
$$(\partial_t+b\cdot\nabla)\Gamma=(\partial_{rr}-\tfrac1r\partial_r+\partial_{zz})\Gamma,\qquad (\partial_t+b\cdot\nabla)q=\Delta_5q-2\tfrac{u^r}{r}q,$$
$$(\partial_t+b\cdot\nabla)\eta=\Delta_5\eta+\partial_z(q^2),\qquad \partial_z(q^2)=-2qJ,\ J=\omega^r/r=-\partial_zu^\theta/r .$$
A computer algebra check derives these from the cylindrical NS system with incompressibility; the residuals are identically zero. Also $-\Delta_5\phi=\eta$, $u^r=-r\partial_z\phi$, $u^z=2\phi+r\partial_r\phi$ give $\omega^\theta=-r\Delta_5\phi$ and $\operatorname{div}u=0$ (direct computation).

**Pointwise bound used repeatedly.** For $r\ge\rho>0$:
$$|\partial_z(q^2)|=2|q||\partial_zq|=2\frac{|\Gamma|}{r^2}\,\frac{|\partial_zu^\theta|}{r}\le\frac{2G\,\|\nabla u(t)\|_\infty}{\rho^3}.\tag{0.1}$$

### 4.1 Elementary facts

#### Lemma 1.1 (circulation maximum principle). $|\Gamma(x,t)|\le G$ on $\mathbb R^3\times[0,T_*)$.

*Proof.* Fix $T<T_*$, $\bar M=\sup_{[0,T]}\|u\|_\infty$. On $\Omega=\{r>0\}\times(0,T]$ the function $\Gamma$ satisfies $L\Gamma=0$ for $L=\partial_t+b\cdot\nabla-\Delta+\frac2r\partial_r$ (3D Laplacian; this is the displayed equation since $\Delta=\partial_{rr}+\frac1r\partial_r+\partial_{zz}$ on axisymmetric functions). Let $w=\varepsilon e^{\lambda t}(1+|x|^2)$. Then $Lw=\varepsilon e^{\lambda t}(\lambda(1+|x|^2)+2b\cdot x-6+4)\ge \varepsilon e^{\lambda t}(\lambda(1+|x|^2)-2\bar M|x|-2)>0$ for $\lambda=\bar M+3$. The function $\Phi=\Gamma-G-w$ satisfies $L\Phi<0$, $\Phi\le0$ at $t=0$, $\Phi<0$ on $r=0$ (where $\Gamma=0$), and $\Phi<0$ for $|x|$ large ($|\Gamma|\le r\bar M$). A positive maximum of $\Phi$ over the closure of a large bounded region would be interior or at the final time, where $\partial_t\Phi\ge0$, $\nabla\Phi=0$, $\Delta\Phi\le0$, contradicting $L\Phi<0$. Hence $\Gamma\le G+w$; let $\varepsilon\to0$, and argue the same way for $-\Gamma$. $\square$

#### Lemma 1.2 (record identity). Let $\bar t\in(0,T_*)$ satisfy $\|u^\theta(\bar t)\|_\infty=S(\bar t)>0$ (this holds in particular at every first-crossing time of $S$), attained at $\bar x$, and choose the sign so that $u^\theta(\bar x,\bar t)=S(\bar t)$ (otherwise apply the reflection $x_2\mapsto-x_2$, which maps solutions to solutions and $u^\theta$ to $-u^\theta$). Then $\bar r=r(\bar x)>0$ and
$$-\bar r\,u^r(\bar x,\bar t)\ge1,\qquad \bar rM(\bar t)\ge1,\qquad S(\bar t)\bar r\le G,\qquad S(\bar t)/M(\bar t)\le G .$$

*Proof.* $u^\theta=0$ on the axis, so $\bar r>0$. For $s<\bar t$: $u^\theta(\bar x,s)\le\|u^\theta(s)\|_\infty\le S(\bar t)=u^\theta(\bar x,\bar t)$, so the left time derivative, which equals $\partial_tu^\theta(\bar x,\bar t)$ since $u$ is $C^1$ in time, is $\ge0$. The scalar $u^\theta(\cdot,\bar t)$ (an axisymmetric smooth function on $\{r>0\}$) has a spatial maximum at $\bar x$: $\partial_ru^\theta=\partial_zu^\theta=0$ and $\Delta u^\theta=(\partial_{rr}+\frac1r\partial_r+\partial_{zz})u^\theta\le0$. The swirl equation $\partial_tu^\theta+b\cdot\nabla u^\theta+\frac{u^r}{r}u^\theta=(\Delta-\frac1{r^2})u^\theta$ gives at $(\bar x,\bar t)$: $\frac{u^r}{r}u^\theta\le-\frac{u^\theta}{r^2}$; divide by $u^\theta/\bar r>0$. Then $\bar r|u^r|\ge1$ gives $\bar rM\ge1$; $\bar rS=\Gamma(\bar x,\bar t)\le G$; and $S/M\le S\bar r\le G$. $\square$

*Remarks.* The time derivative sign needs only that $\bar t$ is a time at which the historical sup is attained by the current profile; strictness is not needed. At $\bar t=0$ no identity holds (no past). The identity concerns the swirl amplitude record, not a $\Gamma$ record; $\partial_z(q^2)=2q\partial_zq=2q\partial_zu^\theta/r$ vanishes at $(\bar x,\bar t)$.

#### Lemma 1.3. $S(t)^2\le G\,D(t)$ for all $t\in[0,T_*)$. Hence $S(t)/M(t)\le\sqrt{G\,D(t)/M(t)^2}$.

*Proof.* $S(t)=\|u^\theta(s)\|_\infty=|u^\theta(\bar x,s)|$ for some $s\le t$, $\bar x$ (continuity and attainment). If $S(t)>0$ then $\bar r>0$ and $S^2=|\Gamma(\bar x,s)|\,|u^\theta(\bar x,s)|/\bar r\le G|\nabla u(\bar x,s)|\le GD(t)$. $\square$

#### Lemma 1.4 (parabolic upper bound $D\lesssim M^2$). There are absolute constants $\lambda_0,C_0$ such that $\|\nabla u(t)\|_\infty\le C_0M(t)^2$ whenever $t\ge\lambda_0/M(t)^2$. Consequently $D(t)\le C_0M(t)^2$ for all $t$ close to $T_*$.

*Proof.* Put $s=t-\lambda/M^2$, $M=M(t)$. The mild formula on $[s,t]$ reads $u(\sigma)=e^{(\sigma-s)\Delta}u(s)-\int_s^\sigma e^{(\sigma-\rho)\Delta}\mathbb P\nabla\cdot(u\otimes u)\,d\rho$. Since $\nabla\cdot(u\otimes u)=(u\cdot\nabla)u$ and $\nabla e^{h\Delta}\mathbb P$ has a kernel bounded by $C(|x|+\sqrt h)^{-4}$ (derivative of the Oseen kernel), $\|\nabla e^{h\Delta}\mathbb Pg\|_\infty\le Ch^{-1/2}\|g\|_\infty$. With $f(\sigma)=(\sigma-s)^{1/2}\|\nabla u(\sigma)\|_\infty$ (finite on $(s,t]$),
$$f(\sigma)\le CM+CM(\sigma-s)^{1/2}\int_s^\sigma(\sigma-\rho)^{-1/2}(\rho-s)^{-1/2}f(\rho)\,d\rho\le CM+\pi C\sqrt\lambda\,\sup f .$$
For $\pi C\sqrt{\lambda_0}\le\frac12$ we get $\sup f\le2CM$, i.e. $\|\nabla u(t)\|_\infty\le2C\lambda_0^{-1/2}M^2$. Since $D(t)\to\infty$ and $M(t)\to\infty$, the historical sup $D(t)$ is eventually attained at times where the bound applies: if $D(t)=\|\nabla u(s)\|_\infty$ with $s\le t$, then $s\to T_*$ as $t\uparrow T_*$ (otherwise $\|\nabla u\|_\infty$ would be unbounded on a compact subinterval of $[0,T_*)$), and $\lambda_0/M(s)^2\to0<s$. $\square$

#### Lemma 1.5 (swirl versus Hessian; bounds for the $\eta$ source). For every $t\in[0,T_*)$:
(i) $S(t)^3\le G^2H(t)$;
(ii) for every $s\le t$ and every $x$: $|q(x,s)|\le2\sqrt{S(t)H(t)}$, $|\partial_zq(x,s)|\le H(t)$, hence $|\partial_z(q^2)(x,s)|\le4S(t)^{1/2}H(t)^{3/2}\le4G^{1/3}H(t)^{5/3}$; also $|\partial_z(q^2)|\le2D(t)H(t)$;
(iii) if (A) holds at $t$, then $S(t)/M(t)\le(G^2C_*)^{1/3}\,\big(D(t)/M(t)^2\big)^{2/3}$.

*Proof.* (i) Let $S(t)=|u^\theta(\bar x,s)|>0$ with $s\le t$; rotate so that $\bar x=(\bar r,0,\bar z)$ and reflect if necessary so that $f=u^\theta(\cdot,s)$ has the positive maximum $S(t)$ at $(\bar r,\bar z)$, $\bar r>0$. Then $\partial_rf(\bar r,\bar z)=0$. The Cartesian component $u_2=u^rx_2/r+u^\theta x_1/r$ satisfies $\partial_{x_2}^2u_2(\bar r,0,\bar z)=\partial_rf/\bar r-f/\bar r^2$ (verified symbolically), so $H(t)\ge|\partial^2_{x_2}u_2|=S(t)/\bar r^2$ and $S(t)^3=(\bar rS(t))^2\,S(t)/\bar r^2\le G^2H(t)$ by Lemma 1.1.
(ii) By axisymmetry it suffices to take $x=(r,0,z)$. Along the line $\xi\mapsto(\xi,0,z)$ the component $g(\xi)=u_2(\xi,0,z,s)$ equals $u^\theta(|\xi|,z,s)\operatorname{sgn}\xi$, so $|g|\le S(t)$, $|g''|\le H(t)$, $g(0)=0$. For a function on $\mathbb R$, $|g(\xi+h)-g(\xi)-hg'(\xi)|\le h^2\|g''\|_\infty/2$ gives $|g'|\le2\|g\|_\infty/h+h\|g''\|_\infty/2$, and $h=2(\|g\|_\infty/\|g''\|_\infty)^{1/2}$ gives $\|g'\|_\infty\le2(\|g\|_\infty\|g''\|_\infty)^{1/2}$. Since $q(r,z)=g(r)/r=\int_0^1g'(\sigma r)\,d\sigma$, $|q|\le2\sqrt{S(t)H(t)}$; and $\partial_zq(r,z)=\int_0^1\partial_z\partial_{x_1}u_2(\sigma r,0,z,s)\,d\sigma$ gives $|\partial_zq|\le H(t)$. The bound with $G^{1/3}$ follows from (i), and $|q|\le|\nabla u|\le D(t)$ (Section 4.0) gives the last bound.
(iii) By (i) and (A): $S^3\le G^2H\le G^2C_*D^2/M$; divide by $M^3$. $\square$

*Consequence for Case 3.* Along a Case 3 sequence, (iii) and Remark 2.3 give $S_n/M_n\le(G^2C_*)^{1/3}\nu_n^{2/3}\to0$. So the swirl is negligible at the records without the branch negation (NSE).

#### Lemma 1.6 ($\eta$ maximum principle with source). For $0<t_0\le t<T_*$,
$$\|\eta(t)\|_\infty\le\|\eta(t_0)\|_\infty+\int_{t_0}^t\|\partial_z(q^2)(s)\|_\infty\,ds .$$

*Proof.* Since $\omega_2(\xi,0,z)$ is smooth and odd in $\xi$, $\eta(r,z)=\omega_2(r,0,z)/r=F(r^2,z)$ with $F$ smooth, so $\eta$ is a smooth axisymmetric function on $\mathbb R^3\times(0,T_*)$; it is bounded on $\mathbb R^3\times[t_0,t]$ because $|\eta|\le\sup|\partial_r\omega^\theta|\le\sqrt2\|\nabla^2u\|_\infty$. On $\{r>0\}$ the equation of Section 4.0 reads $L\eta=f$ with $L=\partial_t+b\cdot\nabla-\Delta-\frac2r\partial_r$ (3D Laplacian) and $f=\partial_z(q^2)$; at points of the axis the term $\frac2r\partial_r\eta$ extends continuously by $4\partial_sF=2\partial_{rr}\eta$. Put $\bar M=\sup_{[t_0,t]}\|u\|_\infty$, $w=\epsilon e^{\lambda s}(1+|x|^2)$ with $\lambda=\bar M+11$; since $(\Delta+\frac2r\partial_r)|x|^2=10$, $Lw\ge\epsilon e^{\lambda s}(\lambda(1+|x|^2)-2\bar M|x|-10)>0$. Let $\Psi(s)=\|\eta(t_0)\|_\infty+\int_{t_0}^s\|f\|_\infty+w$. Then $L(\Psi-\eta)>0$, $\Psi-\eta>0$ at $s=t_0$ and for $|x|$ large. At a first nonpositive minimum of $\Psi-\eta$ over a large ball, $\partial_s(\Psi-\eta)\le0$, $\nabla(\Psi-\eta)=0$, $\Delta(\Psi-\eta)\ge0$; off the axis also $\frac2r\partial_r(\Psi-\eta)=0$, and on the axis $\frac2r\partial_r(\cdot)$ is replaced by $2\partial_{rr}(\Psi-\eta)\ge0$ (minimum along the $x_1$ line). Either way $L(\Psi-\eta)\le0$, a contradiction. Apply the same to $-\eta$ and let $\epsilon\to0$. $\square$

### 4.2 The branch reduction (B)

#### Lemma 2.1 (B1). Under the no-swirl-extraction branch negation, $S(t)/M(t)\to0$.

*Proof.* If not, there are $c>0$ and $t'_k\uparrow T_*$ with $S(t'_k)\ge cM(t'_k)\to\infty$. Let $t_k=\min\{t:S(t)=S(t'_k)\}$; then $\|u^\theta(t_k)\|_\infty=S(t_k)$ (first crossing), $t_k\to T_*$ (because $S$ is bounded on compact subintervals of $[0,T_*)$), and $S(t_k)/M(t_k)\ge S(t'_k)/M(t'_k)\ge c$. With $(r_k,z_k)$ the attainment point, Lemma 1.2 gives $c\le (r_kM_k)(S_k/M_k)=r_kS_k\le G$. The swirl scaling $W_k(y,\tau)=S_k^{-1}u(r_ky',z_k+r_ky_3,t_k+(r_k/S_k)\tau)$ is defined for $\tau\in[-t_kS_k/r_k,0]$, and $t_kS_k/r_k\ge t_kS_k^2/G\to\infty$. For $\tau\le0$: $|W_k|\le M(t_k)/S_k\le1/c=:K$ on all of $\mathbb R^3$, and $\int_{|y|>R}|W_k|^2|y|^{-4}dy\le4\pi K^2/R$. For each fixed $A$, the interval $[-A,0]$ is available for $k\ge N(A)$. Hence $\limsup_k\sup_{B_R\times[-A,0]}|W_k|\le K$ for all $R,A$ and the tail term is $\le4\pi K^2/R\to0$: this is exactly the witness excluded by (NSE), a contradiction. (The swirl scaling has viscosity $(r_kS_k)^{-1}\in[1/G,1/c]$.) $\square$

#### Lemma 2.2 (B2, conditional on cited Liouville theorems). Under (NSE), $D(t)/M(t)^2\to0$. (Subcase (ii) of the proof, which is all that Case 3 needs, does not use (NSE); see Remark 2.3.)

*Proof.* Suppose $D(t'_k)\ge cM(t'_k)^2$ along $t'_k\uparrow T_*$. Take first-crossing gradient records $t_k=\min\{t:D(t)=D(t'_k)\}$, attained at $x_k$; then $D_k/M_k^2\ge c$, $t_k\to T_*$. Rotate so that $x_k=(r_k,0,z_k)$ and set $V_k(y,s)=M_k^{-1}u(x_k+y/M_k,t_k+s/M_k^2)$, $s\in[-t_kM_k^2,0]$, $t_kM_k^2\to\infty$. Then $|V_k|\le1$, and $V_k$ is a bounded mild NS solution with unit viscosity on each interval $[-T,0]$; by the standard parabolic smoothing of bounded mild solutions, $\|\nabla^jV_k\|_{L^\infty(\mathbb R^3\times[-T+1,0])}\le C_j$ for all $j$, and $\partial_s\nabla^jV_k$ are bounded likewise. (Induction on $j$, the case $j=1$ being Lemma 1.4: fix $a$, put $f_j(\sigma)=\sup_{a<\rho\le\sigma}(\rho-a)^{j/2}\|\nabla^jV_k(\rho)\|_\infty$, finite since $V_k$ is a rescaled smooth solution, and for $\sigma\in(a,a+\lambda_j]$ restart the mild formula at the midpoint $s'=(a+\sigma)/2$, with one derivative on the kernel and $j-1$ on $(V_k\cdot\nabla)V_k$. The free term is at most $C(\sigma-s')^{-1/2}\|\nabla^{j-1}V_k(s')\|_\infty\le C_j(\sigma-a)^{-j/2}$; in the Duhamel term $\rho-a\ge(\sigma-a)/2$, the Leibniz terms other than $V_k\cdot\nabla^jV_k$ involve only lower derivatives, and $\int_{s'}^\sigma(\sigma-\rho)^{-1/2}d\rho\le2\sqrt{\lambda_j}$. Hence $f_j\le C_j+C_j\sqrt{\lambda_j}f_j$, and $\lambda_j$ small gives $f_j\le2C_j$, uniformly in $k$; apply this with $a=\sigma-\lambda_j\ge-T$. The time derivatives follow from the equation, the pressure gradient and its derivatives being bounded by the near/far splitting of Lemma 3.2.) Moreover $|\nabla V_k(0,0)|=D_k/M_k^2\in[c,C_0]$. By Arzela-Ascoli and a diagonal argument, a subsequence converges in $C^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^3\times(-\infty,0])$ to a bounded ancient mild solution $V$ with $|\nabla V(0,0)|\ge c$ (passage to the limit in the mild formula by dominated convergence, the Oseen kernel derivative being integrable). The original axis is at distance $r_kM_k$ from the origin.
(i) If $r_kM_k$ stays bounded, the limit is axisymmetric about a line at finite distance and its swirl is $\lim u^\theta/M_k=0$ by Lemma 2.1: a bounded ancient axisymmetric mild solution without swirl, hence constant by Koch-Nadirashvili-Seregin-Sverak [KNSS09], contradicting $|\nabla V(0,0)|\ge c$.
(ii) If $r_kM_k\to\infty$, the argument of Section 4.4 below (with $\ell=1/M_k$) shows $V$ is independent of $y_2$ with $V_2\equiv0$, i.e. a bounded ancient mild solution of the 2D NS system (the kernel $O_{ij}(x,h)=\delta_{ij}\Phi_h(x)+\int_h^\infty\partial_i\partial_j\Phi_s(x)\,ds$ of $e^{h\Delta}\mathbb P$, $\Phi_s$ the heat kernel, has $|\nabla O(x,h)|\le C(|x|+\sqrt h)^{-4}$, so in the mild formula the $y_2$ integration may be carried out first; since $\int_{\mathbb R}\Phi^{(3)}_s(\xi,y_2)\,dy_2=\Phi^{(2)}_s(\xi)$, the $y_2$ integral of $\partial_kO^{(3)}_{ij}$ is $\partial_kO^{(2)}_{ij}$ for planar indices, and it vanishes when exactly one index is $2$, by oddness), hence constant by [KNSS09], again a contradiction. $\square$

*Status.* This uses the precise statements of the Liouville theorems in [KNSS09] for bounded ancient mild solutions (2D; axisymmetric without swirl). The statements were checked against the paper.

#### Remark 2.3 (Case 3 does not need the branch negation). Along gradient records with $\alpha_k=r_kD_k/M_k\to\infty$, Lemma 1.4 gives $r_kM_k=\alpha_kM_k^2/D_k\ge\alpha_k/C_0\to\infty$. So if $D_k/M_k^2\ge c$ along such records we are always in subcase (ii), where the swirl of the limit vanishes because $|u^\theta|\le G/r$ gives $|u^\theta|/M_k\le 2G/(r_kM_k)\to0$ near the record. Thus $\nu_k=D_k/M_k^2\to0$ along every Case 3 sequence using only the 2D Liouville theorem, without Lemma 2.1.

#### Lemma 2.4 (what (NSE) forces at swirl records: meridional dominance in shrinking cylinders). For a swirl first-crossing record $(s,x)$ as in the definition of (NSE), with level $S=S(s)$, distance $\rho=r(x)$ to the axis and axis projection $c=(0,0,z(x))$, put $L=\log(e+E_0S)$, $\delta=L\,(E_0\rho/S^2)^{1/4}$ and, when $\delta^2<s$,
$$\mathcal J(s,x)=S^{-1}\sup\{|b(y,t)|:\ |y-c|<\delta,\ s-\delta^2\le t\le s\},\qquad b=(u^r,u^z).$$
Under (NSE), $\mathcal J\to\infty$ as the level tends to infinity, uniformly over records: for every $K<\infty$ there is $S_K$ such that every swirl first crossing $(s,x)$ with $S(s)\ge S_K$ (and every maximizing point $x$ at that time) has $\delta^2<s$ and $\mathcal J(s,x)>K$. Since $|b|\le M(s)$, this contains Lemma 2.1 and localizes it: the meridional velocity exceeds $KS$ within distance $\delta$ of the axis projection of the record and within time $\delta^2$ before it, where $\delta\to0$, $\delta/\rho\to\infty$ and $\delta^2S/\rho\to\infty$.

*Proof.* By Lemma 1.2, $\rho S\le G$. Hence $\delta\le L(E_0G)^{1/4}S^{-3/4}\to0$, $\delta/\rho=L(E_0/(S^2\rho^3))^{1/4}\ge L(E_0S/G^3)^{1/4}\to\infty$ and $\delta^2/(\rho/S)=L^2(E_0/\rho)^{1/2}\to\infty$ as $S\to\infty$ (note $\rho\le G/S\to0$); also $s\to T_*$, because $S$ is bounded on compact subintervals of $[0,T_*)$, so $\delta^2<s$ for large levels. Suppose the claim fails for some $K$. Then there are swirl first crossings $(s_k,x_k)$ with strictly increasing levels $S_k\to\infty$ (so $s_k$ is strictly increasing, $s_k\to T_*$) and $\mathcal J(s_k,x_k)\le K$. Let $W_k$ be their swirl scalings, centred at $c_k$ as in the definition of (NSE); $s_kS_k/\rho_k\ge s_kS_k^2/G\to\infty$, so $W_k$ is defined on $[-A,0]$ for large $k$. Fix $R,A$. For large $k$, $R\rho_k<\delta_k$ and $A\rho_k/S_k<\delta_k^2$, so the cylinder $B_R\times[-A,0]$ is mapped into the physical cylinder of $\mathcal J$, where $|b|\le KS_k$; with $|u^\theta|\le S(t)\le S_k$ for $t\le s_k$ this gives $\sup_{B_R\times[-A,0]}|W_k|\le(K^2+1)^{1/2}$. For the tail, the change of variables $x=c_k+\rho_ky$ gives
$$\int_{|y|>R}|W_k(y,\tau)|^2|y|^{-4}dy=\frac{\rho_k}{S_k^2}\int_{|x-c_k|>R\rho_k}\frac{|u(x,t)|^2}{|x-c_k|^4}dx,\qquad t=s_k+\frac{\rho_k}{S_k}\tau .$$
Split at $|x-c_k|=\delta_k$. On the inner part $|u|^2\le(K^2+1)S_k^2$, which contributes at most $(K^2+1)\int_{|y|>R}|y|^{-4}dy=4\pi(K^2+1)/R$. On the outer part the energy inequality $\|u(t)\|_2^2\le E_0$ gives at most $\rho_kE_0/(S_k^2\delta_k^4)=L_k^{-4}\to0$. Hence $\limsup_k\sup_{B_R\times[-A,0]}|W_k|\le(K^2+1)^{1/2}$ for all $R,A$ and $\lim_{R\to\infty}\limsup_k\sup_{[-A,0]}\int_{|y|>R}|W_k|^2|y|^{-4}dy=0$ for all $A$: this is a sequence excluded by (NSE). $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) The proof uses only (NSE), the energy inequality, $|\Gamma|\le G$ and the record identity; it does not use (A) or Case 3. The same argument works for any radii $\delta_k$ with $\delta_k\to0$, $\delta_k/\rho_k\to\infty$, $\delta_k^2S_k/\rho_k\to\infty$ and $E_0\rho_k/(S_k^2\delta_k^4)\to0$. It is a necessary condition for (NSE), not a sufficient one: large meridional velocity somewhere in the expanding scaled cylinder need not lie in a fixed scaled cylinder or contribute to the weighted tail. (2) The obstruction in (NSE) is quantified over sequences: a failure of the common bound $K$ means $\sup_{R,A}\limsup_k\sup_{B_R\times[-A,0]}|W_k|=\infty$, which can hold with every fixed cylinder bounded (for instance with a scaled velocity growing linearly in $|y|$), and a failure of tightness means that for some $A$ the weighted tail does not vanish; by the exterior estimate in the proof any nonvanishing tail comes from meridional velocity at distances between $R\rho_k$ and $\delta_k$ from $c_k$. (3) Lemma 2.4 gives no contradiction with Case 3. Along a Case 3 sequence $S(t_n)\nu_n\to\infty$ (Lemma 7.25), so every level $S\in(S(0),S(t_n)]$ has a first crossing at or before $t_n$, and as these levels tend to infinity there is, uniformly over the maximizing points, meridional velocity $\gg S$ near the axis; but these first crossings are not related in time, height or radius to the gradient record, and a meridional velocity of this size near the axis is what a near-axis stagnation point flow with strain $\lambda$ provides: in swirl variables it is $\kappa(-y_1,-y_2,2y_3)$ with $\kappa=\lambda\rho/S$, whose weighted tail over $R<|y|<\Lambda$ equals $8\pi\kappa^2(\Lambda-R)$. Consequently, if a flow of this kind (strain $\lambda\asymp a^{-2}$ on a ball of radius $\Lambda\rho\asymp Na$ centred at the axis projection of the record, around cells of radius $\rho\asymp a$, so that, since $\kappa\asymp(aS)^{-1}$ and $aS\lesssim\rho S\le G$, the tail is $\asymp N/(aS)^2\gtrsim N/G^2$, with uniform constants and with $N\to\infty$ as the swirl level tends to infinity) is present at every sufficiently high swirl first crossing and for every choice of maximizing point, within one fixed number $A_0$ of swirl time units before it, then no sequence of swirl first crossings can be extracted and (NSE) holds. This is a scaling computation, not a construction: the universal occurrence hypothesis has not been constructed or verified for the scenario of 8.3 (vi), whose parameters do not specify all swirl first crossings. The stagnation ball also costs kinetic energy $\frac{8\pi}5\lambda^2(\Lambda\rho)^5\asymp N^5a$, so finite energy requires $N^5a\lesssim E_0$, which is stronger than the condition $aN^3\to0$ of 8.3 (vi) (it holds for instance for $a=N^{-6}$, where the core energy is $N^{-3}$ and the ball energy $N^{-1}$); an axisymmetric divergence-free cutoff (cut off the stream function $\lambda r^2(z-z_c)$) costs the same order. (NSE) does not supply an upper bound for the swirl source, for its retention, or for the inward strain, which is what an exclusion of Case 3 along the lines of Section 4.8 would need.

### 4.3 Normalized bounds (C), canonical pressure, and convergence

Let $(t_n,x_n)$ be historical gradient first-crossing records: $D_n=D(t_n)=|\nabla u(x_n,t_n)|$, $t_n=\min\{t:D(t)=D_n\}$, $D_n\to\infty$, $t_n\uparrow T_*$, $x_n=(r_n,0,z_n)$ after a rotation about the $x_3$ axis (allowed since $u(Qx)=Qu(x)$). Note $r_n>0$ is not needed for the record itself, but $\alpha_n=r_nD_n/M_n\to\infty$ forces it. Put $\ell_n=M_n/D_n$, $\nu_n=D_n/M_n^2$, and
$$U_n(y,\tau)=M_n^{-1}u(x_n+\ell_ny,t_n+\tau/D_n),\qquad P_n(y,\tau)=M_n^{-2}p(x_n+\ell_ny,t_n+\tau/D_n),\qquad \tau\in[-t_nD_n,0].$$

#### Lemma 3.1 (C). For every $n$ with $t_n\ge t_A$ (all large $n$; discard the finitely many others and relabel), $\tau\in[-t_nD_n,0]$ and all $y$: $|U_n|\le1$, $|\nabla U_n|\le1$, $|\nabla^2U_n|\le C_*$, $|\nabla U_n(0,0)|=1$, and
$\partial_\tau U_n+U_n\cdot\nabla U_n+\nabla P_n=\nu_n\Delta U_n$, $\nabla\cdot U_n=0$. Moreover $t_nD_n\to\infty$.

*Proof.* All times $t_n+\tau/D_n$ lie in $[0,t_n]$, so $|u|\le M_n$, $|\nabla u|\le D_n$, $|\nabla^2u|\le H_n:=H(t_n)$. The chain rule gives $\nabla_yU_n=\ell_nM_n^{-1}\nabla u=D_n^{-1}\nabla u$ and $\nabla_y^2U_n=\ell_n^2M_n^{-1}\nabla^2u=M_nD_n^{-2}\nabla^2u$, bounded by $M_nH_n/D_n^2\le C_*$ by (A) at $t_n$ (for $n$ large, so that $t_n$ is "sufficiently late"). In the general first-rescaling formula, $B_n=M_n$, $T_n=1/D_n$, so $\beta_n=T_nB_n/\ell_n=1$, $\nu_n=T_n/\ell_n^2=D_n/M_n^2$ and $T_n/(B_n\ell_n)=1/M_n^2$. $t_nD_n\to\infty$ since $t_n\to T_*>0$. $\square$

#### Lemma 3.2 (canonical pressure, near/far split). Fix $R\ge1$ and $\chi\in C_c^\infty(B_{4R})$ with $\chi=1$ on $B_{2R}$, $|\nabla\chi|\le C/R$, $|\nabla^2\chi|\le C/R^2$ (a rescaled fixed cutoff). For $|y|<R$,
$$\nabla P_n(y)=\nabla R_iR_j\big(\chi U_{n,i}U_{n,j}\big)(y)+\int_{\mathbb R^3}\nabla K_{ij}(y-w)(1-\chi(w))U_{n,i}U_{n,j}(w)\,dw,$$
where $K_{ij}=\partial_i\partial_j\frac{1}{4\pi|x|}$ up to sign convention ($|\nabla K_{ij}(x)|\le C|x|^{-4}$), and
$$\sup_{B_R\times[-t_nD_n,0]}|\nabla P_n|\le C(R,C_*).$$
The same formula, with $U$ in place of $U_n$, defines the limit pressure gradient, and $\nabla P_n\to\nabla P$ locally uniformly along any subsequence for which $U_n\to U$ in $C^1_{\rm loc}$.

*Proof.* Riesz transforms commute with translations and dilations, so $P_n(\tau)=R_iR_j(U_{n,i}U_{n,j})(\tau)$ exactly, with $U_{n,i}U_{n,j}(\tau)\in L^1\cap L^\infty$ (finite energy at each fixed $n$; no uniformity in $n$ is used). Splitting $f=U_iU_j=\chi f+(1-\chi)f$: the second piece vanishes on $B_{2R}$, so for $|y|<R$ its Riesz transform is given by the absolutely convergent integral with the kernel $K_{ij}(y-w)$, $|y-w|\ge|w|/2$, and may be differentiated under the integral; $|\nabla K_{ij}(y-w)|\le C|w|^{-4}$ gives the bound $C\int_{|w|>2R}|w|^{-4}dw\le C/R$. For the first piece, $\nabla R_iR_j(\chi f)=R_iR_j(\nabla(\chi f))$ and $g=\nabla(\chi f)$ is supported in $B_{4R}$ with $\|g\|_\infty\le C$ and $\|\nabla g\|_\infty\le C(1+C_*)$ (since $|\nabla^2(U_iU_j)|\le 2|\nabla U|^2+2|U||\nabla^2U|$, and the terms containing $\nabla\chi,\nabla^2\chi$ are bounded by $C/R+C/R^2$ because $|U|,|\nabla U|\le1$ and $R\ge1$). For compactly supported Lipschitz $g$ one has $R_iR_jg(y)=c_{ij}g(y)+\mathrm{p.v.}\int K_{ij}(y-w)g(w)\,dw$; splitting the p.v. integral at $|y-w|=1$ and using the cancellation $\int_{a<|x|<b}K_{ij}=0$ on the inner part together with the Lipschitz bound, and $|K_{ij}(x)|\le C|x|^{-3}$ with $\operatorname{supp}g\subset B_{4R}$ on the outer part, gives $|R_iR_jg(y)|\le C\|\nabla g\|_\infty+C\|g\|_\infty(1+\log(1+8R))$. Hence the bound. For convergence: if $U_n\to U$ and $\nabla U_n\to\nabla U$ uniformly on compact subsets of $\mathbb R^3\times[-A,0]$ then $g_n\to g$ uniformly with uniformly bounded Lipschitz constants, so (same splitting, with the inner region $|y-w|<\epsilon$ controlled by the Lipschitz bound and the outer region by uniform convergence) $R_iR_jg_n\to R_iR_jg$ uniformly on $B_R$. The far integrals converge uniformly on $B_R\times[-A,0]$ by tail truncation: for $L>4R$, the difference of the far terms for $U_n$ and $U$ is at most $C\sup_{B_L\times[-A,0]}|f_n-f|\int_{2R<|w|<L}|w|^{-4}dw+2C\int_{|w|>L}|w|^{-4}dw\le C(R)\sup_{B_L\times[-A,0]}|f_n-f|+C/L$, where $f_n=U_{n,i}U_{n,j}$, $f=U_iU_j$, $|f_n|,|f|\le1$; let $n\to\infty$ and then $L\to\infty$. The limit formula does not depend on $R,\chi$ (two choices differ by the compactly supported $(\chi-\chi')f$, for which both representations coincide). $\square$

*Remark (no parasitic harmonic forcing).* The limit pressure gradient is the explicit functional of the limit velocity above. No additional term $a(\tau)$ (spatially constant or harmonic) appears: such a term could only arise if one used an unspecified pressure normalization; the canonical pressure of the fixed finite-energy solution is the Riesz formula, and the passage to the limit was carried out in that formula.

#### Lemma 3.3 (convergence through $\tau=0$). Assume $\nu_n\to0$ (Remark 2.3). Then there is a subsequence and a limit $U\in C^1(\mathbb R^3\times(-\infty,0])$ with $U_n\to U$ and $\nabla_yU_n\to\nabla_yU$ uniformly on every compact subset of $\mathbb R^3\times(-\infty,0]$, $\|U\|_\infty\le1$, $\|\nabla U\|_\infty\le1$, $\nabla U(\cdot,\tau)$ Lipschitz with constant $C_*$, $|\nabla U(0,0)|=1$, $\nabla\cdot U=0$, and $\partial_\tau U+U\cdot\nabla U+\nabla P=0$ pointwise, with $\nabla P$ the canonical limit pressure of Lemma 3.2.

*Proof.* On $B_R\times[-A,0]$ (available once $t_nD_n>A$), Lemmas 3.1-3.2 give $|\partial_\tau U_n|\le1+C(R,C_*)+\sqrt3\,C_*\nu_n\le L(R,C_*)$. For $\tau,\tau'\in[-A,0]$ put $g=U_n(\cdot,\tau)-U_n(\cdot,\tau')$; on $B_{R+1}$, $|g|\le L|\tau-\tau'|$ and $|\nabla^2g|\le 2C_*$. The elementary interpolation $|\nabla g(y)|\le 2\sup_{B_h(y)}|g|/h+h\sup|\nabla^2g|$ ($0<h\le1$; Taylor along $y\pm he$) with $h=\min(1,|\tau-\tau'|^{1/2})$ gives $|\nabla g|\le (2L+2C_*)|\tau-\tau'|^{1/2}$ when $|\tau-\tau'|\le1$. Thus $U_n,\nabla U_n$ are equibounded and equicontinuous on compact sets of $\mathbb R^3\times(-\infty,0]$; Arzela-Ascoli and a diagonal argument give the subsequence. Uniform limits preserve the sup bounds and Lipschitz constants and the value $|\nabla U(0,0)|=\lim|\nabla U_n(0,0)|=1$. Passing to the limit in $U_n(y,\tau)-U_n(y,\tau')=-\int_{\tau'}^\tau(U_n\cdot\nabla U_n+\nabla P_n-\nu_n\Delta U_n)$, using local uniform convergence of the integrand except for $\nu_n\Delta U_n$, which is $O(\nu_n)$, gives $U(y,\tau)-U(y,\tau')=-\int_{\tau'}^\tau(U\cdot\nabla U+\nabla P)$ with a continuous integrand; hence $U$ is $C^1$ in $\tau$ and the Euler equation holds pointwise. $\square$

### 4.4 Planarity (PLANAR)

#### Lemma 4.1. Let $\alpha_n\to\infty$. Then on every compact set $K\subset\mathbb R^3\times(-\infty,0]$:
$$\sup_K|\partial_{y_2}U_n|\le\frac{1+R}{\alpha_n-R},\qquad \sup_K|U_n\cdot e_2|\le\frac{2R}{\alpha_n}+\frac{2G\nu_n}{\alpha_n},\qquad (K\subset B_R\times[-A,0],\ \alpha_n>2R).$$
Consequently every limit of Lemma 3.3 has the form $U=(V_1(y_1,y_3,\tau),0,V_3(y_1,y_3,\tau))$, and $\partial_{y_2}P=0$.

*Proof.* Let $Q_s$ be the rotation by angle $s$ about the $x_3$ axis and $J=\frac{d}{ds}Q_s|_{s=0}$, $Je_1=e_2$, $Je_2=-e_1$, $Je_3=0$. Differentiating $u(Q_sx)=Q_su(x)$ at $s=0$ gives $(x_1\partial_{x_2}-x_2\partial_{x_1})u=Ju$. In the variables $x=x_n+\ell_ny$, $x_1=r_n+\ell_ny_1=\ell_n(\alpha_n+y_1)$, $x_2=\ell_ny_2$, $\partial_{x_i}=\ell_n^{-1}\partial_{y_i}$, hence
$$(\alpha_n+y_1)\,\partial_{y_2}U_n=JU_n+y_2\,\partial_{y_1}U_n .$$
With $|JU_n|\le1$, $|\partial_{y_1}U_n|\le1$, $|y|\le R$ the first bound follows. For the second: at $x=x_n+\ell_ny$, $|y|\le R$, the cylindrical radius is $\rho\ge r_n-\ell_nR\ge r_n/2$ and the angle satisfies $|\sin\theta|=\ell_n|y_2|/\rho\le2R/\alpha_n$; $u_2=u^r\sin\theta+u^\theta\cos\theta$ with $|u^r|\le M_n$ and $|u^\theta|=|\Gamma|/\rho\le2G/r_n$ (Lemma 1.1). Divide by $M_n$ and use $r_nM_n=\alpha_n\ell_nM_n=\alpha_n/\nu_n$. Both bounds tend to $0$ (Remark 2.3 gives $\nu_n\le C_0$). Uniform convergence of $U_n$ and $\partial_{y_2}U_n$ (Lemma 3.3) gives $\partial_{y_2}U\equiv0$ and $U_2\equiv0$. The $e_2$ component of the Euler equation then reads $\partial_{y_2}P=0$. $\square$

*Remarks.* (i) Only $|\Gamma|\le G$, $\alpha_n\to\infty$ and $\nu_n$ bounded are used; the global branch fact $S/M\to0$ (Lemma 2.1) gives the swirl part independently since $|u^\theta|\le S_n$ on $[0,t_n]$. (ii) In the $y$ variables the original axis is the line $\{y_1=-\alpha_n,\ y_2=0\}$; it leaves every compact set. (iii) (A lower bound for the constant of (A).) Let $F:\mathbb R^n\to\mathbb R^m$ be bounded with Lipschitz gradient, $A=\sup|F|$, $B=\sup|\nabla F|_F$ and $C=\operatorname{ess\,sup}|\nabla^2F|_F$ (Frobenius norms, the Hessian being the tensor $\partial_i\partial_jF_\alpha$). Then $B^2\le\frac{4n}{\sqrt{3n+1}}AC$, that is $B^2\le\frac8{\sqrt7}AC$ for $n=2$ and $B^2\le\frac{12}{\sqrt{10}}AC$ for $n=3$ (for $n=1$ this is Landau's inequality $B^2\le2AC$), and for $m\ge n$ the constant is optimal. Proof: mollification preserves the upper bounds $A$ and $C$ and converges in $C^1_{\rm loc}$, so we may assume that $F$ is smooth with bounded derivatives; by translation it suffices to bound $|\nabla F(0)|_F$, and we may assume $C>0$ (otherwise $F$ is affine and bounded, hence constant). Write $x=\rho\omega$ with $\rho>0$ and $|\omega|=1$, let $\langle\cdot\rangle$ denote the average over the unit sphere $S^{n-1}$ with respect to the normalized surface measure, and put $\Lambda(\rho)=\frac n\rho\langle F(\rho\omega)\omega^{\mathsf T}\rangle$, an $m\times n$ matrix. Since $\langle\omega\omega^{\mathsf T}\rangle=\frac1nI$, $F_1(x)=\Lambda(|x|)x$ is, on each sphere $|x|=\rho$, the $L^2$ projection of $F$ onto the span of the coordinate functions $\omega_1,\dots,\omega_n$ (the spherical harmonics of degree one, with coefficients in $\mathbb R^m$), and $\frac{\rho^2}n|\Lambda(\rho)|_F^2=\langle|F_1(\rho\omega)|^2\rangle\le\langle|F(\rho\omega)|^2\rangle\le A^2$. Since $\langle\omega^{\mathsf T}\rangle=0$, $\Lambda(\rho)-\nabla F(0)=\frac n\rho\langle(F(\rho\omega)-F(0)-\rho\nabla F(0)\omega)\omega^{\mathsf T}\rangle$ has norm at most $\frac n2C\rho$, so $\Lambda(\rho)\to\nabla F(0)$ as $\rho\to0$; similarly $\Lambda'(\rho)=\frac n\rho\langle(\nabla F(\rho\omega)\omega-\rho^{-1}(F(\rho\omega)-F(0)))\omega^{\mathsf T}\rangle$ has norm at most $\frac n2C$. For a $C^2$ map $H$ on $\mathbb R^n\setminus\{0\}$ and orthonormal vectors $\tau,\sigma$ tangent to the sphere at $\omega$, $\nabla^2H[\omega,\omega]=\partial_\rho^2H$, $\nabla^2H[\omega,\tau]=\partial_\rho(\rho^{-1}\nabla_SH)\cdot\tau$ and $\nabla^2H[\tau,\sigma]=\rho^{-2}\nabla_S^2H[\tau,\sigma]+\rho^{-1}\partial_\rho H\,\tau\cdot\sigma$, where $\nabla_S$ and $\nabla_S^2$ are the gradient and Hessian on the unit sphere acting on $\omega\mapsto H(\rho\omega)$; so $|\nabla^2H|_F^2$ is the sum of $|\partial_\rho^2H|^2$, twice $|\partial_\rho(\rho^{-1}\nabla_SH)|^2$ and the squared norm of the tangential block $\rho^{-2}\nabla_S^2H+\rho^{-1}\partial_\rho H\,g$, $g$ the metric of the sphere. Apply this to $F=F_1+R$. On each sphere, $\partial_\rho$ and the Laplacian $\Delta_S$ of the sphere preserve the degree-one space and its orthogonal complement; a degree-one function $U$ satisfies $\Delta_SU=-(n-1)U$ and $\nabla_S^2U=-U\,g$; and $\langle\nabla_SU\cdot\nabla_SW\rangle=-\langle W\cdot\Delta_SU\rangle$. Hence the three cross terms between $F_1$ and $R$ have zero average over each sphere: the first because $\partial_\rho^2F_1$ has degree one and $\partial_\rho^2R$ is orthogonal to that space; the second because it equals $(n-1)\langle\partial_\rho(\rho^{-1}F_1)\cdot\partial_\rho(\rho^{-1}R)\rangle=0$; the third because the tangential block of $F_1$ is $\varphi\,g$ with $\varphi=\rho^{-1}\partial_\rho F_1-\rho^{-2}F_1$ of degree one, whose pairing with the block of $R$ is $\langle\varphi\cdot(\rho^{-2}\Delta_SR+(n-1)\rho^{-1}\partial_\rho R)\rangle=0$. Therefore $\langle|\nabla^2F_1(\rho\omega)|_F^2\rangle\le\langle|\nabla^2F(\rho\omega)|_F^2\rangle\le C^2$ for every $\rho>0$. For a field $\Lambda(\rho)x$ the three entries are $(2\Lambda'+\rho\Lambda'')\omega$, $\Lambda'\tau$ and $\Lambda'\omega\,\tau\cdot\sigma$, so that $|\nabla^2(\Lambda x)|_F^2=|(2\Lambda'+\rho\Lambda'')\omega|^2+2\sum_\tau|\Lambda'\tau|^2+(n-1)|\Lambda'\omega|^2$ with $\tau$ running over an orthonormal basis of the tangent space; since $\langle|X\omega|^2\rangle=\frac1n|X|_F^2$ and $\langle\sum_\tau|X\tau|^2\rangle=\frac{n-1}n|X|_F^2$ for every $m\times n$ matrix $X$, $\langle|\nabla^2F_1|_F^2\rangle=\frac1n(|2\Lambda'+\rho\Lambda''|_F^2+3(n-1)|\Lambda'|_F^2)$. With $\gamma=|\Lambda'|_F^2$ and $|2\Lambda'+\rho\Lambda''|_F^2\ge4\gamma+4\rho\langle\Lambda',\Lambda''\rangle=4\gamma+2\rho\gamma'$ this gives $2\rho\gamma'+(3n+1)\gamma\le nC^2$, that is $(\rho^{(3n+1)/2}\gamma)'\le\frac n2C^2\rho^{(3n-1)/2}$; since $\gamma$ is bounded near $0$, integration from $0$ gives $\gamma\le\frac n{3n+1}C^2$. Hence $|\nabla F(0)|_F\le|\Lambda(\rho)|_F+\int_0^\rho|\Lambda'|_F\le\frac{\sqrt n\,A}\rho+\big(\frac n{3n+1}\big)^{1/2}C\rho$ for every $\rho>0$, and $\rho^2=\sqrt{3n+1}\,A/C$ gives $|\nabla F(0)|_F^2\le\frac{4n}{\sqrt{3n+1}}AC$. Optimality: let $F_0(x)=(1-\frac{|x|}2)x$ for $|x|\le1$ and $F_0(x)=\frac x{2|x|}$ for $|x|\ge1$ (composed, if $m>n$, with an isometric embedding of $\mathbb R^n$ into $\mathbb R^m$). Its gradient, $(1-\frac\rho2)I-\frac\rho2\omega\omega^{\mathsf T}$ for $\rho\le1$ and $\frac1{2\rho}(I-\omega\omega^{\mathsf T})$ for $\rho\ge1$, is continuous, and the second derivatives are bounded on both pieces, so $\nabla F_0$ is Lipschitz; $A=\frac12$, and $B=\sqrt n$ is attained at $0$, since $|\nabla F_0|_F^2=(1-\rho)^2+(n-1)(1-\frac\rho2)^2$ for $\rho\le1$ and $\frac{n-1}{4\rho^2}$ for $\rho\ge1$. The formula above with $\Lambda=(1-\frac\rho2)I$ gives $|\nabla^2F_0|_F^2=\frac{3n+1}4$ for $0<\rho<1$, and with $\Lambda=\frac1{2\rho}I$ it gives $\frac{3(n-1)}{4\rho^4}\le\frac{3(n-1)}4$ for $\rho>1$; so $C=\frac{\sqrt{3n+1}}2$ and $B^2=\frac{4n}{\sqrt{3n+1}}AC$. For $n=2$ the rotated field $V_0=JF_0$, $J$ the rotation by a right angle, is the circular vortex with azimuthal velocity $\rho-\rho^2/2$ for $\rho\le1$ and $\frac12$ for $\rho\ge1$; it has the same three norms and is a steady planar Euler flow. Consequences. With $n=3$, applied to $u(\cdot,s)$ at a time $s\le t$ with $\|\nabla u(s)\|_\infty=D(t)$, the inequality shows that (A) can hold at a time with $D(t)>0$ only if $C_*\ge\sqrt{10}/12\approx0.264$ ($\sqrt{10}/12$ is optimal among all fields, but $F_0$ is not divergence free). For velocity fields the threshold improves to $C_*\ge1/c_{\mathrm{div}}\approx0.2701$, where $c_{\mathrm{div}}=4\big(\frac{(7p+50q)^3}{6125q}\big)^{1/2}\approx3.7025$ with $\theta=\arcsin\sqrt{2/3}$, $p=\frac{\sqrt7}{112}\big(11+\frac{19\theta}{\sqrt2}\big)\approx0.56304$ and $q=\frac{\sqrt7}{24}\big(\frac{5\theta}{\sqrt2}-3\big)\approx0.041621$, because every bounded divergence-free $F:\mathbb R^3\to\mathbb R^3$ with Lipschitz gradient satisfies $B^2\le c_{\mathrm{div}}AC$. Proof: after mollification (which keeps $\operatorname{div}F=0$) and a translation it suffices to bound $|\nabla F(0)|_F$ for smooth $F$ with $\nabla F(0)\ne0$. Let $b=\nabla F(0)/|\nabla F(0)|_F$, a trace-free matrix with symmetric part $S$, $s=|S|_F^2\le1$, $m(\rho)=\Lambda(\rho):b=\frac3\rho\langle F(\rho\omega)\cdot b\omega\rangle$, and $G(x)=|x|\,bx$, a field of the form $\Lambda_G(\rho)x$ with $\Lambda_G=\rho\, b$, whose Hessian $K(\omega)=\nabla^2G(\rho\omega)$, $K_{\alpha ij}=b_{\alpha i}\omega_j+b_{\alpha j}\omega_i+\delta_{ij}(b\omega)_\alpha-\omega_i\omega_j(b\omega)_\alpha$, does not depend on $\rho$. The cross-term argument above applies to $G$ and $R$, and polarizing the formula for $\langle|\nabla^2(\Lambda x)|_F^2\rangle$ (a quadratic form in $(\Lambda',\Lambda'')$) gives $\langle\nabla^2F(\rho\omega):K\rangle=\langle\nabla^2F_1(\rho\omega):K\rangle=\frac13(2\rho m''+10m')$. Since $\operatorname{div}F=0$, the tensor $\nabla^2F(x)$ lies in the kernel of the map $\mathcal D$, $(\mathcal DT)_j=\sum_\alpha T_{\alpha\alpha j}$, on tensors symmetric in $(i,j)$; with $\Pi$ the orthogonal projection onto this kernel, $|\Pi T|_F^2=|T|_F^2-\frac12|\mathcal DT|^2$ (as $\mathcal D\mathcal D^*=2I$), and $\langle\nabla^2F:K\rangle=\langle\nabla^2F:\Pi K\rangle\ge-Ck$ with $k=\langle|\Pi K|_F\rangle$. Direct computation gives $|K|_F^2=2+4|b\omega|^2$ and $\mathcal DK=2S\omega-(\omega\cdot S\omega)\omega$, with averages $\langle|K|_F^2\rangle=\frac{10}3$ and $\langle|\mathcal DK|^2\rangle=\frac{14}{15}s$; hence $k^2\le\frac{50-7s}{15}$. Integrating $(\rho^5m')'=\frac{\rho^4}2(2\rho m''+10m')\ge-\frac32Ck\rho^4$ from $0$ gives $m(\rho)\ge|\nabla F(0)|_F-\frac3{10}Ck\rho$, while $m(\rho)\le\frac{3A}\rho\,a$ with $a=\langle|b\omega|\rangle$; optimizing in $\rho$, $|\nabla F(0)|_F^2\le\frac{18}5AC\,ak$. The skew part of $b$ has a unit null vector $e$, so $be=Se$ and the smallest eigenvalue $d$ of $b^{\mathsf T}b$ satisfies $d\le|Se|^2\le\frac23s$ (the square of an eigenvalue of the trace-free $S$ is at most $\frac23|S|_F^2$). If $e_1$ is a unit eigenvector of $b^{\mathsf T}b$ for $d$, Jensen's inequality over the rotations about $e_1$ and the uniform distribution of $\omega\cdot e_1$ on $[-1,1]$ give $a\le f(d)$ with $f(d)=\int_0^1\big(\frac{1-d}2+\frac{3d-1}2z^2\big)^{1/2}dz$, which is concave on $[0,\frac13]$ with $f'(\frac13)=0$, hence increasing there; as $d\le\frac13$, $a\le f(\min(\frac23s,\frac13))\le p+qs$, where $p+qs$ is the tangent of the concave function $s\mapsto f(\frac23s)$ at $s=\frac3{14}$ ($f(\frac17)=p+\frac3{14}q$ and $\frac23f'(\frac17)=q>0$), which is at least $f(\frac13)$ for $s\ge\frac12$. Finally, with $v=p+qs$ and $L=7p+50q$, the square of $\frac{18}5(p+qs)\big(\frac{50-7s}{15}\big)^{1/2}$ equals $\frac{108}{125q}v^2(L-7v)\le\frac{108}{125q}\cdot\frac{4L^3}{1323}=c_{\mathrm{div}}^2$. The constant $c_{\mathrm{div}}$ is not shown to be optimal. In even dimensions $n$ the divergence condition gives no improvement, since $OF_0$ with $O$ orthogonal and skew is divergence free and has the same three norms as $F_0$. The planar threshold below concerns a planar field and is not affected by this improvement. With $n=2$, applied to the planar field $V=(U_1,U_3)(y_1,0,y_3,0)$ of a limit of Lemmas 3.3 and 4.1, it shows that a Case 3 sequence can exist only if $C_*\ge\sqrt7/8\approx0.331$: this field satisfies $|V|\le1$ and $|\nabla V(0,0)|_F=1$, and $|\nabla^2V|_F\le C_*$ almost everywhere, because the Hessians of $U_n(\cdot,0)$ are bounded by $C_*$ in the Frobenius norm (Lemma 3.1) and converge weakly-* in $L^\infty_{\rm loc}$ to the distributional Hessian of the limit, so that $\int_E|\nabla^2U(\cdot,0)|_F\le C_*|E|$ for every bounded measurable $E$. These are necessary conditions on the constant of (A): they exclude Case 3 only when $C_*<\sqrt7/8$ and say nothing for larger $C_*$. The planar threshold cannot be raised using only the properties of $V$ listed here: $W(y)=2V_0(y/(2\sqrt2))$ is a steady planar Euler flow with $|W|\le1$, $|\nabla W|_F\le|\nabla W(0)|_F=1$ and $\operatorname{ess\,sup}|\nabla^2W|_F=\sqrt7/8$. Whether the further structure of a Case 3 limit (a limit of rescaled axisymmetric Navier-Stokes solutions satisfying (A)) raises the threshold is not settled. The inequality and its proof were first obtained in a separate analysis and were re-derived and checked here; we have not determined whether it appears in the literature.

### 4.5 Planar vorticity, nontriviality, (RIGIDITY)

Write $V=(V_1,V_3)$ as a field of $(y_1,y_3)\in\mathbb R^2$ and $\zeta=\partial_{y_3}V_1-\partial_{y_1}V_3$ (the $e_2$ component of $\operatorname{curl}U$; the other components vanish). From Lemma 3.3: $V$ is bounded by $1$, $C^1$ in $(y,\tau)$, $\nabla V(\cdot,\tau)$ is $C_*$-Lipschitz, $\operatorname{div}V=0$, so $\zeta$ is bounded by $\sqrt2$ and $\sqrt2C_*$-Lipschitz in $y$, and continuous in $(y,\tau)$.

#### Lemma 5.1 (vorticity transport). $\partial_\tau\zeta+\operatorname{div}(V\zeta)=0$ in $\mathcal D'(\mathbb R^2\times(-\infty,0))$, and $V\cdot\nabla\zeta=\operatorname{div}(V\zeta)$ a.e.

*Proof.* Take the distributional planar curl of $\partial_\tau V=-(V\cdot\nabla)V-\nabla P$; $\operatorname{curl}\nabla P=0$ in $\mathcal D'$. $(V\cdot\nabla)V$ is Lipschitz in $y$, so its distributional derivatives are its a.e. derivatives; the product rule for Lipschitz functions gives $\operatorname{curl}((V\cdot\nabla)V)=V\cdot\nabla\zeta+(\operatorname{div}V)\zeta=V\cdot\nabla\zeta$ a.e., and $\operatorname{div}(V\zeta)=V\cdot\nabla\zeta$ a.e. as well. $\square$

#### Lemma 5.2 (nontriviality). $\zeta(\cdot,0)\not\equiv0$.

*Proof.* If $\zeta(\cdot,0)\equiv0$, $V(\cdot,0)$ is divergence free and curl free, so $V_1,V_3$ are weakly harmonic, hence smooth harmonic and bounded on $\mathbb R^2$, hence constant. But $\nabla U(0,0)$ consists of the $(y_1,y_3)$ derivatives of $V_1,V_3$ ($U_2\equiv0$, $\partial_{y_2}U\equiv0$), and $|\nabla U(0,0)|=1$. $\square$

#### Lemma 5.3 (RIGIDITY). Let $X(a,\tau)$ solve $\partial_\tau X=V(X,\tau)$, $X(a,0)=a$, for $\tau\le0$. Then $X(\cdot,\tau)$ is a measure preserving bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism of $\mathbb R^2$, $\zeta(X(a,\tau),\tau)=\zeta(a,0)$ for all $a$, $\tau\le0$, and $\|\zeta(\cdot,-A)\|_{L^\infty}=\|\zeta(\cdot,0)\|_{L^\infty}>0$ for every $A\ge0$.

*Proof.* $V$ is continuous, bounded and globally $1$-Lipschitz in $y$, so the flow exists for all times, is a homeomorphism with Lipschitz constants $\le e^{|\tau|}$ in both directions, and measure preserving since $\operatorname{div}V=0$ (Liouville's formula for the $C^1$ flow, $V$ being $C^1$). Let $\rho_\epsilon$ be a standard mollifier in $y$ and $\zeta^\epsilon=\zeta*\rho_\epsilon$. By Lemma 5.1, $\partial_\tau\zeta^\epsilon=-\operatorname{div}(V\zeta)*\rho_\epsilon=-(V\zeta)*\nabla\rho_\epsilon$, which is continuous in $(y,\tau)$, so $\zeta^\epsilon\in C^1$ and
$$\partial_\tau\zeta^\epsilon+V\cdot\nabla\zeta^\epsilon=R_\epsilon,\qquad R_\epsilon(y)=\int\big(\zeta(w)-\zeta(y)\big)\big(V(y)-V(w)\big)\cdot\nabla\rho_\epsilon(y-w)\,dw,$$
where we used $\int(V(y)-V(w))\cdot\nabla_y\rho_\epsilon(y-w)\,dw=-(\operatorname{div}V)*\rho_\epsilon(y)=0$. Since $|\zeta(w)-\zeta(y)|\le\sqrt2C_*\epsilon$, $|V(y)-V(w)|\le\epsilon$ on the support and $\int|\nabla\rho_\epsilon|\le C/\epsilon$, $\|R_\epsilon\|_\infty\le C C_*\epsilon$. Along the $C^1$ curve $\tau\mapsto X(a,\tau)$, $|\zeta^\epsilon(X(a,\tau),\tau)-\zeta^\epsilon(a,0)|\le CC_*\epsilon|\tau|$; let $\epsilon\to0$ (uniform convergence $\zeta^\epsilon\to\zeta$). Bijectivity of $X(\cdot,\tau)$ gives equality of sup norms, and Lemma 5.2 positivity. $\square$

#### Corollary 5.4 (TARGET-3 is equivalent to the case being empty). For a Case 3 sequence let $d(A)=\limsup_n D(t_n-A/D_n)/D_n$. Along any subsequence with a limit as in Lemma 3.3, $\|\zeta(\cdot,-A)\|_\infty\le\sqrt2\,\liminf_n D(t_{n}-A/D_{n})/D_{n}$ (along that subsequence). Hence $d$ is nonincreasing and $\inf_Ad(A)\ge\|\zeta(\cdot,0)\|_\infty/\sqrt2>0$ for every Case 3 sequence. Consequently the universally quantified statement "every Case 3 sequence satisfies $\lim_{A\to\infty}d(A)=0$" is equivalent to "no Case 3 sequence exists" (if none exists it holds vacuously). Proving TARGET-3 for every Case 3 sequence therefore excludes the case; the equivalence itself supplies no backward upper estimate and does not prevent an independent analytic proof of one.

*Proof.* $|\zeta_n(y,-A)|\le|\operatorname{curl}U_n|\le\sqrt2|\nabla U_n(y,-A)|\le\sqrt2D(t_n-A/D_n)/D_n$ and $\zeta_n\to\zeta$ pointwise. $D$ is nondecreasing, so $d$ is nonincreasing. $\square$

#### Corollary 5.5 (velocity persistence is also forced). Along a convergent subsequence let $m(A)=\liminf_nM(t_n-A/D_n)/M_n$. Then $\|V(\cdot,-A)\|_\infty\le m(A)$. For a bounded field $g$ on $\mathbb R^2$ with bounded second derivatives and a unit vector $e$, Taylor's formula $g(y+he)-g(y)=h\,\partial_eg(y)+O(h^2\|\nabla^2g\|_\infty/2)$ gives $|\partial_eg|\le2\|g\|_\infty/h+h\|\nabla^2g\|_\infty/2$, and the choice $h=2(\|g\|_\infty/\|\nabla^2g\|_\infty)^{1/2}$ gives $|\partial_eg|\le2(\|g\|_\infty\|\nabla^2g\|_\infty)^{1/2}$. Applied to $\partial_{y_3}V_1$ and $\partial_{y_1}V_3$ at $\tau=-A$ (with $\|\nabla^2V\|_\infty\le C_*$) and combined with Lemma 5.3:
$$\|\zeta(\cdot,0)\|_\infty=\|\zeta(\cdot,-A)\|_\infty\le4\sqrt{C_*\,m(A)},\qquad\text{so}\qquad m(A)\ge\frac{\|\zeta(\cdot,0)\|_\infty^2}{16\,C_*}>0\quad\forall A\ge0 .$$
Thus the historical velocity maximum at $t_n-A/D_n$ stays comparable to $M_n$ for every fixed $A$; any exclusion argument must be consistent with this.

#### Lemma 5.6 (uniform local vorticity). There are $R_0,\kappa_0>0$ depending only on $C_*$ such that for all large $n$, $\sup_{|y|\le R_0}|\zeta_n(y,0)|\ge\kappa_0$, where $\zeta_n$ is the $e_2$ component of $\operatorname{curl}U_n$.

*Proof.* Let $\mathcal F$ be the set of planar fields $V$ on $\mathbb R^2$ with $\|V\|_\infty\le1$, $\|\nabla V\|_\infty\le1$, $\nabla V$ $C_*$-Lipschitz, $\operatorname{div}V=0$, $|\nabla V(0)|=1$. $\mathcal F$ is compact in $C^1_{\rm loc}$. If $\inf_{V\in\mathcal F}\sup_{B_k}|\operatorname{curl}V|\to 0$ along $k\to\infty$... precisely, if for every $k$ some $V^k\in\mathcal F$ has $\sup_{B_k}|\operatorname{curl}V^k|<1/k$, a $C^1_{\rm loc}$ limit $V\in\mathcal F$ is curl free and divergence free, hence constant, contradicting $|\nabla V(0)|=1$. So there is $R_0$ with $\inf_{\mathcal F}\sup_{B_{R_0}}|\operatorname{curl}V|=:2\kappa_0>0$. If the claim failed along a subsequence, a further subsequence of $U_n(\cdot,0)$ converges in $C^1_{\rm loc}$ to $U(\cdot,0)=(V_1,0,V_3)$ with $V\in\mathcal F$ and $\sup_{B_{R_0}}|\zeta(\cdot,0)|\le\kappa_0$, a contradiction. $\square$

### 4.6 Energy restriction (E) and (E')

#### Lemma 6.1 (E). For all large $n$ (namely $\alpha_n\ge 2/(\sqrt3C_*)$ and (A) in force at $t_n$),
$$\frac{r_nM_n^4}{D_n^2}\le\frac{20736}{\pi^2}\,C_*^4\,E_0 .$$

*Proof.* Let $A=\nabla U_n(0,0)$, $|A|_F=1$, so its operator norm is $\ge1/\sqrt3$; pick a unit $e$ with $|Ae|\ge1/\sqrt3$. Taylor's formula with $|\nabla^2U_n|\le C_*$ gives $|U_n(se,0)-U_n(-se,0)-2sAe|\le C_*s^2$. With $s=1/(\sqrt3C_*)$: $|U_n(se,0)-U_n(-se,0)|\ge 2/(3C_*)-1/(3C_*)=1/(3C_*)$, so one of $y^*=\pm se$ has $|U_n(y^*,0)|\ge1/(6C_*)$. Since $|\nabla U_n|\le1$, $|U_n(\cdot,0)|\ge1/(12C_*)$ on $B(y^*,\rho)$, $\rho=1/(12C_*)$. Physically, $|u(\cdot,t_n)|\ge M_n/(12C_*)$ on the ball $B(x^*,\ell_n\rho)$, $x^*=x_n+\ell_ny^*$, whose centre has cylindrical radius $r^*\ge r_n-\ell_ns\ge r_n/2>\ell_n\rho$. $|u|$ is invariant under rotations about the axis, so the bound holds on the union of the rotated balls, which is the solid torus $\{x:|(r(x),z(x))-(r^*,z^*)|<\ell_n\rho\}$ (for each $x$, $\min_Q|Qx-x^*|$ equals that meridional distance). Its volume is $2\pi r^*\cdot\pi\ell_n^2\rho^2$ (Pappus). Hence
$$E_0\ge\|u(t_n)\|_2^2\ge\frac{M_n^2}{144C_*^2}\cdot\frac{\pi^2r_n\ell_n^2}{144C_*^2}=\frac{\pi^2}{20736\,C_*^4}\,\frac{r_nM_n^4}{D_n^2}. \qquad\square$$

#### Corollary 6.2 (E'). $r_n\le C(C_*)E_0\nu_n^2\to0$ and $D_n^3/M_n^5\ge\alpha_n/(C(C_*)E_0)\to\infty$, with $C(C_*)=20736C_*^4/\pi^2$.

*Proof.* $M_n^4/D_n^2=\nu_n^{-2}$, and $\alpha_n=r_nD_n/M_n\le C E_0D_n^3/M_n^5$. $\square$

So $r_n\to0$ physically, while the axis escapes in the normalized variables because $r_n/\ell_n=\alpha_n\to\infty$. Combined with $\nu_n\to0$: $M_n^{5/3}\ll D_n\ll M_n^2$.

### 4.7 Long-window persistence, dissipation, and (NECESSARY)

Let $R_0,\kappa_0$ be as in Lemma 5.6. For each large $n$ pick $y^0_n$, $|y^0_n|\le R_0$, with $|\zeta_n(y^0_n,0)|\ge\kappa_0$, and put $x^0=x_n+\ell_ny_n^0$, with cylindrical coordinates $(r^0,\theta^0,z^0)$. Here $\zeta_n=\omega_2/D_n$ at $x^0$, $|\omega_1|\le\sqrt2D_n$, $|\sin\theta^0|\le2R_0/\alpha_n$ and $\omega^\theta=-\omega_1\sin\theta+\omega_2\cos\theta$, so
$$|\omega^\theta(x^0,t_n)|\ge\kappa_0D_n\cos\theta^0-\sqrt2D_n|\sin\theta^0|\ge\tfrac34\kappa_0D_n\quad(n\text{ large}),\qquad |r^0-r_n|\le R_0\ell_n=R_0r_n/\alpha_n .$$

#### Lemma 7.1 (LONG-WINDOW, localized and instantaneous). Fix $\delta\in(0,1/8]$ and set $\rho_n=3\delta r_n$, $\mathcal D_n=\{(r,z):|(r,z)-(r^0,z^0)|<\rho_n\}$. For the fixed solution and Case 3 sequence there is $N$ (depending on $\delta,C_*,G,\kappa_0$ and on the sequence through the rates of $\alpha_n\to\infty$ and $r_nM_n\to\infty$, but not on $t^-$) such that for $n\ge N$ and every $t^-\in[t_n-\delta r_n/M_n,\,t_n]$ (an interval contained in $(0,T_*)$ for large $n$, since $r_n/M_n\to0$ by Corollary 6.2),
$$\sup_{(r,z)\in\mathcal D_n}|\omega^\theta(r,z,t^-)|\ge c_U D_n,\qquad c_U=\kappa_0/4 .$$
In particular $\|\nabla u(t^-)\|_\infty\ge c_UD_n/\sqrt2$ and $D(t_n-h/D_n)\ge(c_U/\sqrt2)D_n$ for $0\le h\le\delta\alpha_n$.

*Proof.* For large $n$, $r^0\ge\frac78r_n$, so on $\overline{\mathcal D_n}$ we have $r\ge r^0-\rho_n\ge r^0/2=:r_{\min}$ and $r\le 2r_n$ (as $\delta\le1/8$). Let $L=\partial_t+u^r\partial_r+u^z\partial_z-(\partial_{rr}+\frac3r\partial_r+\partial_{zz})$, a uniformly parabolic operator with smooth coefficients on $\overline{\mathcal D_n}\times[t^-,t_n]$, which stays away from the axis. $L\eta=f:=\partial_z(q^2)$ with, by (0.1) and $\|\nabla u(s)\|_\infty\le D_n$ for $s\le t_n$: $|f|\le F:=2GD_n/r_{\min}^3$. Set
$$a_0=\sup_{\mathcal D_n}|\eta(t^-)|\le\frac{\sup_{\mathcal D_n}|\omega^\theta(t^-)|}{r_{\min}},\qquad b_0=\sup_{\partial\mathcal D_n\times[t^-,t_n]}|\eta|\le\frac{\sqrt2D_n}{r_{\min}} .$$
Barrier: with $d=|(r,z)-(r^0,z^0)|$, $a=\rho_n/10$, $g=\sqrt{d^2+a^2}$, $g_1=\sqrt{\rho_n^2+a^2}$, $\beta=M_n$, let $w=\exp(\beta(g-g_1)+\gamma(t-t^-))$. Then $|\nabla g|\le1$, $\Delta_{(r,z)}g=a^2/g^3+1/g\le2/a$, $|\frac3r\partial_rg|\le3/r_{\min}$, $|(u^r,u^z)|\le M_n$, so
$$\frac{Lw}{w}\ge\gamma-\beta M_n-\beta^2-\beta\Big(\frac2a+\frac3{r_{\min}}\Big)\ge0\quad\text{for}\quad \gamma:=2M_n^2+M_n\Big(\frac{20}{\rho_n}+\frac3{r_{\min}}\Big).$$
On $\partial\mathcal D_n$, $w\ge1$. The function $\Psi=a_0+F(t-t^-)+b_0w$ satisfies $L(\Psi\mp\eta)=F\mp f+b_0Lw\ge0$, $\Psi\mp\eta\ge0$ at $t=t^-$ and on the lateral boundary; the classical maximum principle on the bounded cylinder gives $|\eta|\le\Psi$. At $(r^0,z^0,t_n)$, where $g=a$, with $\Delta t=t_n-t^-\le\delta r_n/M_n$, $g_1-a=(\sqrt{1.01}-0.1)\rho_n\ge0.9\rho_n$ and $r_{\min}\ge\frac7{16}r_n$:
$$\beta(a-g_1)+\gamma\Delta t\le-2.7\,\delta r_nM_n+2\delta r_nM_n+\frac{20\delta r_n}{\rho_n}+\frac{3\delta r_n}{r_{\min}}\le-0.7\,\delta r_nM_n+8\ \to-\infty .$$
Now $|\eta(x^0,t_n)|\le a_0+F\Delta t+b_0w(x^0,t_n)$. Multiply by $r^0/D_n$, use $|\eta(x^0,t_n)|\ge\frac34\kappa_0D_n/r^0$ and $r^0/r_{\min}=2$:
$$\tfrac34\kappa_0\le\frac{2\sup_{\mathcal D_n}|\omega^\theta(t^-)|}{D_n}+\frac{16G\,\Delta t}{(r^0)^2}+2\sqrt2\,e^{-0.7\delta r_nM_n+8}.$$
The middle term is $\le16G\delta r_n/(M_n(r^0)^2)\le21G\delta/(r_nM_n)\to0$, and the last tends to $0$. Once these two terms total at most $\kappa_0/4$, $\sup_{\mathcal D_n}|\omega^\theta(t^-)|\ge\kappa_0D_n/4=c_UD_n$. The bound $|\omega|\le\sqrt2|\nabla u|$ gives the rest; $t^-=t_n-h/D_n$ with $h\le\delta\alpha_n$ is exactly $\Delta t\le\delta r_n/M_n$. The threshold for $n$ depends only on $\delta,G,\kappa_0$ and the rates of $r_nM_n\to\infty$, $\alpha_n\to\infty$ along the given sequence, not on $t^-$ (only the upper bound $\Delta t\le\delta r_n/M_n$ entered). $\square$

*Remarks.* (i) This is a genuine prelimit statement on the physical solution: no limit is taken in a changing window. The only inputs are $|\Gamma|\le G$, the record bound $\|\nabla u(s)\|_\infty\le D_n$ for $s\le t_n$, $\|u\|_\infty\le M_n$, $r_nM_n\to\infty$ (from $\alpha_n\to\infty$ and $\nu_n\le C_0$), and the quantitative vorticity at $t_n$ (Lemma 5.6, which uses (A) through the compactness of $\mathcal F$). (ii) $\delta$ cannot be taken beyond a fixed fraction: $\mathcal D_n$ must stay away from the axis. In the normalized variables the window $\delta\alpha_n$ is exactly the time in which meridional transport at speed $\le M_n$ could bring in material from the axis region.
