(iii) The constant bookkeeping in the displayed exponent is crude but only its sign for large $n$ matters.

#### Lemma 7.2 (DISS). For fixed $\delta\in(0,1/8]$ and large $n$,
$$\int_{t_n-\delta r_n/M_n}^{t_n}\int_{\mathbb R^3}|\nabla u|^2dx\,dt\ge c_D\,\delta\,r_n^2M_n,\qquad c_D=\frac{\pi^2c_U^4}{64C_*^2}.$$

*Proof.* Fix $t$ in the window. By Lemma 7.1 and continuity there is $x^-$ with meridional coordinates in the closed disk $\overline{\mathcal D_n}$ (so $r(x^-)\ge r_n/2$; the ball used below need not lie in $\mathcal D_n$ since the Hessian bound is global) and $|\nabla u(x^-,t)|\ge|\omega(x^-,t)|/\sqrt2\ge c_UD_n/\sqrt2$. By (A), $|\nabla^2u(t)|\le H_n\le C_*D_n^2/M_n$, so $|\nabla u(\cdot,t)|\ge c_UD_n/(2\sqrt2)$ on $B(x^-,\lambda)$ with $\lambda=c_UM_n/(2\sqrt2C_*D_n)=c_U\ell_n/(2\sqrt2C_*)<r_n/4$. $|\nabla u|$ (Frobenius) is invariant under rotations about the axis, so the bound holds on a solid torus of volume $2\pi r(x^-)\pi\lambda^2\ge\pi^2r_n\lambda^2$. So $\int|\nabla u(t)|^2\ge\frac{c_U^2D_n^2}{8}\pi^2r_n\frac{c_U^2\ell_n^2}{8C_*^2}=\frac{\pi^2c_U^4}{64C_*^2}r_nM_n^2$. Integrate over the window of length $\delta r_n/M_n$. $\square$

#### Corollary 7.3 (NECESSARY). $r_n^2M_n\to0$.

*Proof.* The energy equality for the smooth solution gives $\int_0^{T_*}\|\nabla u\|_2^2\le E_0/2$. The windows $[t_n-\delta r_n/M_n,t_n]$ are contained in $[t_n-\delta r_n/M_n,T_*)$, and $t_n-\delta r_n/M_n\to T_*$ (since $r_n\to0$, $M_n\to\infty$). By absolute continuity of the integral, the dissipation on these windows tends to $0$; Lemma 7.2 gives $r_n^2M_n\to0$. $\square$

#### Remark 7.4 (instantaneous velocity persistence on the long window). For a bounded $C^2$ field $v$ on $\mathbb R^3$ the one-dimensional inequality of Lemma 1.5(ii), applied to each component $g(s)=v_i(x+se_j)$ along coordinate lines, gives $|\partial_jv_i|^2\le4\|v\|_\infty\|\nabla^2v\|_\infty$, hence $\|\nabla v\|_\infty^2\le36\|v\|_\infty\|\nabla^2v\|_\infty$ (Remark (iii) after Lemma 4.1 gives the constant $12/\sqrt{10}$ in place of $36$; the cruder constant suffices here). With Lemma 7.1 and $\|\nabla^2u(t^-)\|_\infty\le H_n\le C_*D_n^2/M_n$: $\|u(t^-)\|_\infty\ge c_U^2M_n/(72C_*)$ for every $t^-\in[t_n-\delta r_n/M_n,t_n]$, $n\ge N$.

#### Corollary 7.5 (a Case 3 solution is not of Type I). Let $s_n=t_n-\delta r_n/M_n$. Then
$$(T_*-s_n)\|\nabla u(s_n)\|_\infty\ge\frac{c_U\delta}{\sqrt2}\,\alpha_n\to\infty,\qquad (T_*-s_n)\|u(s_n)\|_\infty^2\ge\frac{c_U^4\delta}{72^2C_*^2}\,r_nM_n\to\infty .$$
Hence neither $\|\nabla u(t)\|_\infty\le C/(T_*-t)$ nor $\|u(t)\|_\infty\le C/\sqrt{T_*-t}$ can hold on $[0,T_*)$ for a solution carrying a Case 3 sequence.

*Proof.* $T_*-s_n\ge t_n-s_n=\delta r_n/M_n$; Lemma 7.1 gives $\|\nabla u(s_n)\|_\infty\ge c_UD_n/\sqrt2$ and Remark 7.4 gives $\|u(s_n)\|_\infty\ge c_U^2M_n/(72C_*)$. Multiply, using $r_nD_n/M_n=\alpha_n$. $\square$

*Remark.* Thus any argument for Case 3 that uses a global Type I bound assumes something that a Case 3 solution never satisfies. The receding-axis argument of Constantin, Ignatova and Vicol (arXiv:2609.20762, Hessian formulation) assumes local bounds $\|u(t)\|_{L^\infty(B_1)}\le C_0\ell(t)/(-t)$ and $\|\nabla^2u(t)\|_{L^\infty(B_1)}\le C_2/((-t)\ell(t))$ with an Euler length $\ell$; by Taylor's inequality with step $h=\ell(t)<1/2$ these give, for every unit vector $e$, $|\partial_eu(x,t)|\le(2C_0+C_2/2)/(-t)$ on $B_{1/2}$, hence the local interior gradient Type I bound $|\nabla u(x,t)|\le\sqrt3(2C_0+C_2/2)/(-t)$ for the Frobenius norm. Local bounds on one ball do not imply a global bound; they contradict the persistence of Lemma 7.1 when the ball contains the long-window vorticity points uniformly in $n$ (in particular when they hold globally).

#### Corollary 7.6 (source budget for $\eta$). Fix $t_0\in(t_A,T_*)$. For large $n$,
$$\frac{3\kappa_0}{8}\,\frac{D_n}{r_n}\le\|\eta(t_0)\|_\infty+4G^{1/3}\int_{t_0}^{t_n}H(s)^{5/3}\,ds\le\|\eta(t_0)\|_\infty+4G^{1/3}C_*^{5/3}\int_{t_0}^{t_n}\Big(\frac{D(s)^2}{M(s)}\Big)^{5/3}ds .$$

*Proof.* $|\eta(x^0,t_n)|=|\omega^\theta(x^0,t_n)|/r^0\ge\frac34\kappa_0D_n/(2r_n)$. Apply Lemma 1.6 on $[t_0,t_n]$ and Lemma 1.5(ii) at each $s$ (with $H(s)$, using that the proof of 1.5(ii) at time $s$ only uses $S(s),H(s)$), then (A). $\square$

This is a necessary norm budget on the history: $\int_{t_0}^{t_n}\|\partial_z(q^2)(s)\|_\infty ds\ge\frac{3\kappa_0}8\frac{D_n}{r_n}-\|\eta(t_0)\|_\infty$. It does not assert a signed injection of $\eta$ along any particular material trajectory. In the absence of swirl ($G=0$) it is violated, recovering the classical regularity of swirl-free axisymmetric data for this branch.

#### Lemma 7.7 (critical $L^1$ budget for $\eta$). Assume in addition the data decay condition
$$\text{(L1}\eta\text{)}\qquad \eta_0=\omega_0^\theta/r\in L^1(\mathbb R^3),\quad\text{equivalently}\quad \omega_0^\theta\in L^1((0,\infty)\times\mathbb R;dr\,dz),$$
which holds for instance if $|\omega_0(x)|\le C(1+|x|)^{-2-\epsilon}$ (near the axis $\eta_0$ is bounded, and $\|\eta_0\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}=2\pi\iint|\omega_0^\theta|\,dr\,dz$). Then for $0\le t<T_*$
$$\|\eta(t)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}\le\|\eta_0\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}+\int_0^t\|\partial_z(q^2)(s)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}\,ds,$$
where $\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}=2\pi\iint|\partial_z((u^\theta)^2)|\,r^{-1}\,dr\,dz$ may be $+\infty$. Both $\|\eta(t)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}$ and $\int\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}dt$ are invariant under the NS scaling $u\mapsto\lambda u(\lambda x,\lambda^2t)$ (then $\eta\mapsto\lambda^3\eta(\lambda x,\lambda^2t)$ and $q\mapsto\lambda^2q(\lambda x,\lambda^2t)$).

*Proof.* Fix $t<T_*$. On $\mathbb R^3\times[0,t]$ the functions $\eta,\nabla\eta,\nabla^2\eta,\partial_t\eta,u$ and $f=\partial_z(q^2)$ are bounded and continuous ($u\in C([0,T_*);H^k)$ for all $k$, and $\eta=F(r^2,z)$ with $F$ smooth, as in the proof of Lemma 1.6). Let $\phi_\epsilon(\sigma)=\sqrt{\sigma^2+\epsilon^2}-\epsilon$ (convex, $0\le\phi_\epsilon(\sigma)\le|\sigma|$, $|\phi_\epsilon'|\le1$) and $\psi_R(x)=(1+|x|^2/R^2)^{-2}$, which is integrable on $\mathbb R^3$, increases to $1$ as $R\to\infty$, and satisfies
$$|\nabla\psi_R|\le\frac2R\psi_R,\qquad|\Delta\psi_R|\le\frac{12}{R^2}\psi_R,\qquad 0\le-\frac2r\partial_r\psi_R=\frac8{R^2}\Big(1+\frac{|x|^2}{R^2}\Big)^{-3}\le\frac8{R^2}\psi_R$$
(with $\rho=|x|/R$: $|\nabla\psi_R|=\frac{4\rho}{R(1+\rho^2)}\psi_R$ and $\Delta\psi_R=12R^{-2}(\rho^2-1)(1+\rho^2)^{-4}$). Write the $\eta$ equation as $\partial_t\eta=-b\cdot\nabla\eta+\Delta\eta+\frac2r\partial_r\eta+f$ ($\Delta$ the 3D Laplacian), multiply by $\phi_\epsilon'(\eta)\psi_R$ and integrate over $\mathbb R^3$. Every integrand is a bounded function times $\psi_R$ or $|\nabla\psi_R|=O(|x|^{-4})$, so the surface terms on large spheres vanish. Term by term:
- transport: $-\int\phi_\epsilon'(\eta)\,b\cdot\nabla\eta\,\psi_R=-\int b\cdot\nabla(\phi_\epsilon(\eta))\psi_R=\int\phi_\epsilon(\eta)\,b\cdot\nabla\psi_R\le\frac{2\|u(s)\|_\infty}R\int\phi_\epsilon(\eta)\psi_R$, since $\operatorname{div}b=\operatorname{div}u=0$;
- diffusion: $\int\phi_\epsilon'(\eta)\Delta\eta\,\psi_R=-\int\phi_\epsilon''(\eta)|\nabla\eta|^2\psi_R+\int\phi_\epsilon(\eta)\Delta\psi_R\le\frac{12}{R^2}\int\phi_\epsilon(\eta)\psi_R$;
- the $\frac2r\partial_r$ term, with $dx=2\pi r\,dr\,dz$: $\int\phi_\epsilon'(\eta)\frac2r\partial_r\eta\,\psi_R\,dx=4\pi\iint\partial_r(\phi_\epsilon(\eta))\psi_R\,dr\,dz=-4\pi\int\phi_\epsilon(\eta(0,z))\psi_R(0,z)\,dz-\int\phi_\epsilon(\eta)\frac2r\partial_r\psi_R\,dx\le\frac8{R^2}\int\phi_\epsilon(\eta)\psi_R$;
- source: $\int\phi_\epsilon'(\eta)f\psi_R\le\|f(s)\|_{L^1}$.

Hence $X(s)=\int\phi_\epsilon(\eta(s))\psi_R\,dx$ satisfies $X'(s)\le a_R(s)X(s)+\|f(s)\|_{L^1}$ on $(0,t]$ with $a_R(s)=2\|u(s)\|_\infty/R+20/R^2$, and $X$ is continuous on $[0,t]$ with $X(0)\le\|\eta_0\|_{L^1}$. Therefore $X(t)\le\exp\big(\int_0^ta_R\big)\big(\|\eta_0\|_{L^1}+\int_0^t\|f\|_{L^1}ds\big)$, and $\int_0^ta_R\le2tM(t)/R+20t/R^2\to0$ as $R\to\infty$. Monotone convergence in $R$ and then in $\epsilon\downarrow0$ gives the claim. (The exponential factor is harmless: it tends to $1$ for each fixed $t<T_*$; no Gronwall closure in an uncontrolled quantity is used.) $\square$

#### Lemma 7.8 (the record core carries a critical $L^1$ mass of order $1/\nu_n$). For all large $n$,
$$\|\eta(t_n)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}=2\pi\iint|\omega^\theta(r,z,t_n)|\,dr\,dz\ge c_1\,\frac{M_n^2}{D_n}=\frac{c_1}{\nu_n},\qquad c_1=\frac{27\pi^2\kappa_0^3}{512\,C_*^2}.$$

*Proof.* In the half-plane $\theta=0$ we have $e_\theta=e_2$, so $\omega^\theta(r,z,t)=\omega_2(r,0,z,t)$. Since $\partial_k\omega_2=\partial_k\partial_3u_1-\partial_k\partial_1u_3$, $|\nabla\omega_2|\le\sqrt2|\nabla^2u|$, so by (A) $|\nabla\omega_2(\cdot,t_n)|\le\sqrt2H_n\le\sqrt2C_*D_n^2/M_n$. By the preamble of this section $|\omega^\theta(r^0,z^0,t_n)|\ge\frac34\kappa_0D_n$. Hence $|\omega^\theta(\cdot,t_n)|\ge\frac38\kappa_0D_n$ on the meridional disk of radius $\rho_1=\frac{3\kappa_0D_n/8}{\sqrt2C_*D_n^2/M_n}=\frac{3\kappa_0}{8\sqrt2C_*}\ell_n$ about $(r^0,z^0)$, which lies in $\{r>0\}$ for large $n$ because $\rho_1/r^0\le2\rho_1/r_n\to0$ ($\alpha_n\to\infty$). So $\|\eta(t_n)\|_{L^1}\ge2\pi\cdot\frac38\kappa_0D_n\cdot\pi\rho_1^2=2\pi^2\cdot\frac38\cdot\frac{9}{128}\frac{\kappa_0^3}{C_*^2}\,\ell_n^2D_n$, and $\ell_n^2D_n=M_n^2/D_n$. $\square$

#### Corollary 7.9 (critical-norm blowup forced by Case 3). Under (L1$\eta$), along a Case 3 sequence,
$$\frac{c_1}{\nu_n}=c_1\frac{M_n^2}{D_n}\le\|\eta_0\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}+\int_0^{t_n}\|\partial_z(q^2)(s)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}\,ds .$$
Hence the scale-invariant quantities $\sup_{t<T_*}\|\eta(t)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}$ and $\int_0^{T_*}\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}dt$ are both infinite. In physical terms (viscosity $1$), $1/\nu_n=M_n\ell_n$ is the Reynolds number of the record core and, up to the constant, its meridional circulation $\iint_{\text{core}}|\omega^\theta|\,dr\,dz$; the swirl source $\partial_z((u^\theta)^2)/r$ of the $\omega^\theta$ equation must have produced at least this much meridional circulation, measured in $L^1(dt\,dr\,dz)$: $\int\!\iint|\partial_z((u^\theta)^2)|/r\,dr\,dz\,dt=\frac1{2\pi}\int\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}dt$.

*Proof.* Lemmas 7.7 and 7.8 at $t=t_n$. $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) Conversely, any a priori estimate giving $\int_0^{t_n}\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}ds=o(M_n^2/D_n)$ along the records, or $\sup_t\|\eta(t)\|_{L^1}<\infty$, would exclude Case 3 under (L1$\eta$). By Lemma 7.26 the first form does not need (L1$\eta$): an estimate $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}ds=o(1/\nu_n)$ for one fixed $s_0\in(0,T_*)$ excludes Case 3 for every smooth finite-energy datum. No such estimate is proved here. (2) The bound obtainable from scalar information is far from sufficient: pointwise $|q|\le|\nabla u|$ and $|\partial_zq|\le|\nabla^2u|$ (Section 4.0; $\partial_{x_2}\partial_{x_3}u_1=-\partial_zu^\theta/r$ at $\theta=0$), so $\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}\le2\|\nabla u\|_2\|\nabla^2u\|_2$, and the enstrophy inequality $\frac12\|\omega(t)\|_2^2+\int_0^t\|\nabla\omega\|_2^2\le\frac12\|\omega_0\|_2^2+D(t)\int_0^t\|\omega\|_2^2$ with $\|\nabla^2u\|_2=\|\nabla\omega\|_2$ gives $\int_0^{t_n}\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}\le\sqrt{2E_0}\,\big(\tfrac12\|\omega_0\|_2^2+\tfrac12D_nE_0\big)^{1/2}$. Corollary 7.9 then only yields $M_n^2\lesssim D_n^{3/2}$, weaker than $D_n\gg M_n^{5/3}$ from (E'). In the power-law test of 8.2, $M^2/D=s^{-1/10}$ while $\sqrt D=s^{-7/10}$, so 7.9 is compatible with it. (3) For $G=0$ the source vanishes and $\|\eta(t)\|_{L^1}\le\|\eta_0\|_{L^1}$ contradicts 7.8, again recovering the swirl-free case. (4) Corollaries 7.6 and 7.9 are independent budgets: 7.6 asks for $\gtrsim D_n/r_n$ in $L^1_tL^\infty_x$, 7.9 for $\gtrsim M_n^2/D_n$ in $L^1_tL^1_x$.

#### Lemma 7.10 (stopped backward representation and drift-independent annular smoothing).
Notation: $Z(s)=\|\nabla u(s)\|_{L^2(\mathbb R^3)}^2$, $Q_n(T)=\int_{t_n-T}^{t_n}Z(s)\,ds$, $\mu=r\,dr\,dz$ on the meridional half-plane, $\mathcal L_s=\partial_{rr}+\frac3r\partial_r+\partial_{zz}-u^r(s)\partial_r-u^z(s)\partial_z$, so that $\partial_s\eta=\mathcal L_s\eta+f$, $f=\partial_z(q^2)$. Let $\mathcal O$ be an open subset of the half-plane with smooth boundary and $\overline{\mathcal O}\subset\{\vartheta r_n\le r\le Lr_n\}$ for some $0<\vartheta<1<L$ (bounded or a strip unbounded in $z$).

(a) Fix $(r,z)\in\mathcal O$ and $t\le t_n$. Fix $0<h<t$. Let $X_a=(R_a,Z_a)$, $0\le a\le h$, solve $dX_a=\big(\tfrac3{R_a}-u^r,\,-u^z\big)(X_a,t-a)\,da+\sqrt2\,dW_a$, $X_0=(r,z)$, with $W$ a planar Brownian motion, where in $\{r<\vartheta r_n/2\}$ (including $r\le0$) the coefficients are replaced by a smooth bounded extension to the whole plane (it exists for fixed $n,\vartheta,t,h$; this does not affect anything before the exit time), and let $\tau$ be the exit time from $\mathcal O$. Then
$$\eta(r,z,t)=\mathbb E\big[\eta(X_h,t-h);\tau>h\big]+\mathbb E\big[\eta(X_\tau,t-\tau);\tau\le h\big]+\mathbb E\int_0^{\tau\wedge h}f(X_a,t-a)\,da ,$$
and the first term equals $(P_{\mathcal O}(t,t-h)\eta(t-h))(r,z)$, where $P_{\mathcal O}(t,s)\varphi$ denotes, for bounded continuous $\varphi$, the value at time $t$ of the bounded solution of $\partial_tv=\mathcal L_tv$ in $\mathcal O$, $v=0$ on $\partial\mathcal O$, $v(s)=\varphi$; for $\varphi\in L^2(\mathcal O,\mu)$ it is defined by density, using the $L^2(\mu)$ contraction in the proof of (b).

(b) For $s<t\le t_n$ and $\varphi\in L^2(\mathcal O,\mu)$,
$$\|P_{\mathcal O}(t,s)\varphi\|_{L^\infty}\le\frac{(C_N/2)^{1/2}L}{\vartheta^{3/2}}\,\big(r_n(t-s)\big)^{-1/2}\|\varphi\|_{L^2(\mathcal O,\mu)},$$
where $C_N$ is the constant of the planar Nash inequality $\|g\|_{L^2(\mathbb R^2)}^4\le C_N\|g\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^2)}^2\|\nabla g\|_{L^2(\mathbb R^2)}^2$. The bound does not depend on $u$.

(c) Consequently $\big|(P_{\mathcal O}(t,s)\eta(s))(r,z)\big|^2\le\dfrac{C_NL^2}{4\pi\vartheta^5r_n^3(t-s)}\,Z(s)$.

*Proof.* (a) The drift is bounded and smooth on $\overline{\mathcal O}\times[0,t]$, so the equation has a unique global strong solution. Away from the axis the generator of $X$ at backward time $a$ is $\mathcal L_{t-a}$, so Itô's formula and the $\eta$ equation give $d\,\eta(X_a,t-a)=-f(X_a,t-a)\,da+\sqrt2\,\nabla\eta(X_a,t-a)\cdot dW_a$ for $a<\tau$. Stop additionally at the exit time $\tau_k$ from $\{|z|<k\}$, where $\nabla\eta$ is bounded, take expectations (the stochastic integral has mean zero), and let $k\to\infty$; $\eta$ and $f$ are bounded on $\mathbb R^3\times[t-h,t]$ (Lemmas 1.5, 1.6 and $|\eta|\le\sqrt2\|\nabla u\|_\infty/r$), so bounded convergence applies. The identification of the first term with the Dirichlet evolution is the standard probabilistic representation of the Cauchy-Dirichlet problem for a uniformly parabolic operator with smooth bounded coefficients on a smooth domain (for instance A. Friedman, *Stochastic Differential Equations and Applications*, Vol. 1, Academic Press 1975, Chapter 6), applied on a nested sequence of bounded smooth domains $\mathcal O_k\uparrow\mathcal O$ (for instance $\mathcal O\cap\{|z|<k\}$ with the corners smoothed so that $\mathcal O_k\subset\mathcal O_{k+1}$). For bounded $\varphi\ge0$ the survival indicators $\mathbf 1\{\tau_k>h\}$ increase to $\mathbf 1\{\tau>h\}$ (continuous paths are bounded on $[0,h]$), so the representations converge monotonically; interior parabolic regularity and boundary barriers identify the limit as a bounded solution of the Dirichlet problem on $\mathcal O$, and bounded solutions are unique by the same stopped Ito argument. Signed bounded data follow by linearity. (Friedman's chapter is cited for the bounded-domain representation; the exhaustion step is the argument just given.)

(b) Write $\mathcal L_s=\Delta_\mu+c_s\cdot\nabla$ with $\Delta_\mu=\frac1r\partial_r(r\partial_r)+\partial_{zz}$ and $c_s=(\frac2r-u^r,-u^z)$. Incompressibility $\frac1r\partial_r(ru^r)+\partial_zu^z=0$ gives
$$\operatorname{div}_\mu c_s:=\frac1r\partial_r(rc_s^r)+\partial_zc_s^z=\frac1r\partial_r(2-ru^r)-\partial_zu^z=0 .$$
Hence for a solution of the killed problem (on bounded smooth approximating domains first) $\frac{d}{dt}\int v^2d\mu=-2\int|\nabla v|^2d\mu+\int c_t\cdot\nabla(v^2)\,d\mu=-2\int|\nabla v|^2d\mu$, the drift term vanishing after integration by parts because $\operatorname{div}_\mu c_t=0$ and $v=0$ on $\partial\mathcal O$. The adjoint of $\mathcal L_s$ in $L^2(\mu)$ with Dirichlet conditions is $\Delta_\mu-c_s\cdot\nabla$, of the same form, so the backward adjoint problem $-\partial_sw=(\Delta_\mu-c_s\cdot\nabla)w$, $w(t)=\psi$, satisfies the same $L^2(\mu)$ identity in reversed time; it preserves positivity and does not increase $\int|w|\,d\mu$ (for $w\ge0$, $\int\Delta_\mu w\,d\mu=\int_{\partial\mathcal O}r\,\partial_\nu w\,dS\le0$ with $\nu$ the outward normal, and $\int c_s\cdot\nabla w\,d\mu=0$ because $\operatorname{div}_\mu c_s=0$ and $w=0$ on $\partial\mathcal O$; split $\psi=\psi_+-\psi_-$). For $g$ supported in $\vartheta r_n\le r\le Lr_n$, comparing $\mu$ with $dr\,dz$ in the planar Nash inequality (zero extension) gives
$$\|g\|_{L^2(\mu)}^4\le L^2r_n^2\|g\|_{L^2}^4\le\frac{C_NL^2}{\vartheta^3r_n}\,\|g\|_{L^1(\mu)}^2\|\nabla g\|_{L^2(\mu)}^2 .$$
For $y(\sigma)=\|w(t-\sigma)\|_{L^2(\mu)}^2$ this gives $y'\le-2\vartheta^3r_ny^2/(C_NL^2\|\psi\|_{L^1(\mu)}^2)$, so $\|w(s)\|_{L^2(\mu)}\le(C_N/2)^{1/2}L\vartheta^{-3/2}(r_n(t-s))^{-1/2}\|\psi\|_{L^1(\mu)}$. By duality, $\int P_{\mathcal O}(t,s)\varphi\,\psi\,d\mu=\int\varphi\,w(s)\,d\mu$, and taking the supremum over $\|\psi\|_{L^1(\mu)}\le1$ gives (b).

(c) On $\mathcal O$, $|\eta|=|\omega^\theta|/r\le|\omega^\theta|/(\vartheta r_n)$, and $\int|\omega^\theta|^2d\mu=\frac1{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb R^3}|\omega^\theta|^2dx\le\frac1{2\pi}\|\omega\|_{L^2}^2=\frac1{2\pi}Z(s)$. Insert into (b). $\square$

#### Corollary 7.11 (dissipation on the long window, improved). For fixed $\delta\in(0,1/8]$ and large $n$,
$$Q_n(\delta r_n/M_n)\ge c_Q\,\delta^2\,\frac{r_n^3D_n^2}{M_n^2}=c_Q\,\delta^2\,r_n\alpha_n^2,\qquad c_Q=\frac{9\pi\kappa_0^2}{2^{14}\,C_N}.$$
Consequently $r_n\alpha_n^2\to0$. Since $r_n\alpha_n^2=r_n^2M_n\cdot\alpha_n\nu_n$ and $\alpha_n\nu_n=(r_n/M_n)/\ell_n^2$, the ratio of this lower bound to that of Lemma 7.2 is, up to constants, the ratio of the transport time $r_n/M_n$ to the viscous time $\ell_n^2$ of the core. The new bound is larger by a factor tending to infinity when $\alpha_n\nu_n\to\infty$; if $\alpha_n\nu_n$ stays bounded, Corollary 7.3 already gives $r_n\alpha_n^2\to0$; an oscillating ratio has to be treated along subsequences.

*Proof.* Let $x^0=(r^0,z^0)$ and $a_n=|\eta(x^0,t_n)|$; by the preamble of this section and $r^0\le2r_n$, $a_n\ge\frac38\kappa_0D_n/r_n$. Let $\mathcal O=\mathcal D_n$, the meridional disk of radius $3\delta r_n$ about $x^0$; for large $n$, $\overline{\mathcal O}\subset\{r_n/2\le r\le2r_n\}$. Apply Lemma 7.10(a) with $t=t_n$ and $0<h\le\delta r_n/M_n$, multiplied by $\operatorname{sgn}\eta(x^0,t_n)$. On $\mathcal O$ the drift has size $\le M_n+6/r_n$, so its displacement up to time $h$ is $\le\delta r_n+6\delta/M_n\le2\delta r_n$ once $r_nM_n\ge6$; exit before $h$ therefore requires $\sqrt2\sup_{a\le h}|W_a|\ge\delta r_n$, which has probability $\le4\exp(-\delta^2r_n^2/(8h))\le4e^{-\delta r_nM_n/8}$. On $\partial\mathcal O$, $|\eta|\le2\sqrt2D_n/r_n$, and on $\mathcal O$, $|f|\le16GD_n/r_n^3$ by (0.1). Hence
$$(P_{\mathcal O}(t_n,t_n-h)\eta(t_n-h))(x^0)\operatorname{sgn}\eta(x^0,t_n)\ge a_n-8\sqrt2\frac{D_n}{r_n}e^{-\delta r_nM_n/8}-\frac{16G\delta}{r_nM_n}\frac{D_n}{r_n}\ge\frac{a_n}2$$
for large $n$ (as $r_nM_n\to\infty$), uniformly in $h$. Lemma 7.10(c) with $\vartheta=1/2$, $L=2$ gives $a_n^2/4\le32C_NZ(t_n-h)/(\pi r_n^3h)$, so $Z(t_n-h)\ge\pi r_n^3h\,a_n^2/(128C_N)\ge\frac{9\pi\kappa_0^2}{2^{13}C_N}r_nD_n^2h$. Integrate over $h\in[0,\delta r_n/M_n]$ ($\int h\,dh=\delta^2r_n^2/(2M_n^2)$). The limit follows as in Corollary 7.3. $\square$

#### Theorem 7.12 (annular/axis dichotomy). Put $a_n=|\eta(x^0,t_n)|$, $v_n=\operatorname{sgn}(\eta(x^0,t_n))\,\eta$, and fix $\epsilon=3\kappa_0/128$, $L=32/\kappa_0$. For $0<\vartheta\le1/4$ let $T_\vartheta=T_{\vartheta,n}=\epsilon\vartheta^3r_n^2/(1+G)$, $\mathcal A_\vartheta=\{\vartheta r_n<r<Lr_n\}$, let $X$ be as in Lemma 7.10(a) with $(r,z)=x^0$, $t=t_n$, let $\tau_\vartheta$ be its exit time from $\mathcal A_\vartheta$, and define the net inner-boundary contribution
$$I_n(\vartheta,h)=\mathbb E\big[v_n(X_{\tau_\vartheta},t_n-\tau_\vartheta);\ \tau_\vartheta\le h,\ R_{\tau_\vartheta}=\vartheta r_n\big],\qquad J_n(\vartheta)=\sup_{0<h\le T_\vartheta}\big(I_n(\vartheta,h)\big)_+ .$$
(The positive part is taken after the expectation, so a large $J_n$ is a net contribution with the sign of the record.) Note $T_\vartheta\to0$ and $T_\vartheta M_n/r_n=\epsilon\vartheta^3r_nM_n/(1+G)\to\infty$ for fixed $\vartheta$. Then along every Case 3 sequence at least one of the following holds on a subsequence.

(I) *Long-lived annular alternative.* There is a fixed $\vartheta\in(0,1/4]$ with $J_n(\vartheta)\le a_n/4$. Then for all $0<h\le T_\vartheta$: $(P_{\mathcal A_\vartheta}(t_n,t_n-h)v_n(t_n-h))(x^0)\ge a_n/2$, hence $\sup_{\mathcal A_\vartheta}|\omega^\theta(t_n-h)|\ge\frac3{16}\kappa_0\vartheta D_n$ (persistence on a time $\asymp r_n^2\gg r_n/M_n$), and
$$Q_n(T_\vartheta)\ge\frac{27\pi\kappa_0^2\,\epsilon^2}{512\,C_NL^2(1+G)^2}\,\vartheta^{11}\,r_n^5D_n^2,\qquad\text{so}\qquad r_n^5D_n^2\to0 .$$

(II) *Axis alternative.* There are $\vartheta_n\to0$ with $J_n(\vartheta_n)>a_n/4$: a net fraction $>1/4$ of the record value of $\eta$ is carried, in the stopped representation, from the cylinder $r=\vartheta_nr_n$ of radius $o(r_n)$. In this alternative, with $T=T_{\vartheta_n}$,
$$\int_{t_n-T}^{t_n}\|u^r(s)_+\|_{L^\infty}\,ds\ge(1-o(1))\,r_n,\qquad\int_{t_n-T}^{t_n}\Big\|\Big(\frac{u^r(s)}r\Big)_+\Big\|_{L^\infty}ds\ge\log\frac1{\vartheta_n}-o(1),$$
where the suprema may be taken over $\mathcal A_{\vartheta_n}$ only, and in particular $\int_{t_n-T}^{t_n}D(s)\,ds\ge\log(1/\vartheta_n)-o(1)$. In this integrated sense the contribution must have been carried outward by radial velocity, subject to an accumulated positive radial strain $\log(1/\vartheta_n)\to\infty$; this is a statement about a weighted contribution of the stopped representation, not about a single material trajectory.

*Proof.* Choice of constants: on $\mathcal A_\vartheta$ and for $h\le T_\vartheta$, the source term in Lemma 7.10(a) is at most $h\cdot2GD_n/(\vartheta r_n)^3\le2\epsilon\frac{G}{1+G}\frac{D_n}{r_n}$ and the outer-boundary term at most $\sqrt2D_n/(Lr_n)$; their sum is $\le(\frac{3}{64}+\frac{\sqrt2}{32})\kappa_0\frac{D_n}{r_n}\le\frac{3\kappa_0}{32}\frac{D_n}{r_n}\le\frac{a_n}4$.

If for some fixed $\vartheta=1/k$, $k\ge4$, $J_n(\vartheta)\le a_n/4$ for infinitely many $n$, pass to these $n$. By Lemma 7.10(a) (times $\operatorname{sgn}\eta(x^0,t_n)$), for $0<h\le T_\vartheta$ the killed term is $\ge a_n-a_n/4-a_n/4=a_n/2$. The killed evolution does not increase $\sup|\cdot|$, so $\sup_{\mathcal A_\vartheta}|\eta(t_n-h)|\ge a_n/2$, and since $|\omega^\theta|=r|\eta|$ with $r\ge\vartheta r_n$ on $\mathcal A_\vartheta$, $\sup_{\mathcal A_\vartheta}|\omega^\theta(t_n-h)|\ge\vartheta r_na_n/2\ge\frac3{16}\kappa_0\vartheta D_n$. Lemma 7.10(c) gives $Z(t_n-h)\ge\pi\vartheta^5r_n^3h\,a_n^2/(C_NL^2)\ge\frac{9\pi\kappa_0^2}{64C_NL^2}\vartheta^5r_nD_n^2h$; integrating over $h\in[T_\vartheta/2,T_\vartheta]$ (which contributes $\frac38T_\vartheta^2$) gives the displayed bound. Since $T_\vartheta\to0$ and $t_n\to T_*$, $Q_n(T_\vartheta)\to0$ by absolute continuity of $\int_0^{T_*}Z\le E_0/2$, so $r_n^5D_n^2\to0$ along the subsequence. If no such $k$ exists, then for every $k\ge4$ there is $N_k$ with $J_n(1/k)>a_n/4$ for $n\ge N_k$; choosing $N_k$ increasing and $\vartheta_n=1/k$ for $N_k\le n<N_{k+1}$ gives (II).

Transport in (II). Let $\rho=\vartheta_nr_n$, $T=T_{\vartheta_n}$ and $p_n=\mathbb P(\tau_{\vartheta_n}\le T,\ R_{\tau_{\vartheta_n}}=\rho)$. On $\{r=\rho\}$, $|v_n|\le\sqrt2D_n/\rho$, so $a_n/4<J_n(\vartheta_n)\le p_n\sqrt2D_n/\rho$ and $p_n\ge\frac{3\kappa_0}{32\sqrt2}\vartheta_n$. Before $\tau_{\vartheta_n}$, $dR_a=(\frac3{R_a}-u^r)\,da+\sqrt2\,d\beta_a$ with $\beta$ the first coordinate of $W$, so $R_a\ge r^0-\mathcal V_++\sqrt2\beta_a$, $\mathcal V_+=\int_{t_n-T}^{t_n}\|u^r_+\|_{L^\infty(\mathcal A_{\vartheta_n})}$ (the process stays in $\mathcal A_{\vartheta_n}$ before $\tau_{\vartheta_n}$; the same localization applies to $\mathcal K_+$ below). Reaching $r=\rho$ by time $T$ thus requires $\min_{a\le T}\sqrt2\beta_a\le-(r^0-\rho-\mathcal V_+)$, whence, by the reflection principle, $p_n\le\exp(-(r^0-\rho-\mathcal V_+)_+^2/(4T))$. With $p_n\ge c\vartheta_n$ this gives $(r^0-\rho-\mathcal V_+)_+\le2(T\log(C/\vartheta_n))^{1/2}=r_nO\big((\vartheta_n^3\log(C/\vartheta_n))^{1/2}\big)=o(r_n)$, and $r^0/r_n\to1$, $\rho=o(r_n)$ give the first bound. For the second, Itô's formula gives $d\log R_a=(-\frac{u^r}{R_a}+\frac2{R_a^2})\,da+dN_a$, $N_a=\int_0^a\sqrt2R^{-1}d\beta$, with $\langle N\rangle_{a\wedge\tau}\le2a/\rho^2$. Reaching $\rho$ requires $N\le-x$ with $x=\log(r^0/\rho)-\mathcal K_+$, $\mathcal K_+=\int_{t_n-T}^{t_n}\|(u^r/r)_+\|_\infty$. For $\lambda>0$, $\exp(-\lambda N_{a\wedge\tau}-\frac{\lambda^2}2\langle N\rangle_{a\wedge\tau})$ is a positive martingale (Novikov's condition holds because $\langle N\rangle_{a\wedge\tau}$ is bounded); if $x>0$, optional stopping at the first time $N\le-x$ gives $p_n\le\exp(-\lambda x+\lambda^2T/\rho^2)$, and $\lambda=x\rho^2/(2T)$ gives $p_n\le\exp(-x^2\rho^2/(4T))$; if $x\le0$, $p_n\le1$. In both cases $p_n\le\exp(-x_+^2\rho^2/(4T))$. Since $\rho^2/T=(1+G)/(\epsilon\vartheta_n)$, $x_+\le(4\epsilon\vartheta_n\log(C/\vartheta_n))^{1/2}\to0$, and $\log(r^0/\rho)=\log(1/\vartheta_n)+o(1)$. Finally $|u^r(r,z)|=|u_1(r,0,z)|\le r\|\nabla u\|_\infty$ because $u_1=0$ on the axis, so $\|(u^r/r)_+\|_\infty\le D(s)$. $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) The alternatives are not exclusive, and they concern weighted contributions in the stopped representation, not single material trajectories. (2) In (I), $\vartheta$ is fixed; the constant degenerates like $\vartheta^{11}$, so no conclusion for shrinking annuli is drawn from (I). (3) The theorem identifies the obstruction beyond the transport window precisely: by (0.1) the source is small on $\{r\ge\vartheta r_n\}$ for times $\ll\vartheta^3r_n^2$, much longer than $r_n/M_n$ (and, when $G>0$, the one-sided comparison error of Theorem 7.18, Remark (4), is $o(D_n/r_n)$ for $h=o(\vartheta^5r_n^4D_n/G^2)$ provided $\vartheta^3R_n\to\infty$, which includes fixed $\vartheta$); what is not controlled is the inner-boundary term, i.e. $\eta$ carried outward from radii $o(r_n)$ and stretched. (4) The weighted divergence identity, the weight comparison in the Nash inequality, the exit and hitting estimates and the constants were checked step by step. (5) Since $\|u^r\|_\infty\le M_n$, the first bound in (II) forces $T_{\vartheta_n}M_n\ge(1-o(1))r_n$, i.e. $\vartheta_n^3\ge(1-o(1))(1+G)/(\epsilon\,r_nM_n)$: the inner radius $\vartheta_nr_n$ in (II) is at least of order $r_n^{2/3}M_n^{-1/3}$, and the horizon $T_{\vartheta_n}$ is at least the transport time $r_n/M_n$. Conversely, uniformly in the range: put $c_\sharp^3=(1+G)/(4\epsilon)$; for $\vartheta\le c_\sharp(r_nM_n)^{-1/3}$ one has $M_nT_\vartheta\le r_n/4$, so (for large $n$, with $r^0\ge\frac78r_n$, $\vartheta\le\frac14$) reaching $r=\vartheta r_n$ requires a Brownian radial displacement $\ge\frac38r_n$, whence $p_\vartheta\le\exp(-\frac{9(1+G)}{256\epsilon\vartheta^3})$ and $J_n(\vartheta)/a_n\le\frac{8\sqrt2}{3\kappa_0\vartheta}\exp(-\frac{9(1+G)}{256\epsilon\vartheta^3})=o(1)$ uniformly in that range. For such $\vartheta$ the estimates of (I) that precede the fixed-$\vartheta$ limit hold (the killed average $\ge a_n/2$ for $h\le T_\vartheta$, persistence, and the $\vartheta$-dependent dissipation bound), with $T_\vartheta\lesssim r_n/M_n$ (not necessarily comparable: $\vartheta=(r_nM_n)^{-1}$ gives $T_\vartheta M_n/r_n\to0$); the conclusion $r_n^5D_n^2\to0$ is not claimed for $\vartheta=\vartheta_n\to0$, and the dissipation bound is asymptotically weaker than Corollary 7.11, since $\vartheta^{11}r_n^5D_n^2/(r_n^3D_n^2/M_n^2)=\vartheta^{11}(r_nM_n)^2\lesssim(r_nM_n)^{-5/3}$.

#### Corollary 7.13 (meridional circulation before the record). In the setting of Lemma 7.10, the killed evolution also satisfies $\|P_{\mathcal O}(t,s)\varphi\|_{L^\infty}\le\frac{C_NL^2}{\vartheta^3r_n(t-s)}\|\varphi\|_{L^1(\mathcal O,\mu)}$. Consequently, for large $n$:

(a) for $0<h\le\delta r_n/M_n$, $\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal D_n}|\omega^\theta(r,z,t_n-h)|\,dr\,dz\ge\frac{3\kappa_0}{512\,C_N}\,D_nh$; in particular the meridional circulation near the record at the start of the window is at least $\frac{3\kappa_0\delta}{512C_N}\alpha_n$;

(b) in alternative (I) of Theorem 7.12, $\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal A_\vartheta}|\omega^\theta(r,z,t_n-T_\vartheta)|\,dr\,dz\ge\frac{3\kappa_0\epsilon}{16\,C_NL^2(1+G)}\,\vartheta^6D_nr_n^2=\frac{3\kappa_0\epsilon}{16\,C_NL^2(1+G)}\,\vartheta^6\alpha_nr_nM_n$;

(c) under (L1$\eta$), $\int_0^{t_n}\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}ds\ge c\max\{1/\nu_n,\ \alpha_n\}-\|\eta_0\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}$ for some $c=c(C_*,\delta,\vartheta)>0$, and in alternative (I) also $\ge c\,\vartheta^6\alpha_nr_nM_n/(1+G)-\|\eta_0\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}$.

*Proof.* The forward killed evolution has the same structure as the adjoint one in the proof of Lemma 7.10(b) (symmetric part $\Delta_\mu$, $\mu$-divergence free drift, no zero-order term), so the same argument gives $\|P_{\mathcal O}(m,s)\varphi\|_{L^2(\mu)}\le(C_N/2)^{1/2}L\vartheta^{-3/2}(r_n(m-s))^{-1/2}\|\varphi\|_{L^1(\mu)}$. Composing with Lemma 7.10(b) at $m=(s+t)/2$ gives the $L^1(\mu)\to L^\infty$ bound. In (a), the proof of Corollary 7.11 gives a killed term $\ge a_n/2$ with $\mathcal O=\mathcal D_n$, $\vartheta=\frac12$, $L=2$, so $\frac{a_n}2\le\frac{32C_N}{r_nh}\|\eta(t_n-h)\|_{L^1(\mathcal D_n,\mu)}$, and $\|\eta\|_{L^1(\mathcal D_n,\mu)}=\iint_{\mathcal D_n}|\omega^\theta|\,dr\,dz$; use $a_n\ge\frac38\kappa_0D_n/r_n$. In (b), the killed term is $\ge a_n/2$ at $h=T_\vartheta$ by Theorem 7.12(I), so $\iint_{\mathcal A_\vartheta}|\omega^\theta|\ge\vartheta^3r_nT_\vartheta a_n/(2C_NL^2)$. For (c), $\|\eta(t)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}=2\pi\iint|\omega^\theta(t)|\,dr\,dz$; apply Lemma 7.7 at $t=t_n-\delta r_n/M_n$, at $t=t_n-T_\vartheta$, and Corollary 7.9. $\square$

*Remark.* Going backward from $t_n$, the lower bound for the total variation of the meridional circulation near the record grows linearly in $h$ (as $D_nh$): forward in time, a killed parabolic evolution can only spread and cancel this quantity. Thus at the start of the transport window the meridional circulation is $\gtrsim\alpha_n$; the ratio of this lower bound to the core bound $1/\nu_n$ of Lemma 7.8 is $\alpha_n\nu_n$ (up to constants), so it is the stronger of the two only when $\alpha_n\nu_n$ is large (in the test $r=s^{5/8}$ of 8.2, $\alpha\nu=s^{3/40}\to0$); in alternative (I) it is $\gtrsim\vartheta^6\alpha_nr_nM_n\gg\alpha_n$. These are again necessary conditions, consistent with the scalar tests of 8.2 (for $r=s^{5/8}$: $\alpha rM=D r^2=s^{-3/20}$, while the scalar upper bound $\sqrt{2E_0}(\frac12\|\omega_0\|_2^2+\frac12D_nE_0)^{1/2}$ of Remark (2) after Corollary 7.9 is of order $s^{-7/10}$).

#### Lemma 7.14 (radial strain from the meridional vorticity). Let $t<T_*$, write $\eta=\eta(t)$, $D=\|\nabla u(t)\|_\infty$, and let $x=(r,0,z)$ with $r>0$. Then
$$\frac{u^r(x)}{r}=\frac3{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb R^3}\frac{(z-y_3)\,y_2^2}{|x-y|^5}\,\eta(y)\,dy,\qquad\text{hence}\qquad\Big|\frac{u^r(x)}r\Big|\le\frac3{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb R^3}\frac{|\eta(y)|}{|x-y|^2}\,dy. \tag{7.14.1}$$
Consequently: (a) $\|u^r/r\|_\infty\le C_L\|\eta\|_{L^{3,1}}\le3C_L\|\eta\|_{L^1}^{1/3}\|\eta\|_{L^\infty}^{2/3}$ with $C_L=(3/(4\pi))^{1/3}$ and $\|g\|_{L^{3,1}}=\int_0^\infty\tau^{-2/3}g^*(\tau)\,d\tau$ ($g^*$ the decreasing rearrangement for Lebesgue measure on $\mathbb R^3$); (b) $|u^r(x)/r|\le9\|\eta\|_{L^1}^{1/3}(D/r)^{2/3}$; (c) if $0<a<r$, the part of (7.14.1) coming from $\{r(y)\le a\}$ is at most $\frac3{4\pi}\frac{a^2}{(r-a)^4}\|\eta\,\mathbf 1_{\{r(y)\le a\}}\|_{L^1}$. (Norms may be infinite.)

*Proof.* Let $b=u^re_r+u^ze_z$. It is divergence free, $\operatorname{curl}b=\omega^\theta e_\theta$, and $b\in L^2\cap L^\infty$. We have $\omega^\theta e_\theta=\eta\,(-y_2,y_1,0)$, and since $\partial_\theta\omega=\omega^re_\theta-\omega^\theta e_r$, $|\eta|=|\omega^\theta|/r\le|\partial_\theta\omega|/r=|(e_\theta\cdot\nabla)\omega|\le|\nabla\omega|$, so $\eta\in L^2\cap L^\infty$; so the Biot-Savart field $B(x)=\frac1{4\pi}\int\omega^\theta e_\theta(y)\times\frac{x-y}{|x-y|^3}dy$ converges absolutely, lies in $\dot H^1\subset L^6$, and $b-B$ is harmonic and in $L^2+L^6$, hence zero. The first component at $x=(r,0,z)$ is $u^r(x)=\frac1{4\pi}\int\frac{(z-y_3)y_1}{|x-y|^3}\eta(y)\,dy$. In cylindrical coordinates $y=(\rho\cos\varphi,\rho\sin\varphi,\zeta)$, with $d^2=r^2+\rho^2-2r\rho\cos\varphi+(z-\zeta)^2$, the identity $0=\int_0^{2\pi}\partial_\varphi(\sin\varphi\,d^{-3})\,d\varphi=\int_0^{2\pi}\cos\varphi\,d^{-3}d\varphi-3r\rho\int_0^{2\pi}\sin^2\varphi\,d^{-5}d\varphi$ and the $\varphi$-independence of $\eta$ give the formula, and $|z-y_3|\,y_2^2\le|x-y|^3$ (as $x_2=0$) gives the bound. (a): the decreasing rearrangement of $|y|^{-2}$ is $(4\pi/3)^{2/3}\tau^{-2/3}$, so the Hardy-Littlewood rearrangement inequality gives the first bound; for the second, $\|g\|_{L^{3,1}}=3\int_0^{\|g\|_\infty}|\{|g|>\lambda\}|^{1/3}d\lambda\le3\|g\|_1^{1/3}\|g\|_\infty^{2/3}$ by Hoelder. (b): split (7.14.1) at $|x-y|=d_0$. The near part is $\le3d_0\sup_{B(x,d_0)}|\eta|$, the far part $\le\frac3{4\pi}d_0^{-2}\|\eta\|_1$, and for $d_0\le r/2$, $|\eta|\le\sqrt2D/(r-d_0)\le2\sqrt2D/r$ on $B(x,d_0)$. With $d_0=(\|\eta\|_1r/D)^{1/3}$, if $d_0\le r/2$ the sum is $\le(6\sqrt2+\frac3{4\pi})\|\eta\|_1^{1/3}(D/r)^{2/3}$; otherwise $\|\eta\|_1>Dr^2/8$ and the elementary bound $|u^r/r|\le D$ ($u^r=0$ on the axis) is smaller than the right side. (c): on $\{r(y)\le a\}$, $y_2^2\le a^2$ and $|x-y|\ge r-a$. $\square$

#### Corollary 7.15 (Biot-Savart form of alternative (II)). In alternative (II) of Theorem 7.12 put $W_n=[t_n-T_{\vartheta_n},t_n]$, $\rho_n=\vartheta_nr_n$, $\Lambda_n=\log(1/\vartheta_n)$, $A(s)=\|\eta(s)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}\in[0,\infty]$. Then for large $n$:

(a) the hitting probability satisfies $p_n\ge\frac{3\kappa_0}{32\sqrt2}\max\{\vartheta_n,\ (C_*\alpha_n)^{-1}\}$;

(b) $\displaystyle\Lambda_n-o(1)\le\int_{W_n}\min\Big\{D(s),\ C_L\|\eta(s)\|_{L^{3,1}},\ 9A(s)^{1/3}\big(D(s)/\rho_n\big)^{2/3}\Big\}\,ds$;

(c) $\displaystyle\sup_{W_n}A\ge\frac{(1+G)^3}{729\,\epsilon^3}\,\frac{(\Lambda_n-o(1))^3}{\vartheta_n^7r_n^4D_n^2}$.

In particular, an a priori bound $\int_{W_n}\|\eta(s)\|_{L^{3,1}}ds=o(\log(1/\vartheta_n))$ would exclude alternative (II).

*Proof.* (a) On $\{r=\rho\}$, $|\omega^\theta|\le\rho\sup|\partial_r\omega^\theta|\le\sqrt2\rho H_n$ because $\omega^\theta$ vanishes on the axis and $|\nabla\omega_2|\le\sqrt2|\nabla^2u|$ (proof of Lemma 7.8); so $|v_n|\le\min\{\sqrt2H_n,\sqrt2D_n/\rho\}$ there, and $a_n/4<J_n\le p_n\min\{\cdot\}$ with $a_n\ge\frac38\kappa_0D_n/r_n$ and $H_n\le C_*D_n^2/M_n$ gives (a). (b) By the localized form of Theorem 7.12(II), $\int_{W_n}\|(u^r/r)_+\|_{L^\infty(\mathcal A_{\vartheta_n})}ds\ge\Lambda_n-o(1)$; bound the integrand by $D(s)$, by Lemma 7.14(a), and by Lemma 7.14(b) with $r\ge\rho_n$. (c) Use the third bound in (b) with $D(s)\le D_n$ and $|W_n|=\epsilon\vartheta_n^3r_n^2/(1+G)$. $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) The requirement (c) is weak: $r_n^4D_n^2=\alpha_n^2(r_nM_n)^2\to\infty$, and with Remark (5) after Theorem 7.12 the right side of (c) is $\lesssim\Lambda_n^3(r_nM_n)^{1/3}\alpha_n^{-2}$; in the first test of 8.2 (with $\vartheta=1/|\log s|$) it is $O(s^{3/5}|\log s|^{10})\to0$, far below the mass $c_1/\nu_n$ that Lemma 7.8 already requires. With the scalar upper bounds available here ($\|\eta\|_\infty\le\sqrt2H$, and under (L1$\eta$) $A\lesssim\sqrt{D}$ by Remark (2) after Corollary 7.9), Lemma 7.14(a) only gives $\|u^r/r\|_\infty\lesssim D^{3/2}M^{-2/3}$, which is larger than $D$ along Case 3 records because $D_n^3/M_n^4\to\infty$. (2) Two conserved or budgeted quantities were also checked and give no constraint. Assume $\iint r^2|\omega_0^\theta|\,dr\,dz<\infty$ (an ordinary Lebesgue integral; smoothness and finite energy alone do not give it). The signed impulse $\iint r^2\omega^\theta\,dr\,dz$ is then conserved also with swirl (the swirl source $\frac1r\partial_z(u^\theta)^2$ in the conservative form $\partial_t\omega^\theta+\partial_r(u^r\omega^\theta)+\partial_z(u^z\omega^\theta)=\partial_r(\partial_r\omega^\theta+\omega^\theta/r)+\partial_{zz}\omega^\theta+\frac1r\partial_z(u^\theta)^2$ integrates to zero against $r^2$, and $\iint ru^r\omega^\theta=0$), but it is signed. The absolute impulse obeys $\iint r^2|\omega^\theta(t)|\le\iint r^2|\omega^\theta_0|+\frac{E_0}\pi\sqrt{t/2}$ (Kato's inequality, $|u^r\omega^\theta|+|u^\theta\omega^r|\le|u||\omega|$, energy and dissipation), rigorously: with $w_R=r^2\psi_R$, $\psi_R=(1+(r^2+z^2)/R^2)^{-3/2}$, one has $(\partial_{rr}-r^{-1}\partial_r+\partial_{zz})w_R=-15r^2R^{-2}(1+(r^2+z^2)/R^2)^{-7/2}\le0$ and $|\nabla\psi_R|\le\frac3{2R}\psi_R$, so $I_R=\iint w_R|\omega^\theta|$ satisfies $I_R'\le\frac{3M(t)}{2R}I_R+\frac1\pi\|u\|_2\|\omega\|_2$; Gronwall and $R\to\infty$ give the bound and the integrability that justifies the signed conservation by cutoffs. The lower bound for the absolute impulse forced by the record core (Lemma 7.8, $r\ge r_n/2$ there) is only $\frac{c_1}{8\pi}r_n^2/\nu_n\lesssim\nu_n^3\to0$ by (E'). (3) Lemma 7.14, Corollary 7.15 and the impulse computations were checked line by line.

#### Lemma 7.16 (killed averages of $\eta$ are controlled by the meridional velocity). Let $\mathcal O$ be a bounded open subset of the half-plane with smooth boundary and $\overline{\mathcal O}\subset\{\vartheta r_n\le r\le Lr_n\}$, $0<\vartheta<1<L$. Let $t\le t_n$, $x\in\mathcal O$, $0<h_1<h_2<t$, put $V=\sup_{t-h_2\le s\le t}\sup_{\mathcal O}|(u^r,u^z)(s)|$ (so $V\le M_n$), $K=C_NL^2/(\vartheta^3r_n)$ and
$$\Lambda=\max\Big\{1,\ \log\frac{K\,\mu(\mathcal O)}{h_1}\Big\}.$$
Then
$$\int_{h_1}^{h_2}\big|(P_{\mathcal O}(t,t-h)\eta(t-h))(x)\big|\,dh\le\frac{V}{\vartheta r_n}\,\big(e\,(h_2-h_1)\,\Lambda\big)^{1/2}.$$

*Proof.* Since $\mathcal O$ is bounded with smooth boundary and the coefficients of $\mathcal L_s$ are smooth on $\overline{\mathcal O}\times[t-h_2,t]$ ($r\ge\vartheta r_n>0$ there), the Dirichlet problem has a Green function (A. Friedman, *Partial Differential Equations of Parabolic Type*, Prentice-Hall 1964, Chapter 3): $(P_{\mathcal O}(t,s)\varphi)(x)=\int_{\mathcal O}p(s,y)\varphi(y)\,d\mu(y)$, where $p\ge0$ (a density with respect to $\mu$, for the fixed $(t,x)$) is smooth on $\overline{\mathcal O}\times[t-h_2,t-h_1]$, vanishes on $\partial\mathcal O$, and solves the adjoint equation $-\partial_sp=\Delta_\mu p-c_s\cdot\nabla p$ of the proof of Lemma 7.10(b). Write $p_h=p(t-h,\cdot)$, so $\partial_hp_h=\Delta_\mu p_h-c_{t-h}\cdot\nabla p_h$. Three facts are used.

(i) $\int p_h\,d\mu=\mathbb P(\tau>h)\le1$, by Lemma 7.10(a) with $\varphi=1$.

(ii) $\sup p_h\le K/h$: this is the $L^1(\mu)\to L^\infty$ bound of Corollary 7.13, read through the kernel.

(iii) For $1<q\le2$, $q(q-1)\int_{h_1}^{h_2}\int_{\{p_h>0\}}p_h^{q-2}|\nabla p_h|^2\,d\mu\,dh\le\int p_{h_1}^q\,d\mu$. Indeed, for $\varepsilon>0$ let $\phi_\varepsilon(x)=(x+\varepsilon)^q-\varepsilon^q$. Then $\phi_\varepsilon(p_h)=0$ on $\partial\mathcal O$ and $\operatorname{div}_\mu c=0$, so $\int\phi_\varepsilon'(p_h)\,c\cdot\nabla p_h\,d\mu=\int c\cdot\nabla\phi_\varepsilon(p_h)\,d\mu=0$. Moreover $\int\phi'_\varepsilon(p_h)\Delta_\mu p_h\,d\mu=-\int\phi_\varepsilon''(p_h)|\nabla p_h|^2d\mu+q\varepsilon^{q-1}\int_{\partial\mathcal O}r\,\partial_\nu p_h\,dS$, and $\partial_\nu p_h\le0$ on $\partial\mathcal O$ because $p_h\ge0$ vanishes there. Hence $\frac d{dh}\int\phi_\varepsilon(p_h)\,d\mu\le-\int\phi''_\varepsilon(p_h)|\nabla p_h|^2d\mu$. Integrate over $[h_1,h_2]$, drop $\int\phi_\varepsilon(p_{h_2})\ge0$, and let $\varepsilon\downarrow0$: on the left $\phi_\varepsilon''(p)=q(q-1)(p+\varepsilon)^{q-2}$ increases to $q(q-1)p^{q-2}$ on $\{p>0\}$ (monotone convergence; $\nabla p_h=0$ a.e. on $\{p_h=0\}$), and on the right dominated convergence applies.

Now take $\beta=q-1=1/\Lambda\in(0,1]$. By (i) and (ii), $\int p_{h_1}^q\,d\mu\le(K/h_1)^\beta$; by Hoelder and (i), $\int p_h^{1-\beta}d\mu\le\mu(\mathcal O)^\beta$. By Cauchy-Schwarz and (iii),
$$\int_{h_1}^{h_2}\!\!\int|\nabla p_h|\,d\mu\,dh\le\Big(\frac{(K/h_1)^\beta}{q\beta}\Big)^{1/2}\big((h_2-h_1)\mu(\mathcal O)^\beta\big)^{1/2}\le\big(\Lambda(h_2-h_1)\big)^{1/2}\Big(\frac{K\mu(\mathcal O)}{h_1}\Big)^{\beta/2}\le\big(e\Lambda(h_2-h_1)\big)^{1/2}.$$
Finally, for each $h$, with $s=t-h$: since $\eta=\omega^\theta/r$, $d\mu=r\,dr\,dz$, $\omega^\theta=\partial_zu^r-\partial_ru^z$ and $p_h=0$ on $\partial\mathcal O$, the divergence theorem in the $(r,z)$ plane gives
$$(P_{\mathcal O}(t,s)\eta(s))(x)=\iint_{\mathcal O}p_h\,\omega^\theta(s)\,dr\,dz=-\iint_{\mathcal O}\big(u^r\partial_zp_h-u^z\partial_rp_h\big)(s)\,dr\,dz,$$
so $|(P_{\mathcal O}(t,s)\eta(s))(x)|\le V\iint|\nabla p_h|\,dr\,dz\le\frac V{\vartheta r_n}\int|\nabla p_h|\,d\mu$. Integrate over $h\in[h_1,h_2]$. $\square$

*Remark.* Only two structural facts enter: the drift of the $\eta$ equation is divergence free for $\mu$ (so it leaves every $\int\phi(p)\,d\mu$ unchanged, whatever its size), and $\eta\,d\mu=\omega^\theta\,dr\,dz$ is the curl of the meridional velocity. It is the axisymmetric analogue of the planar fact that a viscous vorticity averaged by a heat-type kernel over a time $h$ is bounded by the velocity divided by $\sqrt h$, up to the logarithm $\Lambda$. In particular a positive killed average of $\eta$ of size $a$ cannot persist over a time interval of length $h$ unless $a\lesssim V(\Lambda/h)^{1/2}/(\vartheta r_n)$, whatever the drift does.

#### Corollary 7.17 (the transport window lasts at most logarithmically many viscous core times). For fixed $\delta\in(0,1/8]$ and all large $n$,
$$\alpha_n\nu_n=\frac{r_nD_n^2}{M_n^3}\le\frac{2048\,e}{9\kappa_0^2\,\delta}\,\log\big(1152\pi C_N\,\delta\,r_nM_n\big).$$
Since $\alpha_n\nu_n=(r_n/M_n)/\ell_n^2$, the long window of Lemma 7.1 is at most $O(\log(r_nM_n))$ viscous times $\ell_n^2$ of the core.

*Proof.* As in the proof of Corollary 7.11, with $\mathcal O=\mathcal D_n$, $\vartheta=\frac12$, $L=2$, for large $n$ the killed term satisfies $\operatorname{sgn}(\eta(x^0,t_n))\,(P_{\mathcal D_n}(t_n,t_n-h)\eta(t_n-h))(x^0)\ge a_n/2$ for all $0<h\le\delta r_n/M_n$. Apply Lemma 7.16 with $t=t_n$, $x=x^0$, $h_1=\delta r_n/(2M_n)$, $h_2=\delta r_n/M_n$, $V\le M_n$, $K=32C_N/r_n$ and $\mu(\mathcal D_n)\le2r_n\cdot\pi(3\delta r_n)^2=18\pi\delta^2r_n^3$, so that $K\mu(\mathcal D_n)/h_1\le1152\pi C_N\delta r_nM_n$ and $\Lambda\le\log(1152\pi C_N\delta r_nM_n)$ for large $n$:
$$\frac{a_n}2\cdot\frac{\delta r_n}{2M_n}\le\frac{2M_n}{r_n}\Big(\frac{e\,\delta r_n\Lambda}{2M_n}\Big)^{1/2},\qquad\text{i.e.}\qquad a_n^2\le\frac{32\,e\,M_n^3\Lambda}{\delta\,r_n^3}.$$
Insert $a_n\ge\frac38\kappa_0D_n/r_n$. $\square$

*Remark.* Since $r_n\alpha_n^2=r_n^2M_n\cdot\alpha_n\nu_n$, Corollary 7.11 improves Corollary 7.3 by at most a factor $O(\log(r_nM_n))$. Precisely: a killed average of size $a_n$ cannot persist on the disk $\mathcal D_n$ longer than Lemma 7.16 and the velocity bound $V\le M_n$ allow (note that $\eta$ satisfies a forced equation, with source $\partial_z(q^2)$; the killed average is the homogeneous part of the representation).

#### Theorem 7.18 (quantitative form of the dichotomy). Let $\epsilon,L,T_\vartheta,J_n$ be as in Theorem 7.12 and $C_I=\frac{16384\,e\,(1+G)}{3\kappa_0^3}$. There is $C_1=C_1(G,\kappa_0)$ such that for all large $n$ and every $\vartheta\in(0,1/4]$ with $\vartheta^3r_nM_n\ge C_1$:
$$J_n(\vartheta)\le\frac{a_n}4\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\alpha_n^2\le\frac{C_I}{\vartheta^5}\,\log\frac{12C_NL^4\,r_nM_n}{\vartheta^3}.$$
Consequently: (a) alternative (I) of Theorem 7.12 (a fixed $\vartheta$) can only occur along subsequences with $\alpha_n^2=O(\log(r_nM_n))$; (b) if $\alpha_n^2/\log(r_nM_n)\to\infty$, then for all large $n$, $J_n(\vartheta)>a_n/4$ for every $\vartheta\in[\vartheta_n^\sharp,\frac14]$, where $\vartheta_n^\sharp=(4C_I\log(r_nM_n)/\alpha_n^2)^{1/5}\to0$; in particular alternative (II) holds with $\vartheta_n=\vartheta_n^\sharp$, and its conclusion reads $\int_{t_n-T}^{t_n}\|(u^r/r)_+\|_{L^\infty(\mathcal A_{\vartheta_n^\sharp})}ds\ge\frac15\log\frac{\alpha_n^2}{4C_I\log(r_nM_n)}-o(1)$ with $T=T_{\vartheta_n^\sharp}$.

*Proof.* Fix $\vartheta$ with $J_n(\vartheta)\le a_n/4$ and let $T=T_\vartheta$. The strip $\mathcal A_\vartheta$ is unbounded, so we work on a bounded domain: let $k=4M_nT+2r_n$ and let $\mathcal O_k\subset\mathcal A_\vartheta\cap\{|z-z^0|<k\}$ be the rectangle $(\vartheta r_n,Lr_n)\times(z^0-k,z^0+k)$ with its corners smoothed inside $\{|z-z^0|>k/2\}$. Then $\mu(\mathcal O_k)\le L^2r_n^2k\le6L^2r_n^2M_nT$ once $r_n\le M_nT$, which holds if $\vartheta^3r_nM_n\ge(1+G)/\epsilon$. Let $E$ be the stopped event $\sup_{a\le h\wedge\tau_\vartheta}|Z_a-z^0|<k/2$ for the process $X=(R,Z)$ of Lemma 7.10(a) started at $x^0$ at time $t_n$ (stopping at the exit time $\tau_\vartheta$ from $\mathcal A_\vartheta$ makes the event independent of the extension of the drift below the annulus). Since before $\tau_\vartheta$ the $z$-drift is $-u^z$ with $|u^z|\le M_n$, $\mathbb P(E^c)\le\mathbb P(\sqrt2\sup_{a\le T}|\beta'_a|\ge M_nT+r_n)\le4e^{-M_n^2T/4}$ for $h\le T$ ($\beta'$ the second coordinate of $W$). On $E$ the process exits $\mathcal O_k$ before $h$ only through $\{r=\vartheta r_n\}$ or $\{r=Lr_n\}$, exactly when and where it exits $\mathcal A_\vartheta$. Apply Lemma 7.10(a) on $\mathcal O_k$, multiplied by $\operatorname{sgn}\eta(x^0,t_n)$. The inner-boundary term differs from $I_n(\vartheta,h)$ by at most $2\,\mathbb P(E^c)\sqrt2D_n/(\vartheta r_n)$ (the two agree on $E$, and $|v_n|\le\sqrt2D_n/(\vartheta r_n)$ on $\{r=\vartheta r_n\}$), the boundary term on $\{|z-z^0|\ge k/2\}$ is at most $\mathbb P(E^c)\sqrt2D_n/(\vartheta r_n)$, and the outer-boundary and source terms are at most $a_n/4$ as in the proof of Theorem 7.12. Hence, for $0<h\le T$,
$$\operatorname{sgn}(\eta(x^0,t_n))\,(P_{\mathcal O_k}(t_n,t_n-h)\eta(t_n-h))(x^0)\ge\frac{a_n}2-\frac{12\sqrt2\,D_n}{\vartheta r_n}e^{-M_n^2T/4}\ge\frac{a_n}3,$$
the last step for large $n$ uniformly in $\vartheta$, because $M_n^2T=\epsilon\vartheta^3r_nM_n\cdot r_nM_n/(1+G)\ge r_nM_n$ when $\vartheta^3r_nM_n\ge(1+G)/\epsilon$, and $\vartheta^{-1}\le(r_nM_n/C_1)^{1/3}$. Apply Lemma 7.16 with $\mathcal O=\mathcal O_k$, $h_1=T/2$, $h_2=T$, $V\le M_n$: here $K\mu(\mathcal O_k)/h_1\le12C_NL^4r_nM_n/\vartheta^3=:e^{\Lambda}$, and
$$\frac{a_n}3\cdot\frac T2\le\frac{M_n}{\vartheta r_n}\Big(\frac{e\,T\Lambda}2\Big)^{1/2},\qquad\text{so}\qquad a_n^2\le\frac{18\,e\,(1+G)\,M_n^2\Lambda}{\epsilon\,\vartheta^5r_n^4}.$$
With $a_n\ge\frac38\kappa_0D_n/r_n$ and $\epsilon=3\kappa_0/128$ this is $\alpha_n^2\le C_I\vartheta^{-5}\Lambda$. Take $C_1=(1+G)/\epsilon$.

(a) For fixed $\vartheta$, $\vartheta^3r_nM_n\to\infty$, and the bound gives $\alpha_n^2=O(\log(r_nM_n))$. (b) For $\vartheta^3\ge C_1/(r_nM_n)$ and large $n$, $\log(12C_NL^4r_nM_n/\vartheta^3)\le\log(12C_NL^4(r_nM_n)^2/C_1)\le3\log(r_nM_n)$, so $J_n(\vartheta)\le a_n/4$ forces $\vartheta^5\le3C_I\log(r_nM_n)/\alpha_n^2<(\vartheta_n^\sharp)^5$. It remains to check $(\vartheta_n^\sharp)^3\ge C_1/(r_nM_n)$ for large $n$: with $X=r_nM_n$ and $\alpha_n=\nu_nX$, Corollary 7.17 gives $\nu_n^2X\le C\log X$, hence $\alpha_n^2=\nu_n^2X^2\le C X\log X$, and $(\vartheta_n^\sharp)^{15}=(4C_I\log X/\alpha_n^2)^3\ge c\,X^{-3}\gg X^{-5}$. The transport conclusions of Theorem 7.12(II) were derived from $J_n(\vartheta_n)>a_n/4$ and $\vartheta_n\to0$ alone. $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) In exponent form (8.2), where $\alpha$ is a power of $s$, alternative (I) is therefore excluded altogether; the persistent-ring scenario survives only with $\alpha_n$ growing at most like $(\log(r_nM_n))^{1/2}$. (2) For (II), along any subsequence with $\alpha_n^2/\log(r_nM_n)\to\infty$ (the hypothesis of (b)), the theorem gives $J_n(\vartheta)>a_n/4$ simultaneously for every $\vartheta\in[\vartheta_n^\sharp,\frac14]$ (a fixed $\vartheta$ in this interval is not by itself a shrinking-scale alternative). Applied at each such $\vartheta$, the proof of Theorem 7.12(II) gives $\int_{t_n-T_\vartheta}^{t_n}\|(u^r/r)_+\|_{L^\infty(\mathcal A_\vartheta)}ds\ge\log(1/\vartheta)-O(\vartheta^{1/2}\log^{1/2}(C/\vartheta))-o(1)$. For the global norm $\|(u^r/r)_+\|_{L^\infty}$ the left side increases with $\vartheta$ (longer horizon) while the right side decreases, so the bound at $\vartheta_n^\sharp$ implies the others up to the error terms; the localized bounds at different scales are not comparable in this way, because $\mathcal A_\vartheta$ shrinks as $\vartheta$ increases. (3) Lemma 7.16 is the new ingredient; it was found in the course of this work and checked as stated; it does not apply directly to the inner-boundary contribution $J_n$, whose control is the remaining difficulty in (II). (4) *Comparison on the horizons $T''_\vartheta$: the inner region always feeds the record (proved).* The source estimate in the proofs of Theorems 7.12 and 7.18 can be replaced by a one-sided comparison with the circulation, which bounds the signed stopped-source contribution by a small fixed fraction of the record on horizons that are much longer than $r_n^2$ for fixed $\vartheta$. Assume $G>0$ and put $\rho=\vartheta r_n$. On $\{r>0\}$ the circulation satisfies $\partial_s\Gamma=\mathcal L_s\Gamma-\frac4r\partial_r\Gamma$, hence $\partial_s(\Gamma^2)=\mathcal L_s(\Gamma^2)-2|\nabla\Gamma|^2-\frac8r\Gamma\partial_r\Gamma$, and $f=\partial_z(q^2)=2\Gamma\partial_z\Gamma/r^4$. With $\sigma=\operatorname{sgn}\eta(x^0,t_n)$ and $c>0$, the function $w=v_n+c\Gamma^2$ satisfies $\partial_sw=\mathcal L_sw+g$ with
$$g=\frac{2\sigma\Gamma\partial_z\Gamma}{r^4}-2c(\partial_z\Gamma)^2-\frac{8c}r\Gamma\partial_r\Gamma-2c(\partial_r\Gamma)^2=\frac{\Gamma^2}{2cr^8}+\frac{8c\Gamma^2}{r^2}-2c\Big(\partial_z\Gamma-\frac{\sigma\Gamma}{2cr^4}\Big)^2-2c\Big(\partial_r\Gamma+\frac{2\Gamma}r\Big)^2\le G^2\Big(\frac1{2c\rho^8}+\frac{8c}{\rho^2}\Big)$$
on $\{r\ge\rho\}$ (Lemma 1.1). The stopped Itô argument of Lemma 7.10(a), applied to $w$ on a domain $\mathcal O$ as there with $\overline{\mathcal O}\subset\{r\ge\rho\}$, together with $0\le\Gamma^2\le G^2$, gives for $0<h<t_n$
$$v_n(x^0,t_n)\le\mathbb E\big[v_n(X_{h\wedge\tau},t_n-h\wedge\tau)\big]+cG^2+hG^2\Big(\frac1{2c\rho^8}+\frac{8c}{\rho^2}\Big),$$
and the optimal $c$ for the given $h$ makes the error $E_\rho(h)=\sqrt2G^2\rho^{-4}\big(h(1+8h/\rho^2)\big)^{1/2}\le\sqrt2G^2\rho^{-4}h^{1/2}+4G^2h\rho^{-5}$, which increases with $h$. (The same comparison with the maximum principle in place of the representation bounds $\eta$ on $\{r\ge\rho\}$ by its values at an earlier time and on $\{r=\rho\}$, without any drift or gradient quantity. The same circulation-energy mechanism, in adjoint-weighted integral form, is Step 1 of Lemma 7.30 below.) Only this upper bound is used in the dichotomy. Put $T''_\vartheta=\kappa_0\vartheta^5r_n^4D_n/(256G^2)$. Then $T''_\vartheta\to0$ uniformly in $\vartheta\le\frac14$, because $r_n^4D_n=(r_n^2M_n)^2\nu_n$ (Corollary 7.3), and exactly
$$\frac{E_\rho(T''_\vartheta)}{\kappa_0D_n/r_n}=\Big(\frac1{4096}+\frac{G^2}{128\kappa_0\vartheta^3R_n}\Big)^{1/2},$$
so on horizons $h\le T''_\vartheta$ the error is at most $(\frac1{64}+o(1))\kappa_0D_n/r_n\le(\frac1{24}+o(1))a_n$ whenever $\vartheta^3R_n\to\infty$: a small fixed fraction of the record, not $o(a_n)$. For fixed $\vartheta$, $T''_\vartheta/r_n^2=\kappa_0\vartheta^5R_n/(256G^2)\to\infty$. On the window $T_\vartheta$ of Theorem 7.12 the error is at most $C(G)\vartheta^{-5/2}R_n^{-1}D_n/r_n$, which is $o(a_n)$ for fixed $\vartheta$ and uniformly for $\vartheta\in[\vartheta_n^\flat,\frac14]$ (defined below); in general $E_\rho(h)=o(D_n/r_n)$ for $h=o\big(\min\{\vartheta^5r_n^4D_n/G^2,\ \vartheta^8r_n^6D_n^2/G^4\}\big)$. Let $J''_n(\vartheta)$ be defined as $J_n(\vartheta)$ with $T_\vartheta$ replaced by $T''_\vartheta$. Write $X_n=r_nM_n$ and $\mathcal P_n=\alpha_n^2R_n$, so that $X_n=\alpha_n/\nu_n$, $R_n=\alpha_nX_n$ and $\mathcal P_n=\alpha_n^3X_n$, and let $\vartheta_n^\flat=\big(131072\,e\,G^2\log X_n/(\kappa_0^3\mathcal P_n)\big)^{1/7}$, which tends to $0$ because $\mathcal P_n/\log X_n\to\infty$. For large $n$, uniformly in $\vartheta\in[\vartheta_n^\flat,\frac14]$: $\vartheta^{-1}\le \mathcal P_n^{1/7}\le X_n^{4/7}$, $\vartheta^3R_n\ge\alpha_n^{2/7}\nu_n^{-4/7}\to\infty$, and $M_nT''_\vartheta/r_n=\kappa_0\vartheta^5R_nX_n/(256G^2)\ge\kappa_0\alpha_n^{1/7}\nu_n^{-9/7}/(256G^2)\to\infty$. Consequently the proof of Theorem 7.18 runs with $T=T''_\vartheta$, uniformly in this range: the comparison and outer-boundary errors together are at most $(\frac1{64}+\frac{\sqrt2}{32}+o(1))\kappa_0D_n/r_n<\frac{3\kappa_0}{32}\frac{D_n}{r_n}\le\frac{a_n}4$; the conditions $r_n\le M_nT$ and $M_n^2T\ge X_n$ hold; the exit error is $o(a_n)$ since $\vartheta^{-1}e^{-M_n^2T/4}\le X_n^{4/7}e^{-X_n/4}$; and $\Lambda\le\log(12C_NL^4X_n/\vartheta^3)\le3\log X_n$, a bound independent of $T$ once $r_n\le M_nT$. So $J''_n(\vartheta)\le a_n/4$ would give $a_n^2\le18eM_n^2\Lambda/(\vartheta^2r_n^2T''_\vartheta)$, and with $a_n\ge\frac38\kappa_0D_n/r_n$,
$$\mathcal P_n=\alpha_n^2R_n\le\frac{32768\,e\,G^2}{\kappa_0^3\,\vartheta^7}\,\log\frac{12C_NL^4\,r_nM_n}{\vartheta^3}\le\frac{3\cdot32768\,e\,G^2}{\kappa_0^3\,\vartheta^7}\,\log X_n,$$
which contradicts $\vartheta\ge\vartheta_n^\flat$. Hence, for all large $n$, $J''_n(\vartheta)>a_n/4$ for every $\vartheta\in[\vartheta_n^\flat,\frac14]$, in either alternative of Theorem 7.12. By contraposition, an estimate giving $J''_n(\vartheta_n)\le a_n/4$ for infinitely many $n$ of a Case 3 sequence, at some $\vartheta_n\in[\vartheta_n^\flat,\frac14]$, would exclude Case 3. This single sufficient condition needs no persistence bound of the kind that alternative (I) requires, but it concerns the longer horizon $T''_{\vartheta_n}$, on which inner feeding is less constrained; no such estimate is proved here. A strain consequence follows from the hitting estimate in the last assertion of Lemma 7.19, applied with $\mathcal U=\mathcal A_\vartheta$ (it rests on $r^{-2}$ being annihilated by $\partial_{rr}+\frac3r\partial_r$): the probability of reaching $\{r=\rho\}$ by time $h$ is at most $(\rho/r^0)^2e^{2\mathcal K_+(h)}$ with $\mathcal K_+(h)=\int_{t_n-h}^{t_n}\|(u^r/r)_+\|_{L^\infty(\mathcal A_\vartheta)}ds$; here and below, reaching $\{r=\rho\}$ means reaching it by the stated horizon before exit through $\{r=Lr_n\}$, which is the event in $J''_n(\vartheta)$ (the radial-axial part of the process of Lemma 7.19 solves the same equation as that of Lemma 7.10 up to the exit from $\mathcal A_\vartheta$). Since $|v_n|\le\sqrt2D_n/\rho$ on $\{r=\rho\}$, $J''_n(\vartheta)>a_n/4$ gives
$$\int_{t_n-T''_\vartheta}^{t_n}\Big\|\Big(\frac{u^r}r\Big)_+\Big\|_{L^\infty(\mathcal A_\vartheta)}ds\ge\frac12\log\frac1\vartheta-\frac12\log\frac{32\sqrt2}{3\kappa_0}+\log\frac{r^0}{r_n}\qquad\text{for every }\vartheta\in[\vartheta_n^\flat,\tfrac14],$$
with $\log(r^0/r_n)=o(1)$ uniformly; without outward strain on $\mathcal A_\vartheta$ the probability of reaching $\{r=\rho\}$ would be at most $(\rho/r^0)^2$ at every horizon. At $\vartheta=\vartheta_n^\flat$ the right side tends to infinity. Since also $|v_n|\le\sqrt2H_n\le\sqrt2C_*D_n^2/M_n=\sqrt2C_*\alpha_nD_n/r_n$ on the window ($\omega^\theta$ vanishes on the axis, so $|\eta|\le\|\partial_r\omega^\theta\|_\infty\le\sqrt2H$ as in Lemma 7.26, and (A) holds with the historical suprema), the same argument gives the lower bound $\log\frac1\vartheta-\frac12\log\alpha_n-\frac12\log\frac{32\sqrt2C_*}{3\kappa_0}+\log\frac{r^0}{r_n}$, which is larger than the displayed one exactly when $\vartheta<1/(C_*\alpha_n)$; at $\vartheta=\vartheta_n^\flat$, where $\log\frac1{\vartheta_n^\flat}=\frac17\big(4\log\alpha_n+\log\frac1{\nu_n}-\log\log X_n\big)-C$, it is at least $\frac17\log\frac1{\nu_n}+\frac1{14}\log\alpha_n-\frac17\log\log X_n-C$ with $C=C(G,C_*,\kappa_0)$, which tends to infinity because $\log X_n=\log\alpha_n+\log(1/\nu_n)$. This localized bound is not implied by Corollary 7.24, which concerns the supremum over all of $\mathbb R^3$. The Brownian estimate in the proof of Theorem 7.12(II) also gives $\int_{t_n-T}^{t_n}\|u^r_+\|_{L^\infty(\mathcal A_\vartheta)}ds\ge r^0-\vartheta r_n-2(T\log(C/\vartheta))^{1/2}$ with $T=T''_\vartheta$ and $C=32\sqrt2/(3\kappa_0)$: no displacement of order $r_n$ follows when $T''_\vartheta\gg r_n^2$, but at shrinking scales with $T''_\vartheta\log(1/\vartheta)=o(r_n^2)$ it gives $(1-o(1))r_n$. These are necessary conditions, not exclusions. Their windows satisfy $T''_\vartheta/T_\vartheta=(1+G)\vartheta^2R_n/(6G^2)\to\infty$ uniformly in the stated range, and $T''_\vartheta\gg r_n^2$ for fixed $\vartheta$; the latter need not hold at shrinking scales (for parameter sequences with $\alpha_n\nu_n=1$, for instance, $T''_{\vartheta_n^\flat}/r_n^2\asymp(\log\alpha_n)^{5/7}\alpha_n^{-4/7}\to0$). The lower bound for $J''_n(\vartheta)$ and its strain consequence do not imply corresponding lower bounds on the shorter window $T_\vartheta$ of alternative (I), which is why lengthening the window does not exclude (I). The comparison function $\pm\eta+c\Gamma^2$ and its drift-free bound were first obtained in a separate analysis of the same equations; they were re-derived and checked here, together with the constants above, and the whole remark was checked by an independent audit by a second model.

#### Lemma 7.19 (full-space five-dimensional representation; no inner boundary). Let $0<T<t<T_*$ and $x=(r_0,z_0)$ with $r_0>0$. Put $V=\sup_{[t-T,t]}\|(u^r,u^z)\|_\infty$, $H_*=\sup_{[t-T,t]}\|\nabla^2u\|_\infty$, $k(h)=\|(u^r/r)_+(t-h)\|_{L^\infty}$ and $K(h)=\int_0^hk(a)\,da$. There is an absolute constant $C_5$ such that
$$|\eta(x,t)|\le\frac{\sqrt2\,V\,e^{K(T)}}{r_0\sqrt T}\,\mathcal E_T^{1/2}+\frac{2V\,e^{2K(T)}}{r_0^2}+\frac{2G\,H_*\,T\,e^{2K(T)}}{r_0^2},\qquad\mathcal E_T=C_5+2K(T)+\frac52\log\big(2(1+V^2T)\big). \tag{7.19.1}$$
Moreover, let $\rho<r_0$, let $\mathcal U\subset\{r>\rho\}$ be open with $x\in\mathcal U$, let $\tau_\rho$ be the first time with $R_h=\rho$ and $\tau_{\mathcal U}$ the first exit time from $\mathcal U$, and let $E=\{\tau_\rho\le T,\ \tau_\rho\le\tau_{\mathcal U}\}$ (for an annulus $\mathcal U$ with inner radius $\rho$: exit through the inner boundary by time $T$). Then $\mathbb P(E)\le\min\{1,(\rho/r_0)^2e^{2K_{\mathcal U}(T)}\}$, where $K_{\mathcal U}$ is defined with the supremum over $\overline{\mathcal U}$ only.

*Proof.* (i) Lift. A smooth axisymmetric field has $u^r=rA(r^2,z)$, $u^\theta=rB(r^2,z)$, $u^z=C(r^2,z)$ with $A,B,C$ smooth, so $\eta=A_z-2C_\sigma$ ($\sigma=r^2$) and $q=B$ are smooth functions on $\mathbb R^5=\mathbb R^4_{y'}\times\mathbb R_z$, $r=|y'|$. The lifted field $b(y)=(A\,y',C)$ is Lipschitz with $\operatorname{div}_5b=4A+2r^2A_\sigma+C_z=2A=2u^r/r$, because incompressibility reads $2A+2r^2A_\sigma+C_z=0$; and $\partial_t\eta+b\cdot\nabla_5\eta=\Delta_5\eta+f$, $f=\partial_z(q^2)$. On $[t-T,t]$ all these coefficients are smooth and bounded with bounded derivatives. Let $dY_h=-b(Y_h,t-h)\,dh+\sqrt2\,dW_h$, $Y_0=(y'_0,z_0)$ with $|y'_0|=r_0$, $W$ a five-dimensional Brownian motion, $R_h=|Y'_h|$. Its law has a smooth positive density $p_h$, which solves $\partial_hp=\Delta_5p+\operatorname{div}_5(bp)$. Itô's formula gives, for $0<h\le T$, $\eta(x,t)=\mathbb E\,\eta(Y_h,t-h)+\int_0^h\mathbb E\,f(Y_a,t-a)\,da$ ($\eta$, $f$ and $\nabla\eta$ are bounded on $\mathbb R^3\times[t-T,t]$).

(ii) Inverse-square moment. For $w_\varepsilon=(r^2+\varepsilon)^{-1}$, $\Delta_5w_\varepsilon=-8\varepsilon(r^2+\varepsilon)^{-3}\le0$ and $-b\cdot\nabla w_\varepsilon=2\frac{u^r}r\frac{r^2}{r^2+\varepsilon}w_\varepsilon\le2k(h)w_\varepsilon$. So $e^{-2K(h)}w_\varepsilon(Y_h)$ is a supermartingale ($w_\varepsilon$ and $\nabla w_\varepsilon$ are bounded), $\mathbb Ew_\varepsilon(Y_h)\le e^{2K(h)}w_\varepsilon(Y_0)$, and monotone convergence as $\varepsilon\downarrow0$ gives $\mathbb ER_h^{-2}\le r_0^{-2}e^{2K(h)}$. The same computation for $R^{-2}$ itself, with $K_{\mathcal U}$ in place of $K$, up to the stopping time $\tau_\rho\wedge\tau_{\mathcal U}\wedge T$ (on this interval $R\ge\rho>0$ and the drift is evaluated in $\overline{\mathcal U}$), and optional stopping give $r_0^{-2}\ge\mathbb E[e^{-2K_{\mathcal U}(\tau_\rho)}\rho^{-2};E]\ge\rho^{-2}e^{-2K_{\mathcal U}(T)}\mathbb P(E)$, the hitting bound.

(iii) Smoothing. For $q>1$, $\frac d{dh}\int p^q=-q(q-1)\int p^{q-2}|\nabla p|^2+(q-1)\int(\operatorname{div}_5b)\,p^q$ (integrate by parts; $q\,p^{q-1}\operatorname{div}(bp)=b\cdot\nabla p^q+q(\operatorname{div}b)p^q$). With $q=2$, mass $1$ and the five-dimensional Nash inequality $\|g\|_2^{14/5}\le C\|\nabla g\|_2^2\|g\|_1^{4/5}$, $y=\|p_h\|_2^2$ satisfies $y'\le-cy^{7/5}+2k\,y$, so $e^{-2K}y\le Ch^{-5/2}$. Let $Q(b',a')$ be the density evolution between backward times $a'<b'$ and $\kappa(a',b')=K(b')-K(a')$; the same argument gives $\|Q(b',a')\|_{1\to2}\le C(b'-a')^{-5/4}e^{\kappa(a',b')}$. Its adjoint is the terminal-value problem $-\partial_sv=(\Delta_5-b(\cdot,t-s)\cdot\nabla)v$ on $[a',b']$, that is, a forward evolution in $\tau=b'-s$ with the time-reversed coefficients $b(\cdot,t-b'+\tau)$ (not the same chronological coefficient sequence); its mass grows at most by $e^{2\kappa(a',b')}$ and it has the same energy identity, so with $A(\tau)$ twice the reversed strain integral, $z=e^{-A}\|v\|_2^2$ satisfies $z'\le-c\,e^{-2A/5}z^{7/5}\|v_0\|_1^{-4/5}$, which gives $\|Q(b',a')^*\|_{1\to2}\le C(b'-a')^{-5/4}e^{2\kappa(a',b')}$. Composing at $h/2$ (the two half-intervals carry disjoint parts of $K$) gives $\|p_h\|_\infty\le C_5'h^{-5/2}e^{2K(h)}$.

(iv) Entropy. Divide the integrated $q$-identity by $q-1$ and let $q\downarrow1$: by dominated convergence (using $p\le\|p\|_\infty$ on $[h_1,h_2]$ for the positive part, and for the negative part of $p\log p$ the split at $p=e^{-1-|y-Y_0|^2}$ together with finite second moments) and Fatou's lemma for the gradient term, $\mathcal F(h)=\int|\nabla p_h|^2/p_h$ and $\mathcal H(h)=\int p_h\log p_h$ satisfy $\int_{h_1}^{h_2}\mathcal F\le\mathcal H(h_1)-\mathcal H(h_2)+2(K(h_2)-K(h_1))$. Now $\mathcal H(h_1)\le\log\|p_{h_1}\|_\infty$, and since $\mathbb E|Y_h-Y_0|^2\le2V^2h^2+20h$, the Gaussian maximizes entropy at fixed second moment and gives $\mathcal H(h)\ge-\frac52\log\big(\frac{2\pi e}5(20h+2V^2h^2)\big)$. With $h_1=T/2$, $h_2=T$: $\int_{T/2}^T\mathcal F\le\mathcal E_T$ for a suitable absolute $C_5$.

(v) Velocity averaging. With $\eta=(\partial_zu^r-\partial_ru^z)/r$ and $\operatorname{div}_{y'}(p\,y'/r^2)=\partial_rp/r+2p/r^2$, integration by parts in $\mathbb R^5$ (the density need not be radial in $y'$; justify with cutoffs: the inner cutoff error is at most $CV\int_{\{r<2\varepsilon\}}p\,r^{-2}dy\to0$ by (ii), the outer one at most $CVR^{-1}\int p\,r^{-1}dy\to0$; this holds for a.e. $h$, where $\mathcal F(h)<\infty$ by (iv)) gives $\int p\eta=\int\big(-\frac{u^r}r\partial_zp+\frac{u^z}r\partial_rp+\frac{2u^z}{r^2}p\big)dy$, hence $|\mathbb E\eta(Y_h,t-h)|\le V\mathcal F(h)^{1/2}(\mathbb ER_h^{-2})^{1/2}+2V\,\mathbb ER_h^{-2}$. By (ii), Cauchy-Schwarz in $h$ and (iv), $\frac2T\int_{T/2}^T|\mathbb E\eta(Y_h,t-h)|\,dh\le\frac{\sqrt2Ve^{K(T)}}{r_0\sqrt T}\mathcal E_T^{1/2}+\frac{2Ve^{2K(T)}}{r_0^2}$.

(vi) Source. At $\theta=0$, $q=\int_0^1\partial_{x_1}u_2(\sigma r,0,z)\,d\sigma$, so $|\partial_zq|\le H_*$, and with $|q|\le G/r^2$, $|f|\le2GH_*r^{-2}$; by (ii), $\int_0^T\mathbb E|f(Y_a,t-a)|\,da\le2GH_*Te^{2K(T)}/r_0^2$. Averaging the representation of (i) over $h\in[T/2,T]$ proves (7.19.1). $\square$

#### Corollary 7.20 (early radial strain in the axis regime). Let $L_n=1+\log(r_nM_n)$, $\widehat T_n=r_n^2L_n^{1/2}\alpha_n^{-3/2}$ and $\widehat K_n=\int_{t_n-\widehat T_n}^{t_n}\|(u^r/r)_+(s)\|_{L^\infty}ds$. Along any subsequence of a Case 3 sequence with $\alpha_n\ge L_n$, for all large $n$,
$$\widehat K_n\ge\tfrac14\log(\alpha_n/L_n)-C,\qquad C=C(G,C_*,\kappa_0).$$
In particular a bound $\widehat K_n\le(\frac\delta4-\epsilon')\log(r_nM_n)$ with fixed $\epsilon'>0$ excludes every subsequence with $\alpha_n\ge(r_nM_n)^\delta$; an $O(\log(r_nM_n))$ bound does not.

*Proof.* $\widehat T_n\le r_n^2/\alpha_n\to0$, so the horizon is available. Apply (7.19.1) at $x=x^0$, $t=t_n$, $T=\widehat T_n$, with $r_0=r^0$, $r^0/r_n\to1$, $|\eta(x^0,t_n)|=a_n\ge\frac38\kappa_0D_n/r_n$, $V\le M_n$, $H_*\le C_*D_n^2/M_n$. Since $M_n^2\widehat T_n\le(r_nM_n)^2$, $\mathcal E_{\widehat T_n}\le C(L_n+\widehat K_n)$. Dividing by $D_n/r_n$ and using $M_n/(r_nD_n)=\alpha_n^{-1}$:
$$1\le C\Big[\frac{e^{\widehat K_n}(L_n+\widehat K_n)^{1/2}}{(\alpha_nL_n)^{1/4}}+\frac{e^{2\widehat K_n}}{\alpha_n}+\frac{e^{2\widehat K_n}L_n^{1/2}}{\alpha_n^{1/2}}\Big].$$
If $\widehat K_n\le L_n$, put $\zeta=e^{\widehat K_n}(L_n/\alpha_n)^{1/4}$; the right side is at most $C(\zeta+\zeta^2)$, so $\zeta\ge c$, which is the claim. If $\widehat K_n>L_n$, then $\widehat K_n>\log(r_nM_n)\ge\log\alpha_n$ (as $\nu_n\le1$), which is stronger. $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) The horizon $\widehat T_n$ is shorter than $T_{\vartheta_n^\sharp}$ of Theorem 7.18 by the factor $\asymp\alpha_n^{-3/10}L_n^{-1/10}$; on the longer horizon Theorem 7.18 gives the larger coefficient $\frac25$. The gain of Corollary 7.20 is the horizon, and that it uses the full-space representation with no inner boundary, so it controls both transported and newly generated $\eta$. (2) The hitting bound in Lemma 7.19 with $\mathcal U=\mathcal A_\vartheta$ and $|\eta|\le\sqrt2\min\{H_n,D_n/\rho\}$ on $\{r=\rho\}$ gives $J_n(\vartheta)\le\sqrt2\min\{H_n,D_n/\rho\}\min\{1,\vartheta^2(r_n/r^0)^2e^{2K_{\mathcal A_\vartheta}}\}$, $\rho=\vartheta r_n$; since alternative (II) needs $J_n(\vartheta_n)\gtrsim a_n\gtrsim D_n/r_n$, and the hitting probability is $\gtrsim\max\{\vartheta_n,\alpha_n^{-1}\}$ there (Corollary 7.15), this yields $K_{\mathcal A_{\vartheta_n}}\ge\max\{\frac12\log(1/\vartheta_n),\log(1/\vartheta_n)-\frac12\log\alpha_n\}-C$, which is weaker than Theorem 7.12(II) at $\vartheta_n=\vartheta_n^\sharp$. (3) The factor $e^{K}$ in the Fisher-information term and $e^{2K}$ in the remaining velocity term and the source term come from the inverse-square moment and are not removed by the factor $q-1$ in front of the strain in the unweighted $L^q$ identity: weighting that identity by $r^{-2}$ changes the coefficient of the strain term $\int(u^r/r)\,p^q$ from $2(q-1)$ to $2q$ (the weight contributes $-\int p^q\,b\cdot\nabla r^{-2}=2\int(u^r/r)r^{-2}p^q$). For five-dimensional Brownian motion (from any starting point) $\mathbb ER_h^{-s}=\infty$ for every $h>0$ and $s\ge4$, while inverse moments of order $s<4$ are finite; so the source cannot be estimated through a bound $|f|\lesssim r^{-4}$, and Lemma 7.23 below uses order $3$. (4) For Lemma 7.19 and Corollary 7.20 the lift, the signs of the generator and of the Fokker-Planck equation, the Nash exponents and the total factor $e^{2K}$, the entropy bounds, the integration by parts and the powers in Corollary 7.20 were rechecked; an adversarial review led to the repairs incorporated above (the time reversal in the dual evolution, the precise hitting event, the cutoff justification of the integration by parts).

#### Proposition 7.21 (the logarithm in Lemma 7.16 cannot be removed using only the structural properties used in its proof). There is no constant $C$ such that, for all data satisfying only the structural hypotheses used in the proof of Lemma 7.16, with no evolution equation imposed (a smooth bounded time-dependent meridional field $(u^r,u^z)$ with $\partial_r(ru^r)+\partial_z(ru^z)=0$, $|(u^r,u^z)|\le V$, the drift $c=(2/r-u^r,-u^z)$, and data $\eta=(\partial_zu^r-\partial_ru^z)/r$ taken from the same field at each time), the bound $\int_{h_1}^{h_2}|P_{\mathcal O}(t,t-h)\eta(t-h)(x)|\,dh\le C\,V(h_2-h_1)^{1/2}/(\vartheta r_n)$ holds. (The construction is not a Navier-Stokes solution and not a Case 3 sequence.)

*Proof.* Planar model: for $N\ge2$ and backward time $h\in[0,N]$ let $a(h)=1-e^{-2h}$, $b(h)=e^{2h}-1$, $p_h$ the centered Gaussian density on $\mathbb R^2$ with variances $a,b$, $g_h=p_h/p_h(0)$. It is the law at time $h$ of the diffusion with drift $(-x,z)$ started at $0$ ($a'=2-2a$, $b'=2+2b$). Let $\chi$ be smooth, $\chi=0$ on $[0,\frac14]$, $\chi=1$ on $[\frac12,N]$, $L_0=B\sqrt{N+1}e^N$ with a large universal $B$, $A=L_0$, $\Psi_h=xz+\chi(h)A\sqrt{a(h)}\,g_h$ and $U_h=(\partial_z\Psi_h,-\partial_x\Psi_h)$. The added field is tangent to the level sets of $p_h$, so $p_h$ still solves $\partial_hp=\Delta p+\operatorname{div}(U_hp)$; and $\sqrt a|\nabla g_h|\le e^{-1/2}$, so $|U|\le2A$ on $\Omega=B_{L_0}(0)$. For $h\ge1$ the vorticity is $\omega_h=\Delta\Psi_h=A\sqrt a\,\Delta g_h$ and, using $\int p_h^2=p_h(0)/2$ and $\int|\nabla p_h|^2=(\frac1{2a}+\frac1{2b})\int p_h^2$,
$$\int p_h\,\omega_h=-\frac{A\sqrt a}{p_h(0)}\int|\nabla p_h|^2=-\frac A4\Big(a^{-1/2}+\frac{\sqrt a}b\Big)\le-\frac A4 .$$
Killing on $\Omega$ changes $p_h$ by at most $Ce^{-cB^2(N+1)}$ (maximum principle; the Gaussian exponent on $\partial\Omega$ is $\ge B^2(N+1)/2$), $\int|\omega_h|\le CAe^N$, and the part of $\int p_h\omega_h$ outside $\Omega$ is $\le CAe^{-cB^2(N+1)}$; so for $B$ large $|\int_\Omega p^\Omega_h\omega_h|\ge A/8$ on $[1,N]$ and $\int_1^N|P_\Omega\omega_h(0)|\,dh\ge A(N-1)/8$, with velocity bounded by $CA$ (the order is: Gaussian calculation with the uncut field, then killing on $\Omega$, then a cutoff of $\Psi$ equal to $1$ on $\Omega$ and supported in $B_{2L_0}$, which changes neither the killed process nor the data in $\Omega$). Transfer: with $\psi_{R,h}(r,z)=R\Psi_h(r-R,z)$, $u^r=\partial_z\psi/r$, $u^z=-\partial_r\psi/r$ (so $\partial_r(ru^r)+\partial_z(ru^z)=0$), on the disk $\mathcal O_R=\{(r-R)^2+z^2<L_0^2\}$, $R\to\infty$: the velocity converges with all derivatives to $U$, $R\eta_R\to\omega$, the generator $\partial_{xx}+\partial_{zz}+(3/(R+x)-u^r)\partial_x-u^z\partial_z$ converges with first derivatives to $\Delta-U\cdot\nabla$, and the killed expectations converge (couple the diffusions with the same Brownian motion; a uniformly elliptic diffusion crosses the smooth boundary immediately on hitting it, and a fixed time is a.s. not an exit time). Hence, for each fixed $N$ and then $R$ large, $\int_1^N|P_{\mathcal O_R}\eta_{R,h}(R,0)|\,dh\ge A(N-1)/(16R)$ (physical final time $t=N+1$, field at $s=t-h$). In Lemma 7.16 take $r_n=R$, $\vartheta=\frac12$, outer factor $2$, $h_1=1$, $h_2=N$: then $K\mu(\mathcal O_R)=32\pi C_NL_0^2$, so $\Lambda\asymp N$, and the lower bound is of order $\frac VR\sqrt{(N-1)\Lambda}$, which exceeds $C\frac VR\sqrt{N-1}$ for $N$ large. $\square$

*Remark.* After the cutoff and the transfer the example is a smooth, compactly supported, finite-energy, divergence-free axisymmetric field, and it satisfies the instantaneous Biot-Savart relation: with $\phi_R=-\psi_R/r^2$ (smooth, compactly supported away from the axis), $u^r=-r\partial_z\phi_R$, $u^z=2\phi_R+r\partial_r\phi_R$ and $-\Delta_5\phi_R=\eta_R$, so the strain is generated by the global vorticity of the field. A prescribed energy bound does not help either, since scaling changes the energy and leaves the ratio of the two sides unchanged. The example is not a Navier-Stokes solution: where $\chi=0$ it is a nonzero time-independent finite-energy field, which contradicts the energy equality. Hence a logarithm-free version of Lemma 7.16 for Navier-Stokes solutions (which by Theorem 7.18 would exclude alternative (I)) must use dynamical restrictions absent from the construction, such as the vorticity evolution equation or the energy-dissipation law; which restriction suffices is not known. The Gaussian identities of the construction, the tangency of the perturbation, the killing estimate and the transfer to the meridional structure were checked.

#### Lemma 7.22 (critical swirl energy; the swirl source is controlled by inflow). Let $Y(t)=\int_{\mathbb R^3}\Gamma^2r^{-3}dx=2\pi\iint(u^\theta)^2dr\,dz$ and $X(t)=\int_{\mathbb R^3}|\nabla\Gamma|^2r^{-3}dx$ ($Y$ and $\int X\,dt$ are scale invariant, $X$ itself is not; $Y=\int(u^\theta)^2r^{-1}dx\le\|u^\theta\|_2\|u^\theta/r\|_2\le\|u\|_2\|\nabla u\|_2<\infty$ for $t<T_*$, and $X<\infty$ by the smoothness and decay of the solution). Then:

(a) for $2\le k<4$, $\displaystyle\frac d{dt}\int\Gamma^2r^{-k}dx+2\int|\nabla\Gamma|^2r^{-k}dx=(k^2-2k)\int\Gamma^2r^{-k-2}dx-k\int\Gamma^2\frac{u^r}r\,r^{-k}dx$;

(b) $Y'+\frac23X\le3\int\Gamma^2\big(\frac{u^r}r\big)_-r^{-3}dx$;

(c) $\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}\le\frac23X$, hence $\int_0^t\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}ds\le Y(0)+3\int_0^t\!\!\int(u^\theta)^2\big(\frac{u^r}r\big)_-\frac{dx}r\,ds$;

(d) under (L1$\eta$), along a Case 3 sequence, $3\int_0^{t_n}\!\int(u^\theta)^2(u^r/r)_-r^{-1}dx\,ds\ge c_1/\nu_n-\|\eta_0\|_{L^1}-Y(0)\to\infty$ (by Lemma 7.26(c) the same divergence holds without (L1$\eta$), with $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}$ for any fixed $s_0\in(0,T_*)$).

*Proof.* (a) $\Gamma$ solves $\partial_t\Gamma+b\cdot\nabla\Gamma=\Delta\Gamma-\frac2r\partial_r\Gamma$ in $\mathbb R^3$. Multiply by $2\Gamma r^{-k}$ and integrate (justified by cutoffs: $\Gamma=O(r^2)$ at the axis, and for $k\ge2$ all integrals are finite at infinity by $\Gamma^2r^{-k}\le(u^\theta)^2$, $|\nabla\Gamma|^2r^{-k}\le2|\nabla u^\theta|^2+2(u^\theta)^2$ for $r\ge1$; for $k<2$ the left side need not be finite for smooth finite-energy data with bounded $\Gamma$, e.g. $u^\theta=\chi(r)\varphi(z)/(r\log r)$ with $\chi=1$ for large $r$). $2\int\Gamma r^{-k}\Delta\Gamma=-2\int|\nabla\Gamma|^2r^{-k}+k\int\partial_r(\Gamma^2)r^{-k-1}$ and $\int\partial_r(\Gamma^2)r^{-k-1}dx=2\pi\iint\partial_r(\Gamma^2)r^{-k}dr\,dz=k\int\Gamma^2r^{-k-2}dx$ (the boundary term at $r=0$ is $O(r^{4-k})$); $-4\int\Gamma r^{-k-1}\partial_r\Gamma=-2k\int\Gamma^2r^{-k-2}$; and $-\int r^{-k}b\cdot\nabla\Gamma^2=\int\Gamma^2b\cdot\nabla r^{-k}=-k\int\Gamma^2u^rr^{-k-1}$ since $\operatorname{div}b=0$. (The radial integration by parts was also checked numerically.) (b) Take $k=3$ and use the one-dimensional Hardy inequality $\int_0^\infty f^2r^{-4}dr\le\frac49\int_0^\infty f'^2r^{-2}dr$ ($f=\Gamma$, $f=O(r^2)$), i.e. $\int\Gamma^2r^{-5}dx\le\frac49\int(\partial_r\Gamma)^2r^{-3}dx$: then $3\int\Gamma^2r^{-5}\le\frac43X_r$, which even gives $Y'+\frac23X_r+2X_z\le3\int\Gamma^2(u^r/r)_-r^{-3}dx$, with $X_r,X_z$ the parts of $X$ with $\partial_r\Gamma,\partial_z\Gamma$. (c) $\partial_z(q^2)=2\Gamma\partial_z\Gamma\,r^{-4}$; Cauchy-Schwarz and Hardy give $\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}\le2(\frac49X_r)^{1/2}X_z^{1/2}\le\frac23(X_r+X_z)$. Integrate (b) in time and use $Y\ge0$. (d) Combine (c) with Corollary 7.9. $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) So under (L1$\eta$) the record core can only be fed if the swirl, weighted critically, accumulates a large inflow-weighted integral ($u^r<0$); this may come from large amplitude or concentration on short intervals. Inflow concentrates swirl toward the axis, which is the classical mechanism of the problem. No upper bound for the inflow integral $I(t)=\int_0^t\!\int(u^\theta)^2(u^r/r)_-r^{-1}dx\,ds$ is proved here; the trivial one, from $\int(u^\theta)^2(u^r/r)_-r^{-1}dx\le\|\nabla u\|_\infty\|u^\theta\|_2\|u^\theta/r\|_2$, is $I(t_n)\le E_0D_n\sqrt{t_n/2}$, which gives only $1/\nu_n\lesssim E_0D_n$ with a constant depending on $T_*$. (2) The signed meridional circulation $\iint\omega^\theta dr\,dz$ changes only through the axis: from the conservative form in Remark (2) after Corollary 7.15, $\frac d{dt}\iint\omega^\theta dr\,dz=-2\int\eta(0,z,t)\,dz$, because the swirl source is a $z$-derivative and the viscous term integrates to $-(\partial_r\omega^\theta+\omega^\theta/r)|_{r=0}=-2\eta(0,z)$. The signed circulation need not be a Lebesgue integral for smooth finite-energy data; under (L1$\eta$) it is, the axis trace $\eta(0,z,s)$ is space-time integrable (keep the favourable axis term in the proof of Lemma 7.7), and the identity holds in the integrated form $\iint\omega^\theta(t)\,dr\,dz-\iint\omega^\theta(0)\,dr\,dz=-2\int_0^t\!\int\eta(0,z,s)\,dz\,ds$. The source has equal integrated positive and negative parts; this says nothing about the signs of the full vorticity field. The record core needs one-sign circulation at least $c_1/(2\pi\nu_n)$ in $dr\,dz$ (Lemma 7.8 is stated for $\|\eta\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}=2\pi\iint|\omega^\theta|dr\,dz$). No constraint has been derived from this.

#### Lemma 7.23 (sharper full-space representation: radial occupation and a divergence primitive). In the setting of Lemma 7.19 put also $B=\sup_{[t-T,t]}\|\partial_zu^\theta\|_\infty$. Then:

(a) $\int_0^Te^{-K(h)}\,\mathbb ER_h^{-3}\,dh\le r_0^{-1}$, hence $\int_0^T\mathbb ER_h^{-3}dh\le e^{K(T)}/r_0$;

(b) $B\le2G^{1/3}H_*^{2/3}$;

(c) $\displaystyle|\eta(x,t)|\le\frac{e^{K(T)}}{r_0}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{10}\,V}{\sqrt T}\,\mathcal E_T^{1/2}+2GB\Big)$, with $\mathcal E_T$ as in (7.19.1).

*Proof.* (a) For $v_\varepsilon=(r^2+\varepsilon)^{-1/2}$ on $\mathbb R^5$ ($r=|y'|$, $y'\in\mathbb R^4$), $\Delta_5v_\varepsilon=v_\varepsilon''+\frac3rv_\varepsilon'=-(r^2+4\varepsilon)(r^2+\varepsilon)^{-5/2}$ and $-b\cdot\nabla v_\varepsilon=\frac{u^r}r\frac{r^2}{r^2+\varepsilon}v_\varepsilon\le k(h)v_\varepsilon$. By Itô's formula ($v_\varepsilon$ is smooth with bounded derivatives), $\mathbb E[e^{-K(T)}v_\varepsilon(Y_T)]+\int_0^Te^{-K(h)}\mathbb E\frac{R_h^2+4\varepsilon}{(R_h^2+\varepsilon)^{5/2}}dh\le v_\varepsilon(Y_0)$. Drop the first term and let $\varepsilon\downarrow0$ with Fatou's lemma (no monotonicity in $\varepsilon$ is needed); since $K$ is nondecreasing, $e^{-K(h)}\ge e^{-K(T)}$ gives the second form.

(b) At $\theta=0$, $u^\theta(r,z)=u_2(r,0,z)$ and $\partial_zu_2$ vanishes on the axis, so $|\partial_zu^\theta(r,z)|\le H_*r$. For fixed $r$, $g(z)=u^\theta(r,z)$ has $\|g\|_\infty\le G/r$ and $\|g''\|_\infty\le H_*$, so $\|g'\|_\infty\le2(GH_*/r)^{1/2}$ (Taylor's formula, as in Corollary 5.5). Use the first bound for $r\le(G/H_*)^{1/3}$ and the second for $r>(G/H_*)^{1/3}$. If $G=0$ or $H_*=0$ then $u^\theta\equiv0$ or $\partial_zu^\theta\equiv0$ on the interval and $B=0$.

(c) Divergence primitive: on $\mathbb R^5$ let $F=F_re_r+F_ze_z$ with $F_z=u^r/r$ and $F_r=-r^{-3}\int_0^ra^2\partial_au^z(a,z)\,da$. Then $\operatorname{div}_5F=r^{-3}\partial_r(r^3F_r)+\partial_zF_z=(\partial_zu^r-\partial_ru^z)/r=\eta$. Integrating by parts in $a$, $F_r=-u^z/r+2r^{-3}\int_0^ra\,u^z\,da$, so $|F_r|\le2V/r$, $|F_z|\le V/r$ and $|F|\le\sqrt5\,V/r$. Writing $u^z=C(r^2,z)$, $r^3F_r=-\int_0^{r^2}\sigma C_\sigma(\sigma,z)\,d\sigma$, so $F_r/r$ is a smooth function of $(r^2,z)$ and $F$ is a smooth vector field on $\mathbb R^5$, bounded on $[t-T,t]$ (by $\|\nabla u\|_\infty$). Hence $\mathbb E\eta(Y_h,t-h)=\int p_h\operatorname{div}_5F=-\int F\cdot\nabla p_h$ (cutoff at infinity), and $|\mathbb E\eta(Y_h,t-h)|\le\sqrt5V\mathcal F(h)^{1/2}(\mathbb ER_h^{-2})^{1/2}$. Averaging over $h\in[T/2,T]$ with Lemma 7.19 (ii), (iv) gives $\frac{\sqrt{10}Ve^{K(T)}}{r_0\sqrt T}\mathcal E_T^{1/2}$ for the homogeneous part.
