For the source, $f=\partial_z(q^2)=2\Gamma\,\partial_zu^\theta/r^3$, so $|f|\le2GB/r^3$ and by (a) $\int_0^T\mathbb E|f(Y_a,t-a)|da\le2GBe^{K(T)}/r_0$. Average the representation of Lemma 7.19 (i) over $h\in[T/2,T]$. $\square$

#### Corollary 7.24 (outward strain forced by every Case 3 sequence). Let $L_n=1+\log(r_nM_n)$, $K_n(T)=\int_{t_n-T}^{t_n}\|(u^r/r)_+(s)\|_{L^\infty(\mathbb R^3)}ds$ and $\Lambda_n(T)=1+\log(2(1+M_n^2T))$. There is $C=C(G,C_*,\kappa_0)$ such that for all large $n$ and all $0<T<t_n-t_A$:
$$K_n(T)\ge\min\Big\{\frac12\log\frac{T}{\ell_n^2\Lambda_n(T)},\ \frac13\log\frac1{\nu_n}\Big\}-C. \tag{7.24.1}$$
In particular: (i) with $T_n^\dagger=r_n^2L_n^{1/3}\alpha_n^{-4/3}$, $K_n(T_n^\dagger)\ge\frac13\log(\alpha_n/L_n)-C$; (ii) with $T_n^*=\ell_n^2L_n\nu_n^{-2/3}$, which tends to $0$, $K_n(T_n^*)\ge\frac13\log(1/\nu_n)-C\to\infty$, for every Case 3 sequence, in either alternative of Theorem 7.12. With $T=\widehat T_n$, (7.24.1) contains Corollary 7.20.

*Proof.* Apply Lemma 7.23(c) at $x^0$, $t=t_n$: $r^0/r_n\to1$, $a_n\ge\frac38\kappa_0D_n/r_n$, $V\le M_n$, $H_*\le C_*D_n^2/M_n$, so by (b) $B/D_n\le2G^{1/3}C_*^{2/3}\nu_n^{1/3}$, and $\mathcal E_T\le C(\Lambda_n(T)+K_n(T))$. Dividing by $D_n/r_n$: $1\le Ce^{K_n(T)}\big[\ell_n((\Lambda_n+K_n)/T)^{1/2}+\nu_n^{1/3}\big]$. If $K_n\le\Lambda_n$ this gives (7.24.1). If $K_n>\Lambda_n$, then since $T/\ell_n^2=M_n^2T\nu_n^2\le M_n^2T$, $\frac12\log\frac T{\ell_n^2\Lambda_n}\le\frac12\Lambda_n<K_n$. (i) $M_n^2T_n^\dagger=(r_nM_n)^2L_n^{1/3}\alpha_n^{-4/3}\le(r_nM_n)^2L_n^{1/3}$ for $\alpha_n\ge1$, so $\Lambda_n(T_n^\dagger)\le C+2\log(r_nM_n)+\frac13\log L_n\le CL_n$, and $T_n^\dagger/\ell_n^2=\alpha_n^{2/3}L_n^{1/3}$, so the first entry of (7.24.1) is at least $\frac13\log(\alpha_n/L_n)-C$; for the second entry use $1/\nu_n\ge\alpha_n/(CL_n)$ (Corollary 7.17). The horizon is admissible: $T_n^\dagger/T_n^*=(\alpha_n\nu_n/L_n)^{2/3}\le C$ by Corollary 7.17, and $T_n^*\to0$ (below). (ii) $M_n^2T_n^*=L_n\nu_n^{-8/3}$ and $\log(1/\nu_n)\le\log(r_nM_n)$ (as $\alpha_n\ge1$), so $\Lambda_n(T_n^*)\le CL_n$ and $T_n^*/(\ell_n^2\Lambda_n)\ge c\nu_n^{-2/3}$. By Corollary 6.2, $T_n^*=r_n^2L_n\alpha_n^{-2}\nu_n^{-2/3}\le C\nu_n^{10/3}L_n\alpha_n^{-2}$, and $L_n\alpha_n^{-2}=(1+\log\alpha_n+\log(1/\nu_n))\alpha_n^{-2}\le 2+\log(1/\nu_n)$, so $T_n^*\to0$ (Corollary 7.17 is not needed for this). $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) For Lemma 7.23 and Corollary 7.24 the following were checked: the Laplacian of $(r^2+\varepsilon)^{-1/2}$ in four radial dimensions, the sign of the drift term, the interpolation (b) with the split radius, the divergence of $F$ and its integration by parts and axis regularity, the constant $\sqrt{10}$, and the powers in (i), (ii). The source term no longer grows with $T$ and carries $e^{K}$ instead of $e^{2K}$. (2) The horizon $T_n^*$ need not be $o(r_n^2)$: $T_n^*/r_n^2=L_n/(\alpha_n^2\nu_n^{2/3})$. Corollary 7.24(ii) is a necessary condition, not a contradiction: no upper bound for $K_n$ on short horizons is known beyond $\int\|\nabla u\|_\infty\le D_nT$, which is $L_n\nu_n^{-5/3}$ at $T=T_n^*$. (3) Two natural continuations fail as stated. Swirl depletion: $q$ is not $e^{-2\int u^r/r}$ times its earlier value along material paths; the correct formula is $q(x,t)=\mathbb E\big[e^{-2\int_0^h(u^r/r)(Y_a,t-a)da}q(Y_h,t-h)\big]$, and a lower bound on $K$ (the integral of a spatial supremum) gives no lower bound on the strain along the paths inside the expectation. Iteration over scales: a transported value $\eta\sim D_n/r_n$ at radius $\vartheta r_n$ corresponds to $|\omega^\theta|\sim\vartheta D_n$, below the record scale, so the transported value does not establish another gradient record at the inner point (small $\omega^\theta$ does not imply small $\nabla u$, since an irrotational strain can be large; but nothing provides a record there), and the dichotomy cannot be re-applied there.

*Formal scenario (not a solution; a scalar scaling test for alternative (II)).* With the time history $M=s^{-3/4}$, $D=s^{-7/5}$, $r=s^{11/20}$ of 8.2 ($\alpha\nu=1$), consider formally a pair of opposite-sign toroidal cores of circulation $1/\nu$ expanding from radius $\xi_0r$, $\xi_0=\alpha^{-2/3}$, to $r$, with core size $d(R)=\ell\xi^{-1/2}$, speed $U(R)=M\xi^{1/2}$ and vorticity $\Omega(R)=D\xi$ at radius $R=\xi r$ (so $Rd^2$ and $\Omega d^2$ are constant, $\eta$ is constant on the core, and $U/d=\Omega$, $U/d^2=H\xi^{3/2}$ respect the derivative scales). At the initial radius $R_{\rm init}=\xi_0r$ one has $d(R_{\rm init})/R_{\rm init}=\alpha^{-1}\xi_0^{-3/2}=1$, so the initial core is not a thin torus; this changes only fixed geometric constants at the level of scalings. With the speed law $dR/dt=U(R)$ the expansion takes time $\frac{2r}M(1-\xi_0^{1/2})$, the strain along the material centre path is $\int u^r/R\,dt=\log(1/\xi_0)=\frac23\log\alpha$, which accommodates Corollaries 7.20 and 7.24 (here $r/M=\ell^2$, and the horizons $\widehat T,T^\dagger,T^*$ all exceed the expansion time, while for horizons $T\lesssim\ell^2$ the first entry of (7.24.1) is bounded), and the accumulated viscous time in core units is $\int dt/d^2=\frac23\alpha\nu(1-\xi_0^{3/2})=O(1)$ (with $dR/dt\asymp U$ only, the same up to a constant factor). The kinetic energy of the cores has scale $U^2Rd^2=M^2r\ell^2\xi$, and their dissipation over the expansion has the same scale $Mr^2=M^2r\ell^2=s^{7/20}$, so the energy budget does not obstruct them; any accompanying flow (which the pair needs, since it does not conserve energy by itself) is an additional modelling assumption whose energy and derivatives are unchecked. The scenario passes the scalar restrictions of 8.1 ($r\nu^{-2}=r\alpha^2=s^{7/20}\to0$, $\alpha\nu=1$, circulation scale $1/\nu=D\cdot r/M$, $\vartheta_n^\sharp\asymp L^{1/5}\alpha^{-2/5}\gg\xi_0$ with a horizon longer than the expansion), but it does not verify the items that involve actual swirl-source and inflow integrals ((viii), (ix), (xviii), (xx), and the source-budget parts of (xii), whose short-window circulation scale $D_nh\lesssim D\ell^2=1/\nu$ is compatible) or stopped probabilities, net signed expectations and the Biot-Savart field ((xi), (xiii), (xv)); for (xvi) and (xix) it compares the required strain with an assigned path strain and does not construct the velocity field whose spatial supremum defines $K_n$ (for every horizon $T\ge T_{\rm exp}$ the assigned path gives $\frac23\log\alpha$ while the minimum in (7.24.1) is at most $\frac13\log\alpha$): no swirl field, preparation history or companion flow is supplied. Nothing here constructs a Navier-Stokes evolution; these calculations reveal no contradiction among the scalar comparisons made here, and no proved item is known to exclude a suitably completed version. Possible further approaches include ruling out the required preparation and outward transport, or proving an upper bound incompatible with (7.24.1); compatibility with the unverified field and evolution constraints remains open.

#### Lemma 7.25 (critical swirl inequality; the swirl velocity must exceed the record circulation). With $Y$, $X$ as in Lemma 7.22, $Z(t)=\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2$, $B(t)=\int_0^t\|\partial_z(q^2)(s)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}ds$ and $S(t)=\sup_{[0,t]}\|u^\theta\|_\infty$, for every $t<T_*$:

(a) $\displaystyle Y(t)+\sqrt3\,B(t)\le Y(0)+\frac32\int_0^t\|u^\theta(s)\|_\infty Z(s)\,ds\le Y(0)+\frac34E_0S(t)$;

(b) if $B(t)\to\infty$ as $t\uparrow T_*$, then $S(t)\to\infty$ and $B(t)=o(S(t))$; under (A) also $B(t)=o\big(D(t)^{2/3}M(t)^{-1/3}\big)$;

(c) under (L1$\eta$), along every Case 3 sequence (and, by Lemma 7.26(c), without (L1$\eta$)), $S(t_n)\,\nu_n\to\infty$, that is, the historical maximal swirl velocity is much larger than the record circulation $1/\nu_n=M_n\ell_n$;

(d) $\int_0^{T_*}Y(t)^2dt\le E_0^2/2$ and $\int_0^{T_*}\!\int(u^\theta)^4dx\,dt\le G^2E_0/2$.

*Proof.* Write $I=\int\Gamma^2r^{-5}dx$, $X_r=\int(\partial_r\Gamma)^2r^{-3}dx$, $X_z=\int(\partial_z\Gamma)^2r^{-3}dx$ and $L=\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}$. The identity of Lemma 7.22 (a) with $k=3$ reads $Y'+Q=-3\int\frac{u^r}r\Gamma^2r^{-3}dx$ with $Q=2X_r+2X_z-3I$. By the Hardy inequality $I\le\frac49X_r$ (proof of Lemma 7.22 (b)), $Q\ge\frac32I+2X_z\ge2\sqrt3\sqrt{IX_z}$, and by Cauchy-Schwarz $L=2\int|\Gamma\,\partial_z\Gamma|r^{-4}dx\le2\sqrt{IX_z}$; hence $Q\ge\sqrt3L$ and $Y'+\sqrt3L\le3F_-$ with $F_-=\int(u^r/r)_-\Gamma^2r^{-3}dx=\int(u^r)_-(u^\theta)^2r^{-2}dx$. Since $(u^r)_-|u^\theta|\le\frac12((u^r)^2+(u^\theta)^2)$ and, for an axisymmetric field, $|\nabla u|^2\ge((u^r)^2+(u^\theta)^2)/r^2$ (the angular derivatives of the Cartesian field), $F_-\le\frac12\|u^\theta\|_\infty Z$. Integrating in time gives the first inequality of (a); the second follows from $\int_0^tZ\le E_0/2$ (energy equality). Justification of the integrations near the whole axis (the pointwise statement $\Gamma=O(r^2)$ alone does not control the integral along the axis): fix $t_1<T_*$ and write $\Gamma=r^2q$. Since $q(r,z)=\int_0^1\partial_{x_1}u_2(\sigma r,0,z)\,d\sigma$ and similarly for its derivatives, $H^2(\mathbb R^2_{x'};L^2_z)\subset L^\infty_{x'}L^2_z$ gives $\sup_{0\le r\le1}(\|q(r,\cdot)\|_{L^2_z}+\|\partial_rq(r,\cdot)\|_{L^2_z}+\|\partial_zq(r,\cdot)\|_{L^2_z})<\infty$ uniformly on $[0,t_1]$. Hence near the axis $Y=2\pi\iint r^2q^2$, $I=2\pi\iint q^2$, $X_r=2\pi\iint(2q+r\partial_rq)^2$, $X_z=2\pi\iint r^2(\partial_zq)^2$ (all $dr\,dz$) are finite, and the boundary terms $\int\Gamma^2r^{-3}dz=r\|q(r,\cdot)\|_2^2$ and $\int\Gamma\partial_r\Gamma r^{-2}dz=2r\|q\|_2^2+r^2\int q\partial_rq\,dz$ tend to $0$ as $r\to0$. For $r\ge1$ the weighted integrals are bounded by $L^2$ norms of $u$ and its derivatives, and smooth radial cutoffs at infinity produce errors bounded by $R^{-1}\|u\|_\infty Y+R^{-2}Y+R^{-1}\sqrt{YX}\to0$. (As a sign check, $Q=2\pi\iint(2|\partial_ru^\theta|^2+2|\partial_zu^\theta|^2+(u^\theta)^2r^{-2})dr\,dz$.) (b) If $B\to\infty$, (a) gives $S\to\infty$. For fixed $\tau<t$, $\int_0^t\|u^\theta\|_\infty Z\le S(\tau)E_0/2+S(t)\int_\tau^{T_*}Z$; divide by $S(t)$, let $t\uparrow T_*$ and then $\tau\uparrow T_*$. The bound under (A) follows from $S^3\le G^2H\le G^2C_*D^2/M$ (Lemma 1.5 (i)). (c) Corollary 7.9 gives $c_1/\nu_n-\|\eta_0\|_{L^1}\le B(t_n)$, so $B(t_n)\to\infty$, and $B(t_n)=o(S(t_n))$ by (b). (d) $Y=\int|u^\theta||q|\,dx\le\|u^\theta\|_2\|q\|_2\le\sqrt{E_0Z}$ and $\int(u^\theta)^4dx=\int\Gamma^2q^2dx\le G^2Z$; integrate. $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) For Lemma 7.25 (a)-(c) I rechecked the identity, the Hardy constant, the inequality $Q\ge\sqrt3L$, the bound for $F_-$ and the limit argument. (2) By Lemma 1.5 (iii), (c) is compatible with the upper bound $S_n\le(G^2C_*)^{1/3}M_n\nu_n^{2/3}$, because $M_n\nu_n^{5/3}=D_n^{5/3}M_n^{-7/3}\to\infty$ when $D_n\gg M_n^{5/3}$, which holds along Case 3 ($\alpha_n\to\infty$ and Corollary 6.2 give $\alpha_n\le CE_0D_n^3/M_n^5$). So (c) is a necessary condition, not a contradiction; an upper bound $S(t_n)=O(1/\nu_n)$ would exclude Case 3 (Lemma 7.26(c); no decay assumption on $\eta_0$ is needed). (3) The source is a $z$-derivative, so $\int f_+dx=\int f_-dx$ and the one-sign parts satisfy $\|\eta_\pm(t)\|_{L^1}\le\|(\eta_0)_\pm\|_{L^1}+\frac12B(t)$; and for test functions, $|\int_0^t\!\int f\phi\,dx\,ds|\le\frac{E_0}2\|\partial_z\phi\|_\infty$. At the core scale $\ell_n$ the last bound is $E_0/\ell_n$, far above $1/\nu_n$, so neither yields an upper bound small enough to exclude the one-sign circulation required at the record core.

#### Lemma 7.26 (the critical budgets without (L1$\eta$); inward strain; injection across a plane). Fix $s_0\in(0,T_*)$, put $B_0=\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty$ (finite, since $|\eta|\le\|\partial_r\omega^\theta\|_\infty\le\sqrt2\|\nabla^2u(s_0)\|_\infty$), $\mathcal B(s_0,t)=\int_{s_0}^t\|\partial_z(q^2)(s)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}ds$ and $K_-(s_0,t)=\int_{s_0}^t\|(u^r/r)_-(s)\|_{L^\infty}ds$. Then:

(a) for $s_0\le t<T_*$, $\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb R^3}\big(|\eta(x,t)|-B_0\big)_+dx\le\mathcal B(s_0,t)$;

(b) along every Case 3 sequence, $\mathcal B(s_0,t_n)\ge c_1/(2\nu_n)$ for all large $n$, with $c_1$ from Lemma 7.8;

(c) hence, without (L1$\eta$): $3\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\!\int(u^\theta)^2(u^r/r)_-r^{-1}dx\,ds\ge c_1/(2\nu_n)-Y(s_0)\to\infty$ (the conclusion of Lemma 7.22(d)), and $S(t_n)\nu_n\to\infty$ (the conclusion of Lemma 7.25(c));

(d) $\sqrt3\,\mathcal B(s_0,t)\le Y(s_0)\,e^{3K_-(s_0,t)}$; hence along every Case 3 sequence $K_-(s_0,t_n)\ge\frac13\log\frac1{\nu_n}-C(s_0)\to\infty$;

(e) for every $z$ and $t$, $J(z,t):=\int_0^\infty(u^\theta)^2(r,z,t)\,r^{-1}dr\le2G^{4/3}H(t)^{2/3}$, and $\iint_{z>z_0}r^{-1}\partial_z\big((u^\theta)^2\big)dr\,dz=-J(z_0,t)$. Consequently, for each fixed $c>0$, $\nu_n\sup_{z}\int_{t_n-cr_n/M_n}^{t_n}J(z,t)\,dt\le C(G,C_*,c)\,\nu_n^{1/3}\log(1/\nu_n)\to0$.

*Proof.* (a) As in Lemma 7.7, on $[s_0,t]$, with $\phi_\epsilon$ replaced by $\Phi_\epsilon(\sigma)=\phi_\epsilon\big((|\sigma|-B_0)_+\big)$, which is convex and $C^{1,1}$, vanishes on $[-B_0,B_0]$, has $|\Phi_\epsilon'|\le1$, and increases to $(|\sigma|-B_0)_+$ as $\epsilon\downarrow0$. The four term-by-term bounds of that proof use only these properties (the diffusion term uses $\Phi_\epsilon''\ge0$ a.e.; the axis term $-4\pi\int\Phi_\epsilon(\eta(0,z))\psi_R(0,z)dz$ is again $\le0$). Now $X(s_0)=0$ because $|\eta(s_0)|\le B_0$, so $X(t)\le\exp(\int_{s_0}^ta_R)\,\mathcal B(s_0,t)$; let $R\to\infty$, then $\epsilon\downarrow0$. No integrability of $\eta(s_0)$ is used: $(|\eta|-B_0)_+$ vanishes identically at $s_0$.
(b) Let $\mathcal D$ be the meridional disk of radius $\rho_1$ about $(r^0,z^0)$ from the proof of Lemma 7.8, on which $|\omega^\theta(t_n)|\ge\frac38\kappa_0D_n$, and $\mathcal T$ its solid of revolution, of volume $2\pi^2r^0\rho_1^2$. Then $\int(|\eta(t_n)|-B_0)_+dx\ge\int_{\mathcal T}|\eta|\,dx-B_0|\mathcal T|\ge c_1/\nu_n-2\pi^2B_0r^0\rho_1^2$, and the ratio of the last term to $c_1/\nu_n=2\pi^2\cdot\frac38\kappa_0D_n\rho_1^2$ is $\frac8{3\kappa_0}B_0r^0/D_n\to0$, since $r^0\le2r_n\to0$ and $D_n\to\infty$. Apply (a) with $t=t_n>s_0$. The same computation gives the sharper $\liminf_n\nu_n\mathcal B(s_0,t_n)\ge c_1$; the factor $\frac12$ in (b) is only a margin.
(c) Lemma 7.22 (a)-(c) and the inequalities in the proof of Lemma 7.25 are time-local; integrated over $[s_0,t]$ they give $\mathcal B(s_0,t)\le Y(s_0)+3\int_{s_0}^t\!\int(u^\theta)^2(u^r/r)_-r^{-1}dx\,ds$ and $Y(t)+\sqrt3\mathcal B(s_0,t)\le Y(s_0)+\frac32\int_{s_0}^t\|u^\theta\|_\infty Z\,ds\le Y(s_0)+\frac34E_0S(t)$, where $Y(s_0)<\infty$ (Lemma 7.22). With (b) the first gives the inflow bound; the second gives $S(t_n)\to\infty$, and then, splitting $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|u^\theta\|_\infty Z\le S(\tau)E_0/2+S(t_n)\int_\tau^{T_*}Z$ as in Lemma 7.25(b), $\liminf_n S(t_n)\nu_n\ge\frac{\sqrt3c_1}{3}\big(\int_\tau^{T_*}Z\big)^{-1}$ for every $\tau<T_*$ (if $\int_\tau^{T_*}Z=0$ for some $\tau$, the inequality before the division already contradicts (b)), so $S(t_n)\nu_n\to\infty$.
(d) From the proof of Lemma 7.25, $Y'+\sqrt3\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}\le3F_-$ with $F_-=\int(u^r/r)_-\Gamma^2r^{-3}dx\le\|(u^r/r)_-\|_\infty Y$. Hence $\big(e^{-3K_-(s_0,s)}Y(s)\big)'\le-\sqrt3e^{-3K_-(s_0,s)}\|\partial_z(q^2)(s)\|_{L^1}$; integrating over $[s_0,t]$, dropping $Y(t)\ge0$, and using that $K_-(s_0,\cdot)$ is nondecreasing gives $\sqrt3e^{-3K_-(s_0,t)}\mathcal B(s_0,t)\le Y(s_0)$. If $Y(s_0)=0$ then $\Gamma(s_0)\equiv0$, hence $\Gamma\equiv0$ on $[s_0,T_*)$ by the maximum principle for the $\Gamma$ equation, and $\mathcal B\equiv0$, contradicting (b); so $Y(s_0)>0$ and (b) gives $K_-(s_0,t_n)\ge\frac13\log\frac{\sqrt3c_1}{2Y(s_0)\nu_n}$.
(e) If $G=0$ or $H(t)=0$ the swirl vanishes at time $t$ and there is nothing to prove. Otherwise $|u^\theta|=r|q|\le2G^{1/3}H(t)^{2/3}r$ by Lemma 1.5 (i), (ii), and $|u^\theta|\le G/r$. Split at $a=(G/Q)^{1/2}$, $Q=2G^{1/3}H(t)^{2/3}$: $J\le Q^2\int_0^ar\,dr+G^2\int_a^\infty r^{-3}dr=GQ$. For the identity: for fixed $r>0$, $u^\theta(r,z)\to0$ as $z\to\infty$ (the solution is in $H^k$ for all $k$), so $\int_{z_0}^\infty\partial_z((u^\theta)^2)dz=-(u^\theta)^2(r,z_0)$, and Fubini applies because $\iint|\partial_z((u^\theta)^2)|r^{-1}dr\,dz=\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}/(2\pi)\le X/(3\pi)<\infty$ (Lemma 7.22(c)). Finally, for $t\le t_n$, $H(t)\le H_n\le C_*D_n^2/M_n$, so $\int_{t_n-cr_n/M_n}^{t_n}J\,dt\le2cG^{4/3}C_*^{2/3}D_n^{4/3}M_n^{-5/3}r_n=2cG^{4/3}C_*^{2/3}\alpha_n\nu_n^{1/3}$, and $\alpha_n\nu_n=O(\log(1/\nu_n))$: by Corollary 7.17, $y=\alpha_n\nu_n$ satisfies $y\le C(1+\log y+2\log(1/\nu_n))$, and $C\log y\le y/2+C'$. $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) For Lemma 7.26 I rechecked the convex truncation and the vanishing initial value in (a), the volume comparison in (b), the integrating factor in (d), the interpolation and the split radius in (e), and the logarithmic bound for $\alpha_n\nu_n$. (2) So the hypothesis (L1$\eta$) is not needed for the critical source budget, the inflow budget, or the swirl-velocity condition: Corollary 7.9, Lemma 7.22(d) and Lemma 7.25(c) hold for every smooth finite-energy datum, with $\int_0^{t_n}$ replaced by $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}$ and the constant halved. The conclusion $\sup_{t<T_*}\|\eta(t)\|_{L^1}=\infty$ of Corollary 7.9 still follows from Lemma 7.8 (as an extended real number); what is lost without (L1$\eta$) is the finiteness of the individual norms, hence its reading as growth from a finite initial value. (3) (d) is the backward-in-time counterpart of Corollary 7.24: the full-space inward strain must accumulate at least $\frac13\log(1/\nu_n)$ over the history $[s_0,t_n]$, which is not localized near $t_n$. A sharper conditional statement follows from the proof: if for some $s_0$ the radial velocity satisfies $u^r\ge0$ wherever $u^\theta\ne0$, throughout $[s_0,T_*)$, then $F_-\equiv0$ there and $\sqrt3\mathcal B(s_0,t)\le Y(s_0)$, contradicting (b); so Case 3 requires inflow on the swirl-bearing region, critically weighted. (4) (e) is the rate at which the swirl source moves signed meridional circulation across the plane $\{z=z_0\}$: it adds $-J(z_0,t)$ to $\{z>z_0\}$ and $+J(z_0,t)$ to $\{z<z_0\}$. On the time scale $r_n/M_n$ of the long window this transfer is $o(1/\nu_n)$, so the record circulation cannot be created within that time by a single source dipole split by a plane. Precisely: if on a generating interval $I_n\subset[0,t_n]$ (so that $H(s)\le H_n$ there) the positive and negative parts of $\partial_z(q^2)(s)$ lie on opposite sides of a plane $z=z_0(s)$ for almost every $s\in I_n$, then $\|\partial_z(q^2)(s)\|_{L^1}=4\pi J(z_0(s),s)$, so if $I_n$ supplies a source budget $\ge c/\nu_n$, then $|I_n|\gtrsim(G^{4/3}C_*^{2/3}D_n\nu_n^{4/3})^{-1}$ and $|I_n|M_n/r_n\gtrsim(\alpha_n\nu_n^{4/3})^{-1}\to\infty$. Without such a sign-separation hypothesis (e) gives no conclusion about the preparation time; in particular configurations with several source lobes are not excluded: $\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}$ is not bounded by $\sup_zJ$ (many separated axial bumps of the same pure-swirl profile have the same $G$, $D$, $H$ and $\sup_zJ$ but additive $\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}$). Nor does (e) control the advective and viscous transfer of circulation across the plane. (5) Literature context, used in no proof. The divergence $K_-(s_0,t)\to\infty$ as $t\to T_*$ holds at every first singular time, independently of Case 3. Indeed $q$ solves $\partial_tq+b\cdot\nabla q=\Delta_5q-2\frac{u^r}r\,q$, so the maximum principle for the lifted equation on $\mathbb R^5$ (in the bounded class, as in the proof of Lemma 7.37, applied to $\pm e^{-2K_-(s_0,t)}q$) gives $\|q(t)\|_\infty\le\|q(s_0)\|_\infty e^{2K_-(s_0,t)}$; and a bound on $\|q\|_\infty$ over $[s_0,T_*)$ is excluded by a regularity criterion of Chen, Fang and Zhang (arXiv 1505.00905, Remark 1.3, stated there as a consequence of their Theorem 1.1 by interpolation with the bound on $ru^\theta$): for axisymmetric divergence-free data in $H^2$ with $ru^\theta_0\in L^\infty$, the condition $u^\theta/r\in L^a_tL^b_x$ with $\frac2a+\frac3b\le2$, $\frac32<b\le\infty$ (in particular $L^1_tL^\infty_x$) implies smoothness up to the final time. It is applied after restarting at $s_0$, where $u(s_0)\in H^2$ and $|ru^\theta(s_0)|\le G$. We read the statement in the arXiv version and checked that the interpolation exponents land inside the range of Theorem 1.1 there (at the endpoint of its time exponent); the proof there was not rechecked, and the journal version was not compared. Hence (d), Lemma 7.27(d) and Lemma 7.36(c) add quantitative lower growth bounds along the sequence ($\frac13\log(1/\nu_n)-C$, a lower limit at least $\frac14$ for $K_-/\log(D_n/r_n)$, and $\frac13\log(D_n/r_n)-C$), not the divergence itself. Finiteness of $K_-(s_0,T_*)$ is itself a sufficient regularity criterion. To exclude Case 3, it would suffice to obtain an upper bound along its sequence incompatible with one of the stated lower bounds.

#### Lemma 7.27 (stretching-free $L^p$ budgets for $\eta$; a sharper inward-strain condition). Fix $s_0\in(0,T_*)$ and $p\in[2,\infty)$, and let $K_-(s_0,t)$ be as in Lemma 7.26. Put $W_p(t)=\int_{\mathbb R^3}|\Gamma|^{2p}r^{2-4p}dx=\int(u^\theta)^2|q|^{2p-2}dx$ (finite for $t<T_*$, since $W_p\le\|q\|_\infty^{2p-2}\|u\|_2^2$). Then:

(a) for $s_0\le t<T_*$, $\displaystyle\frac d{dt}\|\eta\|_{L^p}^p\le\frac{p(p-1)}4\int|\eta|^{p-2}q^4dx$, hence $\displaystyle\|\eta(t)\|_{L^p}^2\le\|\eta(s_0)\|_{L^p}^2+\frac{p-1}2\int_{s_0}^t\|q(s)\|_{L^{2p}}^4ds$; for $p=2$: $\|\eta(t)\|_{L^2}^2\le\|\eta(s_0)\|_{L^2}^2+\frac12\int_{s_0}^t\|q\|_{L^4}^4ds$. There is no vortex-stretching term;

(b) along every Case 3 sequence, for large $n$, $\|\eta(t_n)\|_{L^p}^p\ge c_p\,(D_n/r_n)^p\,r_n\ell_n^2=c_p\,(D_n/r_n)^{p-1}/\nu_n$, with $c_p=(3\kappa_0/16)^p\,63\pi^2\kappa_0^2/(512C_*^2)$; in particular $\|\eta(t_n)\|_{L^2}^2\ge c_2M_n^2/r_n$;

(c) $W_p'+\frac{4p-2}p\|q\|_{L^{2p}}^{2p}\le(4p-2)\int|\Gamma|^{2p}\big(\frac{u^r}r\big)_-r^{2-4p}dx\le(4p-2)\|(u^r/r)_-\|_\infty W_p$, hence $\displaystyle\int_{s_0}^t\|q\|_{L^{2p}}^{2p}ds\le\frac p{4p-2}W_p(s_0)\,e^{(4p-2)K_-(s_0,t)}$;

(d) along every Case 3 sequence, for large $n$,
$$K_-(s_0,t_n)\ge\frac{p-1}{4p-2}\log\frac{D_n}{r_n}+\frac1{4p-2}\log\frac1{\nu_n}-C_p(s_0);$$
for $p=2$ this reads $K_-(s_0,t_n)\ge\frac16\log(M_n^2/r_n)-C(s_0)$, and letting $p\to\infty$, $\liminf_n K_-(s_0,t_n)/\log(D_n/r_n)\ge\frac14$. Both are stronger than Lemma 7.26(d): $\frac16\log\frac{M_n^2}{r_n}-\frac13\log\frac1{\nu_n}=\frac16\log\frac1{r_n\ell_n^2}\to\infty$, and $\frac14\log\frac{D_n}{r_n}\ge\frac16\log\frac{M_n^2}{r_n}+\frac1{12}\log\frac{D_n^3}{M_n^4}$ with $D_n^3\ge c\,\alpha_nM_n^5$ (from $r_n\le CE_0\nu_n^2$, Corollary 6.2);

(e) consequently each of the following excludes Case 3: $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|u^\theta/r\|_{L^4}^4ds=o(M_n^2/r_n)$ along a subsequence; $K_-(s_0,t_n)\le(\frac14-\epsilon)\log(D_n/r_n)$ along a subsequence, for some $\epsilon>0$.

*Proof.* Fix $t<T_*$. On $[s_0,t]$ the solution is smooth with $u\in C([s_0,t];H^k)$ for all $k$, and $\eta,q$ are smooth functions of $(r^2,z)$ (proof of Lemma 7.7). They are bounded ($|q|\le|\nabla u|$, $|\eta|\le\sup_\rho|\partial_\rho\omega^\theta|\le\|\nabla\omega\|_\infty$) and lie in $L^2(\mathbb R^3)$: for an axisymmetric vector field $F$, $|\nabla F|^2=|\partial_rF|^2+|\partial_zF|^2+((F^r)^2+(F^\theta)^2)r^{-2}$, so $\|q\|_2\le\|\nabla u\|_2$ and $\|\eta\|_2\le\|\nabla\omega\|_2$. These bounds are linear in $u$, so $\eta,q\in C^1([s_0,t];L^2\cap L^\infty)$ and $y(s)=\|\eta(s)\|_p^p$ is $C^1$ for $p\ge2$. Hence all integrals below are finite.
(a) Use the cutoff $\psi_R$ of Lemma 7.7 and $X(s)=\int|\eta|^p\psi_R$ ($|\sigma|^p$ is $C^2$ for $p\ge2$). The transport, diffusion and $\frac2r\partial_r$ terms are bounded exactly as there, with $\phi_\epsilon$ replaced by $|\cdot|^p$, except that the diffusion term also produces $-p(p-1)\int|\eta|^{p-2}|\nabla\eta|^2\psi_R$ and the axis term $-4\pi\int|\eta(0,z)|^p\psi_R(0,z)dz\le0$ is dropped. The source term is integrated by parts in $z$: $p\int|\eta|^{p-2}\eta\,\partial_z(q^2)\psi_R=-p(p-1)\int|\eta|^{p-2}\partial_z\eta\,q^2\psi_R-p\int|\eta|^{p-2}\eta\,q^2\partial_z\psi_R$, and $|\partial_z\eta|q^2\le|\nabla\eta|^2+\frac14q^4$. So $X'\le a_RX+\frac{p(p-1)}4\int|\eta|^{p-2}q^4\psi_R+\frac{2p}R\int|\eta|^{p-1}q^2\psi_R$ with $a_R\to0$ as in Lemma 7.7; integrating on $[s_0,t']$ and letting $R\to\infty$ (the last integral is at most $\|\eta\|_\infty^{p-1}\|q\|_2^2$, bounded uniformly in $s$) gives the integrated form of the differential inequality. By Hölder, $\int|\eta|^{p-2}q^4\le\|\eta\|_p^{p-2}\|q\|_{2p}^4$; with $y_\epsilon=y+\epsilon$, $(y_\epsilon^{2/p})'\le\frac2py_\epsilon^{2/p-1}\frac{p(p-1)}4y^{1-2/p}\|q\|_{2p}^4\le\frac{p-1}2\|q\|_{2p}^4$; integrate and let $\epsilon\downarrow0$.
(b) Let $\mathcal D$ be the disk of radius $\rho_1$ about $(r^0,z^0)$ from the proof of Lemma 7.8, where $|\omega^\theta(t_n)|\ge\frac38\kappa_0D_n$. For large $n$, $r^0\ge\frac78r_n$ and $r^0+\rho_1\le2r_n$ (preamble of Section 4.7, $\rho_1/r_n\to0$), so $|\eta(t_n)|\ge\frac{3\kappa_0}{16}D_n/r_n$ on the solid torus over $\mathcal D$, whose volume is $2\pi^2r^0\rho_1^2\ge\frac74\pi^2r_n\cdot\frac{9\kappa_0^2}{128C_*^2}\ell_n^2$. Multiply, and use $D_n\ell_n^2=1/\nu_n$ and $D_n^2\ell_n^2=M_n^2$.
(c) For $h=|\Gamma|^{2p}$ (a $C^2$ function, $p\ge1$), the $\Gamma$ equation gives $(\partial_t+b\cdot\nabla)h=\Delta h-\frac2r\partial_rh-2p(2p-1)|\Gamma|^{2p-2}|\nabla\Gamma|^2$. Multiply by $r^{-k}$ with $k=4p-2$ and integrate, exactly as in Lemma 7.22(a) (with $\Gamma^2$ replaced by $h$): with $g=|\Gamma|^p$, so that $2p(2p-1)|\Gamma|^{2p-2}|\nabla\Gamma|^2=\frac{2(2p-1)}p|\nabla g|^2$,
$$\frac d{dt}\int hr^{-k}dx+\frac{2(2p-1)}p\int|\nabla g|^2r^{-k}dx=(k^2-2k)\int hr^{-k-2}dx-k\int h\frac{u^r}rr^{-k}dx .$$
The boundary terms at the axis are the $z$-integrals of $r^{-k}h$ and $r^{1-k}\partial_rh$ in the $dr\,dz$ integrations; with $\Gamma=r^2q$, $r^{-k}h=r^2|q|^{2p}$ and $r^{1-k}\partial_rh=4pr^2|q|^{2p}+2pr^3|q|^{2p-2}q\,\partial_rq$, so by the uniform trace bounds $\sup_{0<r\le1}(\|q(r,\cdot)\|_{L^2_z}+\|\partial_rq(r,\cdot)\|_{L^2_z}+\|\partial_zq(r,\cdot)\|_{L^2_z})<\infty$ of the proof of Lemma 7.25 and the boundedness of $q$, both $z$-integrals are $O(r^2)$ (pointwise orders in $r$ alone would not control the integral along the whole axis); the same bounds show that the integrals converge near the whole axis, including $|z|\to\infty$ with $r\le1$ (for instance the density of $\int|\nabla g|^2r^{-k}dx$ is $2\pi p^2r|q|^{2p-2}((2q+r\partial_rq)^2+r^2(\partial_zq)^2)$ in $dr\,dz$). The integrals are finite for $r\ge1$ because for $r\ge1$, $hr^{-k}\le\|u\|_\infty^{2p-2}(u^\theta)^2$, $hr^{-k-2}\le\|u\|_\infty^{2p-2}(u^\theta)^2$ and $|\nabla g|^2r^{-k}\le p^2G^{2p-2}(2|\nabla u^\theta|^2+2(u^\theta)^2)$. The one-dimensional Hardy inequality $\int_0^\infty g^2r^{-k-1}dr\le\frac4{k^2}\int_0^\infty(\partial_rg)^2r^{1-k}dr$ (integrate $\partial_r(g^2r^{-k})$; the boundary terms vanish since $g^2r^{-k}=O(r^2)$ at $0$ and $\le G^{2p}r^{-k}$ at infinity) gives $\int|\nabla g|^2r^{-k}\ge\frac{k^2}4\int hr^{-k-2}$. The net coefficient of $\int hr^{-k-2}dx=\|q\|_{2p}^{2p}$ on the left is $\frac{(2p-1)k^2}{2p}-(k^2-2k)=2k-\frac{k^2}{2p}=\frac{4p-2}p$ for $k=4p-2$. This is the first inequality; the second is immediate, and the time integral follows with the integrating factor $e^{-(4p-2)K_-(s_0,s)}$ as in Lemma 7.26(d).
(d) If $W_p(s_0)=0$ then $\Gamma\equiv0$ on $[s_0,T_*)$, the source vanishes, and (a) bounds $\|\eta(t_n)\|_p$, contradicting (b) since $(D_n/r_n)^{p-1}/\nu_n\to\infty$; so $W_p(s_0)>0$. By Hölder in time and (c), $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|q\|_{2p}^4\le T_*^{1-2/p}\big(\frac p{4p-2}W_p(s_0)\big)^{2/p}e^{\frac2p(4p-2)K_-(s_0,t_n)}$. Insert this into (a) at $t=t_n$ and compare with (b): for large $n$, $e^{\frac2p(4p-2)K_-}\ge c(p,s_0)\big((D_n/r_n)^{p-1}/\nu_n\big)^{2/p}$, which is the claim. For the comparison with Lemma 7.26(d): $\frac14\log\frac{D}{r}-\frac16\log\frac{M^2}{r}=\frac1{12}\log\frac{D^3}{M^4r}$ and $r\le1$ for large $n$; and $r_n=\alpha_nM_n/D_n\le CE_0D_n^2/M_n^4$ gives $D_n^3\ge\alpha_nM_n^5/(CE_0)$. (e) is (a)-(d) read backwards. $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) The point of (a) is that $\eta=\omega^\theta/r$ obeys a transport-diffusion equation in $\mathbb R^3$ (with the divergence-free field $b$ and the extra drift $\frac2r\partial_r$, whose contribution has a sign) and the only source is $\partial_z(q^2)$, which after one integration by parts is controlled by the dissipation and $q^4$. The record gives $\|\eta(t_n)\|_{L^p}^p\gtrsim(D_n/r_n)^{p-1}/\nu_n$, so the critical source must be large in every $L^{2p}$, and (c) shows that $q$ can be large in $L^{2p}_{t,x}$ only through inward strain. Lemma 7.26(d) is the analogous $L^1$ statement, with the critical swirl energy $Y=\int\Gamma^2r^{-3}dx$ in place of $W_p$. (2) No upper bound for $K_-$ is known; the trivial one is $K_-(s_0,t_n)\le\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|\nabla u\|_\infty ds$, and the bound $\|u^r/r\|_\infty\le C\|\eta\|_{L^{3,1}}$ (Lemma 7.14) together with (a) and (c) closes only through an exponential (Gronwall-type) feedback, which is not used. (3) The formal $N$-cell scalings of 8.3 (vi) match the sizes required by (a)-(c): there $\int\|q\|_{2p}^4ds$ is of the order of $g^4$ times the lower bound $(D_n/r_n)^2(r_n\ell_n^2)^{2/p}$ required by (a) and (b), and the cells generate $\eta$ of size $g^2a^{-3}=g^2D_n/r_n$; for $W_p$ to grow from $W_p(s_0)$ to the cell value, (c) requires $K_-(s_0,s_{\rm cell})\ge\frac{\log N+(4p-5)\log(1/a)}{4p-2}-O(1)$, a necessary condition of order $\log(1/\ell_n)$. The preparation inflow, the retention of the generated $\eta$ after transport and cancellation, and the source-adjoint correlation remain unverified, so this is a size comparison, not a saturating evolution. The budgets $\int\|q\|_{2p}^4dt$ for $p>2$ are not scale invariant (they scale like $\lambda^{6-6/p}$). (4) Since $|q(s)|\le\|q(s_0)\|_\infty e^{2K_-(s_0,s)}$ by the maximum principle for $(\partial_t+b\cdot\nabla-\Delta_5)q=-2\frac{u^r}rq$, (c) is the integrated, $L^{2p}$ form of the elementary fact that $q$ grows only through inward strain.

#### Lemma 7.28 (adjoint representation of the record circulation). Fix $0<s_0<t<T_*$ and $\phi\in C_c^\infty((0,\infty)\times\mathbb R)$ with $0\le\phi\le1$. Let $v$ be the bounded classical solution on $\mathbb R^5\times[s_0,t]$ (functions of $(r,z)$, $r=|y|$, $y\in\mathbb R^4$) of the backward linear problem $\partial_sv+b\cdot\nabla v+2\frac{u^r}rv+\Delta_5v=0$, $v(t)=\phi/r^2$, and put $\psi=r^2v$. Write $w=\omega^\theta$, $P=(u^\theta)^2/r=\Gamma^2r^{-3}$, $Q(s)=\|q(s)\|_\infty$ and $V_\psi(s)=\frac12\sup_{r>0}\int_{\mathbb R}|\partial_z\psi(r,z,s)|dz$. Then:

(a) $0\le\psi\le1$, $\psi$ solves $\partial_s\psi+b\cdot\nabla\psi+\partial_{rr}\psi-\frac1r\partial_r\psi+\partial_{zz}\psi=0$ in $\{r>0\}$, and $\iint\psi(s)\,r\,dr\,dz=\iint\phi\,r\,dr\,dz$ for $s\in[s_0,t]$;

(b) $\displaystyle\iint\phi\,w(t)\,dr\,dz=\iint\psi(s_0)w(s_0)\,dr\,dz-\int_{s_0}^t\!\!\iint P\,\partial_z\psi\,dr\,dz\,ds$, and the same with $s_0$ replaced by any $s_1\in[s_0,t]$;

(c) $\big|\iint P\partial_z\psi(s)\,dr\,dz\big|\le G\,Q(s)\,V_\psi(s)$ and $\big|\iint\psi(s_0)w(s_0)dr\,dz\big|\le\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty\iint\phi\,r\,dr\,dz$;

(d) along every Case 3 sequence, with $t=t_n$ and $\phi_n(r,z)=\chi(|(r,z)-(r^0,z^0)|/\rho_1)$, where $\chi$ is a fixed smooth nonincreasing function, $\chi=1$ on $[0,\frac12]$, $\chi=0$ on $[1,\infty)$ (so $\phi_n=1$ on the concentric disk of radius $\rho_1/2$ and $\operatorname{supp}\phi_n\subset\overline{\mathcal D}$, $\mathcal D$ the disk of Lemma 7.8), and with the sign of $w(t_n)$ on $\mathcal D$ normalized to be positive (if it is negative, apply the reflection $(x_1,x_2,z)\mapsto(x_1,x_2,-z)$, $u\mapsto(u_1,u_2,-u_3)$, a symmetry of the axisymmetric Navier-Stokes equations that preserves $G,M,D,H$ and the records and changes the sign of $\omega^\theta$): for large $n$, $G\int_{s_0}^{t_n}Q(s)V_{\psi_n}(s)ds\ge c_3/\nu_n$, $c_3=27\pi\kappa_0^3/(8192C_*^2)$, while $V_{\psi_n}(t_n)=1$. Moreover, for each fixed $c>0$ and $\tau_n=cr_n/M_n$, either $\iint\psi_n(t_n-\tau_n)w(t_n-\tau_n)dr\,dz\ge c_3/\nu_n$ or $\sup_{[t_n-\tau_n,t_n]}V_{\psi_n}\ge c'(\alpha_n\nu_n^{4/3})^{-1}\to\infty$, with $c'=c_3/(2cG^{4/3}C_*^{2/3})$.

*Proof.* The coefficients $b=(u^ry/|y|,u^z)$ and $u^r/r$ are smooth bounded functions on $\mathbb R^5\times[s_0,t]$ with bounded derivatives (they are smooth functions of $(|y|^2,z)$, times $y$ for the first; proof of Lemma 7.7), and $\phi/r^2$ is smooth with compact support, so the backward problem has a unique bounded classical solution, smooth, with $v$ and its first two derivatives decaying faster than any power at infinity (classical solvability and Gaussian bounds for the fundamental solution of a uniformly parabolic operator with smooth bounded coefficients, A. Friedman, *Partial Differential Equations of Parabolic Type*, Prentice-Hall 1964, Chapter 1); $v\ge0$ by the maximum principle (the constants in these decay bounds depend on $n$; nothing below uses uniformity in $n$). (a) With $\psi=r^2v$: $b\cdot\nabla\psi=r^2b\cdot\nabla v+2ru^rv$ and $\partial_{rr}\psi-\frac1r\partial_r\psi+\partial_{zz}\psi=r^2\Delta_5v$, which gives the equation; $\psi\le1$ by the maximum principle for this equation, which has no zeroth-order term, with $\psi=O(r^2)$ at the axis and terminal datum $\phi\in[0,1]$. For the mass: $\iint\psi r\,dr\,dz=c\int_{\mathbb R^5}v$, and $\frac d{ds}\int_{\mathbb R^5}v=-\int(b\cdot\nabla v+2\frac{u^r}rv+\Delta_5v)=\int v\operatorname{div}_5b-2\int\frac{u^r}rv=0$, since $\operatorname{div}_5b=\partial_ru^r+\frac3ru^r+\partial_zu^z=\frac{2u^r}r$. (b) In $dr\,dz$ the circulation density obeys $\partial_sw=\Delta_2w+\partial_r(w/r)-\operatorname{div}_2(bw)+\partial_zP$ (the $\omega^\theta$ equation, with $\operatorname{div}_2b=-u^r/r$). Then $\frac d{ds}\iint\psi w=\iint w(\partial_s\psi+\Delta_2\psi-\frac1r\partial_r\psi+b\cdot\nabla\psi)-\iint P\partial_z\psi=-\iint P\partial_z\psi$; the boundary terms at $r=0$ vanish because $\psi=O(r^2)$, $\partial_r\psi=O(r)$, $w=O(r)$, $\partial_rw=O(1)$, and those at infinity because of the decay of $v$ and the boundedness of $w$, $\nabla w$, $P$. Integrate over $[s_1,t]$. (c) For fixed $r$ and $s$, $\int\partial_z\psi\,dz=0$ and $0\le P\le\sup_zP=:\bar P(r)$, so $|\int P\partial_z\psi dz|=|\int(P-\frac12\bar P)\partial_z\psi dz|\le\frac12\bar P(r)\int|\partial_z\psi|dz$. Since $\Gamma^2\le\min(G^2,Q^2r^4)$, $\bar P(r)\le\min(G^2r^{-3},Q^2r)$ and $\int_0^\infty\bar P\,dr\le GQ$ (split at $r^2=G/Q$, as in Lemma 7.26(e)). For the second bound, $|w(s_0)|\le\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty r$ and (a). (d) As in Lemma 7.8, $w(t_n)$ has one sign on $\mathcal D$ and $|w(t_n)|\ge\frac38\kappa_0D_n$ there, so $\iint\phi_nw(t_n)\ge\frac38\kappa_0D_n\cdot\frac\pi4\rho_1^2=\frac{27\pi\kappa_0^3}{4096C_*^2}D_n\ell_n^2=2c_3/\nu_n$; and $V_{\psi_n}(t_n)=1$ because $\chi$ is nonincreasing. By (c), $|\iint\psi_n(s_0)w(s_0)|\le\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty\cdot2r_n\pi\rho_1^2=O(r_n\ell_n^2)$, and $r_n\ell_n^2\nu_n=r_n\ell_n/M_n\to0$. So (b) and (c) give the first claim. For the dichotomy apply (b) with $s_1=t_n-\tau_n$: if the first alternative fails, $G\int_{t_n-\tau_n}^{t_n}QV_{\psi_n}\ge c_3/\nu_n$, and $Q(s)\le2G^{1/3}H(s)^{2/3}\le2G^{1/3}H(t_n)^{2/3}\le2G^{1/3}C_*^{2/3}D_n^{4/3}M_n^{-2/3}$ for $s\le t_n$ (Lemma 1.5(ii), monotonicity of the historical $H$, and (A) at $t_n$), so $\sup V_{\psi_n}\ge c_3M_n^{5/3}/(2cG^{4/3}C_*^{2/3}\nu_nD_n^{4/3}r_n)=c'/(\alpha_n\nu_n^{4/3})$; finally $\alpha_n\nu_n^{4/3}\le C\nu_n^{1/3}\log(1/\nu_n)\to0$ (proof of Lemma 7.26(e)). $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) For Lemma 7.28 I rechecked the adjoint computation, the boundary terms, the bound for $\int\bar P\,dr$, and added the mass identity (a), which removes any data decay assumption from the first claim of (d). (2) $\psi_n(s)$ is the backward (ancestor) density of the record core under the meridional flow, with the axis acting as an absorbing boundary for $\psi$ in the sense that $\psi=O(r^2)$ there. The first claim of (d) says that the swirl pressure $P$ must act on this ancestor density, critically weighted, throughout the history. The dichotomy says that, within the long window, the record circulation is either inherited (it was already present, adjoint-weighted, at time $t_n-\tau_n$) or produced by a source acting on an adjoint weight whose vertical variation is unboundedly large. Heuristically this means strong folding of the ancestor set in $z$ (many alternating layers), consistent with Remark (4) after Lemma 7.26; since diffusion is present, the rigorous statement concerns the adjoint weight $\psi_n$, not a material set. (3) On each fixed interval $[s_0,t]$, $\sup_{[s_0,t]}V_\psi<\infty$ by the decay bounds in the proof, with constants depending on $n$. No quantitative upper bound for $V_\psi$ adequate for the Case 3 sequence is established here. In particular a bound of the form $V_\psi\le\exp(\int\|\nabla b\|_\infty ds)$ does not follow from the standard gradient estimates: $\partial_z\psi$ obeys the differentiated equation with the additional source $(\partial_zb^r)\partial_r\psi$, which $V_\psi$ does not control. Already for planar incompressible transport without diffusion, composing the stretch $(x,z)\mapsto(x/L,Lz)$ with the shear $(X,Z)\mapsto(X+L^{-1}\sin(LZ),Z)$ carries a radial cutoff to $\chi\big(((LX-\sin(LZ))^2+(Z/L)^2)^{1/2}\big)$, whose variation along $X=0$, $|Z|\le L/4$, is of order $L^2$, while the generating flows have $\int\|\nabla b\|\,ds=\sqrt2\log L+1$ in the Frobenius norm.

#### Lemma 7.29 (energy of the adjoint weight, and a localized $L^4$ requirement for $q$). In the setting of Lemma 7.28, write $\|f\|_2$ for the $L^2(\mathbb R^3)$ norm of an axisymmetric function ($dx=2\pi r\,dr\,dz$). Then:

(a) for $s\in[s_0,t]$, $\|\psi(s)\|_2^2+2\int_s^t\|\nabla\psi(\sigma)\|_2^2d\sigma=\|\phi\|_2^2$;

(b) for $s_1\in[s_0,t]$ and every measurable $E\subset(0,\infty)\times\mathbb R\times[s_1,t]$, $\big|\int_{s_1}^t\!\iint P\,\partial_z\psi\,1_E\,dr\,dz\,ds\big|^2\le\frac{\|\phi\|_2^2}{8\pi^2}\int_{s_1}^t\!\int q^4\,1_E\,dx\,ds$;

(c) along every Case 3 sequence, with $\phi_n,\psi_n$ as in Lemma 7.28(d), $E_n=\{\partial_z\psi_n\ne0\}$, and every $s_1\in[s_0,t_n]$ with $\iint\psi_n(s_1)w(s_1)\,dr\,dz\le c_3/\nu_n$ (in particular $s_1=s_0$ for large $n$): $\int_{s_1}^{t_n}\!\int q^4\,1_{E_n}\,dx\,ds\ge c_4M_n^2/r_n$, $c_4=81\pi^2\kappa_0^4/(2^{18}C_*^2)$.

*Proof.* (a) With $h=t-s$, $\psi$ solves $\partial_h\psi=b\cdot\nabla\psi+\Delta\psi-\frac2r\partial_r\psi$ ($\Delta$ the three-dimensional Laplacian; $\Delta-\frac2r\partial_r=\partial_{rr}-\frac1r\partial_r+\partial_{zz}$). Multiply by $2\psi$ and integrate over $\mathbb R^3$: the transport term vanishes because $\operatorname{div}b=0$, the Laplacian gives $-2\|\nabla\psi\|_2^2$, and $-4\int\psi\frac1r\partial_r\psi\,dx=-4\pi\iint\partial_r(\psi^2)\,dr\,dz=4\pi\int\psi(0,z)^2dz=0$, since $\psi=O(r^2)$ at the axis. Integration by parts at infinity is justified by the decay of $v=\psi/r^2$ and its derivatives (proof of Lemma 7.28; the constants depend on $n$ but are finite). Integrate in $h$. (b) $P\,dr\,dz=\frac{(u^\theta)^2}r\,dr\,dz=\frac1{2\pi}q^2dx$, so by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in $(x,s)$ and (a), the left side is at most $\frac1{4\pi^2}\int\!\!\int q^41_E\cdot\int_{s_1}^t\|\partial_z\psi\|_2^2\le\frac1{4\pi^2}\cdot\frac12\|\phi\|_2^2\int\!\!\int q^41_E$. (c) By Lemma 7.28(b),(d), $\iint\phi_nw(t_n)\ge2c_3/\nu_n$, so the source term over $[s_1,t_n]$, which is supported in $E_n$, has absolute value at least $c_3/\nu_n$. Since $0\le\phi_n\le1$ is supported in the disk of radius $\rho_1$ about $(r^0,z^0)$ and $r^0+\rho_1\le2r_n$, $\|\phi_n\|_2^2\le2\pi\cdot2r_n\cdot\pi\rho_1^2=4\pi^2r_n\rho_1^2$. Hence (b) gives $\int\!\!\int q^41_{E_n}\ge2c_3^2/(\nu_n^2r_n\rho_1^2)$; insert $\rho_1^2=\frac{9\kappa_0^2}{128C_*^2}\ell_n^2$ and $\nu_n^{-2}\ell_n^{-2}=M_n^4D_n^{-2}\cdot D_n^2M_n^{-2}=M_n^2$, and use $c_3=27\pi\kappa_0^3/(8192C_*^2)$. For $s_1=s_0$ the inherited term is $O(r_n\ell_n^2)=o(1/\nu_n)$ by Lemma 7.28(c),(d). $\square$

*Remark.* (c) has the order of Lemma 7.27 for $p=2$ ($\int\|q\|_4^4\ge2c_2M_n^2/r_n-2\|\eta(s_0)\|_2^2$) but localizes it to the support of $\partial_z\psi_n$, and it holds on every terminal interval $[s_1,t_n]$ on which the record circulation is not inherited. The energy identity (a) was rechecked, including the axis term.

#### Lemma 7.30 (annular depletion of the source pairing). In the setting of Lemma 7.28 fix $s_1\in[s_0,t]$ and $\rho>0$, put $\tau=t-s_1$, $\bar M=\sup_{[s_1,t]}\|u\|_\infty$, $m_\phi=\iint\phi\,r\,dr\,dz$, and let $g(r)=\chi(r/\rho)^2$ with a fixed smooth nondecreasing $\chi$, $\chi=0$ on $[0,\frac12]$, $\chi=1$ on $[1,\infty)$. Then, with a constant $C_\chi$ depending only on $\chi$,

$$\Big|\int_{s_1}^t\!\!\iint g\,P\,\partial_z\psi\,dr\,dz\,ds\Big|^2\le C_\chi\,G^4m_\phi^2\,\tau\rho^{-8}\big(1+\tau\rho^{-2}+\bar M\tau\rho^{-1}\big).\tag{7.30.1}$$

Along every Case 3 sequence, with $\phi_n,\psi_n$ as in Lemma 7.28(d), $R_n=r_n^2D_n$, $\vartheta_n\in(0,\frac12]$, $\lambda_n>0$, any $\tau_n\in(0,\lambda_nr_n^2]$, $s_1=t_n-\tau_n$ and $\rho=\vartheta_nr_n$,
$$\nu_n^2\Big|\int_{s_1}^{t_n}\!\!\iint g\,P\,\partial_z\psi_n\Big|^2\le C\,G^4\Big(\frac{\lambda_n}{\vartheta_n^8R_n^2}+\frac{\lambda_n^2}{\vartheta_n^{10}R_n^2}+\frac{\lambda_n^2}{\vartheta_n^9\alpha_nR_n}\Big),\tag{7.30.3}$$
and the right side tends to zero for fixed $\vartheta$ and $\lambda$, and more generally for fixed $\vartheta$ whenever $\lambda_n=o((\alpha_nR_n)^{1/2})$. In that case the left side of (7.30.1) is $o(\nu_n^{-2})$. Consequently, for large $n$, either $\iint\psi_n(s_1)w(s_1)\,dr\,dz\ge c_3/\nu_n$ (inheritance), or
$$\Big|\int_{s_1}^{t_n}\!\!\iint(1-g)P\,\partial_z\psi_n\,dr\,dz\,ds\Big|\ge(c_3-o(1))/\nu_n\tag{7.30.2}$$
(production at radii $<\vartheta r_n$), and in the second case $\frac2\pi\vartheta^2e^{2K_+}\int_{s_1}^{t_n}\!\int_{\{r<\vartheta r_n\}}|\partial_z(q^2)|\,dx\,ds\ge(c_3-o(1))/\nu_n$, where $K_+=\int_{s_1}^{t_n}\|(u^r/r)_+(s)\|_\infty ds$. The same holds for $\vartheta_n\to0$ and variable $\lambda_n$ whenever the right side of (7.30.3) tends to zero (for fixed $\lambda$, e.g. if $\vartheta_n^5R_n\to\infty$ and $\vartheta_n^9\alpha_nR_n\to\infty$).

*Proof.* Step 1 (a weighted identity). Write $L=\partial_{rr}-\frac1r\partial_r+\partial_{zz}$, so $\partial_s\Gamma+b\cdot\nabla\Gamma=L\Gamma$ and $\partial_s\psi+b\cdot\nabla\psi=-L\psi$. For $f,h$ smooth with the decay and axis behaviour at hand and $m=g/r$: $\iint mf\,Lh\,dr\,dz=-\iint m\nabla f\cdot\nabla h-\iint(m'+\frac mr)f\partial_rh=-\iint m\nabla f\cdot\nabla h-\iint\frac{g'}rf\partial_rh$. Hence $\iint m(2\Gamma\psi L\Gamma-\Gamma^2L\psi)=-2\iint m\psi|\nabla\Gamma|^2-\iint\frac{g'}r\big(\psi\partial_r\Gamma^2-\Gamma^2\partial_r\psi\big)$, and integrating $\frac{g'}r\Gamma^2\partial_r\psi$ by parts in $r$ gives $-2\iint\frac{g'}r\psi\partial_r\Gamma^2-\iint(\frac{g'}r)'\Gamma^2\psi$ for the last integral. For the transport terms, with $F=\Gamma^2\psi$ and $\operatorname{div}_2b=\partial_ru^r+\partial_zu^z=-u^r/r$: $-\iint m\,b\cdot\nabla F=\iint F(m\operatorname{div}_2b+m'u^r)=\iint F\big(-\frac{2g}{r^2}u^r+\frac{g'}ru^r\big)$. Therefore, with $I_g(s)=\iint\Gamma^2\psi\frac gr\,dr\,dz$,
$$I_g'=-2\iint\frac gr\psi|\nabla\Gamma|^2-2\iint\frac{g'}r\psi\partial_r(\Gamma^2)-\iint\big(\tfrac{g'}r\big)'\Gamma^2\psi-2\iint\frac g{r^2}\Gamma^2\psi u^r+\iint\frac{g'}r\Gamma^2\psi u^r .$$
All boundary terms vanish: $g=0$ near the axis, and at infinity $\psi$ decays faster than any power with its derivatives while $\Gamma$, $b$ are bounded and $\nabla\Gamma$ grows at most linearly, $|\nabla\Gamma(r,z,s)|\le\bar M+r\sup_{[s_1,t]}\|\nabla u\|_\infty$.

Step 2 (bounds). All weights are supported in $\{r\ge\rho/2\}$, and $\iint_{\{r\ge\rho/2\}}\psi r^{-k}dr\,dz\le(2/\rho)^{k+1}m_\phi$ for $k\ge-1$, by the mass identity of Lemma 7.28(a) (over the whole half-plane the integral diverges for $k\ge3$ when $\phi\ne0$ and $s<t$, since then $v>0$ on the axis and $\psi=r^2v$); $\Gamma^2\le G^2$, $|u^r|\le\bar M$ (so $(u^r/r)_-\le2\bar M/\rho$ on the support of $g$), and $|g'|\le C\rho^{-1}$, $|g''|\le C\rho^{-2}$, $g'^2/g=4\chi'(r/\rho)^2\rho^{-2}\le C\rho^{-2}$, with $g',g''$ supported in $[\rho/2,\rho]$. Absorb $4|\frac{g'}r\psi\Gamma\partial_r\Gamma|\le\frac gr\psi|\partial_r\Gamma|^2+4\frac{g'^2}{g}\frac{\psi\Gamma^2}r$. Integrating Step 1 over $[s_1,t]$ and dropping $-I_g(t)\le0$,
$$A_g:=\int_{s_1}^t\!\!\iint\frac gr\psi|\nabla\Gamma|^2\le I_g(s_1)+C\int_{s_1}^t\!\!\iint_{\{\rho/2\le r\le\rho\}}\Big(\frac{\psi\Gamma^2}{\rho^2r}+\frac{\psi\Gamma^2}{\rho^3}+\frac{\bar M\psi\Gamma^2}{\rho r}\Big)+2\int_{s_1}^t\!\!\iint\frac gr\Gamma^2\psi\Big(\frac{u^r}r\Big)_-$$
$$\le CG^2m_\phi\big(\rho^{-2}+\tau\rho^{-4}+\bar M\tau\rho^{-3}\big).$$
Also $B_g:=\int_{s_1}^t\iint g\psi\Gamma^2r^{-5}\le64G^2m_\phi\tau\rho^{-6}$. Since $g$ depends only on $r$, $\iint gP\partial_z\psi=-\iint g\psi\partial_zP$ for each $s$, and $\partial_zP=2\Gamma\partial_z\Gamma r^{-3}$; the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality gives $|\int\iint gP\partial_z\psi|\le2A_g^{1/2}B_g^{1/2}$, which is (7.30.1).

Step 3 (Case 3). $m_{\phi_n}\le2\pi r_n\rho_1^2$ and $\rho_1^2=\frac{9\kappa_0^2}{128C_*^2}\ell_n^2$, so $m_{\phi_n}^2\nu_n^2\le Cr_n^2\ell_n^4\nu_n^2=Cr_n^2D_n^{-2}$. Further $\bar M\le M_n$ (historical supremum). With $\tau_n\le\lambda_nr_n^2$ and $\rho=\vartheta_nr_n$, (7.30.1) gives $\nu_n^2|\Pi_g|^2\le CG^4\lambda_n\vartheta_n^{-8}R_n^{-2}(1+\lambda_n\vartheta_n^{-2}+\lambda_n\vartheta_n^{-1}r_nM_n)$, which is (7.30.3) because $r_nM_n=R_n/\alpha_n$. Now $R_n=\alpha_nr_nM_n\to\infty$ and $\alpha_n/R_n=1/(r_nM_n)\to0$; for fixed $\vartheta$ and $\lambda_n=o((\alpha_nR_n)^{1/2})$ the three terms are $o(\alpha_n^{1/2}R_n^{-3/2})$, $o(\alpha_n/R_n)$ and $o(1)$. The record circulation identity of Lemma 7.28(b), $\iint\phi_nw(t_n)\ge2c_3/\nu_n$, then gives the dichotomy. In the second case, $\iint(1-g)P\partial_z\psi_n\,dr\,dz=-\frac1{2\pi}\int(1-g)\psi_n\partial_z(q^2)dx$, with $1-g$ supported in $\{r<\vartheta r_n\}$, and there $\psi_n=r^2v_n\le r^2\|v_n(t_n)\|_\infty e^{2K_+}\le4\vartheta^2e^{2K_+}$, since $v_n$ obeys the backward maximum principle with the zeroth-order coefficient $2u^r/r$, and $\|v_n(t_n)\|_\infty=\|\phi_n/r^2\|_\infty\le4/r_n^2$ ($\phi_n$ is supported in $r\ge r^0-\rho_1\ge r_n/2$). $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) The threshold is far beyond the core and viscous scales: in the parametrization $M=N/a$, $D=N/a^2$, $r=Na$ of 8.3, $R=r^2D=N^3$ and $\alpha=N$, and for fixed $\vartheta$ the annular part is negligible over every window of length $o(r_n^2(\alpha_nR_n)^{1/2})=o(r_n^{7/2}D_nM_n^{-1/2})$, i.e. $o(N^4a^2)$, much longer than $r_n^2=N^2a^2$. (The cruder estimate $K_-\le D_n\tau$ of the inward-strain term gives only the shorter scale $r_n^3D_n^{1/2}$; the bound $(u^r/r)_-\le2\bar M/\rho$ on the support of $g$ improves it.) The cruder bound obtained from $|\partial_zP|\le2GD_n/r^2$ and the adjoint mass excludes annular production only on windows shorter than $c\vartheta^3r_n^2/G$. (2) Lemma 7.30 is the source analogue of the axis alternative of Theorem 7.12: over any window of length $O(r_n^2)$, a record circulation that is not already present (adjoint-weighted) at the beginning of the window must be produced at radii $o(r_n)$, where the adjoint weight is at most $4\vartheta^2e^{2K_+}$. It does not exclude the formal scenario of 8.3 (vi), whose swirl cells sit at radii $\asymp a=r_n/N$. (3) Over the whole history ($\tau\asymp1$) the bound (7.30.1) is useless.

#### Lemma 7.31 (dissipative depletion of annular inheritance). In the setting of Lemma 7.28, with $\rho$, $g$ and $m_\phi$ as in Lemma 7.30, and $0<\tau\le t-s_0$,
$$\int_{t-\tau}^{t-\tau/2}\Big|\iint g\,\psi(s)\,w(s)\,dr\,dz\Big|^2ds\le C_0\,m_\phi^2\,\tau^{-3/2}\rho^{-2}\int_{t-\tau}^{t}\|\nabla u(s)\|_2^2ds,\tag{7.31.1}$$
with an absolute constant $C_0$; the last integral is at most $E_0/2$ by the energy inequality.

*Proof.* If $\phi\equiv0$ then $\psi\equiv0$ and there is nothing to prove; assume $m_\phi>0$. Nash's inequality in $\mathbb R^3$, $\|f\|_2^{10/3}\le C_N^2\|\nabla f\|_2^2\|f\|_1^{4/3}$, applies to $\psi(s)$, and $\|\psi(s)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}=2\pi m_\phi$ for all $s$ (Lemma 7.28(a) and $\psi\ge0$). By Lemma 7.29(a), $y(h)=\|\psi(t-h)\|_2^2$ is absolutely continuous with $y'=-2\|\nabla\psi(t-h)\|_2^2\le-2C_N^{-2}(2\pi m_\phi)^{-4/3}y^{5/3}$, so $(y^{-2/3})'\ge\frac43C_N^{-2}(2\pi m_\phi)^{-4/3}$ and $\|\psi(t-h)\|_2^2\le(3C_N^2/(4h))^{3/2}(2\pi m_\phi)^2$. For $s\in[t-\tau,t-\tau/2]$, $h=t-s\ge\tau/2$. Next, $\iint g\psi w\,dr\,dz=\frac1{2\pi}\int g\psi\eta\,dx$ and $|g\eta|\le\frac2\rho|\omega^\theta|\le\frac2\rho|\omega|$, because $0\le g\le1$ vanishes for $r<\rho/2$; since $\|\omega\|_2=\|\nabla u\|_2$, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality gives $|\iint g\psi w|\le\frac1{\pi\rho}\|\psi(s)\|_2\|\nabla u(s)\|_2$. Square, insert the decay bound and integrate over $[t-\tau,t-\tau/2]$. $\square$

#### Corollary 7.32 (near-axis origin of the record circulation). Along a Case 3 sequence, with $\phi_n,\psi_n$ as in Lemma 7.28(d) and $R_n=r_n^2D_n$, fix $\vartheta\in(0,\frac12]$ and $\epsilon>0$, and let $\lambda_n>0$ satisfy
$$\lambda_n=o\big((\alpha_nR_n)^{1/2}\big)\qquad\text{and}\qquad \frac{\lambda_n^{5/2}r_n^5D_n^2}{e_n}\to\infty,\qquad e_n=\int_{t_n-\lambda_nr_n^2}^{t_n}\|\nabla u(s)\|_2^2ds.\tag{7.32.0}$$
Write $K_+(s_1,t_n)=\int_{s_1}^{t_n}\|(u^r/r)_+(s)\|_\infty ds$, $\mathcal A_n=\{r<\vartheta r_n\}$, $B_{\mathcal A_n}(s_1,t_n)=\int_{s_1}^{t_n}\|\partial_z(q^2)(s)\|_{L^1(\mathcal A_n)}ds$ and $\mathcal E_n(s)=\int_{\mathcal A_n}(|\eta(x,s)|-B_0)_+dx$, with $B_0=\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty$ ($\mathcal E_n(s)\le\mathcal B(s_0,s)<\infty$ by Lemma 7.26(a), whereas $\|\eta(s)\|_{L^1(\mathcal A_n)}$ may be infinite). Then for large $n$
$$\frac2\pi\,\vartheta^2e^{2K_+(s_1,t_n)}\Big(\mathcal E_n(s_1)+B_{\mathcal A_n}(s_1,t_n)\Big)\ge\frac{2c_3-\epsilon}{\nu_n}\tag{7.32.1}$$
for all $s_1\in[t_n-\lambda_nr_n^2,\,t_n-\frac12\lambda_nr_n^2]$ outside an exceptional set of measure $o(\lambda_nr_n^2)$. The first condition in (7.32.0) holds for every fixed $\lambda$; if $\liminf_nr_n^5D_n^2>0$, every fixed $\lambda$ satisfies (7.32.0), because $e_n\to0$.

*Proof.* Put $\tau_n=\lambda_nr_n^2$ and $\rho=\vartheta r_n$. The windows shrink to $T_*$, since $\tau_n=o(r_n^{7/2}D_nM_n^{-1/2})$ and $r_n^{7/2}D_nM_n^{-1/2}=(r_n^2M_n)\,\nu_n\,(r_n^3M_n)^{1/2}\to0$ by 8.1 (iv); so they lie in $[s_0,t_n]$ for large $n$. (i) Annular source. For every $s_1$ in the window, $t_n-s_1\le\tau_n$, and (7.30.3) with fixed $\vartheta$ gives $\nu_n|\Pi_g(s_1)|\to0$ uniformly, where $\Pi_g(s_1)=\int_{s_1}^{t_n}\iint gP\partial_z\psi_n$. (ii) Annular inheritance. By (7.31.1) with $\tau=\tau_n$ and Chebyshev's inequality, the set of $s_1\in[t_n-\tau_n,t_n-\tau_n/2]$ with $|\iint g\psi_n(s_1)w(s_1)|\ge\epsilon/(2\nu_n)$ has measure at most $4\epsilon^{-2}\nu_n^2C_0m_{\phi_n}^2\tau_n^{-3/2}\rho^{-2}e_n\le C\epsilon^{-2}\vartheta^{-2}e_n\lambda_n^{-3/2}r_n^{-3}D_n^{-2}$, using $m_{\phi_n}^2\nu_n^2\le Cr_n^2D_n^{-2}$ (Lemma 7.30, Step 3); its ratio to $\tau_n$ is $C\epsilon^{-2}\vartheta^{-2}e_n/(\lambda_n^{5/2}r_n^5D_n^2)\to0$. (iii) Off this set, Lemma 7.28(b),(d) gives
$$\frac{2c_3}{\nu_n}\le\iint\phi_nw(t_n)=\iint g\psi_nw(s_1)+\iint(1-g)\psi_nw(s_1)-\Pi_g(s_1)-\Pi_{1-g}(s_1),$$
so $|\iint(1-g)\psi_nw(s_1)|+|\Pi_{1-g}(s_1)|\ge(2c_3-\epsilon/2)/\nu_n$ for large $n$ (run steps (i), (ii) with $\epsilon/4$). Finally $1-g$ is supported in $\mathcal A_n$, where $\psi_n(s)\le4\vartheta^2e^{2K_+(s,t_n)}\le4\vartheta^2e^{2K_+(s_1,t_n)}$ for $s\ge s_1$ (Lemma 7.30, Step 3), $|\iint(1-g)\psi_nw\,dr\,dz|\le\frac1{2\pi}\int(1-g)\psi_n(B_0+(|\eta|-B_0)_+)\,dx\le B_0m_{\phi_n}+\frac2\pi\vartheta^2e^{2K_+(s_1,t_n)}\mathcal E_n(s_1)$ with $\nu_nB_0m_{\phi_n}\le CB_0r_n/D_n\to0$, and $\Pi_{1-g}=-\frac1{2\pi}\int_{s_1}^{t_n}\!\int(1-g)\psi_n\partial_z(q^2)\,dx\,ds$. The last claim: for fixed $\lambda$, $\lambda^2/(\alpha_nR_n)\to0$, and $e_n\to0$ because $\|\nabla u\|_2^2\in L^1(0,T_*)$. $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) Condition (7.32.0) involves only scalar sequences. Put $\widehat L_n=(\alpha_nR_n)^{1/2}$, $A_n=\widehat L_n^{5/2}r_n^5D_n^2=r_n^{35/4}D_n^{9/2}M_n^{-5/4}$ and $\bar e_n=\int_{t_n-\widehat L_nr_n^2}^{t_n}\|\nabla u\|_2^2ds\to0$. If $\liminf_nA_n>0$ (more generally if $A_n/\bar e_n\to\infty$), then $\lambda_n=\widehat L_n(\bar e_n/A_n)^{1/5}$ is admissible, since $\lambda_n=o(\widehat L_n)$ and $\lambda_n^{5/2}r_n^5D_n^2/e_n\ge(A_n/\bar e_n)^{1/2}\to\infty$. In the parametrization $M=N/a$, $D=N/a^2$, $r=Na$ of 8.3 (vi), $r^5D^2=N^7a$, $A_n=N^{12}a$ and $\widehat L_nr_n^2=N^4a^2$, so the corollary applies if $\liminf N^{12}a>0$, and for every fixed $\lambda$ if $\liminf N^7a>0$ (a condition such as $N^7a\not\to0$ only gives this along a subsequence). In the regime where $a$ is smaller than every power of $1/N$ these unconditional sufficient tests give no conclusion; the corollary may still apply there if the dissipation $e_n$ on the window is small enough, but no estimate proved here guarantees that. No restriction of 8.1 excludes that regime. (2) Meaning (take $0<\epsilon<2c_3$): on admissible windows (of length $\gtrsim r_n^2$ when $\inf_n\lambda_n>0$) before the record, at most times the record circulation $\asymp1/\nu_n$ must already sit, adjoint-weighted, at radii $<\vartheta r_n$ as excess vorticity above the level $B_0$, or be produced there afterwards, with the hitting factor $\vartheta^2e^{2K_+}$. In particular, if the near-axis critical budget $\mathcal E_n(s_1)+B_{\mathcal A_n}(s_1,t_n)$ is $O(1/\nu_n)$, then $K_+(s_1,t_n)\ge\log(1/\vartheta)-C$. With $\vartheta=\vartheta_n\to0$ the same argument works whenever the right side of (7.30.3) tends to zero and $\vartheta_n^2\lambda_n^{5/2}r_n^5D_n^2/e_n\to\infty$, and then a budget $O(1/\nu_n)$ forces $K_+(s_1,t_n)\to\infty$. This is a necessary condition of the type of the axis alternative of Theorem 7.12, now for the critical circulation. It is compatible, as a scalar comparison, with the formal scenario of 8.3 (vi), whose circulation is made at radii $\asymp a=r_n\nu_n$ and carried outward; no adjoint solution for that scenario has been constructed. (3) The near-axis quantities in (7.32.1) have no a priori upper bound; Lemma 7.26(a) controls them only through the full source budget. (4) In the proof, $\Pi_{1-g}$ and the inherited term may be estimated with the sharper weight, valid on $\{r<\vartheta r_n\}$ (not globally: $\psi_n(t_n)=1$ where $\phi_n=1$), $\psi_n(s)\le\beta_n(s)=\min\{1,4\vartheta^2e^{2K_+(s,t_n)},C_\psi\mu_n(t_n-s)^{-3/2}\}$ (Lemma 7.33(a)), which gives $\frac1{2\pi}[\beta_n(s_1)\mathcal E_n(s_1)+\int_{s_1}^{t_n}\beta_n(s)\|\partial_z(q^2)(s)\|_{L^1(\mathcal A_n)}ds]\ge(2c_3-\epsilon)/\nu_n$ at the same times. Since $\beta_n(s_1)\le C\lambda_n^{-3/2}\alpha_n^{-2}$ on the window, if the subsequent near-axis production is $o(1/\nu_n)$ then $\mathcal E_n(s_1)\ge c\lambda_n^{3/2}\alpha_n^2/\nu_n$.

#### Lemma 7.33 (pointwise decay of the adjoint weight; cost of inheritance at one earlier time). In the setting of Lemma 7.28 put $\mu=\int_{\mathbb R^3}\phi\,dx=2\pi m_\phi$ and $I(s)=\iint\psi(s)w(s)\,dr\,dz=\frac1{2\pi}\int\psi(s)\eta(s)\,dx$. Then:

(a) there is an absolute constant $C_\psi$ with $\|\psi(t-h)\|_\infty\le A(h):=\min\{1,C_\psi\mu h^{-3/2}\}$ for $0<h\le t-s_0$;

(b) with $B_0=\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty$ and $\mathcal B(s_0,s)$ as in Lemma 7.26, $|I(t-h)|\le\frac1{2\pi}\big(B_0\mu+A(h)\,\mathcal B(s_0,t-h)\big)$;

(c) along every Case 3 sequence, with $\phi_n,\psi_n$ as in Lemma 7.28(d) and fixed $\lambda>0$: if $I(t_n-\lambda r_n^2)\ge\epsilon/\nu_n$ for some fixed $\epsilon>0$, then $\mathcal B(s_0,t_n-\lambda r_n^2)\ge c\,\epsilon\,\lambda^{3/2}r_n^2D_n=c\,\epsilon\,\lambda^{3/2}\alpha_n^2/\nu_n$ for large $n$, with $c=c(C_*,\kappa_0)>0$.

*Proof.* If $\phi\equiv0$ all assertions are immediate; assume $\mu>0$. (a) The bound $\|\psi\|_\infty\le1$ is Lemma 7.28(a). With $h=t-s$, $\psi$ solves $\partial_h\psi=b\cdot\nabla\psi+\Delta\psi-\frac2r\partial_r\psi$ ($\Delta$ the three-dimensional Laplacian). Write $\psi(t-h)=S_h\phi$ and, for $0<h'<h$, $S_h=S_{h',h}S_{h'}$ (the solution operator from age $h'$ to age $h$). Step 1 ($L^1\to L^2$). For nonnegative data the $L^1(\mathbb R^3)$ norm is conserved (Lemma 7.28(a)), and $\frac d{dh}\|\psi\|_2^2=-2\|\nabla\psi\|_2^2$ (Lemma 7.29(a)); Nash's inequality $\|f\|_2^{10/3}\le C_N^2\|\nabla f\|_2^2\|f\|_1^{4/3}$ gives, as in the proof of Lemma 7.31, $\|S_{h'}\phi\|_2\le C\mu h'^{-3/4}$. Step 2 ($L^2\to L^\infty$ by duality). For a smooth compactly supported axisymmetric scalar function $f_0$ (rotational averaging preserves the pairing with $\psi$ and does not increase $\|f_0\|_1$, so these suffice for the duality) and an age $h'<h$, let $f$ solve the forward problem $\partial_sf+b\cdot\nabla f=\Delta f+\frac2r\partial_rf=\Delta_5f$ on $[t-h,t-h']$, $f(t-h)=f_0$; this is a uniformly parabolic equation in $\mathbb R^5$ with smooth bounded coefficients, so $f$ is smooth, regular at the axis ($\partial_rf=O(r)$), decays at infinity, and $f\ge0$ if $f_0\ge0$. A direct computation, using $\operatorname{div}b=0$ in $\mathbb R^3$ and $\psi=O(r^2)$ at the axis (so the axis term $-4\pi\int\psi f\,dz$ produced by $\int\psi\frac2r\partial_rf\,dx=4\pi\iint\psi\partial_rf\,dr\,dz$ vanishes), gives $\frac d{ds}\int\psi(s)f(s)\,dx=0$. For $f\ge0$, $\frac d{ds}\int f\,dx=\int\frac2r\partial_rf\,dx=-4\pi\int f(0,z,s)dz\le0$; for general $f_0$ split $f_0=f_0^+-f_0^-$, so $\|f(s)\|_1\le\|f_0\|_1$. Also $\frac d{ds}\|f\|_2^2=-2\|\nabla f\|_2^2-4\pi\int f(0,z,s)^2dz\le-2\|\nabla f\|_2^2$. Nash's inequality then gives $\|f(t-h')\|_2\le C\|f_0\|_1(h-h')^{-3/4}$, and by duality $\big|\int\psi(t-h)f_0\big|=\big|\int\psi(t-h')f(t-h')\big|\le C\|\psi(t-h')\|_2\|f_0\|_1(h-h')^{-3/4}$. Taking the supremum over $\|f_0\|_1\le1$ and $h'=h/2$, Step 1 gives $\|\psi(t-h)\|_\infty\le C\mu(h/2)^{-3/4}(h/2)^{-3/4}$. The integrations by parts at infinity are justified by the decay of $v=\psi/r^2$ (proof of Lemma 7.28) and of $f$. (b) $|\eta|\le B_0+(|\eta|-B_0)_+$, $\psi\ge0$, $\int\psi=\mu$, and Lemma 7.26(a): $2\pi|I(t-h)|\le B_0\mu+\|\psi(t-h)\|_\infty\int(|\eta(t-h)|-B_0)_+dx\le B_0\mu+A(h)\mathcal B(s_0,t-h)$. (c) By the proof of Lemma 7.29(c), $\mu_n=2\pi m_{\phi_n}\le4\pi^2r_n\rho_1^2\le Cr_n\ell_n^2$, so $B_0\mu_n\nu_n\le Cr_n\ell_n^2\nu_n=Cr_n/D_n\to0$ and $A(\lambda r_n^2)\le C\lambda^{-3/2}\ell_n^2r_n^{-2}=C\lambda^{-3/2}\alpha_n^{-2}$. Insert into (b). Finally $\alpha_n^2/\nu_n=r_n^2D_n$. $\square$

#### Lemma 7.34 (cost of inheritance throughout a window). Along a Case 3 sequence, with $\phi_n,\psi_n,\mu_n,I_n=I$ as in Lemma 7.33, fix $s_0\in(0,T_*)$ and $\epsilon>0$ independently of $n$, let $0<\tau_n\le t_n-s_0$, and $E_n=\{h\in[\tau_n/2,\tau_n]:|I(t_n-h)|\ge\epsilon/\nu_n\}$. Then:

(a) $|E_n|/\tau_n\le C\epsilon^{-2}\,(\mathcal W_n/D_n)\,r_n^2D_n^{-1}\tau_n^{-5/2}$, where $\mathcal W_n=\int_0^{t_n}\|\nabla\omega(s)\|_2^2ds$ satisfies $\mathcal W_n/D_n\to0$. In particular, if $\tau_n=\lambda r_n^2$ with fixed $\lambda$ and $\liminf_nr_n^3D_n>0$, then $|E_n|=o(\tau_n)$;

(b) if $|E_n|\ge\delta\tau_n$ for a fixed $\delta>0$, then $K_+(t_n-\tau_n,t_n)=\int_{t_n-\tau_n}^{t_n}\|(u^r/r)_+\|_\infty ds\ge\frac12\log\frac{\tau_n}{\ell_n^2\Lambda_n}-C$, where $\Lambda_n=1+\log(2+r_n^2/\tau_n+M_n^2\tau_n)$ and $C=C(\epsilon,\delta,C_*,\kappa_0)$. For $\tau_n=\lambda r_n^2$ with fixed $\lambda$ this reads $K_+\ge\log\alpha_n-\frac12\log(1+\log(r_nM_n))-C_\lambda$, which tends to infinity if $\alpha_n^2/(1+\log(r_nM_n))\to\infty$ (without this hypothesis the right side may tend to $-\infty$ under the scalar restrictions of 8.1, e.g. $\nu_n=e^{-n}$, $\alpha_n=\log n$, $M_n=e^{4n}$, $D_n=e^{7n}$).

*Proof.* (a) For $h\in E_n$, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Lemma 7.31's bound $\|\psi_n(t_n-h)\|_2\le C\mu_nh^{-3/4}$ give $\epsilon/\nu_n\le\frac1{2\pi}\|\psi_n\|_2\|\eta(t_n-h)\|_2$, so $\|\eta(t_n-h)\|_2^2\ge c\epsilon^2h^{3/2}\nu_n^{-2}\mu_n^{-2}\ge c\epsilon^2h^{3/2}D_n^2r_n^{-2}$ (since $\nu_n\mu_n\le Cr_n/D_n$). For an axisymmetric field, $|\nabla\omega|^2\ge|\frac1r\partial_\theta\omega|^2=((\omega^r)^2+(\omega^\theta)^2)/r^2$, so $\|\eta\|_2\le\|\nabla\omega\|_2$. Integrating over $E_n$, $|E_n|\,c\epsilon^2(\tau_n/2)^{3/2}D_n^2r_n^{-2}\le \mathcal W_n$. For $\mathcal W_n$: with $Z=\|\omega\|_2^2=\|\nabla u\|_2^2$, the vorticity equation gives $\frac12Z'+\|\nabla\omega\|_2^2=\int\omega\cdot\nabla u\,\omega\le\|\nabla u\|_\infty Z$, so for any fixed $t'<t_n$, $\mathcal W_n\le\frac12Z(0)+D(t')\int_0^{t'}Z+D_n\int_{t'}^{T_*}Z$; dividing by $D_n\to\infty$ and letting $t'\to T_*$ (note $\int_0^{T_*}Z\le E_0/2$) gives $\mathcal W_n/D_n\to0$. For $\tau_n=\lambda r_n^2$, $r_n^2D_n^{-1}\tau_n^{-5/2}=\lambda^{-5/2}(r_n^3D_n)^{-1}$. (b) Put $p_h=\psi_n(t_n-h)/\mu_n$, a probability density on $\mathbb R^3$, $\mathcal F(h)=\int|\nabla p_h|^2/p_h\,dx$. First, $|I|=|\iint\psi(\partial_zu^r-\partial_ru^z)dr\,dz|=|\iint(u^z\partial_r\psi-u^r\partial_z\psi)dr\,dz|\le M_n\iint|\nabla\psi|dr\,dz$ (the axis term $\psi u^z|_{r=0}$ vanishes), and $\iint|\nabla\psi|dr\,dz=\frac{\mu_n}{2\pi}\int\frac{|\nabla p|}r\,dx\le\frac{\mu_n}{2\pi}\mathcal F^{1/2}\big(\int p\,r^{-2}dx\big)^{1/2}$. Next, $j(h)=\int p_hr^{-2}dx=\mu_n^{-1}\int v_n(t_n-h)\,dx$ with $v_n=\psi_n/r^2$, and $v_n$ solves $\partial_hv=b\cdot\nabla v+\Delta_5v+2\frac{u^r}rv$; integrating over $\mathbb R^3$ (transport term zero, $\int\Delta_5v\,dx=-4\pi\int v(0,z)dz\le0$) gives $j'\le2\|(u^r/r)_+\|_\infty j$ and $j(0)\le4r_n^{-2}$ ($\phi_n$ is supported in $r\ge r_n/2$), so $j(h)\le4r_n^{-2}e^{2K_+(t_n-\tau_n,t_n)}$ for $h\le\tau_n$. With $\mu_nM_n\le Cr_n\ell_n^2M_n=Cr_n\ell_n/\nu_n$ we get, for $h\in E_n$, $\epsilon/\nu_n\le C\ell_n\nu_n^{-1}e^{K_+}\mathcal F(h)^{1/2}$, i.e. $\mathcal F(h)\ge c\epsilon^2\ell_n^{-2}e^{-2K_+}$. Third, the entropy budget: $\frac d{dh}\int p\log p\,dx=-\mathcal F(h)$ (the transport term integrates to zero, and the axis term $4\pi\int(p\log p)(0,z)dz$ vanishes because $p=O(r^2)$; regularize with $H_\varepsilon(p)=(p+\varepsilon)\log(p+\varepsilon)-\varepsilon\log\varepsilon$, which vanishes at $p=0$, so $\int H_\varepsilon(p)\,dx$ is finite and $\frac d{dh}\int H_\varepsilon(p)\,dx=-\int|\nabla p|^2/(p+\varepsilon)\,dx$, then let $\varepsilon\downarrow0$). The upper bound $\int p_{\tau/2}\log p_{\tau/2}\le\log\|p_{\tau/2}\|_\infty\le\log(C_\psi(\tau_n/2)^{-3/2})$ is Lemma 7.33(a). For the lower bound, the second moment $V(h)=\int(r^2+(z-z^0)^2)p_h\,dx$ about the axis point $(0,0,z^0)$ satisfies $V'=\int p\,(-2b\cdot(x-x^0)+6+4)\,dx\le10+2M_n\sqrt V$ (the last $4$ is $-\int\frac2r\partial_rp\,(r^2+(z-z^0)^2)dx=4\int p\,dx$, the axis term vanishing), and $V(0)\le5r_n^2$; comparison with the supersolution $(\sqrt{5}r_n+M_nh+\sqrt{10h})^2$ gives $V(\tau_n)\le C(r_n^2+\tau_n+M_n^2\tau_n^2)$, and the Gaussian entropy bound gives $\int p_{\tau}\log p_{\tau}\ge-\frac32\log(2\pi eV(\tau_n)/3)$. Hence $\int_{\tau_n/2}^{\tau_n}\mathcal F\le C\Lambda_n$, and with the lower bound on $E_n$: $\delta\tau_nc\epsilon^2\ell_n^{-2}e^{-2K_+}\le C\Lambda_n$. For $\tau_n=\lambda r_n^2$: $\tau_n/\ell_n^2=\lambda\alpha_n^2$ and $\Lambda_n\le C_\lambda+2\log(1+r_nM_n)$. $\square$

#### Lemma 7.35 (the adjoint-weighted swirl identity). In the setting of Lemma 7.28 write $U=u^\theta$, $\sigma=u^r/r$, and let $c(r)$ be either $c\equiv1$ or $c=\cos^2\beta(r/\rho)$, where $\rho>0$ and $\beta$ is a fixed smooth nondecreasing function with $\beta=0$ on $[0,\frac12]$, $\beta=\frac\pi2$ on $[1,\infty)$ (so $1-c=g$ is an admissible cutoff for Lemma 7.30, with $\chi=\sin\beta$). Put $J_c(s)=\iint c\,\psi U^2dr\,dz$ and $F_c(s_1,t)=\int_{s_1}^t\iint c\,\psi\,\sigma_-U^2dr\,dz\,ds$. Then:

(a) for $c\equiv1$: $\displaystyle J'+\iint\psi\big(2(\partial_rU)^2+2(\partial_zU)^2+U^2/r^2\big)dr\,dz=-3\iint\sigma\psi U^2dr\,dz$;

(b) for every $s_1\in[s_0,t]$, with $\tau=t-s_1$, $\bar M=\sup_{[s_1,t]}\|u\|_\infty$ and a constant $C_\beta$ depending only on $\beta$ ($C_\beta=0$ for $c\equiv1$),
$$\sqrt2\Big|\int_{s_1}^t\!\!\iint c\,P\,\partial_z\psi\,dr\,dz\,ds\Big|\le J_c(s_1)+3F_c(s_1,t)+C_\beta G^2m_\phi\tau(\rho^{-5}+\bar M\rho^{-4});\tag{7.35.1}$$

(c) along every Case 3 sequence, with $\phi_n,\psi_n$ as in Lemma 7.28(d), $F_n=F_1(s_0,t_n)=\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\iint\psi_n\sigma_-(u^\theta)^2dr\,dz\,ds\ge(\frac{2\sqrt2}3c_3-o(1))/\nu_n$; consequently any bound $\limsup_n\nu_nF_n<\frac{2\sqrt2}3c_3$ excludes Case 3;

(d) along every Case 3 sequence with $\liminf_nr_n^5D_n^2>0$, for fixed $\vartheta\in(0,\frac12]$, $\lambda>0$, $\epsilon>0$, $\rho=\vartheta r_n$ and $c=\cos^2\beta(r/\rho)$: for all $s_1\in[t_n-\lambda r_n^2,t_n-\frac12\lambda r_n^2]$ outside a set of measure $o(r_n^2)$,
$$\Big|\iint c\,\psi_nw(s_1)\,dr\,dz\Big|+\frac{J_c(s_1)+3F_c(s_1,t_n)}{\sqrt2}\ge\frac{2c_3-\epsilon}{\nu_n},$$
with $|\iint c\psi_nw(s_1)|\le B_0m_{\phi_n}+\frac2\pi\vartheta^2e^{2K_+(s_1,t_n)}\mathcal E_n(s_1)$ ($\mathcal E_n$ as in Corollary 7.32, $\nu_nB_0m_{\phi_n}\to0$) and $J_c(s_1)\le\frac2\pi\vartheta^2e^{2K_+(s_1,t_n)}\int_{\{r<\vartheta r_n\}}(u^\theta)^2r^{-1}dx$.

*Proof.* (a), (b). For a radial weight $m(r)$ and $F=\Gamma^2$, using $\partial_s\Gamma+b\cdot\nabla\Gamma=L\Gamma$, $\partial_s\psi+b\cdot\nabla\psi=-L\psi$ and $\operatorname{div}_2b=-u^r/r$, integration by parts gives, with $b_m=m'+m/r$,
$$\frac d{ds}\iint m\psi\Gamma^2=-2\iint m\psi|\nabla\Gamma|^2-4\iint b_m\psi\Gamma\partial_r\Gamma-\iint b_m'\psi\Gamma^2+\iint(m'-\tfrac mr)u^r\psi\Gamma^2$$
(the transport term is $-\iint m\,b\cdot\nabla(\psi\Gamma^2)=\iint\psi\Gamma^2(m\operatorname{div}_2b+m'u^r)$; the $\partial_z$ parts of $L$ give $-2\iint m\psi(\partial_z\Gamma)^2$; the $r$ parts give $-2\iint m\psi(\partial_r\Gamma)^2-4\iint b_m\psi\Gamma\partial_r\Gamma-\iint b_m'\psi\Gamma^2$ after two integrations by parts; this is Step 1 of Lemma 7.30 for $m=g/r$). Take $m=c/r^2$ and substitute $\Gamma=rU$: then $b_m=c'r^{-2}-cr^{-3}$, $b_m'=c''r^{-2}-3c'r^{-3}+3cr^{-4}$, $m'-m/r=c'r^{-2}-3cr^{-3}$, and a direct expansion gives
$$J_c'=-\iint c\psi\big(2U_r^2+2U_z^2+U^2r^{-2}\big)-3\iint c\,\sigma\psi U^2-4\iint c'\psi UU_r-\iint(c''+c'/r)\psi U^2+\iint c'u^r\psi U^2 .$$
All boundary terms vanish: at the axis each boundary product is $O(r)$ or smaller, because $\Gamma,\psi=O(r^2)$, $\partial_r\Gamma,\partial_r\psi=O(r)$ and $m=O(r^{-2})$; at infinity by the decay of $\psi$ and its derivatives. For $c\equiv1$ this is (a). In general, $4|c'UU_r|\le2cU_r^2+2(c')^2U^2/c$, and $(c')^2/c=4\sin^2\beta\,\beta'^2\rho^{-2}\le C\rho^{-2}$, $|c''|\le C\rho^{-2}$, $|c'|\le C\rho^{-1}$, all supported in $\rho/2\le r\le\rho$, where $U^2\le4G^2\rho^{-2}$ and $\iint_{\{\rho/2\le r\le\rho\}}\psi\,dr\,dz\le\frac2\rho\iint\psi\,r\,dr\,dz=2m_\phi/\rho$ by Lemma 7.28(a) (over the whole half-plane no such bound holds). Integrating over $[s_1,t]$ and dropping $J_c(t)\ge0$,
$$\int_{s_1}^t\!\!\iint c\psi\big(2U_z^2+U^2r^{-2}\big)\le J_c(s_1)+3F_c(s_1,t)+C_\beta G^2m_\phi\tau(\rho^{-5}+\bar M\rho^{-4}).$$
Finally $P=U^2/r$ and $c$ is radial, so $\iint cP\partial_z\psi=-2\iint c\psi UU_z/r$, and $2\sqrt2|U_z||U|/r\le2U_z^2+U^2/r^2$; this gives (7.35.1). (c) Take $c\equiv1$, $s_1=s_0$, $t=t_n$ in (7.35.1) and use Lemma 7.28(b),(d): $2c_3/\nu_n\le|I(s_0)|+(J(s_0)+3F_n)/\sqrt2$. Here $|I(s_0)|\le B_0m_{\phi_n}$ and, with $Q_0=\|q(s_0)\|_\infty$, $U(s_0)^2/r=\Gamma^2r^{-3}\le\min(G^2r^{-3},Q_0^2r)\le G^{1/2}Q_0^{3/2}$, so $J(s_0)\le G^{1/2}Q_0^{3/2}m_{\phi_n}$; both are $o(1/\nu_n)$ since $\nu_nm_{\phi_n}\le Cr_n/D_n\to0$. (d) By Lemma 7.28(b),(d), $2c_3/\nu_n\le I(s_1)-\Pi_g(s_1)-\Pi_c(s_1)$, with $g=1-c$ and $\Pi_h(s_1)=\int_{s_1}^{t_n}\iint hP\partial_z\psi_n$. Since $\liminf r_n^5D_n^2>0$, Corollary 7.32 applies for fixed $\lambda$: $\nu_n|\Pi_g(s_1)|\to0$ uniformly on the window, and $\nu_n|\iint g\psi_nw(s_1)|\le\epsilon/2$ outside a set of measure $o(r_n^2)$ (proof of Corollary 7.32, steps (i), (ii)). Apply (7.35.1) to $\Pi_c$: its error term times $\nu_n$ is at most $CG^2\lambda(\vartheta^{-5}(r_n^2D_n)^{-1}+\vartheta^{-4}\alpha_n^{-1})\to0$, using $\nu_nm_{\phi_n}\le Cr_n/D_n$. The pointwise bounds follow from $c\le1_{\{r<\vartheta r_n\}}$, $\psi_n(s_1)\le4\vartheta^2e^{2K_+(s_1,t_n)}$ there (Lemma 7.30, Step 3), $|w|\,dr\,dz\le\frac1{2\pi}(B_0+(|\eta|-B_0)_+)dx$ and $\iint U^2dr\,dz=\frac1{2\pi}\int U^2r^{-1}dx$. $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) For Lemmas 7.33-7.35 I rechecked each step: the duality argument of Lemma 7.33(a) (including the sign of the axis terms of the dual five-dimensional evolution), the reduction of Lemma 7.33(b) to Lemma 7.26(a), the moment, entropy and inverse-moment computations of Lemma 7.34(b), and the weighted identity of Lemma 7.35, which I also verified symbolically and numerically for an explicit smooth test configuration (residual below $10^{-16}$). (2) Meaning. Lemma 7.33(c): if the record circulation is already present, adjoint-weighted, at a time $\asymp r_n^2$ before the record, then the total swirl source before that time is at least of order $r_n^2D_n=\alpha_n^2/\nu_n$, a factor $\alpha_n^2$ above the record circulation. Lemma 7.34: inheritance at a positive fraction of the times of such a window forces $\int\|\nabla\omega\|_2^2$ large unless $r_n^3D_n\to0$, and in any case forces $K_+\ge\log\alpha_n-\frac12\log\log(r_nM_n)-C$: the adjoint density must be pushed towards the axis by outward strain, in the precise sense of its inverse radial moment, because sustained large planar total variation of $\psi_n$ is limited by the Fisher-information budget. Lemma 7.35(c): along the Case 3 sequence, an order-$\nu_n^{-1}$ contribution of the swirl pressure to the adjoint circulation requires inward strain on swirl that the adjoint density sees, because the initial budget $J(s_0)$ is $o(\nu_n^{-1})$; no scalar budget proved above bounds $F_n$ by $o(1/\nu_n)$, but $F_n\le\frac1{2\pi}\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\int\sigma_-(u^\theta)^2r^{-1}dx\,ds$, so (c) localizes Lemma 7.26(c) to the swirl seen by the adjoint density; it does not improve the numerical global bound (since $c_1=16\pi c_3$, (c) gives $\nu_n\int\!\int\sigma_-(u^\theta)^2r^{-1}dx\,ds\ge c_1/(6\sqrt2)-o(1)$, while Lemma 7.26(c) gives $c_1/6-o(1)$). (3) None of these conditions contradicts the scalar power counts of the formal scenario of 8.3 (vi): for instance with $r=Na$ and $a=N^{-6}$, $r^3D=N^{-2}\to0$, $S\lesssim N^{19/3}$ against the cost $N\alpha^2=N^3$ of Lemma 7.33(c), and $D_nr_n^2=N^3$ against the logarithmic requirement of Lemma 7.34(b). These are compatibility calculations only.

#### Lemma 7.36 (an inflow clock for the production of the record circulation). In the setting of Lemma 7.35 with $c\equiv1$, put $\mathcal D(s)=\iint\psi\big(2(\partial_rU)^2+2(\partial_zU)^2+U^2/r^2\big)dr\,dz$ and $\Pi'(s)=\iint P\,\partial_z\psi\,dr\,dz$ at time $s$, let $k\ge0$ be a locally integrable function on $[s_0,t]$ with $\iint\sigma_-\psi U^2dr\,dz\le k(s)J(s)$ (for instance $k(s)=\|(u^r/r)_-(s)\|_\infty$), and put $\mathcal K(s_0,s)=\int_{s_0}^sk$. Then:

(a) $\sqrt2|\Pi'(s)|\le\mathcal D(s)$ for every $s$, and for $s_0\le t'\le t$,
$$J(t')e^{-3\mathcal K(s_0,t')}+\int_{s_0}^{t'}e^{-3\mathcal K(s_0,s)}\mathcal D(s)\,ds\le J(s_0),\qquad\text{hence}\qquad\sqrt2\int_{s_0}^{t'}|\Pi'(s)|\,ds\le e^{3\mathcal K(s_0,t')}J(s_0);\tag{7.36.1}$$

(b) $-3\int_{s_0}^t\iint\sigma\psi U^2\,dr\,dz\,ds=J(t)-J(s_0)+\int_{s_0}^t\mathcal D\,ds\ge\sqrt2\big|\int_{s_0}^t\Pi'\,ds\big|-J(s_0)$;

(c) along every Case 3 sequence, with $\phi_n,\psi_n$ as in Lemma 7.28(d) and $K_-(s_0,t)=\int_{s_0}^t\|(u^r/r)_-(s)\|_\infty ds$:
$$K_-(s_0,t_n)\ge\frac13\log\frac{D_n}{r_n}-C,\qquad C=C(s_0,G,C_*,\kappa_0,\|q(s_0)\|_\infty);$$
the net inward strain acting on the adjoint-weighted swirl satisfies $-\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\iint\sigma\psi_nU^2\,dr\,dz\,ds\ge(\frac{2\sqrt2}3c_3-o(1))/\nu_n$; and for every sequence $\omega_n\to\infty$ with $\omega_n\le\frac12\log(D_n/r_n)$, the first time $s_n^*\ge s_0$ with $3K_-(s_0,s_n^*)=\log(D_n/r_n)-\omega_n$ exists for large $n$, $\nu_n\iint\psi_n(s_n^*)w(s_n^*)\,dr\,dz\to0$, and $|\int_{s_n^*}^{t_n}\Pi'\,ds|\ge(2c_3-o(1))/\nu_n$;

(d) (an a priori bound for $\eta$) with $B_0=\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty$ and $A_0=G^{1/2}\|q(s_0)\|_\infty^{3/2}$, for every $t\in[s_0,T_*)$,
$$\|\eta(t)\|_\infty\le B_0+\frac{A_0}{\sqrt2}\,e^{3K_-(s_0,t)}.\tag{7.36.2}$$

*Proof.* (a) As in the proof of Lemma 7.35, $\Pi'=-\iint\psi\,\partial_zP=-2\iint\psi UU_z/r$, and $2\sqrt2|U_z||U|/r\le2U_z^2+U^2/r^2$. By Lemma 7.35(a), $J'=-\mathcal D-3\iint\sigma\psi U^2\le-\mathcal D+3\iint\sigma_-\psi U^2\le-\mathcal D+3kJ$, so $(Je^{-3\mathcal K(s_0,\cdot)})'\le-e^{-3\mathcal K(s_0,\cdot)}\mathcal D$ (both factors are absolutely continuous); integrate over $[s_0,t']$. For the last inequality use $\sqrt2|\Pi'|\le\mathcal D$ and $e^{-3\mathcal K(s_0,s)}\ge e^{-3\mathcal K(s_0,t')}$ for $s\le t'$. (b) Integrate Lemma 7.35(a) over $[s_0,t]$, drop $J(t)\ge0$ and use (a). (c) Lemma 7.28(b) gives $\iint\psi(s)w(s)=\iint\psi(s')w(s')-\int_{s'}^s\Pi'$ for $s_0\le s'\le s\le t_n$, and Lemma 7.28(d) gives $\iint\phi_nw(t_n)\ge2c_3/\nu_n$. As in the proof of Lemma 7.35(c), $\nu_n|I(s_0)|\le\nu_nB_0m_{\phi_n}\to0$ and $J(s_0)\le G^{1/2}Q_0^{3/2}m_{\phi_n}$ with $\nu_nm_{\phi_n}\le Cr_n/D_n$. Hence $|\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\Pi'|\ge(2c_3-o(1))/\nu_n$, and (a) with $t'=t_n$ and $k=\|(u^r/r)_-\|_\infty$ gives $\sqrt2(2c_3-o(1))/\nu_n\le e^{3K_-(s_0,t_n)}G^{1/2}Q_0^{3/2}Cr_n/(D_n\nu_n)$, which is the first claim. The second follows from (b). For the third, $K_-(s_0,\cdot)$ is continuous and nondecreasing with $K_-(s_0,s_0)=0$, and $3K_-(s_0,t_n)\ge\log(D_n/r_n)-3C>\log(D_n/r_n)-\omega_n>0$ for large $n$, so $s_n^*\in(s_0,t_n)$ exists. By (a) with $t'=s_n^*$, $\sqrt2\nu_n\int_{s_0}^{s_n^*}|\Pi'|\le(D_n/r_n)e^{-\omega_n}\nu_nJ(s_0)\le Ce^{-\omega_n}\to0$, so $\nu_n|I(s_n^*)|\le\nu_n|I(s_0)|+\nu_n\int_{s_0}^{s_n^*}|\Pi'|\to0$, and the identity on $[s_n^*,t_n]$ gives the last claim. (d) Let $\phi\in C_c^\infty((0,\infty)\times\mathbb R)$, $0\le\phi\le1$, with terminal time $t$, and $\psi,J,I$ as in Lemma 7.28. Then $|I(s_0)|\le B_0m_\phi$ and $J(s_0)\le A_0m_\phi$ (as in (c)), so Lemma 7.28(b) and (a) with $k=\|(u^r/r)_-\|_\infty$ give $|\iint\phi\,r\eta(t)\,dr\,dz|\le(B_0+A_0e^{3K_-(s_0,t)}/\sqrt2)\iint\phi\,r\,dr\,dz$. The constant does not depend on $\phi$; letting $\phi$ concentrate at a point with $r>0$ and using the continuity of $\eta(t)$ (also at the axis) gives (7.36.2). $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) The first claim of (c) sharpens Lemma 7.27 ($\liminf K_-/\log(D_n/r_n)\ge\frac14$) and Lemma 7.26(d) ($K_-\ge\frac13\log(1/\nu_n)-C$; note $D_n\nu_n/r_n=\alpha_n^2/r_n^3\to\infty$): it reads $e^{3K_-(s_0,t_n)}\gtrsim D_n/r_n$. By (d), $e^{3K_-(s_0,t)}\gtrsim\|\eta(t)\|_\infty-B_0$ at every time, and along the records $\|\eta(t_n)\|_\infty\ge\frac38\kappa_0D_n/r_n$; the reverse comparison is not available (only $\|\eta(t_n)\|_\infty\le\sqrt2H_n\le\sqrt2C_*\alpha_nD_n/r_n$), so (d) is formally stronger than the first claim of (c). The admissible constant in (c) is $C=\frac13\log^+(C_mA_0/(\sqrt2c_3))$ with $C_m=9\pi\kappa_0^2/(64C_*^2)$ ($\nu_nm_{\phi_n}\le C_mr_n/D_n$); if $A_0=0$ then $J(s_0)=0$, (a) forces $\Pi'\equiv0$, and the record is impossible for large $n$, so this degenerate case does not occur. The integrating factor is linear with a given coefficient (no nonlinear Gronwall argument); $k$ may be taken as the $\psi U^2$-weighted mean of $\sigma_-$, so the claim holds for the inward strain seen by the adjoint-weighted swirl. (2) Meaning: in the clock $\mathcal K$, the record circulation is produced late: the adjoint pairing produced before $3\mathcal K$ reaches $\log(D_n/r_n)-\omega_n$ is $o(1/\nu_n)$, and the time-integrated production obeys the budget $\sqrt2\int_{s_0}^{t'}e^{-3\mathcal K(s_0,s)}|\Pi'(s)|ds\le J(s_0)=O(r_n/(D_n\nu_n))$. This is an integrated budget, not a pointwise bound on the production rate, and $|\Pi'(s)|$ is the absolute value after spatial integration, not the unsigned source. (3) Compatibility: in the parametrization of 8.3 (vi), $\frac13\log(D_n/r_n)=\log(1/a)$, the inward strain needed to compress a swirl ring from a radius of order one to the radius $a$ of the cells; so the clock does not exclude the formal scenario; it shows that the inward strain seen by the adjoint-weighted swirl, integrated over the history, is at least the strain that compresses a ring from radius of order one to radius $(r_n/D_n)^{1/3}$, which in that scenario is the cell radius $a$. (4) Lemma 7.36 was derived in this work from Lemma 7.35(a). Parts (a)-(c), including the constants, the sign of the net inflow and the stopping-time argument, and part (d) were rechecked in an adversarial review, which also corrected an unproved global comparison in Remark (1). The review also showed that the elementary bound $K_-\le\int D\,ds$ and Biot-Savart interpolation ($\|\eta\|_{L^{3,1}}$ via (d)) do not give $K_-(s_0,t_n)\le(\frac13-\epsilon)\log(D_n/r_n)$: the scalar history $M=\tau^{-3/4}$, $D=\tau^{-7/5}$, $r=\tau^{11/20}$ ($\tau=T_*-t$) with $\|(u^r/r)_-\|_\infty=\frac{13}{20\tau}$ is compatible with all of these comparisons. (5) Since $|q|$ is controlled along the backward-weighted dynamics by $\|q(t)\|_\infty\le\|q(s_0)\|_\infty e^{2K_-(s_0,t)}$ (maximum principle for $(\partial_t+b\cdot\nabla-\Delta_5)q=-2\sigma q$), one also has $\|u^\theta(t)\|_\infty\le(G\|q(s_0)\|_\infty)^{1/2}e^{K_-(s_0,t)}$, and with 8.1 (xx) ($S(t_n)\nu_n\to\infty$) this gives $K_-(s_0,t_n)\ge\log(1/\nu_n)+\omega_n$ for some $\omega_n\to\infty$; in the parametrization of 8.3 (vi) this is weaker than (c), since there $\log(D_n/r_n)=3\log(1/a)\ge9\log N$.

#### Lemma 7.37 (the source-generated meridional component; the strain clocks are not supplied by transported initial vorticity). Fix $s_0\in(0,T_*)$ and put $B_0=\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty$, $A_2=\|\eta(s_0)\|_{L^2(\mathbb R^3)}$ (finite, as in the proof of Lemma 7.14), $U=u^\theta$, $\sigma=u^r/r$, $Z(s)=\|\nabla u(s)\|_2^2$. Let $\eta_A$ be the solution of $(\partial_t+b\cdot\nabla)\eta_A=\Delta_5\eta_A+\partial_z(q^2)$ on $[s_0,T_*)$ with $\eta_A(s_0)=0$ whose $O(4)$-invariant lift to $\mathbb R^5$ is classical and bounded on $\mathbb R^5\times[s_0,t]$ for every $t<T_*$ (it exists because the equation is linear with smooth bounded coefficients and a smooth bounded source on each such interval, and it is unique in that class by the maximum principle), and put $h=\eta-\eta_A$, so that $(\partial_t+b\cdot\nabla)h=\Delta_5h$, $h(s_0)=\eta(s_0)$. Let $v_A$, $v_B$ be the Biot-Savart fields of $r\eta_Ae_\theta$ and $rh\,e_\theta$ (so $v_A+v_B=b$), $\sigma_A=v_A^r/r$, $\sigma_B=v_B^r/r$, $E_A=\frac12\|v_A\|_2^2$, $Z_A=\|\nabla v_A\|_2^2$, $S_B=\frac12(\nabla v_B+\nabla v_B^{T})$ and $W_A=-\int v_A^TS_Bv_A\,dx$. Then for $s_0\le t<T_*$:

(a) $|h|\le B_0$, $\|h(t)\|_2\le A_2$, and $\|\sigma_B(t)\|_\infty\le C_{\rm init}:=\big(3+\frac3{2\sqrt\pi}\big)B_0^{1/3}A_2^{2/3}$;

(b) $E_A'=W_A-Z_A+Q_A$, where $Q_A=\int\sigma_AU^2dx$, $Z_A=2\pi\iint r^3\eta_A^2\,dr\,dz$, and $|Q_A|\le\frac12Z_A+\frac{G^2}2Z$;

(c) along every Case 3 sequence, for large $n$, $\frac12\int_{\mathcal T_n}|v_A(t_n)|^2dx\ge c_{\rm rec}\,r_nM_n^4/D_n^2$ with $c_{\rm rec}=\frac{729\pi^2\kappa_0^6}{2^{26}C_*^4}$, where $\mathcal T_n$ is the solid torus generated by the disk of Lemma 7.8; hence
$$\int_{s_0}^{t_n}W_A^+\,ds+\frac{G^2}2\int_{s_0}^{t_n}Z\,ds\ge c_{\rm rec}\frac{r_nM_n^4}{D_n^2}+\frac12\int_{s_0}^{t_n}Z_A\,ds;\tag{7.37.1}$$

(d) along every Case 3 sequence, for large $n$, with $C=\max(C_-,C_+)$, where $C_-$ is an admissible constant of Lemma 7.36(c) and $C_+$ one of Corollary 7.24(ii), and $T_n^*$ as in Corollary 7.24(ii),
