$$\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|(\sigma_A)_-\|_\infty ds\ge\frac13\log\frac{D_n}{r_n}-C-C_{\rm init}(t_n-s_0),\qquad\int_{t_n-T_n^*}^{t_n}\|(\sigma_A)_+\|_\infty ds\ge\frac13\log\frac1{\nu_n}-C-o(1).\tag{7.37.2}$$

*Proof.* On $[s_0,t]$, $t<T_*$, the coefficients $b$ and $u^r/r$ are smooth and bounded and $\partial_z(q^2)=2qq_z$ is bounded, so $\eta_A,h\in L^\infty$ by the maximum principle for $\Delta_5$ (the radial Laplacian of $\mathbb R^5$). The field $\Omega_B=rh\,e_\theta$ solves $\partial_t\Omega_B=\Delta\Omega_B+\operatorname{curl}(b\times\Omega_B)$ with data $\omega^\theta(s_0)e_\theta$; since the stretching coefficient $u^r/r$ is bounded, $\Omega_B\in L^\infty(s_0,t;L^2\cap L^\infty)$, and $v_B$ is the solution of the linear equation $\partial_tv_B=\Delta v_B+\mathbb P(b\times\operatorname{curl}v_B)$ with $v_B(s_0)=b(s_0)$ (its curl solves the $\Omega_B$ equation with the same data), which lies in $L^\infty(s_0,t;L^2)\cap L^2(s_0,t;\dot H^1)$ by the energy estimate; so does $v_A=b-v_B$. (a) The maximum principle gives $|h|\le B_0$. Multiplying the $h$ equation by $h$ and integrating with $dx=2\pi r\,dr\,dz$, the transport term vanishes and $\Delta_5=\Delta+\frac2r\partial_r$ with $\int\frac2rhh_r\,dx=2\pi\iint\partial_r(h^2)\,dr\,dz=-2\pi\int h(0,z)^2dz$, so $\frac12\frac d{dt}\|h\|_2^2+\|\nabla h\|_2^2+2\pi\int h(0,z,t)^2dz=0$. The computation in the proof of Lemma 7.14 uses only the $\varphi$-independence of the scalar, so it gives $|\sigma_B(x)|\le\frac3{4\pi}\int|h(y)||x-y|^{-2}dy$; the part $|x-y|<R$ is at most $3B_0R$, and by Cauchy-Schwarz the rest is at most $\frac3{4\pi}A_2(4\pi/R)^{1/2}=\frac{3A_2}{2\sqrt{\pi R}}$; take $R=(A_2/B_0)^{2/3}$ (if $B_0=0$ then $h\equiv0$ and there is nothing to prove). (b) $\Omega_A=r\eta_Ae_\theta$ solves $\partial_t\Omega_A=\Delta\Omega_A+\operatorname{curl}(b\times\Omega_A)+r\partial_z(q^2)e_\theta$, and $r\partial_z(q^2)e_\theta=\operatorname{curl}(r^{-1}U^2e_r)$, so $\partial_tv_A=\Delta v_A+\mathbb P(b\times\operatorname{curl}v_A)+\mathbb P(r^{-1}U^2e_r)$. By $a\times\operatorname{curl}c=\nabla(a\cdot c)-(\nabla a)^Tc-(a\cdot\nabla)c$ (with $((\nabla a)^Tc)_i=\sum_jc_j\partial_ia_j$), applied with $a=v_B,c=v_A$ and with $a=c=v_A$, and $\operatorname{div}b=\operatorname{div}v_A=0$, one gets $\int v_A\cdot(b\times\operatorname{curl}v_A)\,dx=-\int v_A^T(\nabla v_B)v_A\,dx=W_A$; also $\int v_A\cdot\Delta v_A=-Z_A$, $\int v_A\cdot\mathbb P(r^{-1}U^2e_r)=Q_A$, and $Z_A=\|\operatorname{curl}v_A\|_2^2=\|\Omega_A\|_2^2$. Finally $U^2=\Gamma q$, $|\Gamma|\le G$, $\|\sigma_A\|_2^2\le Z_A$ and $\|q\|_2^2\le Z$ (the terms $(v^r/r)^2$ and $(u^\theta/r)^2$ of $|\nabla v|^2$, $|\nabla u|^2$ in cylindrical coordinates), so $|Q_A|\le G\sqrt{Z_AZ}$. (c) By Lemma 7.8 and continuity, $\varepsilon_n\omega^\theta(t_n)\ge\frac38\kappa_0D_n$ ($\varepsilon_n=\pm1$) on the disk $C_n$ of radius $\rho_1=\frac{3\kappa_0}{8\sqrt2C_*}\ell_n$ about $(r^0,z^0)$, and $r_n/2\le r\le2r_n$ on $C_n$ for large $n$; so by (a) $\varepsilon_nr\eta_A(t_n)\ge\frac38\kappa_0D_n-2r_nB_0\ge\frac3{16}\kappa_0D_n$ on $C_n$ for large $n$. With $\zeta=(1-|(r,z)-(r^0,z^0)|^2/\rho_1^2)_+$ ($\iint\zeta=\frac{\pi\rho_1^2}2$, $\iint|\nabla\zeta|^2=2\pi$) and $r\eta_A=\partial_zv_A^r-\partial_rv_A^z$, $I=\iint\zeta r\eta_A\,dr\,dz=\iint(v_A^z\zeta_r-v_A^r\zeta_z)\,dr\,dz$, so $\frac3{32}\pi\kappa_0D_n\rho_1^2\le|I|\le(\iint_{C_n}r|v_A|^2)^{1/2}(4\pi/r_n)^{1/2}$, and $\frac12\int_{\mathcal T_n}|v_A|^2dx=\pi\iint_{C_n}r|v_A|^2dr\,dz\ge\frac{r_n}4|I|^2$ gives the claim. Integrate (b) over $[s_0,t_n]$ with $E_A(s_0)=0$, $E_A(t_n)\ge\frac12\int_{\mathcal T_n}|v_A(t_n)|^2$ and $W_A\le W_A^+$. (d) $\sigma=\sigma_A+\sigma_B$ gives $\|\sigma_\mp\|_\infty\le\|(\sigma_A)_\mp\|_\infty+C_{\rm init}$; insert this in Lemma 7.36(c) and Corollary 7.24(ii) ($T_n^*\to0$). $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) Meaning: whatever $s_0$ is, the diverging inward and outward strain clocks of Lemma 7.36(c) and Corollary 7.24 are supplied by azimuthal vorticity generated by the swirl source after $s_0$, not by the transported initial azimuthal vorticity, whose radial strain stays bounded by $C_{\rm init}$ up to $T_*$ (this does not bound its full strain tensor, and does not prevent initial kinetic energy from feeding the generated structures). Part (c) is the energy counterpart: the generated field carries at least the core energy $r_nM_n^4/D_n^2$ at the record and acquires it either by compressive work of the complementary field or by swirl conversion. (2) Limits. In the formal scenario of 8.3 (vi), $r_nM_n^4/D_n^2=N^3a\to0$, so the weaker inequality obtained from (7.37.1) by dropping the nonnegative term $\frac12\int Z_A$ produces no divergent energy obstruction ($\int Z\le E_0/2$). Whether the scenario is compatible with the full inequality, including the component dissipation $\int Z_A$ and the work $W_A$, is not checked: neither quantity is determined by the scalar sizes of the scenario, and $Z_A\le Z$ is not available because the vorticities of $v_A$ and $v_B$ can cancel. Component energies are not additive ($\|b\|_2^2=\|v_A\|_2^2+\|v_B\|_2^2+2\langle v_A,v_B\rangle$), so neither $E_A\le E_0/2$ nor $\|v_A\|_\infty\le M$ follows. The work $W_A$ says neither where the compressive strain acts nor that it is coherent on a larger scale; an attempted iteration (each source-generated structure needs a larger companion reservoir at the same strain, which needs its own source, and so on) fails because the transverse diffusion clock of a structure of scale $N_na_n$ is $(N_na_n)^{-2}$ rather than $a_n^{-2}$, no lifetime bound for a coherent strain is known, and interaction energies do not sum. (3) Lemma 7.37 is not used elsewhere.

#### Lemma 7.38 (a vertical-dispersion refinement of the logarithm of Theorem 7.18). Fix $0<\vartheta<1<L$, $t\le t_n$ and $x=(r_x,z_x)\in\mathcal A_\vartheta=\{\vartheta r_n<r<Lr_n\}$; let $X=(R,Z)$ be the process of Lemma 7.10(a) started at $x$ at time $t$, $\tau$ its exit time from $\mathcal A_\vartheta$, $K=C_NL^2/(\vartheta^3r_n)$, and $\bar\mu_\vartheta=\int_{\vartheta r_n}^{Lr_n}r\,dr=(L^2-\vartheta^2)r_n^2/2$ (the $\mu$-measure of $\mathcal A_\vartheta$ per unit height). Let $\mathcal O_j$ be bounded smooth domains with $\overline{\mathcal O_j}\subset\{\vartheta r_n\le r\le Lr_n\}$, $\mathcal O_j\subset\mathcal O_{j+1}$, such that every compact subset of $\mathcal A_\vartheta$ lies in some $\mathcal O_j$ (smoothed rectangles as in the proof of Lemma 7.10(a)), with exit times $\tau_j$, and let $p^{(j)}_h$ be the Green function of $\mathcal O_j$ for $(t,x)$ at time $t-h$, as in the proof of Lemma 7.16. For $\varphi\ge0$, $\int p^{(j)}_h\varphi\,d\mu=\mathbb E[\varphi(X_h);\tau_j>h]$ increases to $\mathbb E[\varphi(X_h);\tau>h]$ (a continuous path that stays in $\mathcal A_\vartheta$ on $[0,h]$ has compact range there), so $p^{(j)}_h$ increases in $j$ and $p_h=\sup_jp^{(j)}_h$ is the killed density of $X$ on $\mathcal A_\vartheta$ with respect to $\mu$: $\int p_h\varphi\,d\mu=\mathbb E[\varphi(X_h);\tau>h]$, $\int p_h\,d\mu\le1$, and $p_h\le K/h$ by Corollary 7.13 applied to each $\mathcal O_j$.

(a) *Moment form of Lemma 7.16 on the strip.* Let $0<h_1<h_2<t$, $V=\sup_{t-h_2\le s\le t}\sup_{\mathcal A_\vartheta}|(u^r,u^z)(s)|$, let $c:[h_1,h_2]\to\mathbb R$ be measurable and $s>0$ with $\int p_h(z-c(h))^2\,d\mu\le s^2$ for $h_1\le h\le h_2$, and put $\Lambda_s=\max\{2,\log(\pi K\bar\mu_\vartheta s/h_1)\}$. Then
$$\int_{h_1}^{h_2}\Big|\int p_h\,\eta(t-h)\,d\mu\Big|\,dh\le\frac V{\vartheta r_n}\big(2e\,\Lambda_s\,(h_2-h_1)\big)^{1/2}.$$

(b) *Dispersion forced by alternative (I).* Let $\epsilon,L,T=T_\vartheta,a_n,v_n,J_n$ be as in Theorem 7.12, $0<\vartheta\le\frac14$, $x=x^0$, $t=t_n$, put
$$s_n(\vartheta)=\sup_{T/2\le h\le T}\ \inf_{c\in\mathbb R}\Big(\int p_h(z-c)^2\,d\mu\Big)^{1/2}\qquad\text{and}\qquad C_D=\frac{131072\,e\,(1+G)}{27\,\kappa_0^3}=\frac89\,C_I .$$
For all large $n$, uniformly in $\vartheta\in(0,\frac14]$,
$$J_n(\vartheta)\le\frac{a_n}4\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\alpha_n^2\le\frac{C_D}{\vartheta^5}\,\max\Big\{2,\ \log\frac{\pi C_NL^4(1+G)\,s_n(\vartheta)}{\epsilon\,\vartheta^6\,r_n}\Big\}.$$
Equivalently, if $J_n(\vartheta)\le a_n/4$ and $\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2>2C_D$, then $s_n(\vartheta)\ge\dfrac{\epsilon\,\vartheta^6}{\pi C_NL^4(1+G)}\,r_n\exp\Big(\dfrac{\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2}{C_D}\Big)$.

(c) *Bounds for the spread.* For $0<h<t$ put $e(a)=\int p_a\,(u^z(t-a))^2\,d\mu$, $V_h=\sup_{t-h\le s\le t}\sup_{\mathcal A_\vartheta}|u^z(s)|$ and $E_\vartheta(t,h)=\sup_{t-h\le s\le t}\int_{\{\vartheta r_n<r<Lr_n\}}|u(s)|^2\,dx$. Then
$$\Big(\int p_h(z-z_x)^2\,d\mu\Big)^{1/2}\le\sqrt{2h}+\int_0^he(a)^{1/2}\,da,\qquad e(a)\le\min\Big\{V_h^2,\ \frac{K\,E_\vartheta(t,h)}{2\pi a}\Big\},$$
so the left side is at most $\min\{\sqrt{2h}+V_hh,\ 2(h(1+KE_\vartheta(t,h)/\pi))^{1/2}\}$.

(d) *Two explicit forms.* In (b), put $E_\vartheta=E_\vartheta(t_n,T_\vartheta)\le E_0$ and $C_E=2\pi C_NL^4((1+G)/\epsilon)^{1/2}$. For all large $n$, uniformly in $\vartheta\in(0,\frac14]$, $J_n(\vartheta)\le a_n/4$ implies both
$$\alpha_n^2\le\frac{C_D}{\vartheta^5}\max\Big\{2,\ \log\frac{C_E}{\vartheta^{9/2}}+\frac12\log\Big(1+\frac{C_NL^2E_\vartheta}{\pi\vartheta^3r_n}\Big)\Big\}\quad\text{and}\quad\alpha_n^2\le\frac{C_D}{\vartheta^5}\max\Big\{2,\ \log\Big(\frac{\pi C_NL^4\,r_nM_n}{\vartheta^3}+\frac{C_E}{\sqrt2\,\vartheta^{9/2}}\Big)\Big\}.$$

*Proof.* (a) Fix $j$. On $\mathcal O_j$ the facts (i)-(iii) of the proof of Lemma 7.16 hold for $p^{(j)}$ with the same $K$, and $\int p^{(j)}_h(z-c(h))^2\,d\mu\le\int p_h(z-c(h))^2\,d\mu\le s^2$. Let $\beta=1/\Lambda_s\le\frac12$, $q=1+\beta$ and $w_h=1+(z-c(h))^2/s^2$. Then $\int p^{(j)}_hw_h\,d\mu\le2$, and since $(1-\beta)/\beta\ge1$ and $w_h\ge1$, $\int_{\mathcal O_j}w_h^{-(1-\beta)/\beta}\,d\mu\le\int_{\mathcal A_\vartheta}w_h^{-1}\,d\mu=\pi\bar\mu_\vartheta s$. Hoelder's inequality with exponents $1/(1-\beta)$ and $1/\beta$ gives
$$\int\big(p^{(j)}_h\big)^{1-\beta}d\mu=\int\big(p^{(j)}_hw_h\big)^{1-\beta}w_h^{-(1-\beta)}\,d\mu\le2^{1-\beta}(\pi\bar\mu_\vartheta s)^\beta .$$
By Cauchy-Schwarz and (iii), as in the proof of Lemma 7.16,
$$\int_{h_1}^{h_2}\!\!\int|\nabla p^{(j)}_h|\,d\mu\,dh\le\Big(\frac{(K/h_1)^\beta}{q\beta}\Big)^{1/2}\big((h_2-h_1)\,2^{1-\beta}(\pi\bar\mu_\vartheta s)^\beta\big)^{1/2}\le\big(2\Lambda_s(h_2-h_1)\big)^{1/2}\Big(\frac{\pi K\bar\mu_\vartheta s}{h_1}\Big)^{\beta/2}\le\big(2e\Lambda_s(h_2-h_1)\big)^{1/2},$$
and the curl identity of that proof (on $\mathcal O_j$, where $p^{(j)}_h$ vanishes on the boundary) gives $\int_{h_1}^{h_2}|\int p^{(j)}_h\eta(t-h)\,d\mu|\,dh\le\frac V{\vartheta r_n}(2e\Lambda_s(h_2-h_1))^{1/2}$. For each $h$, $\int p^{(j)}_h\eta(t-h)\,d\mu\to\int p_h\eta(t-h)\,d\mu$ by dominated convergence ($\eta(t-h)$ is bounded on $\mathcal A_\vartheta$, $0\le p^{(j)}_h\uparrow p_h$, $\int p_h\,d\mu\le1$), and these integrals are bounded uniformly in $h$ and $j$, so bounded convergence in $h$ gives (a).

(b) By the proof of Theorem 7.12 (alternative (I) and the choice of constants there, valid for large $n$ uniformly in $\vartheta\in(0,\frac14]$), $J_n(\vartheta)\le a_n/4$ gives $\int p_hv_n(t_n-h)\,d\mu\ge a_n/2$ for $0<h\le T$; in particular $\int p_h\,d\mu>0$. If $s_n(\vartheta)=\infty$ there is nothing to prove. Otherwise, for $h\in[T/2,T]$ the infimum over $c$ is attained at the mean $c(h)=\int p_hz\,d\mu/\int p_h\,d\mu$, a measurable function of $h$ (write $z=z_+-z_-$; the integrals $\int p_hz_\pm\,d\mu$ are finite, because $\int p_h(z-c)^2\,d\mu<\infty$ for some $c$ and $\int p_h\,d\mu\le1$, and each is a monotone limit of the integrals $\int p^{(j)}_hz_\pm\,d\mu$, which are continuous in $h$), and $\int p_h(z-c(h))^2\,d\mu\le s_n(\vartheta)^2$. Apply (a) with $h_1=T/2$, $h_2=T$, $V\le M_n$ and $s=s_n(\vartheta)$. Since $K\bar\mu_\vartheta\le C_NL^4r_n/(2\vartheta^3)$ and $h_1=\epsilon\vartheta^3r_n^2/(2(1+G))$, the logarithm is $\Lambda=\max\{2,\log(\pi C_NL^4(1+G)s_n(\vartheta)/(\epsilon\vartheta^6r_n))\}$ or smaller, and
$$\frac{a_n}2\cdot\frac T2\le\frac{M_n}{\vartheta r_n}\,(e\Lambda T)^{1/2},\qquad\text{so}\qquad a_n^2\le\frac{16\,e\,M_n^2\,\Lambda}{\vartheta^2r_n^2T}=\frac{16\,e\,(1+G)\,M_n^2\,\Lambda}{\epsilon\,\vartheta^5r_n^4}.$$
With $a_n\ge c_aD_n/r_n$, $c_a=\frac38\kappa_0$, and $\epsilon=\frac{3\kappa_0}{128}$, this is $\alpha_n^2\le\frac{16e(1+G)}{c_a^2\epsilon}\vartheta^{-5}\Lambda=C_D\vartheta^{-5}\Lambda$. If $\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2>2C_D$, the maximum must be the logarithm, which is the equivalent form.

(c) Before $\tau$, $Z_a=z_x-\int_0^au^z(X_b,t-b)\,db+\sqrt2\,W^{(2)}_a$, with $W^{(2)}$ the second component of $W$. Since $\{\tau>h\}\subset\{\tau>b\}$ for $b\le h$,
$$|Z_h-z_x|\,\mathbf 1_{\{\tau>h\}}\le\sqrt2\,|W^{(2)}_h|+\int_0^h|u^z(X_b,t-b)|\,\mathbf 1_{\{\tau>b\}}\,db ,$$
and Minkowski's inequality in $L^2(\mathbb P)$ gives the first bound, because $\mathbb E[(Z_h-z_x)^2;\tau>h]=\int p_h(z-z_x)^2\,d\mu$ and $\mathbb E[u^z(X_b,t-b)^2;\tau>b]=e(b)$. Clearly $e(a)\le V_h^2$; and $p_a\le K/a$ together with $\int_{\mathcal A_\vartheta}(u^z)^2\,d\mu\le\frac1{2\pi}\int_{\{\vartheta r_n<r<Lr_n\}}|u|^2\,dx$ gives the second bound for $e$. Finally $\int_0^h(KE/(2\pi a))^{1/2}da=(2KEh/\pi)^{1/2}$ and $(\sqrt{2h}+\sqrt{2KEh/\pi})^2\le4h(1+KE/\pi)$.

(d) $s_n(\vartheta)\le\sup_{T/2\le h\le T}(\int p_h(z-z^0)^2\,d\mu)^{1/2}$. Insert (c), with $h\le T$, $V_h\le M_n$ and $T=\epsilon\vartheta^3r_n^2/(1+G)$, into (b): $2\sqrt T\,\pi C_NL^4(1+G)/(\epsilon\vartheta^6r_n)=C_E\vartheta^{-9/2}$, $KE_\vartheta/\pi=C_NL^2E_\vartheta/(\pi\vartheta^3r_n)$, and $\pi C_NL^4(1+G)(\sqrt{2T}+M_nT)/(\epsilon\vartheta^6r_n)=C_E/(\sqrt2\,\vartheta^{9/2})+\pi C_NL^4r_nM_n/\vartheta^3$. $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) Meaning: the logarithmic bound of Theorem 7.18 admits a refinement in terms of the vertical dispersion of the killed backward density of the record point, namely $s_n(\vartheta)/r_n$, where $s_n(\vartheta)$ is the supremum over $T/2\le h\le T$ of the square root of the centred, unnormalised second moment $\inf_c\int p_h(z-c)^2d\mu=m_h\operatorname{Var}(Z_h\mid h<\tau)$, $m_h=\int p_h\,d\mu$; a common vertical translation of the density does not count. Where $\int p_hv_n\,d\mu\ge a_n/2$ (as under $J_n(\vartheta)\le a_n/4$), $v_n\le B_\vartheta=\sqrt2D_n/(\vartheta r_n)$ gives $m_h\ge a_n/(2B_\vartheta)\ge c_a\vartheta/(2\sqrt2)$, so for fixed $\vartheta$ this moment is comparable to the conditional variance. No equality or asymptotic equivalence between the two logarithms is asserted. The second inequality in (d) is Theorem 7.18 with the smaller constant $C_D$, for every $\vartheta\in(0,\frac14]$, without the condition $\vartheta^3r_nM_n\ge C_1$ and without an artificial vertical boundary. (2) Consequently an a priori bound $\log^+(s_n(\vartheta)/r_n)=o(\alpha_n^2)$, for one fixed $\vartheta$ along a subsequence with $J_n(\vartheta)\le a_n/4$, would exclude alternative (I) at that $\vartheta$, and $s_n(\vartheta)\le C(\vartheta)r_n$ would give $\alpha_n$ bounded. No such bound is proved here. The threshold matters with its constant: a bound $s_n(\vartheta)=O(r_ne^{c\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2})$ suffices only for $c<1/C_D$. (3) The energy form cannot by itself exclude (I). The solid torus of Lemma 6.1 lies in $\mathcal A_\vartheta$ for large $n$, so $E_\vartheta\ge\frac{\pi^2}{20736C_*^4}\frac{r_nM_n^4}{D_n^2}$ and the logarithm in the first inequality of (d) is at least $\log(1/\nu_n)-C(\vartheta)$. In (I), $\alpha_n^2=O(\log(r_nM_n))$ and $\log(r_nM_n)=\log\alpha_n+\log(1/\nu_n)$, so there the energy form is not better than the velocity form, up to a factor $1+o(1)$; with only $E_\vartheta\le E_0$ known, neither form is uniformly sharper. (4) Lemmas 7.38, 7.39 and 7.40 were checked line by line, including the constants (the constant $C_D$ comes from that check). An adversarial review found no defect in the statements or constants of Lemmas 7.38, 7.39 and 7.40(a); its corrections to the remarks (the difference between gradient-weighted and unweighted occupation, the two-level form of the width statement, uniformity in $\vartheta$) are incorporated.

#### Lemma 7.39 (the dispersed part of the killed density carries high vorticity). In the setting of Lemma 7.38(b), let $n$ be large and $J_n(\vartheta)\le a_n/4$; put $B_\vartheta=\sqrt2D_n/(\vartheta r_n)$ and, for $0<h\le T$, $E_h=\{v_n(t_n-h)\ge a_n/4\}\cap\mathcal A_\vartheta$.

(a) For every measurable $c:[T/2,T]\to\mathbb R$ and every $k>0$, with $\Lambda_k=\max\{1,\log(8K\bar\mu_\vartheta k/T)\}$,
$$\frac2T\int_{T/2}^T\!\int_{E_h\cap\{|z-c(h)|>k\}}p_h\,d\mu\,dh\ \ge\ \frac1{B_\vartheta}\Big[\frac{a_n}4-\frac{M_n}{\vartheta r_n}\Big(\Big(\frac{2e\Lambda_k}T\Big)^{1/2}+\frac2k\Big)\Big].$$

(b) Fix $\vartheta\in(0,\frac14]$ and put $k_n=r_n\exp(\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2/(64C_D))$. Along a subsequence with $J_n(\vartheta)\le a_n/4$, for all large $n$ and every measurable $c$,
$$\frac2T\int_{T/2}^T\mathbb P\big(\tau>h,\ v_n(X_h,t_n-h)\ge a_n/4,\ |Z_h-c(h)|>k_n\big)\,dh\ \ge\ \frac{3\kappa_0}{64\sqrt2}\,\vartheta .$$

*Proof.* (a) Fix $\chi\in C^\infty(\mathbb R)$ with $0\le\chi\le1$, $\chi=1$ on $[-1,1]$, $\chi=0$ outside $(-2,2)$ and $|\chi'|\le2$, and put $\chi_h(z)=\chi((z-c(h))/k)$ and $S_h=\mathcal A_\vartheta\cap\{|z-c(h)|<2k\}$, so $\mu(S_h)=4k\bar\mu_\vartheta$. On $\mathcal O_j$ apply the curl identity of the proof of Lemma 7.16 to $\chi_hp^{(j)}_h$, which vanishes on $\partial\mathcal O_j$:
$$\Big|\int\chi_hp^{(j)}_h\eta\,d\mu\Big|=\Big|\iint\big(u^r\partial_z(\chi_hp^{(j)}_h)-u^z\partial_r(\chi_hp^{(j)}_h)\big)\,dr\,dz\Big|\le\frac{M_n}{\vartheta r_n}\Big(\int_{S_h}|\nabla p^{(j)}_h|\,d\mu+\frac2k\int p^{(j)}_h\,d\mu\Big).$$
By Hoelder, $\int_{S_h}(p^{(j)}_h)^{1-\beta}d\mu\le(4k\bar\mu_\vartheta)^\beta$; with $\beta=1/\Lambda_k$ and the exponent $q=1+\beta$ of (iii) of the proof of Lemma 7.16 (here $q$ is not the swirl variable $u^\theta/r$), Cauchy-Schwarz and (iii) on $[T/2,T]$ give, since $q\ge1$,
$$\int_{T/2}^T\!\!\int_{S_h}|\nabla p^{(j)}_h|\,d\mu\,dh\le\Big(\frac{(2K/T)^\beta}{q\beta}\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\frac T2(4k\bar\mu_\vartheta)^\beta\Big)^{1/2}\le\Big(\frac{\Lambda_kT}2\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\frac{8K\bar\mu_\vartheta k}T\Big)^{\beta/2}\le\Big(\frac{e\Lambda_kT}2\Big)^{1/2}.$$
Letting $j\to\infty$ as in the proof of Lemma 7.38(a),
$$\frac2T\int_{T/2}^T\Big|\int\chi_hp_hv_n(t_n-h)\,d\mu\Big|\,dh\le\frac{M_n}{\vartheta r_n}\Big(\Big(\frac{2e\Lambda_k}T\Big)^{1/2}+\frac2k\Big).$$
For each $h\in[T/2,T]$ (where $c(h)$ and $\chi_h$ are defined), $\frac{a_n}2\le\int p_hv_n\,d\mu=\int\chi_hp_hv_n\,d\mu+\int(1-\chi_h)p_hv_n\,d\mu$. Since $1-\chi_h$ vanishes on $\{|z-c(h)|\le k\}$, $v_n<a_n/4$ off $E_h$ and $v_n\le B_\vartheta$ on $\mathcal A_\vartheta$, the last integral is at most $\frac{a_n}4\int p_h\,d\mu+B_\vartheta\int_{E_h\cap\{|z-c(h)|>k\}}p_h\,d\mu$. Average over $h\in[T/2,T]$ and use $\int p_h\,d\mu\le1$.

(b) $8K\bar\mu_\vartheta k/T\le4C_NL^4(1+G)k/(\epsilon\vartheta^6r_n)$, so with $k=k_n$, $\log(8K\bar\mu_\vartheta k_n/T)\le\log\frac{4C_NL^4(1+G)}{\epsilon\vartheta^6}+\frac{\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2}{64C_D}$, and for large $n$ (as $\alpha_n\to\infty$ and $\vartheta$ is fixed) $\Lambda_{k_n}\le\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2/(32C_D)=c_a^2\epsilon\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2/(512e(1+G))$. Then $2e\Lambda_{k_n}/T\le c_a^2\vartheta^2\alpha_n^2/(256r_n^2)$, so the first error term is at most $\frac{M_n}{\vartheta r_n}\cdot\frac{c_a\vartheta\alpha_n}{16r_n}=\frac{c_aD_n}{16r_n}\le\frac{a_n}{16}$. The second is $\frac{2M_n}{\vartheta r_nk_n}\le\frac{2M_n}{\vartheta r_n^2}\le\frac{a_n}{16}$ as soon as $\alpha_n\ge32/(c_a\vartheta)$. So the bracket in (a) is at least $a_n/8$, and the right side of (a) is at least $\frac{a_n}{8B_\vartheta}\ge\frac{c_a\vartheta}{8\sqrt2}=\frac{3\kappa_0\vartheta}{64\sqrt2}$. The inner integral in (a) is the probability in (b). $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) Meaning: in alternative (I), for no measurable choice of centres $c(h)$ is the high set $E_h$, up to $p_h$-null sets, contained in the slab $\{|z-c(h)|\le k_n\}$ for almost every $h\in[T/2,T]$: a time-averaged fraction $\gtrsim\vartheta$ of the killed density lies on high vorticity at vertical distance $>r_ne^{\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2/(64C_D)}$ from any chosen centre. So the persistence of (I) is not carried by a single coherent structure of vertical size $O(r_n)$ near the record. The quantifier is a time average over $[T/2,T]$, not a bound at every age, and the statement concerns the stopped representation, not a material ring. (2) A sufficient condition for excluding (I) at a fixed $\vartheta$: measurable centres $c_n(h)$ and heights $k_n\ge r_n$ with $\log(k_n/r_n)=o(\alpha_n^2)$ such that the time-averaged probability in (b), with these $k_n$, tends to $0$ along the subsequence. Indeed for such $k_n$ both error terms in (a) are $o(a_n)$ ($\Lambda_{k_n}=o(\alpha_n^2)$ and $M_n/(\vartheta r_n^2)=O(a_n/(\vartheta\alpha_n))$), so (a) gives the lower bound $(1-o(1))a_n/(4B_\vartheta)\ge c\vartheta$. This is tightness of the high-vorticity part of the killed density, not of the whole density. It is not proved here.

#### Lemma 7.40 (further restrictions on the persistence). In the setting of Lemma 7.38(b) (any $\vartheta\in(0,\frac14]$, $x=x^0$, $t=t_n$, $\tau$ the exit time from $\mathcal A_\vartheta$), put $B_\vartheta=\sqrt2D_n/(\vartheta r_n)$ and $Y_h=v_n(X_{h\wedge\tau},t_n-h\wedge\tau)$, and let $f=\partial_z(q^2)$.

(a) *Stopped-square identity.* For $0<h\le T$,
$$2\int_0^h\!\!\int p_b\,|\nabla v_n(t_n-b)|^2\,d\mu\,db=\mathbb E[Y_h^2]-a_n^2+2\int_0^h\!\!\int p_b\,(\eta f)(t_n-b)\,d\mu\,db .$$
Under (A) at $t_n$, with $C_f=4\sqrt2\,\epsilon\,G^{4/3}C_*^{2/3}/(1+G)$,
$$\int_0^T\!\!\int p_h\,|\nabla v_n(t_n-h)|^2\,d\mu\,dh\le\frac{D_n^2}{\vartheta^2r_n^2}\big(1+C_f\vartheta\nu_n^{1/3}\big)-\frac{a_n^2}2 ,$$
and hence, for all large $n$,
$$\frac2T\int_{T/2}^T\!\!\int p_h\,\frac{\ell_n^2|\nabla v_n(t_n-h)|^2}{a_n^2}\,d\mu\,dh\le\big(1+C_f\vartheta\nu_n^{1/3}\big)\frac{C_D}{8e\,\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2}.$$
In particular, for each fixed $\vartheta\in(0,\frac14]$, a lower bound $(2/T)\int_{T/2}^T\int p_h\ell_n^2|\nabla v_n|^2\,d\mu\,dh\ge c\,a_n^2$, with $c>0$ independent of $n$, along a subsequence would give $\alpha_n^2\le(1+o(1))C_D/(8ec\vartheta^5)$, contradicting $\alpha_n\to\infty$.

(b) *Thinness of the high set.* For every $s\le t_n$ and every closed square $Q$ of side $\sigma$ in the meridional half-plane $\{r>0\}$, $|\iint_Q\omega^\theta(s)\,dr\,dz|\le4\sigma M_n$. Hence, if $Q\subset\mathcal A_\vartheta$ and $v_n(s)\ge\lambda>0$ on $Q$, then $\sigma\le4M_n/(\vartheta r_n\lambda)$. For large $n$, therefore, at no time $s\le t_n$ does $\{v_n(s)\ge a_n/4\}\cap\mathcal A_\vartheta$ contain a square of side larger than $16\ell_n/(c_a\vartheta)=128\ell_n/(3\kappa_0\vartheta)$, and $\{v_n(s)\ge a_n/8\}\cap\mathcal A_\vartheta$ none of side larger than $256\ell_n/(3\kappa_0\vartheta)$.

(c) *Perimeter of the high set in alternative (I).* Let $n$ be large and suppose $J_n(\vartheta)\le a_n/4$; let $0<h\le T$ and $0\le\lambda<a_n/2$, and let $E=\{v_n(t_n-h)>\lambda\}\cap\mathcal A_\vartheta$, with $|E|$ its area and $\mathrm{Per}(E)\in[0,\infty]$ its perimeter as a subset of the $(r,z)$ plane (De Giorgi perimeter; boundary portions on the lines $r=\vartheta r_n$ and $r=Lr_n$ count). Then
$$\mathrm{Per}(E)\ge\frac{(a_n/2-\lambda)\,\vartheta^3r_n\,h}{C_NL^2M_n},$$
and if $\lambda>0$, $\mathrm{Per}(E)<\infty$ and $\mathrm{Per}(E)\le Q|E|^{1/2}$ with $Q<\infty$, then $h\le C_NL^2M_n^2Q^2/(4\lambda(a_n/2-\lambda)\vartheta^4r_n^2)$. For $\lambda=a_n/4$, using $a_n\ge c_aD_n/r_n$, this gives, with the ratio read as $+\infty$ when the perimeter is infinite,
$$\mathrm{Per}(E)\ge\frac{c_a\vartheta^3}{4C_NL^2}\,\frac h{\ell_n},\qquad\frac{\mathrm{Per}(E)}{|E|^{1/2}}\ge\frac{c_a\vartheta^2}{2L\sqrt{C_N}}\,\frac{h^{1/2}}{\ell_n},$$
and at $h=T$: $\mathrm{Per}(E)\ge\frac{c_a\epsilon}{4C_NL^2(1+G)}\vartheta^6\alpha_nr_n$ and $\mathrm{Per}(E)/|E|^{1/2}\ge\frac{c_a}{2L}\big(\frac\epsilon{C_N(1+G)}\big)^{1/2}\vartheta^{7/2}\alpha_n$.

*Proof.* (a) For $a<\tau$, $X_a\in\mathcal A_\vartheta$, where $v_n$ is smooth and $v_n,\nabla v_n$ are bounded on $\mathcal A_\vartheta\times[t_n-T,t_n]$ ($\eta=\omega^\theta/r$ with $\omega,\nabla\omega$ bounded and $r\ge\vartheta r_n$). Itô's formula, as in the proof of Lemma 7.10(a), gives $dY_a=-\mathbf 1_{\{a<\tau\}}f_v(X_a,t_n-a)\,da+\sqrt2\,\mathbf 1_{\{a<\tau\}}\nabla v_n(X_a,t_n-a)\cdot dW_a$ with $f_v=\operatorname{sgn}(\eta(x^0,t_n))f$, hence $d(Y^2)=2Y\,dY+2\cdot\mathbf 1_{\{a<\tau\}}|\nabla v_n|^2\,da$. The stochastic integral $\int_0^h2\sqrt2\,Y_a\mathbf 1_{\{a<\tau\}}\nabla v_n\cdot dW_a$ has a bounded integrand, so it is a martingale with mean zero. Take expectations, using $Y_0=a_n$, $Y_af_v(X_a,t_n-a)=(\eta f)(X_a,t_n-a)$ for $a<\tau$, and $\mathbb E[g(X_a);\tau>a]=\int p_ag\,d\mu$. For the bound: on $\{\tau>h\}$, $|Y_h|\le\sqrt2D(t_n-h)/(\vartheta r_n)\le B_\vartheta$, and on $\{\tau\le h\}$ the exit point lies on $\{r=\vartheta r_n\}$ or $\{r=Lr_n\}$, where $|v_n|\le B_\vartheta$; so $\mathbb E[Y_T^2]\le B_\vartheta^2=2D_n^2/(\vartheta^2r_n^2)$. On $\mathcal A_\vartheta$, $f=2\Gamma\partial_zu^\theta/r^3$ with $|\Gamma|\le G$ and, by Lemma 7.23(b) and (A), $|\partial_zu^\theta|\le2G^{1/3}H(t_n)^{2/3}\le2G^{1/3}C_*^{2/3}D_n^{4/3}M_n^{-2/3}$, so $|f|\le4G^{4/3}C_*^{2/3}D_n^{4/3}M_n^{-2/3}/(\vartheta r_n)^3$ and
$$\Big|2\int_0^T\!\!\int p_h\,\eta f\,d\mu\,dh\Big|\le2B_\vartheta T\sup_{\mathcal A_\vartheta\times[t_n-T,t_n]}|f|\le\frac{8\sqrt2\,\epsilon\,G^{4/3}C_*^{2/3}}{(1+G)\,\vartheta}\,\frac{D_n^2}{r_n^2}\,\nu_n^{1/3}=2C_f\vartheta\nu_n^{1/3}\frac{D_n^2}{\vartheta^2r_n^2}.$$
This gives the second display. For the third, restrict the time integral to $[T/2,T]$, multiply by $2\ell_n^2/(Ta_n^2)$ and use $a_n\ge c_aD_n/r_n$ and $T=\epsilon\vartheta^3r_n^2/(1+G)$: the factor is $2(1+G)\ell_n^2/(c_a^2\epsilon\vartheta^5r_n^2)=C_D/(8e\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2)$. The last statement follows by comparing the two bounds.

(b) By Green's theorem in the $(r,z)$ plane, $\iint_Q(\partial_zu^r-\partial_ru^z)\,dr\,dz=-\oint_{\partial Q}(u^r\,dr+u^z\,dz)$, and $|(u^r,u^z)|\le\|u(s)\|_\infty\le M(s)\le M_n$ on $\partial Q$, whose length is $4\sigma$. If $v_n(s)\ge\lambda$ on $Q\subset\mathcal A_\vartheta$, then $\operatorname{sgn}(\eta(x^0,t_n))\,\omega^\theta(s)=r\,v_n(s)\ge\vartheta r_n\lambda$ on $Q$, so $\sigma^2\vartheta r_n\lambda\le4\sigma M_n$. Take $\lambda=a_n/4$ or $a_n/8$ with $a_n\ge c_aD_n/r_n$ and $M_n/D_n=\ell_n$. $\square$

(c) Let $p_h$ be the killed density of Lemma 7.38 with $x=x^0$, $t=t_n$, so that $\int p_h\varphi\,d\mu=(P_{\mathcal A_\vartheta}(t_n,t_n-h)\varphi)(x^0)$ for bounded $\varphi$, $\int p_h\,d\mu\le1$ and $p_h\le K/h$ with $K=C_NL^2/(\vartheta^3r_n)$. Put $w=v_n(t_n-h)$ and $g=(w-\lambda)\mathbf 1_E\ge0$; on $\mathcal A_\vartheta$, $w\le\lambda+g$ and $|w|\le B_\vartheta$. By Theorem 7.12(I) and $\lambda\ge0$,
$$\frac{a_n}2\le\int p_hw\,d\mu\le\lambda\int p_h\,d\mu+\int p_hg\,d\mu\le\lambda+\frac Kh\int g\,d\mu,$$
so $\int g\,d\mu\ge(a_n/2-\lambda)h/K>0$ (possibly $+\infty$). If $\mathrm{Per}(E)=\infty$ the perimeter bound and the ratio bound (with the value $+\infty$) are immediate; assume henceforth $\mathrm{Per}(E)<\infty$. The complement of $E$ contains a half-plane, so the isoperimetric inequality for sets of finite perimeter gives $|E|<\infty$; hence $\mu(E)\le Lr_n|E|<\infty$, and all terms below are finite. Since $r\,w=\operatorname{sgn}(\eta(x^0,t_n))\,\omega^\theta(t_n-h)$ and $d\mu=r\,dr\,dz$,
$$\int g\,d\mu=\operatorname{sgn}(\eta(x^0,t_n))\iint_E\omega^\theta(t_n-h)\,dr\,dz-\lambda\,\mu(E),\qquad\mu(E)\ge\vartheta r_n|E|.$$
The field $F=(-u^z,u^r)(t_n-h)$ is $C^1$ on $\{r>0\}$, with $\partial_rF_1+\partial_zF_2=\partial_zu^r-\partial_ru^z=\omega^\theta$ and $|F|\le M_n$. Let $0\le\psi\le1$ be smooth, equal to $1$ on $\{r\ge3\vartheta r_n/4\}$ and to $0$ on $\{r\le\vartheta r_n/2\}$, with $\psi F$ extended by zero across the axis, and $\chi_R=\chi(\cdot/R)$ with $\chi\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^2)$, $0\le\chi\le1$, equal to $1$ on the unit disk. The Gauss-Green formula for sets of finite perimeter, applied to the compactly supported $C^1$ field $\chi_R\psi F$ (equal to $\chi_RF$ near $\overline E$), gives $|\iint_E(\chi_R\omega^\theta+F\cdot\nabla\chi_R)\,dr\,dz|\le M_n\mathrm{Per}(E)$; as $R\to\infty$ ($\omega^\theta$ is bounded, $|\nabla\chi_R|\le C/R$ and $|E|<\infty$) this gives $|\iint_E\omega^\theta\,dr\,dz|\le M_n\mathrm{Per}(E)$. Hence
$$\frac{(a_n/2-\lambda)\,h}K\le\int g\,d\mu\le M_n\mathrm{Per}(E)-\lambda\vartheta r_n|E|,$$
which gives the first bound after the last term is dropped. If $\lambda>0$ and $\mathrm{Per}(E)\le Q|E|^{1/2}$, the right side is at most $\max_{x\ge0}(M_nQx-\lambda\vartheta r_nx^2)=M_n^2Q^2/(4\lambda\vartheta r_n)$, which gives the bound on $h$. For $\lambda=a_n/4$ use $a_nr_n\ge c_aD_n$ and $M_n/D_n=\ell_n$, and take $Q=\mathrm{Per}(E)/|E|^{1/2}$ (finite and positive when the perimeter is finite, since then $0<|E|<\infty$); at $h=T$ insert $T=\epsilon\vartheta^3r_n^2/(1+G)$ and $r_n=\alpha_n\ell_n$. $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) Meaning of (a): up to the negligible source, the stopped value $Y$ is a bounded martingale, so its expected quadratic variation, that is, the expected time spent by the backward process weighted by $2|\nabla v_n|^2$, is at most $B_\vartheta^2-a_n^2$ up to the source term, with $B_\vartheta^2\asymp a_n^2/\vartheta^2$ (this is a bound in expectation, not on every path). In units of the core scale, the time-averaged occupation of $\ell_n^2|\nabla v_n|^2/a_n^2$ is $O(\vartheta^{-5}\alpha_n^{-2})$. The bound holds for every $\vartheta$; it restricts alternative (I), where by Lemma 7.39 the density keeps mass $\gtrsim\vartheta$ on the high set. (2) Meaning of (b): at no time before $t_n$ does the high set $\{v_n\ge a_n/4\}\cap\mathcal A_\vartheta$ contain a square of side larger than $128\ell_n/(3\kappa_0\vartheta)$, and $\{v_n\ge a_n/8\}\cap\mathcal A_\vartheta$ none of side larger than $256\ell_n/(3\kappa_0\vartheta)$. In the other direction there is only a two-level statement. Smoothness on the axis gives $\omega^\theta(0,z,s)=0$ and $|\partial_r\omega^\theta|\le\sqrt2H(t_n)$, so $|\omega^\theta(r,z,s)|\le\sqrt2H(t_n)r$, and on $\mathcal A_\vartheta$, for $s\le t_n$, $|\nabla v_n|\le|\nabla\omega^\theta|/r+|\omega^\theta|/r^2\le2\sqrt2H(t_n)/r\le\frac{2\sqrt2C_*}{c_a\vartheta}\frac{a_n}{\ell_n}$ by (A) and $a_n\ge c_aD_n/r_n$, uniformly in $\vartheta$. Hence, with $d_n=c_a\vartheta\ell_n/(16\sqrt2C_*)$, every point of $\{v_n(s)\ge a_n/4\}\cap\mathcal A_\vartheta$ at distance at least $d_n$ from $\partial\mathcal A_\vartheta$ is the centre of a disk of radius $d_n$ on which $v_n(s)\ge a_n/8$. This gives no lower bound for the width of $\{v_n\ge a_n/4\}$ itself (near a local maximum slightly above $a_n/4$ its component can be arbitrarily small), and the no-square property bounds inscribed squares only, not the length, number or topology of components. By Lemma 7.39 the high set reaches vertical distances far beyond $r_n$ in (I). (3) What is missing in (I). By (a) and Chebyshev's inequality, for each fixed $\delta>0$, $\frac2T\int_{T/2}^T\mathbb P\big(\tau>h,\ \ell_n|\nabla v_n(X_h,t_n-h)|\ge\delta a_n\big)\,dh\le(1+C_f\vartheta\nu_n^{1/3})C_D/(8e\delta^2\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2)$: in time average the killed density rarely visits regions where the gradient is of order $a_n/\ell_n$. This is not a bound for its unweighted occupation of the transition region $\{a_n/8<v_n<a_n/4\}$, since a value between the two thresholds can be attained where $v_n$ is flat. A sufficient missing estimate is a weighted coercivity bound: $\frac2T\int_{T/2}^T\int p_h\frac{\ell_n^2|\nabla v_n|^2}{a_n^2}\,d\mu\,dh\ge c(\vartheta)\frac2T\int_{T/2}^T\int_{E_h}p_h\,d\mu\,dh$ with $c(\vartheta)>0$ independent of $n$, where $E_h=\{v_n(t_n-h)\ge a_n/4\}\cap\mathcal A_\vartheta$. Under $J_n(\vartheta)\le a_n/4$, $\int p_hv_n\,d\mu\ge a_n/2$ and $v_n\le B_\vartheta$ give $\int_{E_h}p_h\,d\mu\ge a_n/(4B_\vartheta)\ge c_a\vartheta/(4\sqrt2)$, so such a bound would contradict (a) at that fixed $\vartheta$. A quantitative Harnack or hitting estimate for $p_h$ at the scale $\ell_n/\vartheta$, with constants uniform in $n$ and with control near the killed boundary, might give it together with (b), but it would have to be proved for this family: the Péclet number of the drift at the scale $\ell_n$ is $M_n\ell_n=1/\nu_n\to\infty$, and constants uniform in an unrestricted drift do not exist (the stationary solution $e^{bx_1}$ of $w_t-\Delta w+b\,\partial_{x_1}w=0$ has ratio $e^{bR}$ across a distance $R$). The density may concentrate, for instance, near the backward-attracting set of a strain. This is the point at which a dynamical argument for (I) is needed; none is given here. (4) *An energy form of Theorem 7.18.* In alternative (I) at a fixed $\vartheta\in(0,\frac14]$, for all large $n$,
$$\alpha_n^2\le C\vartheta^{-5}\big(\Lambda_n'+\vartheta^{-2}\big),\qquad\Lambda_n'=\max\Big\{1,\ \log\frac{C\,E_0}{\vartheta^{10}r_n^3M_n^2}\Big\},\qquad C=C(G,\kappa_0,C_*),$$
hence $r_n^3M_n^2\le CE_0\vartheta^{-10}\exp(\vartheta^{-2}-\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2/C)$, which sharpens the conclusion $r_n^5D_n^2=\alpha_n^2r_n^3M_n^2\to0$ of Theorem 7.12(I). Proof. Covering: let $m=c_a\vartheta D_n/24$ and $E_h=\{v_n(t_n-h)>a_n/24\}\cap\mathcal A_\vartheta$; on $E_h$ the signed vorticity $\operatorname{sgn}(\eta(x^0,t_n))\,\omega^\theta=rv_n$ exceeds $m$. By (A), $|\nabla\omega^\theta|\le\sqrt2H_n\le\sqrt2\bar H$ with $\bar H=C_*D_n^2/M_n$, so with $d=m/(2\sqrt2\bar H)$ (a fixed multiple of $\vartheta\ell_n$, hence $\le\vartheta r_n/4$ for large $n$) the signed vorticity exceeds $m/2$ on the disk $B_d(y)$ for every $y\in E_h$. For $\rho\le d$ the circulation of $(u^r,u^z)$ around $\partial B_\rho(y)$ has absolute value at least $\pi\rho^2m/2$, so by Cauchy-Schwarz $\int_{\partial B_\rho}|(u^r,u^z)|^2ds\ge\pi m^2\rho^3/8$ and $\iint_{B_d(y)}|(u^r,u^z)|^2dr\,dz\ge\pi m^2d^4/32$; the solid torus generated by $B_d(y)$ carries kinetic energy at least $3\pi^2R_ym^2d^4/128$, $R_y$ the radial coordinate of $y$. For a maximal family $y_i\in E_h$ with disjoint disks $B_d(y_i)$ the doubled disks cover $E_h$, $\mu(B_{2d}(y_i))\le6\pi d^2R_{y_i}$, and the tori are disjoint, so $\mu(E_h)\le256E_0/(\pi m^2d^2)\le C(\kappa_0)C_*^2E_0/(\vartheta^4M_n^2)=:V_*$. Persistence: on the bounded domain $\mathcal O_k$ of the proof of Theorem 7.18, with $p_h$ its Green function for $(t_n,x^0)$, $\int p_hv_n\,d\mu\ge a_n/3$ for $0<h\le T$. Let $\chi$ be smooth and nondecreasing with $\chi=0$ on $(-\infty,a_n/24]$, $\chi=1$ on $[a_n/12,\infty)$, $|\chi'|\le48/a_n$. Since $v(1-\chi(v))\le a_n/12$ for all $v$, $\int p_hv_n\chi(v_n)\,d\mu\ge a_n/4$, and the divergence identity of Lemma 7.16, applied to $p_h\chi(v_n)$ (which vanishes on $\partial\mathcal O_k$), gives (with $p_h$ extended by zero outside $\mathcal O_k$) $\frac{a_n}4\le\frac{M_n}{\vartheta r_n}\big(\int_{E_h}|\nabla p_h|\,d\mu+\frac{48}{a_n}\int p_h|\nabla v_n|\,d\mu\big)$. Integrate over $h\in[T/2,T]$. In step (iii) of the proof of Lemma 7.16 apply Hoelder on $E_h$ only ($\int_{E_h}p_h^{1-\beta}d\mu\le\mu(E_h)^\beta\le V_*^\beta$, using $\int p_h\,d\mu\le1$), and Cauchy-Schwarz on the space-time set $\{(h,y):y\in E_h\}$ with the entropy dissipation integral enlarged to the whole domain (so no localized entropy inequality is needed), which gives $\int_{T/2}^T\int_{E_h}|\nabla p_h|\,d\mu\,dh\le(eT\Lambda_n'/2)^{1/2}$ with $\Lambda_n'=\max\{1,\log(2KV_*/T)\}$, $K=C_NL^2/(\vartheta^3r_n)$; and since $p_h$ is dominated by the killed density of $\mathcal A_\vartheta$, (a) and Cauchy-Schwarz give $\int_{T/2}^T\int p_h|\nabla v_n|\,d\mu\,dh\le T^{1/2}D_n/(\vartheta r_n)$ for large $n$. With $a_n\ge c_aD_n/r_n$ and $T=\epsilon\vartheta^3r_n^2/(1+G)$ this yields $\alpha_n^2\le\frac{128(1+G)}{c_a^2\epsilon\vartheta^5}\big(e\Lambda_n'+\frac{4608}{c_a^2\vartheta^2}\big)$, and $2KV_*/T=CE_0/(\vartheta^{10}r_n^3M_n^2)$. Since $\alpha_n\to\infty$, $\Lambda_n'>1$ for large $n$, which gives the exponential bound. The two logarithms have no universal comparison: with $M_n=\alpha_n/(\nu_nr_n)$, $D_n=M_n^2\nu_n$ one has $\log(E_0/(r_n^3M_n^2))=\log(E_0\nu_n^2/(r_n\alpha_n^2))$, and for $\alpha_n=n$, $\nu_n=e^{-n^4}$ the choice $r_n=e^{-2n^4-\lambda n^2}$ gives $\log(r_nM_n)\sim n^4$ and $\log(E_0/(r_n^3M_n^2))=\lambda n^2+O(\log n)$, while $r_n=e^{-2n^4-n^6}$ gives the reverse order; both choices satisfy $r_n\nu_n^{-2}\to0$, $r_n^2M_n\to0$, $r_n\alpha_n^2\to0$, $\alpha_n\nu_n\to0$, $r_n^5D_n^2\to0$ and, for $\lambda\ge\lambda_0(\vartheta)$, both bounds. Together the two bounds leave a nonempty range of logarithmic record-scale parameters, although the energy bound excludes some parameters allowed by Theorem 7.18 (for instance $\alpha_n=n$, $\nu_n=e^{-n^2}$, $r_n=e^{-2n^2-n}$, where $\log(E_0/(r_n^3M_n^2))=n+O(\log n)$). These are record-scale parameter choices only; no time history, source or field is constructed. The covering, the cutoff identity and the constants were rechecked. (5) Source form of (4). In alternative (I) of Theorem 7.12 at a fixed $\vartheta$, fix $s_0\in(0,T_*)$ and put $\bar t_n=t_n-T/2$, $\bar{\mathcal B}_n=\mathcal B(s_0,\bar t_n)$ (Lemma 7.26) and $R_n=r_n^2D_n=\alpha_n^2/\nu_n$. Then, for all large $n$,
$$\alpha_n^2\le C_\vartheta\Big(1+\log_+\frac{\bar{\mathcal B}_n}{R_n}\Big),\qquad\text{hence}\qquad\bar{\mathcal B}_n\ge c_\vartheta\,R_n\,e^{c\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2},$$
with $c=c(G,\kappa_0,C_*,\epsilon)>0$ and $C_\vartheta,c_\vartheta>0$ depending also on $\vartheta$; no (L1$\eta$) assumption is used. Consequently, by Lemma 7.25(b), $R_ne^{c\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2}=o(S(\bar t_n))$, and since $S(\bar t_n)\le S_n\le(G^2C_*)^{1/3}D_n^{2/3}M_n^{-1/3}$ (Lemma 1.5(i) and (A) at $t_n$), $R_ne^{c\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2}=o(D_n^{2/3}M_n^{-1/3})$; and by Lemma 7.26(d), $K_-(s_0,t_n)\ge\frac13\big(\log R_n+c\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2\big)-C(s_0,\vartheta)$; likewise, by Lemma 7.46(c) below at $t=\bar t_n$ (with $Y(s_0)>0$), the swirl clock satisfies $\frac{\sqrt3}2\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2ds\ge\log R_n+c\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2-C(s_0,\vartheta)$, which exceeds the general requirement (7.46.1) by $2\log\alpha_n+c\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2-C$; this is again a necessary condition, not a contradiction, since no upper bound for the swirl clock over the history is proved. Proof. Repeat the proof of (4) with the covering step replaced as follows. Here $\mu$ is the measure $r\,dr\,dz$, so $dx=2\pi\,d\mu$, and $v_n=\operatorname{sgn}(\eta(x^0,t_n))\,\eta$. For large $n$, $t_n-T\ge s_0$ and $a_n\ge c_aD_n/r_n\ge48B_0$, $B_0=\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty$; then $(|\eta|-B_0)_+\ge|\eta|/2>a_n/48$ on $E_h$, and Lemma 7.26(a) gives, for $h\in[T/2,T]$, $\mu(E_h)\le\frac{24}{\pi a_n}\mathcal B(s_0,t_n-h)\le\frac{24}{\pi a_n}\bar{\mathcal B}_n=:V$, since $\mathcal B(s_0,\cdot)$ is nondecreasing. The rest of the proof of (4) uses only a bound $\mu(E_h)\le V$ valid for $h\in[T/2,T]$, and gives $\alpha_n^2\le\frac{128(1+G)}{c_a^2\epsilon\vartheta^5}\big(e\Lambda_n''+\frac{4608}{c_a^2\vartheta^2}\big)$ with $\Lambda_n''=\max\{1,\log(2KV/T)\}$; and $2KV/T=\frac{48C_NL^2(1+G)}{\pi\epsilon\vartheta^6}\frac{\bar{\mathcal B}_n}{r_n^3a_n}\le C\vartheta^{-6}\bar{\mathcal B}_n/R_n$. Since $\alpha_n\to\infty$, $\Lambda_n''>1$ for large $n$, which gives the lower bound for $\bar{\mathcal B}_n$; the consequences follow from $\mathcal B(s_0,t)\le B(t)$ and Lemma 7.25(b), and from $\sqrt3\,\mathcal B(s_0,t)\le Y(s_0)e^{3K_-(s_0,t)}$ and the monotonicity of $K_-(s_0,\cdot)$. Comparison: Lemma 7.26(b) gives only $\mathcal B(s_0,t_n)\gtrsim1/\nu_n=R_n/\alpha_n^2$, so (5) strengthens the source requirement by the factor $\alpha_n^2e^{c\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2}$ and places it before $\bar t_n$. Its scalar consequence $r_n^2(D_nM_n)^{1/3}e^{c\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2}\to0$ is weaker than (4) once the constant $c$ of (5) is taken no larger than the constant $1/C$ in the exponent of (4) (both come from the same entropy coefficient, so this choice is allowed), since $r_n^2(D_nM_n)^{1/3}/(r_n^3M_n^2)=\nu_n^{1/3}/X_n\to0$; in the logarithmic histories of 8.2 it holds for small $\kappa$. The new content is the lower bound on the actual source integral, which no estimate proved here bounds from above at the needed precision: an upper bound $\log_+(\bar{\mathcal B}_n/R_n)=o(\alpha_n^2)$ would exclude (I) at the scale $\vartheta$, but the available upper bound $\mathcal B=o(S)$ does not give it. The replacement of the covering step, the measure normalization and the constants of $2KV/T$ were rechecked. (6) Meaning of (c). In alternative (I), at every age $h\le T$ the set $\{v_n(t_n-h)>a_n/4\}\cap\mathcal A_\vartheta$ has perimeter $\gtrsim\vartheta^3h/\ell_n$ and isoperimetric ratio $\gtrsim\vartheta^2h^{1/2}/\ell_n$; for fixed $\vartheta$ and ages $h\in[cT,T]$ with $c>0$ fixed, its perimeter is $\gtrsim\vartheta^6\alpha_nr_n\gg r_n$ and its ratio $\gtrsim\vartheta^{7/2}\alpha_n\to\infty$ (as $\alpha_n\to\infty$ in Case 3), while by (b) it contains no square of side larger than $128\ell_n/(3\kappa_0\vartheta)$. A large ratio may come from elongation, boundary complexity or many components, so this is consistent with the tall thin high sets allowed by (b) and Lemma 7.39. The bounds contain no logarithm and use neither (A) nor any bound on the source beyond Theorem 7.12(I). Consequently alternative (I) fails at $\vartheta$ along any subsequence for which there are ages $h_n\in(0,T]$ with $h_n/\ell_n^2\to\infty$ at which $\{v_n(t_n-h_n)>a_n/4\}\cap\mathcal A_\vartheta$ has bounded isoperimetric ratio; no such bound is proved here. The argument is the counterpart, for the killed density, of the planar estimate in 8.3 (iii).

#### Proposition 7.41 (a logarithm-free persistence bound for passive scalars in affine drifts; a planar model of alternative (I)). Let $d\ge1$, $T>0$, let $b(x,s)=A(s)x+c(s)$ with $A:[0,T]\to\mathbb R^{d\times d}$ and $c:[0,T]\to\mathbb R^d$ continuous (no trace condition), let $f$ be bounded and continuous, and let $v$ be a bounded classical solution of $\partial_sv+b\cdot\nabla v=\Delta v+f$ on $\mathbb R^d\times[0,T]$ such that for every $s\in[0,T]$ and every smooth probability density $m$ with $\nabla m\in L^1$
$$\Big|\int m\,v(s)\,dx\Big|\le V\,\|\nabla m\|_{L^1}.\tag{C}$$
Put $E_f=\int_0^T\|f(s)\|_\infty ds$. Then:

(a) for every $y\in\mathbb R^d$ and $\kappa\in(0,1]$, $|v(y,T)|\le\sqrt{d/2}\,\big(\kappa\|v(0)\|_\infty+V/\sqrt{\kappa T}\big)+2E_f$;

(b) $\|v(T)\|_\infty\le C_dV/\sqrt T+2E_f$ with $C_d=\frac{3\sqrt3}2d^{3/4}$; the constant does not depend on the numerical bound $B$ on $\|v\|_\infty$ (qualitative boundedness of $v$ is used to pass to the limit in the dyadic iteration). Up to the constant, (b) is sharp: for $b=0$, $f=0$, $v(x,s)=Vk\,e^{-k^2s}\sin(kx_1)$ with $k=(2T)^{-1/2}$ satisfies (C) and $\|v(T)\|_\infty=e^{-1/2}V/\sqrt{2T}$;

(c) (model consequence) if $d=2$, $f=0$, $a>0$ and $v(y,T)\ge a/2$, then $T\le54\sqrt2\,V^2/a^2$; with the dictionary of alternative (I) ($V=M_n/(\vartheta r_n)$, $a=a_n\ge c_aD_n/r_n$, $c_a=\frac38\kappa_0$, $T=T_\vartheta=\epsilon\vartheta^3r_n^2/(1+G)$) this reads $\alpha_n^2\le54\sqrt2\,(1+G)/(\epsilon c_a^2\vartheta^5)$, the bound of Theorem 7.18 without the logarithm.

*Proof.* (a) Let $X$ be the backward diffusion from $(y',T)$, $dX_h=-b(X_h,T-h)\,dh+\sqrt2\,dW_h$, and let $P_{T,s}(y',\cdot)$ be the law of $X_{T-s}$. Itô's formula, localized and then passed to the limit using the boundedness of $v$ and $f$, gives $v(T)=P_{T,s}v(s)+\int_s^TP_{T,\tau}f(\tau)\,d\tau$. Let $F'=AF$, $F(0)=I$, $F_{\tau\leftarrow s}=F(\tau)F(s)^{-1}$. Then $P_{T,s}(y',\cdot)$ is Gaussian with mean $F_{s\leftarrow T}y'+\gamma(s)$, $\gamma(s)=-F(s)\int_s^TF(\tau)^{-1}c(\tau)\,d\tau$ (independent of $y'$) and covariance $P(s)=2\int_s^TF_{\tau\leftarrow s}^{-1}F_{\tau\leftarrow s}^{-T}d\tau$, independent of $y'$. Put $\Sigma=P(0)$ (positive definite) and $E(s)=F(s)\Sigma F(s)^T$. Since $\operatorname{tr}E(s)^{-1}=\operatorname{tr}(\Sigma^{-1}F(s)^{-1}F(s)^{-T})$,
$$\int_0^T\operatorname{tr}E(s)^{-1}ds=\operatorname{tr}\big(\Sigma^{-1}\tfrac12\Sigma\big)=\frac d2,\tag{7.41.1}$$
with no condition on $A$. Let $\rho=N(y,\kappa F(T)\Sigma F(T)^T)$ and $m_s=\int\rho(y')P_{T,s}(y',\cdot)\,dy'$. As $F_{s\leftarrow T}F(T)=F(s)$, $m_s$ is Gaussian with covariance $P(s)+\kappa E(s)$; in particular $m_0=N(\mu_0,(1+\kappa)\Sigma)$ and $p:=P_{T,0}(y,\cdot)=N(\mu_0,\Sigma)$ have the same mean, and Pinsker's inequality with the Gaussian relative entropy $\frac d2(\kappa-\log(1+\kappa))\le\frac d4\kappa^2$ gives $\|m_0-p\|_{L^1}\le\kappa\sqrt{d/2}$. Let $u$ be the bounded solution of the homogeneous equation with $u(0)=v(0)$; then $u(T)=P_{T,s}u(s)$ and $\|v(s)-u(s)\|_\infty\le\int_0^s\|f\|_\infty\le E_f$. Now $u(y,T)=\int p\,u(0)$ and $\int m_0u(0)=\int\rho\,u(T)=\int m_su(s)$ for every $s$, so by (C) applied to $m_s$,
$$|u(y,T)|\le\kappa\sqrt{d/2}\,\|v(0)\|_\infty+V\|\nabla m_s\|_{L^1}+E_f .$$
For a Gaussian density with covariance $C$ and $Y\sim N(0,C)$, $\|\nabla N(\cdot,C)\|_{L^1}=\mathbb E|C^{-1}Y|\le(\operatorname{tr}C^{-1})^{1/2}$, and $P(s)+\kappa E(s)\succeq\kappa E(s)$, so $\|\nabla m_s\|_{L^1}\le\kappa^{-1/2}(\operatorname{tr}E(s)^{-1})^{1/2}$. Averaging over $s\in[0,T]$ and using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and (7.41.1) gives $\frac1T\int_0^T\|\nabla m_s\|_{L^1}ds\le(d/(2\kappa T))^{1/2}$. With $|v(y,T)|\le|u(y,T)|+E_f$ this is (a).

(b) An affine drift restricted to a subinterval is affine, and (C) holds on it. Let $T_j=2^{-j}T$, $e_j=\int_{T_{j+1}}^{T_j}\|f\|_\infty$ and $N_j=\|v(T_j)\|_\infty$. Part (a) on $[T_{j+1},T_j]$ with $\kappa=1/(3\sqrt d)$ gives $N_j\le qN_{j+1}+KVT_j^{-1/2}+2e_j$ with $q=1/(3\sqrt2)$ and $K=\sqrt3d^{3/4}$. Iterating $k$ times, $N_0\le q^k\sup|v|+KVT^{-1/2}\sum_{j<k}(q\sqrt2)^j+2\sum_je_j$; since $q\sqrt2=\frac13$ and $q<1$, letting $k\to\infty$ gives (b). The example: $F=-Ve^{-k^2s}\cos(kx_1)$ satisfies $\partial_1F=v$ and $|F|\le V$, so $|\int mv|=|\int F\partial_1m|\le V\|\nabla m\|_{L^1}$.

(c) From (b), $a/2\le C_2V/\sqrt T$ with $C_2^2=\frac{27\sqrt2}2$, so $T\le4C_2^2V^2/a^2=54\sqrt2\,V^2/a^2$. Insert the dictionary: $\frac{c_a^2D_n^2}{4r_n^2}\le\frac{27\sqrt2}2\frac{M_n^2(1+G)}{\epsilon\vartheta^5r_n^4}$, that is $\alpha_n^2=r_n^2D_n^2/M_n^2\le54\sqrt2(1+G)/(\epsilon c_a^2\vartheta^5)$. $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) The proof tests (C) not on the thin backward densities $P_{T,s}(y,\cdot)$ of the record point (as Lemma 7.16 does, which produces the entropy logarithm) but on the backward evolutions $m_s$ of a needle $\rho$ at the record time, aligned with the forward noise covariance $F(T)\Sigma F(T)^T$; the identity (7.41.1) says that these evolutions are fat on average over the window. Passivity is used exactly in $\int\rho\,u(T)=\int m_su(s)$. (2) Proposition 7.21 is not a counterexample: there the data are prescribed at each time and are not transported by the drift (the forward flow $(x,-z)$ would squash its strip along $z$). (3) For alternative (I) itself the proposition does not apply: the first-order part of the operator relative to $\Delta_\mu$ is $(2/r-u^r,-u^z)\cdot\nabla$ (relative to the Euclidean Laplacian in $(r,z)$ it is $(3/r-u^r,-u^z)\cdot\nabla$), which is not affine, and the measure is $\mu=r\,dr\,dz$. Killing on leaving $\mathcal A_\vartheta$ does not by itself destroy the pairing: for the homogeneous Dirichlet evolution and its compatible killed adjoint, $\int m_sU(s)\,d\mu$ is conserved (Lemma 7.10 uses this). The obstructions are that killing replaces the Gaussian kernels by subprobability kernels, for which the identity (7.41.1) and the Gaussian comparison have not been proved, that the actual scalar has a source and nonzero boundary values, and that alternative (I) controls the inner-boundary term $J_n$ only for kernels issued from the original terminal point, not for the family of terminal points in the needle. The source can be absorbed on the annulus (not across the axis): with $E_{\rm ann}=\int_{t_n-T_\vartheta}^{t_n}\|f(s)\|_{L^\infty(\mathcal A_\vartheta)}ds$, the estimates of Theorem 7.12 give $E_{\rm ann}\le\frac G{8(1+G)}a_n<\frac{a_n}8$; in an application in which the hypotheses of Proposition 7.41 hold with $E_f\le E_{\rm ann}$ (the whole-space supremum in $E_f$ is not bounded by these estimates), it follows that $a_n/2-2E_f\ge a_n/4$, and the constant $54\sqrt2$ in (c) becomes $216\sqrt2$; this does not remove the obstructions just listed. Proposition 7.42 shows that the step from affine to general drifts is false for passive data. (4) The proof of (a), the source term, the dyadic iteration in (b) and the sharpness example were rechecked.

#### Proposition 7.42 (the logarithm cannot be removed for passive scalars in nonlinear drifts, even with the ratios controlled in Case 3). For every $A\ge256$ there are a smooth steady divergence-free drift $b_A$ on $\mathbb R^2$ with all derivatives bounded and a bounded smooth solution $v_A$ of $\partial_sv+b_A\cdot\nabla v=\Delta v$ on $\mathbb R^2\times[0,T_A]$, $T_A=A^{-1}e^{A/32}$, such that $|v_A|\le1$, (C) holds with $V=\frac12$ at every $s\in[0,T_A]$, and $v_A(0,0,T_A)>\frac12$. With $M_A=\sup|b_A|$, $D_A=\sup|\nabla b_A|$, $H_A=\sup|\nabla^2b_A|$, $\ell_A=M_A/D_A$ and $\mathrm{Pe}_A=M_A\ell_A$: $M_AH_A\le(4+o(1))D_A^2$, $\ell_A\in[\frac1{4\pi}-o(1),\frac1\pi+o(1)]$, and $T_A/\ell_A^2\ge c\log\mathrm{Pe}_A/\log\log\mathrm{Pe}_A\to\infty$ as $A\to\infty$, with an absolute $c>0$ (here $\chi$ below is fixed independently of $A$, the $o(1)$ terms refer to $A\to\infty$ for this fixed $\chi$, persistence holds for every $A\ge256$ and the asymptotic comparisons for $A$ large). The rescaled scalar $\tilde v_A=D_Av_A$ satisfies $|\tilde v_A|\le D_A$, $\tilde v_A(0,0,T_A)>D_A/2$ and (C) with constant $D_A/2\le(2\pi+o(1))M_A$.

*Proof.* Fix an even $\chi\in C_c^\infty((-2,2))$, $0\le\chi\le1$, $\chi=1$ on $[-1,1]$, let $R_A=10\sqrt{T_A}\,e^{2\pi AT_A}$, $\psi_A=A\sin(2\pi x)\,z\,\chi(z/R_A)$, $b_A=(\partial_z\psi_A,-\partial_x\psi_A)$ and $v_A(\cdot,0)=\cos(\pi x)$. The drift is $1$-periodic in $x$, so by translation invariance and uniqueness of bounded solutions $v_A(x+1,z,s)=-v_A(x,z,s)$. Hence $F_A=\frac12\int_{x-1}^xv_A(q,z,s)\,dq$ satisfies $\partial_xF_A=v_A$ and $|F_A|\le\frac12$, and integration by parts gives (C) with $V=\frac12$ at every slice. On $|z|\le R_A$, $b_A=(A\sin(2\pi x),-2\pi A\cos(2\pi x)z)$, so the backward process from the origin solves $dX=-A\sin(2\pi X)dh+\sqrt2dW^1$, $dZ=2\pi A\cos(2\pi X)Z\,dh+\sqrt2dW^2$ until $|Z|=R_A$. With $H(x)=e^{Ax^2}$ and $x\sin(2\pi x)\ge4x^2$ on $|x|\le\frac14$, $(\partial_{xx}-A\sin(2\pi x)\partial_x)H\le2A$ there (from $(1-2u)e^u\le1$), so the probability of reaching $|X|=\frac14$ before $T_A$ and before $|Z|=R_A$ is at most $(1+2AT_A)e^{-A/16}\le3e^{-A/32}$. Gronwall gives $\sup_{h\le T_A}|Z_h|\le\sqrt2e^{2\pi AT_A}\sup_{h\le T_A}|W^2_h|$ before the exit, so the probability of reaching $|Z|=R_A$ before $T_A$ is at most $4\exp(-R_A^2e^{-4\pi AT_A}/(4T_A))=4e^{-25}$. On the remaining event $\cos(\pi X_{T_A})\ge2^{-1/2}$, so $v_A(0,0,T_A)\ge2^{-1/2}-(1+2^{-1/2})(3e^{-A/32}+4e^{-25})>\frac12$. For the ratios write $R=R_A$: the second component of $b_A$ is $-2\pi A\cos(2\pi x)z\chi(z/R)$ and the first is $O(A)$, so $2\pi AR\le M_A\le4\pi AR+CA$; the derivative $4\pi^2A\sin(2\pi x)z\chi$ dominates, so $4\pi^2AR\le D_A\le8\pi^2AR+CA$; similarly $8\pi^3AR\le H_A\le16\pi^3AR+CA$, where $C$ depends on $\chi$ only. This gives the bounds on $M_AH_A/D_A^2$ and $\ell_A$, and $\log\mathrm{Pe}_A=2\pi AT_A+O(A)$ with $A=32\log(AT_A)$, so $T_A=(1+o(1))\log\mathrm{Pe}_A/(2\pi A)$ and $A=(32+o(1))\log\log\mathrm{Pe}_A$. $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) Thus the whole-space passive bounds listed here (passivity, (C) at every slice, $|b|\le M$, $|\nabla b|\le D$, a bounded ratio $MH/D^2$, and $a\gtrsim D$, $B\lesssim D$, $V\lesssim M$ after amplitude scaling) are compatible with persistence for $T\asymp\ell^2\log\mathrm{Pe}/\log\log\mathrm{Pe}$; in fact, for fixed $\chi$, $\ell_A\to1/(2\pi)$, $M_AH_A/D_A^2\to1$ and $T_A/\ell_A^2=(\pi/16+o(1))\log\mathrm{Pe}_A/\log\log\mathrm{Pe}_A$. Under the formal dictionary $T/\ell^2=\epsilon\vartheta^3\alpha^2/(1+G)$, $\mathrm{Pe}=1/\nu$, this corresponds to $\alpha_n^2$ growing like $\log(1/\nu_n)/\log\log(1/\nu_n)$; it is not a construction of alternative (I) (the drift is not a Navier-Stokes velocity, has no active coupling and infinite energy, and no $J_n$ or annulus is involved). A logarithm-free exclusion of (I) must use additional structure not captured by these passive estimates; possible sources include the exact coupling $r\eta=\omega^\theta=\partial_zu^r-\partial_ru^z$ and its evolution (here the drift vorticity is $\Delta\psi_A=A\sin(2\pi x)(g''-4\pi^2g)$ with $g(z)=z\chi(z/R_A)$, equal to $-4\pi^2A\sin(2\pi x)z$ on $|z|\le R_A$, and is not $v_A$), energy constraints, and the weighted annular geometry; no sufficiency claim for any one of them is proved. The mechanism is spatially nonlinear: the direction of expansion alternates across the cells, which no single affine map provides. (2) Geometry: the expansion in the example is in the direction $z$, which is unbounded also in the strip $\mathcal A_\vartheta$, while the confinement is in the direction $x$, which would correspond to $r$; the transfer to the meridional annulus with killing at $r=\vartheta r_n$ and $r=Lr_n$ has not been carried out. In the same terms the logarithm of Theorem 7.18 is the logarithm of the reachable vertical extent $k\asymp M_nT$ in units of the diffusive scale (Lemma 7.38); the example illustrates how a very large available vertical extent permits long passive persistence, but it neither proves saturation of the spread bound of Lemma 7.38 nor shows that the full logarithmic loss is sharp. (3) On $|z|\le R_A$ the drift is a steady Euler flow (its vorticity is $-4\pi^2\psi_A$), but not a Navier-Stokes flow, and the persistent scalar is not its vorticity. (4) The antiperiodicity, the two exit estimates, the final inequality and the ratio computation were checked. (5) In the same cell the active analogue of the trap does not persist, in the following reduced model. The fields $u=(F(x,t),\,-F_x(x,t)z+V(x,t))$ on $\mathbb R^2$ are divergence free with vorticity $V_x-F_{xx}z$, and they solve the two-dimensional Navier-Stokes equations exactly when $F_{xt}+FF_{xx}-F_x^2-F_{xxx}$ and $V_t+FV_x-F_xV-V_{xx}$ depend only on $t$ (the first is the Proudman-Johnson equation); then $w=V_x$, the vorticity of the shear riding on the cell, solves $w_t+Fw_x-F_{xx}V=w_{xx}$, which is the passive equation of the proposition plus the term $-F_{xx}V$. For $F=A\sin(2\pi x)$, $V=c(t)\sin(2\pi x)$ solves the second equation with $c'=-4\pi^2c$ for every amplitude history $A(t)$ (the whole field is an exact unforced Navier-Stokes solution precisely when $A'=-4\pi^2A$; a nonzero frozen amplitude requires a spatially varying force), so $w$ decays at the viscous rate $4\pi^2$ of the cell uniformly in $A$, against the persistence of the passive scalar through $T_A=A^{-1}e^{A/32}$ proved in the proposition.

On the antiperiodic class of the datum $\cos(\pi x)$ the decay is proved for every amplitude history $A\ge0$ that is measurable and locally bounded: let $V$ be the $2$-periodic solution of $V_t=V_{xx}-FV_x+F_xV$ with $V(x,0)=\sin(\pi x)/\pi$; by the symmetries of the coefficients and uniqueness, $V$ stays odd, antiperiodic under $x\mapsto x+1$ and even about $x=1/2$, so $V=\int_0^xw$, $V(0,t)=0$ and $V_x(1/2,t)=0$. The function $W=e^{-\pi^2t}\sin(\pi x)/\pi$ satisfies the same boundary conditions and $W_t-(W_{xx}-FW_x+F_xW)=2\pi A(t)\sin^2(\pi x)\,W\ge0$; after multiplication of both by $\exp(-2\pi\int_0^tA)$ the zeroth-order coefficient becomes $F_x-2\pi A\le0$, so the comparison principle on $[0,1/2]$ (Dirichlet at $0$, Neumann at $1/2$) gives $0\le V\le W$, and the derivatives at the common zero $x=0$ give $0\le w(0,t)\le e^{-\pi^2t}$, uniformly in $A(\cdot)$. The same $W$ and the same argument apply to every prescribed odd $1$-periodic smooth cell $F$ with $F/\sin(\pi x)$ nonincreasing on $(0,\frac12]$ (multiply by $\exp(-\int_0^t\sup_x|F_x(x,s)|\,ds)$ instead, for a cell with locally bounded $F_x$), because then $W_t-(W_{xx}-FW_x+F_xW)=-\frac{e^{-\pi^2t}}\pi\sin^2(\pi x)\,\partial_x\big(F/\sin(\pi x)\big)\ge0$; the sinusoid satisfies this condition, with $F/\sin(\pi x)=2A\cos(\pi x)$.

Spectrally, the frozen-cell generator acts on $\cos(n\pi x)$, $n$ odd, as $-n^2\pi^2\cos(n\pi x)+\frac{A\pi}2(n-\frac{4\epsilon}n)[\cos((n-2)\pi x)-\cos((n+2)\pi x)]$ (with $\cos(-\pi x)=\cos(\pi x)$), with $\epsilon=1$ for the active and $\epsilon=0$ for the passive equation; the slowest decay rates of this truncated tridiagonal system, computed in 60-digit arithmetic and unchanged when the number of modes is halved, are $34.30$, $38.91$, $39.4727$, $39.4784$ (active) against $2.246$, $0.3508$, $0.004566$, $1.02\cdot10^{-15}$ (passive) at $A=8,16,32,128$. The active rates lie in $[\pi^2,4\pi^2)$ for every frozen $A\ge0$ (the lower bound by the comparison above, the upper bound because the operator $V\mapsto V_{xx}-FV_x+F_xV$ is self-adjoint with weight $e^{-\int_0^xF}$ and replacing the Dirichlet condition at $x=1/2$, under which $\sin(2\pi x)$ is the principal eigenfunction, by the Neumann condition strictly lowers the decay rate), and the computed values approach $4\pi^2$.

*Passive rates.* The passive rates satisfy $1/u(0)\le\mu_{\rm p}(A)\le1/(u(0)-2\vartheta)$ whenever $u(0)>2\vartheta$ (for instance for $A\ge8$), with $u(0)=\int_0^{1/2}e^{\Phi(y)}\int_0^ye^{-\Phi(z)}\,dz\,dy=\frac14I_0(\frac A{2\pi})^2-\vartheta$, $0\le\vartheta\le\frac18$, where $\Phi(x)=\frac A{2\pi}(1-\cos2\pi x)$ and $I_0$ is the modified Bessel function, so that $\mu_{\rm p}(A)=4Ae^{-A/\pi}(1-\frac\pi{2A}+O(A^{-2}))$, $A/\pi$ being the barrier height $U(1/2)-U(0)$ of the potential $U=-\frac A{2\pi}\cos(2\pi x)$. Proof: by the same symmetries the passive antiperiodic class reduces to $w\mapsto w''-Fw'=\frac1m(mw')'$, $m=e^{-\Phi}$, on $(0,\frac12)$ with $w'(0)=0$ and $w(\frac12)=0$. The expected exit time $u(x)=\int_x^{1/2}e^{\Phi(y)}\int_0^ye^{-\Phi(z)}\,dz\,dy$ solves $(mu')'=-m$ with the same boundary conditions and is decreasing, so integrating $(m(w_1u'-w_1'u))'$ over $(0,\frac12)$, with $w_1>0$ the principal eigenfunction, gives $\mu_{\rm p}\int mw_1u=\int mw_1$, hence $\mu_{\rm p}\ge1/u(0)$. Next, $\int_0^{1/2}e^{\pm\Phi}=\frac12e^{\pm A/(2\pi)}I_0(\frac A{2\pi})$; the product of these two integrals is the integral of $e^{\Phi(y)-\Phi(z)}$ over $[0,\frac12]^2$, whose part $z<y$ is $u(0)$, and on the part $z>y$, of area $\frac18$, the integrand lies in $(0,1]$ because $\Phi$ is increasing, so $\vartheta=\int_0^{1/2}e^{\Phi(y)}\big(\int_y^{1/2}m(z)\,dz\big)\,dy\in[0,\frac18]$. Conversely $\mu_{\rm p}$ is at most the Rayleigh quotient $\int mu'^2/\int mu^2=\int mu/\int mu^2$. With $d=u(0)-u\ge0$ and $\bar m=\int_0^{1/2}m$, Fubini gives $\int md=\int_0^{1/2}e^{\Phi(y)}\big(\int_0^ym\big)\big(\int_y^{1/2}m\big)\,dy\le\bar m\vartheta$, so $\int mu^2\ge u(0)^2\bar m-2u(0)\int md\ge\bar m\,u(0)(u(0)-2\vartheta)$, while $\int mu\le u(0)\bar m$; hence $\mu_{\rm p}\le1/(u(0)-2\vartheta)$, and $u(0)\asymp e^{A/\pi}/A$ gives the asymptotic form. The computed rates satisfy $\mu_{\rm p}u(0)-1=0.050$ and $0.0068$ at $A=8,16$ (confirmed by an independent shooting computation for the one-dimensional problem, which also reproduces the value at $A=32$ below), and a recomputation in $60$-digit arithmetic (with $40$ and $80$ modes at $A=32$, and $80$ and $120$ modes at $A=128$, where $40$ modes do not suffice) gives $\mu_{\rm p}u(0)-1=6.3\cdot10^{-5}$ at $A=32$ and $5.4\cdot10^{-18}$ at $A=128$, below the proved bound $2\vartheta/(u(0)-2\vartheta)$, which is at most $1.2\cdot10^{-3}$ and $2.6\cdot10^{-16}$ there. For an odd $1$-periodic cell $F=Af$ with $f>0$ on $(0,\frac12)$, $f'(0)>0$ and $f'(\frac12)<0$, the same argument and Laplace's method give $\mu_{\rm p}(A)\sim\frac2\pi A\sqrt{f'(0)|f'(\frac12)|}\,e^{-A\int_0^{1/2}f}$. These rates concern the uncut frozen one-dimensional cell: the proposition proves persistence through $T_A$ by stopped-process estimates, which is a lower bound on the persistence time of its cut-off example, whose sharp persistence scale is not determined. For the uncut frozen cell, $1/\mu_{\rm p}\sim e^{A/\pi}/(4A)$ is much longer than $T_A=A^{-1}e^{A/32}$, so $T_A$ is far from sharp there.

*Dependence on the cell shape.* The destruction depends on the shape of the prescribed cell: for $F=A(\sin(2\pi x)-\sin(6\pi x)/27)$, whose strain is flat at the stagnation line ($F_{xxx}(0)=0$), the slowest active antiperiodic rates are $24.05$, $8.583$, $2.370$ at $A=32,128,512$; the last two lie below $\pi^2$, as they may, since this profile violates the condition above near $x=0$ ($F/\sin(\pi x)$ increases there); they decrease across these samples (for small $A$ the rate increases from its value $\pi^2$ at $A=0$), which suggests slower decay at large amplitude, but monotonicity is not proved (the large-amplitude law proved below shows that the rate tends to $0$ like $\frac{81\pi^3}{2A}$). Along a smooth odd Proudman-Johnson evolution $\partial_tF_{xxx}(0,t)=F_{xxxxx}(0,t)$ (differentiate the Proudman-Johnson equation twice in $x$ and use $F(0)=F_{xx}(0)=0$), which equals $-256\pi^5A\neq0$ for this profile, so its flatness is lost immediately. For a second flat profile, $f=\sin(2\pi x)+\frac16\sin^3(2\pi x)+\sin^5(2\pi x)$ ($f'''(0)=0$; it also violates the condition above near $x=0$), the frozen active antiperiodic operator with $F=Af$ has a positive principal eigenvalue, about $70.6$, $67.0$, $24.0$, $6.5$ at $A=32,128,512,2048$, so for this prescribed frozen cell the shear mode grows at these amplitudes and, by the law below, at every sufficiently large amplitude.

*Large-amplitude law.* For odd profiles with $f'(0)>0$, $f>0$ on $(0,\frac12)$ and $f'(\frac12)<0$ the principal eigenvalue of the frozen operator with $F=Af$ obeys the large-amplitude law $\lambda_1(A)=\frac{f'''(0)}{f'(0)}+\frac{f'(0)f^{(5)}(0)-f'''(0)^2}{2Af'(0)^3}+O(A^{-2})$, proved below under explicit regularity assumptions. Heuristically it is a harmonic-oscillator expansion at the stagnation line: the substitution $V=e^{\Phi/2}y$, $\Phi'=F$, turns the operator into $y\mapsto y''-(\frac14F^2-\frac32F_x)y$ with $y(0)=0$ and $y'(\frac12)+\frac12F(\frac12)y(\frac12)=0$, and the lowest Dirichlet level $\frac32F_x(0)$ of the half-line oscillator $-y''+\frac14F_x(0)^2x^2y$ cancels the constant $-\frac32F_x(0)$, so the limit is the first-order correction from the cubic term of $F$. The values are: $-4\pi^2$ for the sinusoid, for which $L\sin(2\pi x)=-4\pi^2\sin(2\pi x)$ exactly, $L$ the frozen operator, the Neumann condition failing only at $x=\frac12$ (all higher coefficients of the expansion below vanish, so $\lambda_1(A)=-4\pi^2+O(A^{-N})$ for every $N$, while $\lambda_1(A)>-4\pi^2$ at every finite $A$ by the Dirichlet-Neumann comparison above); $0$ for the first flat profile above, with next term $-81\pi^3/(2A)$, so that its frozen antiperiodic shear mode decays, at the rate $\frac{81\pi^3}{2A}+O(A^{-2})$, for all large $A$ (the computed decay rates are $2.370$ and $0.608$ at $A=512,2048$, so $A$ times the rate approaches $81\pi^3/2\approx1255.75$); $0$ for the second flat profile, with next term $444\pi^3/A$, about $26.9$ and $6.7$ at $A=512,2048$ against the computed $24.0$ and $6.5$; and $\frac{68}7\pi^2\approx95.88$ for $f=\sin(2\pi x)-\frac1{10}\sin(6\pi x)$, for which the computed principal eigenvalues are positive: $3.44$, $55.4$, $83.4$ at $A=32,128,512$; for this profile a shooting computation (a Riccati formulation integrated from both ends by an eighth-order Runge-Kutta scheme and matched at the turning point of the oscillator) gives $A^2\big(\lambda_1-\frac{68}7\pi^2+\frac{76800\pi^3}{343A}\big)=2.82\cdot10^5$, $3.00\cdot10^5$, $3.05\cdot10^5$, $3.07\cdot10^5$ at $A=512,2048,8192,32768$, against the second coefficient $\mu_2=\frac{52992000}{16807}\pi^4\approx3.071\cdot10^5$ of the expansion below, and the next scaled remainder, about $-1.40\cdot10^7$, matches $\mu_3=-\frac{37735680000}{823543}\pi^5\approx-1.402\cdot10^7$. The law is proved in two steps.

*Leading term.* If $f$ is of class $C^2$ on $[0,\frac12]$ and extends to an odd function of class $C^5$ near $0$, $f>0$ on $(0,\frac12)$, $f'(0)>0$ and $f'(\frac12)<0$, then $\lambda_1(A)\to g_0:=f'''(0)/f'(0)$ as $A\to\infty$, and $\lambda_1(A)\ge g_0-C/A$ for $A\ge1$. Proof. Put $g=f''/f$ on $(0,\frac12)$, so that $g(x)=g_0+O(x^2)$ near $0$ and $fg=f''$ is bounded. For $\psi=fh$ with $h=\exp\int_0^x\rho$ one has $L\psi=(g+2\rho f'/f+\rho'+\rho^2-Af\rho)\psi$; in particular $Lf=f''$ for every $A$. Lower bound: $\lambda_1$ is the supremum of $\int m(F'V^2-V'^2)/\int mV^2$, $m=e^{-\Phi}$, over $V\in H^1(0,\frac12)$ with $V(0)=0$ (the Neumann condition is natural). The trial function $V=f$ is admissible, and since $f(0)=0$ an integration by parts turns its quotient into $\int mf^2g/\int mf^2$ plus the boundary term $-m(\frac12)f(\frac12)f'(\frac12)/\int mf^2\ge0$ (which vanishes for odd $1$-periodic cells, where $f(\frac12)=0$); as $\Phi'=F>0$ on $(0,\frac12)$ and $\Phi(x)\asymp Ax^2$ near $0$, the weight $mf^2$ concentrates at $0$ on the scale $A^{-1/2}$, and the quotient is $g_0+O(1/A)$. Upper bound, by a Barta inequality: if $\psi>0$ on $(0,\frac12]$, $\psi=f$ near $0$, $\psi$ is constant near $\frac12$ and $L\psi\le\mu\psi$ on $(0,\frac12)$ in the sense of distributions, then integrating $(m(V_1\psi'-V_1'\psi))'=mV_1(L\psi-\lambda_1\psi)$ over $(0,\frac12)$, with $V_1>0$ the principal eigenfunction, gives $\lambda_1\le\mu$, because both boundary terms vanish. Fix small $\delta,\delta'>0$ with $2\delta<\frac12-\delta'$ and $f'\le\frac12f'(\frac12)$ on $[\frac12-\delta',\frac12]$. Take $\rho=\kappa\theta/A+\lambda\theta_2$, where $\theta$ is a smooth cutoff vanishing on $[0,\delta]$ and equal to $1$ on $[2\delta,\frac12]$, $\theta_2$ is a nonzero smooth bump with values in $[0,1]$ supported in $[\frac12-\delta',\frac12-\frac{\delta'}2]$, and $\kappa,\lambda$ are large constants independent of $A$. With $K=3\max f$ put $\psi=\min(fh,K)$ on $[0,\frac12-\frac{\delta'}2]$ and $\psi=K$ on $[\frac12-\frac{\delta'}2,\frac12]$. For large $A$: $fh<K$ on $[0,\frac12-\delta']$, and $fh>K$ at $\frac12-\frac{\delta'}2$ by the choice of $\lambda$; on $[\delta,\frac12-\frac{\delta'}2]$, where $f$ is bounded below, the term $-Af\rho$ dominates $\rho'+\rho^2+2\rho f'/f$ up to $O(1/A)$ (using $|\theta_2'|^2\le2\|\theta_2''\|_\infty\theta_2$) and, where $\theta=1$, also $g-g_0$; on $[0,2\delta]$, $g\le g_0+C\delta^2$; where $\psi=K$, $LK/K=Af'\le\frac12Af'(\frac12)<g_0$; and the minimum is taken only where both branches satisfy the inequality, so by Kato's inequality it satisfies it too. Hence $\lambda_1(A)\le g_0+C\delta^2+C_\delta/A$; let $A\to\infty$ and then $\delta\to0$. The condition $f'(\frac12)<0$ can be relaxed to $f'\le0$ near $\frac12$ (replace the cap $K$ by $K\cos(\omega(\frac12-x))$ with $\omega^2\ge-g_0$, for which $LP/P\le-\omega^2$), but it cannot be dropped: in the variable $u=e^{-\Phi/2}V$ the quadratic form becomes $\int((\frac32Af'-\frac14A^2f^2)u^2-u'^2)$, and for $p(t)=1+t^2$ the form $\int((3t-\frac14p^2)u^2-u'^2)\,dt$ equals $\frac5{16}\int u^2$ at $u=e^{-(t-1)^2/2}$; adding to a smooth profile $f_b$ that is positive on $(0,\frac12)$ and so flat at $\frac12$ that $|f_b|+|f_b'|\le\exp(-e^{1/(1/2-x)})$ near $\frac12$ the rescaled copies $e^{-4^n}p(2^{3n}(x-x_n))$, $x_n=\frac12-2^{-n}$, each multiplied by a smooth cutoff supported in $|x-x_n|\le2^{-n-2}$ and equal to $1$ on $|x-x_n|\le2^{-n-3}$ (the supports are disjoint, the sum converges in every $C^k$ because $e^{-4^n}$ beats every power of $2^n$, and on the scale $2^{-3n}$ of the trial function $u$, itself cut off, $A_nf_b$ and $A_nf_b'$ are negligible) therefore gives a $C^\infty$ profile with $f'(\frac12)=0$ and $\lambda_1(A_n)\ge c\,2^{6n}$ along $A_n=2^{3n}e^{4^n}$.

*Next terms.* Suppose in addition that $f$ is of class $C^4$ on $(0,\frac12)$ and that its odd extension is of class $C^7$ near $0$; then $\lambda_1(A)=g_0+\mu_1/A+O(A^{-2})$ with $\mu_1=\frac{f'(0)f^{(5)}(0)-f'''(0)^2}{2f'(0)^3}$. Proof. One has $L(fW)=f''W+2f'W'+fW''-Af^2W'$. Put $W_1'=(g-g_0)/f$ and $W_2'=\big((g-g_0)W_1+2\frac{f'}fW_1'+W_1''-\mu_1\big)/f$, with $W_1(0)=W_2(0)=0$ and $\mu_1:=3W_1''(0)$. Near $0$ Hadamard's lemma ($u(x)/x=\int_0^1u'(tx)\,dt$ when $u(0)=0$, which costs one derivative and reverses parity) shows that $g$ is even of class $C^4$ with $g-g_0=O(x^2)$, so $W_1'$ is odd of class $C^3$; then $\frac{f'}fW_1'$ is even of class $C^2$ with value $W_1''(0)$ at $0$, so the numerator of $W_2'$ is even of class $C^2$ and vanishes at $0$ by the choice of $\mu_1$, and $W_2'$ is odd of class $C^1$; on $(0,\frac12)$ the interior regularity gives $W_2\in C^2$. Writing $f=ax+bx^3+cx^5+O(x^7)$ one finds $W_1'=(\frac{20c}{a^2}-\frac{6b^2}{a^3})x+O(x^3)$, which gives the stated value of $\mu_1$. For $\psi_{\rm out}=f(1+W_1/A+W_2/A^2)$ the orders $A^0$ and $A^{-1}$ cancel and $L\psi_{\rm out}-(g_0+\frac{\mu_1}A)\psi_{\rm out}=\frac f{A^2}\big(R-\frac{\mu_1}AW_2\big)$, where $R=(g-g_0)W_2+2\frac{f'}fW_2'+W_2''-\mu_1W_1$ is bounded on $[0,\frac12-\eta]$ for each $\eta>0$; since $\psi_{\rm out}\ge f/2$ there for large $A$, $|L\psi_{\rm out}/\psi_{\rm out}-g_0-\mu_1/A|\le C_\eta A^{-2}$ on $[0,\frac12-\eta]$. Upper bound: in the construction above put $\rho=\lambda\theta_2$ with $\theta_2=\exp(-\frac1s-\frac1{\delta'/2-s})$ for $s=x-\frac12+\delta'\in(0,\frac{\delta'}2)$ and $\theta_2=0$ elsewhere, and $\psi=\min(\psi_{\rm out}h,K)$ on $[0,\frac12-\frac{\delta'}2]$, $\psi=K$ beyond. On the support of $\theta_2$, where $f\ge f_*>0$, put $B=\frac12Af_*$; then $2\rho\psi_{\rm out}'/\psi_{\rm out}+\rho^2\le B\rho$ for large $A$, and, since $\theta_2'/\theta_2\le s^{-2}$, $\rho'\le B\rho$ where $s\ge B^{-1/2}$, while $\rho'\le\lambda s^{-2}e^{-1/s}\le\lambda Be^{-\sqrt B}$ where $s<B^{-1/2}$ (for $B\ge4$); with $Af\ge2B$ this gives $L(\psi_{\rm out}h)\le(g_0+\mu_1/A+CA^{-2})\psi_{\rm out}h$ on $[0,\frac12-\frac{\delta'}2]$ (the estimate of the previous sentence applied with $\eta=\frac{\delta'}2$); the constant branch and the minimum are handled as before, so $\lambda_1\le g_0+\mu_1/A+CA^{-2}$. Lower bound: with $\delta'<\frac14$ and a smooth cutoff $\chi$ equal to $1$ on $[0,\frac12-2\delta']$ and to $0$ on $[\frac12-\delta',\frac12]$, the trial function $V=\chi\psi_{\rm out}$ satisfies $\int m(F'V^2-V'^2)=\int m\chi^2\psi_{\rm out}L\psi_{\rm out}-\int m\chi'^2\psi_{\rm out}^2$ (integrate by parts; all boundary terms vanish); the last integral is at most $Ce^{-cA}$ with $c=\int_0^{1/2-2\delta'}f$, while $\int mV^2\ge c'A^{-3/2}$, so the quotient is at least $g_0+\mu_1/A-CA^{-2}$. The same construction carried to order $N+1$, with $W_0=1$, $W_{k+1}'=\big((g-g_0)W_k+2\frac{f'}fW_k'+W_k''-\sum_{j=1}^k\mu_jW_{k-j}\big)/f$, $W_{k+1}(0)=0$ and $\mu_k=3W_k''(0)$, the trial function and supersolution built from $f\sum_{k\le N+1}A^{-k}W_k$, gives for $f$ of class $C^\infty$ on $(0,\frac12)$ with a $C^\infty$ odd extension at $0$ the complete asymptotic expansion $\lambda_1(A)=\sum_{k=0}^N\mu_kA^{-k}+O_N(A^{-N-1})$ for every $N$, because the localization errors are smaller than every power of $1/A$; $\mu_k$ depends only on the derivatives of $f$ at $0$ up to order $2k+3$, and $\mu_2=\frac{a^2f^{(7)}(0)-5af'''(0)f^{(5)}(0)+4f'''(0)^3}{8a^5}$ with $a=f'(0)$. The expansion is asymptotic; its convergence is not claimed. The coefficients were also computed by a symbolic Taylor recursion for the four profiles above (all $\mu_k=0$, $k\ge1$, for the sinusoid; $\mu_2=\frac{3645}{16}\pi^4$ for the first flat profile and $-8220\pi^4$ for the second), and the shooting values quoted above confirm $\mu_2$ and $\mu_3$ for $f=\sin(2\pi x)-\frac1{10}\sin(6\pi x)$, whose first coefficients are $\mu_0=\frac{68}7\pi^2$ and $\mu_1=-\frac{76800}{343}\pi^3$. When $f'''(0)\ne0$ its sign thus decides, at large frozen amplitude, between decay and growth of the shear mode; when $f'''(0)=0$ the sign of $f^{(5)}(0)$ decides, and in general the sign of the first nonzero coefficient $\mu_k$.

*Coupled class.* In the coupled class, with $F$ evolving by the Proudman-Johnson equation from $F(\cdot,0)=A_0f$, $f$ the second flat profile, and $w(\cdot,0)=\cos(\pi x)$, the cell itself decays in the computed runs ($\max_xF(x,t)$ decreases at every sampled time), but, after a brief initial decrease (at $t=0$ one has $w_t(0,0)=w_{xx}(0,0)=-\pi^2$, since $F$ and $F_{xx}$ vanish at $x=0$), $w(0,t)$ rises to about $2.1$, $28.7$ and $5.4\cdot10^4$ times its initial value at $A_0=32,64,128$ (at $t\approx0.016$, $0.021$, $0.027$) and then decays, at a rate that approaches $\pi^2$ after a faster transient; the amplification comes from the term $F_{xx}V$ in the equation for $w$, which is the vertical advection by the shear velocity $V$ of the vorticity $-F_{xx}z$ of the cell, unbounded in $z$ in this class (in a flow with bounded vorticity the maximum principle caps $|\omega|$ by its initial supremum); this term vanishes on the stagnation line $x=0$, where $w_t=w_{xx}$, so it acts beside the line, and diffusion carries its effect to the line. So outside the condition above some prescribed cells have growing modes (proved at every sufficiently large amplitude for the second flat profile, computed at the sampled amplitudes), and the sampled coupled evolutions show substantial transient amplification; the failure of a bound uniform in the initial amplitude for the coupled class is not proved. The comparison bound $0\le w(0,t)\le e^{-\pi^2t}$ proved above for prescribed cells with $F/\sin(\pi x)$ nonincreasing does not extend to the coupled class through invariance of that condition: the set of cells with $F/\sin(\pi x)$ nonincreasing on $(0,\frac12]$ is not invariant under the Proudman-Johnson flow. Indeed, write $F=R\sin(\pi x)$ ($R$ smooth and even, with $R(x+1)=-R(x)$) and $E=F_{xxx}(0)+\pi^2F_x(0)=3\pi R_{xx}(0)$; evaluating the equation and its second $x$-derivative at $x=0$ (where $F=F_{xx}=0$), with the time-dependent term equal to $-2\int_0^1F_x^2\,dx$ by periodicity, gives $E_t=F_{xxxxx}(0)+\pi^2F_{xxx}(0)+\pi^2\big(F_x(0)^2-2\int_0^1F_x^2\,dx\big)$, where $F_{xxxxx}(0)+\pi^2F_{xxx}(0)=5\pi R_{xxxx}(0)-7\pi^3R_{xx}(0)$, $F_x(0)=\pi R(0)$ and $\int_0^1F_x^2\,dx=2\int_0^{1/2}(R_x^2+\pi^2R^2)\sin^2(\pi x)\,dx$ (one integration by parts). Take $R=A\beta(x/\delta)$ on $[-\frac12,\frac12]$, extended by $R(x+1)=-R(x)$, with $A>0$, $0<\delta<\frac12$ and $\beta$ smooth, even, equal to $1$ near $0$, nonincreasing on $[0,\infty)$ and supported in $(-1,1)$. The cell lies in the set, $E(0)=0$, and, since $\sin^2(\pi x)\le\pi^2x^2$, $\int_0^{1/2}(R_x^2+\pi^2R^2)\sin^2(\pi x)\,dx\le\frac{\pi^2A^2\delta}3(\|\beta'\|_\infty^2+\pi^2\delta^2)<\frac{\pi^2A^2}4$ for small $\delta$, so $E_t(0)>0$. The solution with smooth data is smooth on $[0,T]\times\mathbb R$ for some $T>0$, so $R(0,t)$, $R_{xx}(0,t)$ and $R_{xxxx}(0,t)$ are continuously differentiable in $t$ on $[0,T]$; hence, for every small $t>0$, $R_{xx}(0,t)>0$ and therefore $R_x(x,t)>0$ for small $x>0$: the cell leaves the set at once. (Numerical runs for narrow flat-topped cells at two resolutions confirm the exit and show $R_{xx}(0,t)$ becoming negative again soon afterwards; whether the cell returns to the set was not checked.) Two smaller sets were also examined, each obtained by adding one condition to the set above; neither is invariant, numerically for the first and by proof for the second. The first adds the condition $\int_0^{1/2}(R_x^2+\pi^2R^2)\sin^2(\pi x)\,dx\ge\pi^2R(0)^2/4$ (equality for $F=B\sin(2\pi x)$; equivalently $2\int_0^1F_x^2\,dx\ge F_x(0)^2$). Take $F(x,0)=AR_0(x)\sin(\pi x)$ with $R_0(x)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb Z}(-1)^ng(x-n)$, $g(y)=e^{-(y/0.3)^8}$, a wide flat-topped cell. It lies in the smaller set: with $h=-g'$ on $(0,\infty)$, $R_0'(x)=-h(x)-\sum_{m\ge1}(-1)^{m+1}\big(h(m-x)-h(m+x)\big)<0$ on $(0,\frac12]$, because $h$ is decreasing and convex on $[\frac12,\infty)$, so the differences are positive and decrease in $m$; and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality on $[0,\frac14]$ and $[\frac14,\frac12]$, with $F(0)=F(\frac12)=0$, gives $2\int_0^1F_x^2\,dx\ge32F(\frac14)^2=16A^2R_0(\frac14)^2>\pi^2A^2>F_x(0)^2$, since $R_0(\frac14)>0.79$ and $0<R_0(0)<1$. The ratio $\big(2\int_0^1F_x^2\,dx-F_x(0)^2\big)/F_x(0)^2$, equal to $0.946$ at $t=0$, is negative at $t=0.003$ in the computed evolutions (about $-0.13$ at $A=8$ and $-0.006$ at $A=32$; $-0.24$ and $-0.046$ at $t=0.005$), with agreement to the digits shown between a pseudo-spectral integrating-factor Runge-Kutta computation with $512$ and with $1024$ Fourier modes, while at every grid point of $(0,\frac12]$ and every output time the computed $R_x\sin^2(\pi x)=F_x\sin(\pi x)-\pi F\cos(\pi x)$ stays below $10^{-7}F_x(0)$, so that up to discretization error the cell stays in the set above. This part is a numerical observation. The second smaller set adds, for a fixed $\kappa>0$, the condition $-R_{xx}(0)\ge\kappa R(0)$; it is not invariant, and this is proved. For a nonzero cell of the set above $a=R(0)>0$, since $R(\frac12)=0$ and $R$ is nonincreasing on $[0,\frac12]$. With $b=R_{xx}(0)$, $c=R_{xxxx}(0)$ and $I=\int_0^1F_x^2\,dx$, the formula for $E_t$ above and $\partial_tF_x(0)=F_{xxx}(0)+F_x(0)^2-2I$, with $F_{xxx}(0)=3\pi b-\pi^3a$, give $a_t=3b-\pi^2a+\pi a^2-2I/\pi$ and $b_t=\frac53c-\frac73\pi^2b+\frac{\pi^3}3a^2-\frac{2\pi}3I$, so $\rho=-b/a$ satisfies $\rho_t=-\frac{5c}{3a}+\frac{4\pi^2b}{3a}+\frac{3b^2}{a^2}+\pi b-\frac{\pi^3a}3+\big(\frac{2\pi}{3a}-\frac{2b}{\pi a^2}\big)I$. Take a cell in the set with $\rho=\kappa$; one exists, for instance $R=(1-\kappa x^2/2)\beta(x/\eta)$ on $[-\frac12,\frac12]$, extended by antiperiodicity, with $\beta$ as above and $0<\eta<\min\{\frac12,(2/\kappa)^{1/2}\}$. Then $R_{xx}\le-\frac12\kappa a$ on some $[0,4\delta_0]$ with $\delta_0\le\frac18$, so $R_x(x)\le-\frac12\kappa ax$ there. Add to $R$ on $[-\frac12,\frac12]$ the term $\varepsilon(x/\delta)^4\gamma(x/\delta)$, extended by antiperiodicity, where $0<\delta\le\delta_0$, $\varepsilon=\kappa a\delta^2/32$, and $\gamma$ is smooth, even, equal to $1$ on $[-1,1]$, nonincreasing on $[0,\infty)$ and supported in $(-2,2)$. Since $0\le\gamma\le1$, the added term has derivative at most $4\varepsilon x^3/\delta^4\le\frac12\kappa ax$ on $(0,2\delta)$, and it vanishes elsewhere on $(0,\frac12]$, so the new cell lies in the set; it does not change $a$ or $b$, it changes $I$ by an amount that tends to $0$ with $\delta$, and it raises $c$ by $24\varepsilon/\delta^4=3\kappa a/(4\delta^2)$, which changes $\rho_t$ by $-5\kappa/(4\delta^2)$. For small $\delta$ therefore $\rho_t<0$ at $t=0$, and, by the time regularity noted above, $\rho(t)<\kappa$ for every small $t>0$: the cell leaves the set at once. These statements concern the Proudman-Johnson model class only. The finite-amplitude eigenvalues and the coupled evolutions are numerical observations, obtained with two independent codes (the eigenvalues by finite differences at two resolutions, the coupled evolution by two pseudo-spectral Runge-Kutta schemes at two resolutions each); the values agree to three digits, and both codes give the coupled peak at $A_0=32$ as $2.0995$ at $t=0.0157$. These facts were first obtained in a separate analysis and checked here (the class, the slaved solution, the comparison bound, and the rates recomputed by an independent code); two independent reviews by a second model confirmed the class, the slaved solution, the matrix and the rates, and one of them supplied the comparison argument.
