An earlier exploratory numerical computation had already observed that the active shear mode of this class, for the sinusoidal cell, decays at a viscous rate of the cell; what is added here is the exact slaved solution, the comparison bound and the frozen-cell spectra. For the sinusoidal reduced problem with datum $\cos(\pi x)$ the bound gives $w(0,t)\ge\frac12$ only for $t\le(\log2)/\pi^2$, whatever the nonnegative amplitude history. This concerns the reduced model only, an exact class whose velocity is unbounded in $z$; it does not establish a corresponding result for the bounded cut-off drift of the proposition or for general active solutions, and it excludes nothing about alternative (I).

#### Proposition 7.43 (a logarithm-free survival bound in the fixed-affine drift model). Let $b:\mathbb R^2\times[0,t_0]\to\mathbb R^2$ be smooth with $|b|\le M$. Let $\mathcal O\subset\mathbb R^2\times(0,t_0]$ be a deterministic relatively open set, and suppose that there are a real $2\times2$ matrix $A$ with $\operatorname{tr}A=0$ and $S:=A_{21}-A_{12}\ne0$ (the planar curl $\partial_1b_2-\partial_2b_1$ on $\mathcal O$; the meridional orientation used in the Remarks after Proposition 7.42 has the opposite sign) and $a\in\mathbb R^2$ such that $b(x,t)=Ax+a$ for $(x,t)\in\mathcal O$. Let $(x_0,t_0)\in\mathcal O$, let $X$ solve $dX_s=-b(X_s,t_0-s)\,ds+\sqrt2\,dW_s$, $X_0=x_0$, and let $\sigma=\inf\{s\in[0,t_0]:(X_s,t_0-s)\notin\mathcal O\}$. Then for $0<t<t_0$
$$\mathbb P(\sigma>t)\le\frac{4M}{|S|\sqrt{\pi t}},$$
and the constant $4$ may be replaced by $2$ if $\det A\le0$. Model consequence: if $D,\kappa>0$, $|S|\ge\kappa D$, $\ell=M/D$ and $\mathbb P(\sigma>t)\ge p\in(0,1]$, then the survival horizon satisfies $t\le16\ell^2/(\pi\kappa^2p^2)$ (and $t\le4\ell^2/(\pi\kappa^2p^2)$ if $\det A\le0$), uniformly in $\mathrm{Pe}=M\ell$ for fixed $\kappa,p$.

*Proof.* $\sigma$ is a stopping time (the first hitting time of the closed complement of $\mathcal O$ in each slab by a continuous adapted process), and $\sigma>0$ almost surely. Let $Z$ solve $dZ_s=-(AZ_s+a)\,ds+\sqrt2\,dW_s$, $Z_0=x_0$, with the same Brownian motion. For $s<\sigma$ the drifts agree at the current point, so $X_s-Z_s=-\int_0^sA(X_r-Z_r)\,dr$ and Gronwall gives $X=Z$ on $[0,\sigma]$. On $\{\sigma>t\}$ we have $(X_t,t_0-t)\in\mathcal O$, hence $|AZ_t+a|=|b(X_t,t_0-t)|\le M$. Put $V_s=AZ_s+a$. Then $dV_s=-AV_s\,ds+\sqrt2A\,dW_s$, so $V_t=e^{-At}(Ax_0+a)+\sqrt2\int_0^te^{-A(t-r)}A\,dW_r$ is Gaussian with covariance $Q_t=2\int_0^tAe^{-Ar}e^{-A^{\mathsf T}r}A^{\mathsf T}dr$ and $\operatorname{tr}Q_t=2\int_0^t\|Ae^{-Ar}\|_F^2dr$. By Cayley-Hamilton $A^2=-\det(A)\,I$. If $\det A=-\gamma^2<0$, then $Ae^{-Ar}=\cosh(\gamma r)A-\gamma\sinh(\gamma r)I$; if $\det A=0$, then $Ae^{-Ar}=A$; if $\det A=\beta^2>0$, then $Ae^{-Ar}=\cos(\beta r)A+\beta\sin(\beta r)I$. Since $\langle A,I\rangle_F=\operatorname{tr}A=0$, the cross terms vanish and $\|Ae^{-Ar}\|_F^2\ge\|A\|_F^2$ in the first two cases and $\ge\cos^2(\beta r)\|A\|_F^2$ in the third, where $\int_0^t\cos^2(\beta r)\,dr\ge t/4$ (for $\beta t\le1$ because $\cos^21>1/4$, for $\beta t\ge1$ because the integral is $t/2+\sin(2\beta t)/(4\beta)\ge t/2-1/(4\beta)$). As $S^2\le2(A_{12}^2+A_{21}^2)\le2\|A\|_F^2$, $\operatorname{tr}Q_t\ge S^2t$ if $\det A\le0$ and $\operatorname{tr}Q_t\ge S^2t/4$ otherwise. Let $e$ be a unit eigenvector of $Q_t$ for its largest eigenvalue $v\ge\frac12\operatorname{tr}Q_t>0$. The scalar $e\cdot V_t$ is Gaussian with variance $v$ and some mean, so its density is at most $(2\pi v)^{-1/2}$ and $\mathbb P(\sigma>t)\le\mathbb P(|V_t|\le M)\le\mathbb P(|e\cdot V_t|\le M)\le2M/\sqrt{2\pi v}$. Inserting the two lower bounds for $v$ gives $4M/(|S|\sqrt{\pi t})$ and $2M/(|S|\sqrt{\pi t})$. The model consequence follows by solving $p\le4M/(\kappa D\sqrt{\pi t})$ for $t$. $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) Every trace-free affine field $Ax+a$ is locally a steady Navier-Stokes and Euler field, with pressure $\frac12\det(A)|x|^2-(Aa)\cdot x$, since $(u\cdot\nabla)u=-\det(A)x+Aa$. The region $\mathcal O$ may have any shape and may move in time; only the affine field must be the same throughout $\mathcal O$. This local computation does not give a bounded global Navier-Stokes extension. If the bounded mild solution is spatially real analytic at positive times (the introduction of [DZ20] states that bounded mild solutions on $\mathbb R^d$ are analytic in space and time; Theorem 3.1 there proves the time analyticity, and the space analyticity is attributed to earlier work cited there, which was not checked against its primary sources here), exact agreement with a nonconstant affine field on an open set is impossible by analytic continuation and boundedness; no result of this report depends on this supplementary observation. The proposition is therefore a statement about an affine drift model; an application to a genuine Navier-Stokes solution would require a proved approximation argument. (2) Contrast with Proposition 7.42: there the drift vorticity vanishes on the confining line $x=0$, and the persistent scalar is not that vorticity. For a bounded drift agreeing exactly with one trace-free affine field on the sampled spacetime region, the simultaneous assumptions $|S|\ge\kappa D$ and $\mathbb P(\sigma>t)\ge p>0$ force $t\le16\ell^2/(\pi\kappa^2p^2)$, uniformly in $\mathrm{Pe}$ for fixed $\kappa,p>0$. For $\gamma>0$, the unrestricted backward affine diffusion with $A=\begin{pmatrix}\gamma&0\\S&-\gamma\end{pmatrix}$ has tilted coordinate $q=z-Sx/(2\gamma)$ with variance $(1+S^2/(4\gamma^2))(e^{2\gamma s}-1)/\gamma$. This shows how shear amplifies fluctuations in that coordinate. The survival estimate is logarithm-free under the fixed-affine hypothesis; it is a model analogue and gives no improvement of Theorem 7.18 itself. (3) Scope. The proposition does not exclude alternative (I), does not control $J_n$ and does not resolve the planar question of subsection 8.3, item (iii). Even exactly constant vorticity leaves an undetermined, potentially nonlinear and time-dependent harmonic velocity component; a near-constant vorticity also leaves a Poisson remainder. Neither this observation nor (A) provides the required approximation by one fixed affine field on the full sampled spacetime region. If $|b-(Ax+a)|\le\varepsilon M$ throughout the comparison region, a Girsanov comparison stopped at its first exit has relative entropy at most $\varepsilon^2M^2t/4$; for $t=K\ell^2$, Pinsker's inequality bounds the error in any stopped-event probability by $\varepsilon\,\mathrm{Pe}\sqrt{K/8}$. A sufficient condition for this estimate to give an $o(1)$ error is $\varepsilon\,\mathrm{Pe}\sqrt K=o(1)$; hypothesis (A) supplies no such approximation. (4) The proof was checked by hand, and an adversarial review found no defect in the statement or proof.

#### Lemma 7.44 (circulation absorption at the axis; a swirl bound free of the swirl history). Let $u$ be the smooth axisymmetric solution on $[0,T_*)$, $\Gamma=ru^\theta$, $G=\|\Gamma_0\|_\infty$ (so $|\Gamma|\le G$ by Lemma 1.1). For $0<h<t<T_*$ and $a\in[0,h]$ put $k(a)=\|(u^r/r)_-(\cdot,t-a)\|_{L^\infty}$, $K(a)=\int_0^ak$ (so $K(h)=K_-(t-h,t)$), and $w=\big(4\int_0^he^{-2K(a)}da\big)^{-1}$. Then for all $(r,z)$
$$|\Gamma(r,z,t)|\le G\big(1-e^{-r^2w}\big)\le G\min\{1,r^2w\}\le G\min\Big\{1,\frac{r^2e^{2K_-(t-h,t)}}{4h}\Big\},\qquad\text{hence}\qquad\|q(t)\|_\infty\le\frac{G\,e^{2K_-(t-h,t)}}{4h}.\tag{7.44.1}$$
The first inequality is attained in the limit $h\to\infty$ by the (infinite-energy) Burgers vortex $u^r=-\gamma r/2$, $u^z=\gamma z$, $\Gamma=G(1-e^{-\gamma r^2/4})$, for which $k=\gamma/2$ and $w=\gamma/(4(1-e^{-\gamma h}))$.

*Proof.* For $r>0$, $L\Gamma:=\partial_s\Gamma+u^r\partial_r\Gamma+u^z\partial_z\Gamma-(\partial_{rr}-r^{-1}\partial_r+\partial_{zz})\Gamma=0$, and $|\Gamma(r,z,s)|\le Br^2$ on $[t-h,t]$ with $B=\sup_{[t-h,t]}\|\nabla u\|_\infty$ (since $u^\theta$ vanishes on the axis). The function $k$ is continuous ($u^r/r=\int_0^1\partial_1u_1(\sigma r,0,z,s)d\sigma$ and $\nabla u\in C([0,t];C_0)$). Assume $G>0$. Fix $\delta\in(0,h)$, $s_1=t-h+\delta$, write $\tilde k(s)=k(t-s)$, and let $\phi(s)=4\int_{s_1}^se^{-2\int_\sigma^s\tilde k}d\sigma$ for $s\in[s_1,t]$, so $\phi'=4-2\tilde k\phi$, and $w(s)=1/\phi(s)$ on $(s_1,t]$, so $w'=2\tilde kw-4w^2$ and $w(s)\to\infty$ as $s\downarrow s_1$. For $\Psi(r,s)=G(1-E)$, $E=e^{-r^2w(s)}$, one computes $\partial_s\Psi=Gr^2w'E$, $\partial_r\Psi=2GrwE$, $(\partial_{rr}-r^{-1}\partial_r)\Psi=-4Gr^2w^2E$, hence, using $u^r/r\ge-\tilde k$,
$$L\Psi=Gr^2E\big(w'+2(u^r/r)w+4w^2\big)\ge Gr^2E\big(w'-2\tilde kw+4w^2\big)=0.$$
Fix $\epsilon'\in(0,1)$, choose $s_0\in(s_1,t)$ with $w(s_0)\ge B/(G\epsilon')$ and $\epsilon\in(0,1)$ with $\epsilon^2B\le G\epsilon'$, and put $\Phi_\pm=\Psi+G\epsilon'\mp\Gamma$ on $Q=\{r\ge\epsilon\}\times\mathbb R\times[s_0,t]$, where $L$ is uniformly parabolic with bounded coefficients and $L\Phi_\pm=L\Psi\ge0$. On $r=\epsilon$, $\Phi_\pm\ge G\epsilon'-\epsilon^2B\ge0$. At $s=s_0$: if $x:=r^2w(s_0)\ge\Lambda:=\log(1/\epsilon')$ then $\Psi\ge G(1-\epsilon')$ and $\Phi_\pm\ge0$; if $x<\Lambda$ then $(1-e^{-x})/x\ge(1-e^{-\Lambda})/\Lambda\ge e^{-\Lambda}=\epsilon'$, so $\Psi\ge G\epsilon'r^2w(s_0)\ge Br^2\ge|\Gamma|$. The maximum principle for bounded functions (Phragmen-Lindelof form, or the barrier $e^{\lambda(s-s_0)}(1+r^2+z^2)$ with $\lambda$ larger than $2$ plus the velocity bound) gives $\Phi_\pm\ge0$ on $Q$, i.e. $|\Gamma(r,z,t)|\le\Psi(r,t)+G\epsilon'$ for $r\ge\epsilon$. Let $\epsilon\to0$, $\epsilon'\to0$ ($\Psi(\cdot,t)$ does not depend on $s_0$), then $\delta\to0$; the substitution $\sigma=t-a$ turns $w(t)$ into the $w$ of the statement. The other inequalities follow from $1-e^{-x}\le\min\{1,x\}$ and $K(a)\le K(h)$. For the Burgers vortex, $u^r\Gamma_r=\Gamma_{rr}-\Gamma_r/r$ is checked directly and $\int_0^he^{-\gamma a}da=(1-e^{-\gamma h})/\gamma$. $\square$

#### Corollary 7.45 (an a priori bound for $\eta$ free of the swirl history). For $0<h<s_0<t<T_*$, with $B_0=\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty$,
$$\|\eta(t)\|_\infty\le B_0+\frac{G^2\,e^{3K_-(s_0-h,t)}}{8\sqrt2\,h^{3/2}}.$$
Along a Case 3 sequence, for fixed $s_0$ and $h<s_0$: $K_-(s_0-h,t_n)\ge\frac13\log(D_n/r_n)+\frac12\log h+\frac13\log(3\sqrt2\kappa_0/G^2)-o(1)$.

*Proof.* Insert (7.44.1) at time $s_0$ into (7.36.2): $2^{-1/2}G^{1/2}(Ge^{2K_-(s_0-h,s_0)}/(4h))^{3/2}=G^2e^{3K_-(s_0-h,s_0)}/(8\sqrt2h^{3/2})$, and $K_-(s_0-h,s_0)+K_-(s_0,t)=K_-(s_0-h,t)$. The clock bound follows from $\|\eta(t_n)\|_\infty\ge\frac38\kappa_0D_n/r_n$ (Lemma 7.8 and the preamble of Section 4.7). $\square$

*Remark.* Lemma 7.44 is sharp up to constants on Burgers cells. The clock condition of Corollary 7.45 introduces no contradiction among the scalar size constraints: since $\|(u^r/r)_-\|_\infty\le D$, the largest inward clock the gradient bound allows is $\int D\,dt$, which on every power-law scalar history with $D=(T_*-t)^{-b}$, $b>1$ (in particular on the formal history of 8.2 and the multi-cell scenario of 8.3), grows like a power of $1/(T_*-t_n)$, while the required clock is logarithmic. This is a comparison of scalar sizes only; no joint swirl, vorticity and strain history satisfying Lemma 7.44 and Corollary 7.45 is constructed. So they are tools and do not exclude any alternative.

*Remark (a pointwise proof of (7.36.2) with a better constant).* The factor $2^{-1/2}$ in (7.36.2) can be replaced by $\frac12$; then Corollary 7.45 holds with $16$ in place of $8\sqrt2$, and its clock bound with $\log(6\kappa_0/G^2)$ in place of $\log(3\sqrt2\kappa_0/G^2)$ (since $\frac12G^{1/2}(Ge^{2K_-}/(4h))^{3/2}=G^2e^{3K_-}/(16h^{3/2})$). Let $Q_0=\|q(s_0)\|_\infty$ with $GQ_0>0$ (otherwise $q\equiv0$ on $[s_0,t]$ and $\|\eta(t)\|_\infty\le B_0$), $k(s)=\|(u^r/r)_-(s)\|_\infty$, $K(s)=K_-(s_0,s)$, $L=\partial_s+b\cdot\nabla-\Delta_5$, $R(s)=(G/Q_0)^{1/2}e^{-K(s)}$, $f=(r^2+R^2)/(4R)$ and $J=fq^2$. From $Lq=-2(u^r/r)q$, $L\eta=\partial_z(q^2)$, $R'=-kR$ and $\Delta_5r^2=8$, the terms of $L(\pm\eta+J)$ containing $u^r/r$ or $k$ sum to $-\frac{u^r}r\frac{(r^2+2R^2)q^2}{2R}+\frac{k(r^2-R^2)q^2}{4R}\le3kJ$, and the remaining terms are $-2f(\partial_rq)^2-\frac{2rq}R\partial_rq-2f(\partial_zq)^2\pm2q\partial_zq-\frac{2q^2}R\le\frac{r^2q^2}{2R^2f}+\frac{q^2}{2f}-\frac{2q^2}R=0$. So $L(\pm\eta+J)\le3kJ$. Since $|q|\le Q_0e^{2K}$ (maximum principle) and $r^2q^2=|\Gamma||q|\le GQ_0e^{2K}$, one has $0\le J\le C_0e^{3K}$ with $C_0=\frac12G^{1/2}Q_0^{3/2}$, and the maximum principle for bounded subsolutions on the five-dimensional lift, applied to $\pm\eta+J-C_0(e^{3K}-1)$, gives $\|\eta(s)\|_\infty\le B_0+C_0e^{3K(s)}$ on $[s_0,t]$. Among the barriers $(r^2+\lambda R^2)q^2/(4R)$, with $R$ a multiple of $(G/Q_0)^{1/2}e^{-K}$, the quadratic form is nonpositive if and only if $\lambda\ge1$, and, with the two pointwise caps used above, $\lambda=1$ with the stated $R$ gives the smallest constant ($\frac12$); this is optimality within this family, not sharpness. Like Corollary 7.45, this is a tool and excludes nothing.

#### Lemma 7.46 (self-compression bounded by the swirl maximum and the $L^1$ norm of the compressing vorticity; a swirl clock). Fix $s_0\in(0,T_*)$, let $\eta_A$, $v_A$, $\sigma_A$, $C_{\rm init}$ be as in Lemma 7.37, $Y$ as in Lemma 7.22, $\mathcal B(s_0,t)$ as in Lemma 7.26, and put $m(t)=\|u^\theta(t)\|_\infty^2$, $\Pi(t)=-\int\frac{u^r}r\,q^2r\,dx$, $\Pi_A(t)=-\int\sigma_A\,q^2r\,dx$. Then for $s_0\le t<T_*$:

(a) $\|\eta_A(t)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}\le\mathcal B(s_0,t)$;

(b) $|\Pi_A(t)|\le\frac12m(t)\,\|\eta_A(t)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}$, and $|\Pi(t)|\le\frac12m(t)\,\|\eta(t)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}$ whenever $\eta(t)\in L^1(\mathbb R^3)$;

(c) $\displaystyle Y(t)+\sqrt3\,\mathcal B(s_0,t)\le Y(s_0)\exp\Big(3C_{\rm init}(t-s_0)+\frac{\sqrt3}2\int_{s_0}^tm(s)\,ds\Big)$;

(d) under (L1$\eta$), with $Z_3=Y+\sqrt3\|\eta\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}$: $Z_3(t)\le Z_3(s)\exp\big(\frac{\sqrt3}2\int_s^tm\big)$ for $0\le s\le t<T_*$;

(e) along every Case 3 sequence, for large $n$,
$$\frac{\sqrt3}2\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|u^\theta(s)\|_\infty^2ds\ge\log\frac{\sqrt3\,c_1}{2Y(s_0)\nu_n}-3C_{\rm init}T_*,\tag{7.46.1}$$
and under (L1$\eta$) also $\frac{\sqrt3}2\int_s^{t_n}\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2\ge\log\frac{\sqrt3c_1}{\nu_nZ_3(s)}$ for every $s<t_n$; on the other hand, for every fixed $A>0$, $\int_{t_n-A/D_n}^{t_n}\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2\le AG^{4/3}C_*^{2/3}\nu_n^{1/3}\to0$.

*Proof.* (a) The function $h=\eta-\eta_A$ solves the homogeneous equation with smooth bounded coefficients and data $\eta(s_0)$; lifted to $\mathbb R^5$ ($r=|y|$, $y\in\mathbb R^4$) the drift $u^re_R+u^ze_z$ is smooth, so $h$, hence $\eta_A$, is a smooth function of $(r^2,z)$ with $\eta_A,\nabla\eta_A,\nabla^2\eta_A,\partial_t\eta_A$ bounded and continuous on $\mathbb R^3\times[s_0,t]$. The proof of Lemma 7.7 uses only these properties, $\operatorname{div}b=0$, the bound $|b|\le\|u\|_\infty$ and the equation; applied to $\eta_A$ on $[s_0,t]$ with $X(s_0)=0$ it gives (a).
(b) Write $X=(y,z)\in\mathbb R^5$, $dX=2\pi^2r^3dr\,dz$, so $\int_{\mathbb R^3}fr^2dx=\frac1\pi\int_{\mathbb R^5}f\,dX$ for axisymmetric $f$, and $G_5(X)=(8\pi^2|X|^3)^{-1}$, $\partial_zG_5(X)=-3x_5(8\pi^2|X|^5)^{-1}$. Put $\phi_A=G_5*\eta_A$. Since $\eta_A$ is bounded and $\int_{\mathbb R^5}|\eta_A|r^{-2}dX=\pi\|\eta_A\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}<\infty$ by (a), and $r_Y^2|X-Y|^{-4}\le2(1+r_X^2)$ for $|X-Y|\ge1$, the integral $\int|\partial_zG_5(X-Y)||\eta_A(Y)|dY\le C\|\eta_A\|_\infty+C(1+r_X^2)\|\eta_A\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}$ is locally bounded, and $\partial_z\phi_A=(\partial_zG_5)*\eta_A$. Moreover $v_A^r=-r\partial_z\phi_A$: for compactly supported data in place of $\eta_A$ this is the stream-function representation of Section 4.0 ($v_A(t)$ is the divergence-free $\dot H^1$ field with curl $\Omega_A=r\eta_Ae_\theta\in L^2$, Lemma 7.37); for $\eta_A$ itself approximate by $\chi_R\eta_A$ with cutoffs $\chi_R\uparrow1$: the curls converge in $L^2$, so the fields converge in $L^6$ and a.e. along a subsequence, while $\partial_z(G_5*(\chi_R\eta_A))\to\partial_z\phi_A$ everywhere by dominated convergence. So $\sigma_A=-\partial_z\phi_A$. With $g=q^2/r=(u^\theta)^2r^{-3}\ge0$,
$$\Pi_A=\int\partial_z\phi_A\,g\,r^2dx=\frac1\pi\int_{\mathbb R^5}\!\int_{\mathbb R^5}\partial_zG_5(X-Y)\eta_A(Y)g(X)\,dY\,dX=-\frac1\pi\int_{\mathbb R^5}\eta_A(Y)\Phi(Y)\,dY,\qquad\Phi(Y)=\int\partial_zG_5(Y-X)g(X)\,dX,$$
where Fubini is justified by the bound below applied to $|\partial_zG_5|$ (oddness of $\partial_zG_5$ in $x_5$ gives the sign); $\Phi(Y)$ is defined for $r_Y>0$, which suffices since the axis is a null set (on the axis the absolute integral can diverge logarithmically when $q(0,z_Y)\ne0$). Integrating in $x_5$ first, $\int_{\mathbb R}3|t|(a^2+t^2)^{-5/2}dt=2a^{-3}$, and in $\mathbb R^4$, $\int|e-y'|^{-3}|y'|^{-3}dy'=4\pi^2|e|^{-2}$ (composition of two Riesz potentials of order one in $\mathbb R^4$: $\int_{\mathbb R^n}|x-y|^{a-n}|y|^{b-n}dy=\frac{\gamma(a)\gamma(b)}{\gamma(a+b)}|x|^{a+b-n}$, $\gamma(a)=\pi^{n/2}2^a\Gamma(\frac a2)/\Gamma(\frac{n-a}2)$, $\gamma(1)=\gamma(2)=4\pi^2$ for $n=4$). Hence $\int|\partial_zG_5(Y-X)|\,|X'|^{-3}dX=\frac{2}{8\pi^2}\cdot4\pi^2r_Y^{-2}=r_Y^{-2}$, and, $\partial_zG_5$ being odd in $x_5$, its positive and negative parts each contribute $\frac12r_Y^{-2}$. Since $0\le g\le m|X'|^{-3}$, $\Phi=P_+-P_-$ with $0\le P_\pm\le\frac m2r_Y^{-2}$, so $|\Phi|\le\frac m2r_Y^{-2}$ and $|\Pi_A|\le\frac m{2\pi}\int|\eta_A|r^{-2}dY=\frac m2\|\eta_A\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}$. The same computation with $\eta$ and $b$ in place of $\eta_A$ and $v_A$ gives the second bound. (c) By Lemma 7.22(a) with $k=3$ and the proof of Lemma 7.25, $Y'+\sqrt3\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}\le3\Pi=3\Pi_A+3\Pi_B$ with $\Pi_B=-\int\sigma_Bq^2r\,dx\le C_{\rm init}Y$ (Lemma 7.37(a)). With $F=Y+\sqrt3\mathcal B(s_0,\cdot)$, (a) and (b) give $F'\le3C_{\rm init}Y+\frac32m\mathcal B\le(3C_{\rm init}+\frac{\sqrt3}2m)F$ on $[s_0,t]$, and $F(s_0)=Y(s_0)$. (d) Lemma 7.7 from the initial time $s$ ($\eta(s)\in L^1$ by Lemma 7.7) gives $\|\eta(t)\|_{L^1}\le\|\eta(s)\|_{L^1}+\mathcal B(s,t)$; the inequality of (c) integrated over $[s,t]$ without the split gives $Y(t)+\sqrt3\mathcal B(s,t)\le Y(s)+3\int_s^t|\Pi|$. Adding, and using (b), $Z_3(t)\le Z_3(s)+\frac32\int_s^tm\|\eta\|_{L^1}\le Z_3(s)+\frac{\sqrt3}2\int_s^tmZ_3$; $Z_3$ is bounded and lower semicontinuous on $[s,t]$, and Gronwall's inequality applies. (e) Lemma 7.26(b) gives $\mathcal B(s_0,t_n)\ge c_1/(2\nu_n)$, and $Y(s_0)>0$ as in the proof of Lemma 7.26(d); insert in (c) and use $t_n-s_0\le T_*$. Under (L1$\eta$), Lemma 7.8 gives $Z_3(t_n)\ge\sqrt3c_1/\nu_n$; insert in (d). For the window: for $s\le t_n$, $\|u^\theta(s)\|_\infty^2\le S(t_n)^2\le G^{4/3}H(t_n)^{2/3}\le G^{4/3}C_*^{2/3}D_n^{4/3}M_n^{-2/3}=G^{4/3}C_*^{2/3}\nu_n^{1/3}D_n$ (Lemma 1.5 (i) and (A) at $t_n$). $\square$

*Remarks.* (1) Meaning. Compared with Lemma 7.26(d) (the inward strain clock $K_-\ge\frac13\log(1/\nu_n)$) and with an energy estimate for the generated field, no norm of $\nabla b$ enters: the self-compression is paired as (swirl in $L^\infty$) against (compressing vorticity in the transport-invariant critical norm $L^1$), and the only clock left is the swirl clock $\int\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2dt$ (scale invariant; with viscosity $\nu$ restored it is $\nu^{-1}\int\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2dt$). Inside a record window this clock is $o(1)$, so self-compression by vorticity generated within one window is negligible against the window production; the order-one self-compression required by (7.46.1) has to accumulate over a long history. (2) Limits. (7.46.1) is a necessary condition, not a contradiction. In the time-history test of 8.2 ($M=s^{-3/4}$, $D=s^{-7/5}$, $r=s^{11/20}$), augmented by $\|u^\theta\|_\infty=s^{-3/5}$ and $\|\nabla u\|_2^2=s^{-97/100}$, the swirl bound $S\le(G^2C_*)^{1/3}M\nu^{2/3}=(G^2C_*)^{1/3}s^{-41/60}$ of Lemma 1.5, the condition of Lemma 7.25 in the form $\int\|u^\theta\|_\infty\|\nabla u\|_2^2\gtrsim1/\nu$, the tube bound $\|\nabla u\|_2^2\ge\int(u^\theta)^2r^{-2}dx\ge c\|u^\theta\|_\infty^5/(GD^2)$ and the record dissipation $\|\nabla u\|_2^2\gtrsim rM^2$ all hold, and $\int\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2\sim s^{-1/5}$ exceeds $\log(1/\nu)$ by a power. In exponent form ($\|u^\theta\|_\infty=s^{-\sigma}$, $\sigma\le(2b-a)/3$ by Lemma 1.5) the new condition removes only the part $4b<2a+3$ of the exponent region of 8.2, a thin set at the self-similar exponents (on the points $(a,b,c)=(i,j,k)/60$ with $1\le i\le239$, $1\le j\le359$, $1\le k\le179$, that is in the box $0<a<4$, $0<b<6$, $0<c<3$, the inequalities of 8.2 taken as stated there and alternative (I) not imposed, 7 of the 109964 admissible points, by exact rational enumeration; the region itself is unbounded, so this count describes the box only; the whole part $4b<2a+3$, not only these 7 points, satisfies the condition $1+c-2a>0$ of Section 8.2 (its closure has the vertices $(\frac12,1,\frac12)$, $(\frac{19}{31},\frac{32}{31},\frac{12}{31})$, $(\frac58,\frac{17}{16},\frac38)$, $(\frac9{14},\frac{15}{14},\frac37)$, where $1+c-2a\ge\frac18$), and with that condition the region is bounded and has 819 of the grid points; for example $(a,b,c)=(\frac{11}{20},\frac{51}{50},\frac9{20})$, where $\sigma\le\frac{149}{300}<\frac12$ and the clock stays bounded). Where $4b>2a+3$ a history with swirl exponent above $\frac12$ satisfies (7.46.1) with polynomial room; at $4b=2a+3$ the available clock is only logarithmic and compatibility depends on its coefficient (for $\|u^\theta\|_\infty=A_\theta s^{-1/2}$ it needs $\frac{\sqrt3}2A_\theta^2\ge2a-b$, equality requiring lower-order analysis). An upper bound with a uniform strict gap below the necessary logarithmic clock, $\frac{\sqrt3}2\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2\le(1-\delta)\log(1/\nu_n)+C$ with $\delta>0$, would close this comparison (a bound $o(\log(1/\nu_n))$ would suffice); no such critical bound on the swirl is available. (3) Literature. Regularity criteria of Prodi-Serrin type in terms of $u^\theta$ alone are known for axisymmetric Navier-Stokes (Chen, Fang, Zhang, Discrete Contin. Dyn. Syst. 37 (2017)). In the arXiv version (1505.00905), Remark 1.3 states the criterion $u^\theta\in L^a_tL^b_x$, $\frac2a+\frac3b\le1$, $3<b\le\infty$, for axisymmetric divergence-free data in $H^2$; its range includes the endpoint $L^2_tL^\infty_x$ (statement read here, proof not rechecked, journal version not compared). By this criterion the swirl clock $\int\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2dt$ diverges at every first singular time, so what is used here is the quantitative rate in $\nu_n$, not the qualitative divergence. (4) Constants. The sharp constant $\frac12$ comes from the oddness of $\partial_zG_5$ (the cruder bound $|\partial_zG_5|\le3/(8\pi^2|X|^4)$ gives $3\pi/4$). The kernel integrals, the constants, the Fubini step and the Gronwall steps were rechecked by hand and in a second, independent audit. (5) A localized form. For $0<\theta<\frac34$, interpolating $|\Gamma|\le G$ against $|\Gamma|\le\|q\|_\infty r^2$ gives $0\le g=\Gamma^2r^{-5}\le G^{2\theta}\|q\|_\infty^{2-2\theta}|X'|^{-1-4\theta}$, and the computation of (b) with the Riesz orders $1$ and $3-4\theta$ in $\mathbb R^4$ (admissible exactly for $0<\theta<\frac34$) gives $\int|\partial_zG_5(Y-X)|\,|X'|^{-1-4\theta}dX=c_\theta r_Y^{-4\theta}$ with $c_\theta=\gamma(3-4\theta)/\gamma(4-4\theta)$ ($c_{1/4}=c_{1/2}=1$, $c_\theta\to\infty$ as $\theta\to\frac34$; the closed form was checked against a direct quadrature). Hence $|\Pi|\le\frac{c_\theta}2G^{2\theta}\|q\|_\infty^{2-2\theta}\int|\eta|\,r^{2-4\theta}dx$ whenever the right side is finite (this weighted integrability is a hypothesis, not a consequence of smoothness and finite energy), and the same for $\Pi_A$ with $\eta_A$; as $\theta\uparrow\frac34$, $c_\theta\sim\frac1{2(3-4\theta)}$. At distance $\rho$ from the axis the compressing vorticity is thus paired with the rate $\frac{c_\theta}2G\|q\|_\infty(r_c/\rho)^{4\theta-2}$, $r_c=(G/\|q\|_\infty)^{1/2}$, instead of $m\le G\|q\|_\infty$. This does not close the argument either. In the history of Remark (2), with $\|q\|_\infty\le CG^{1/3}H^{2/3}=Cs^{-41/30}$, for each fixed $\frac12<\theta<\frac34$ the upper bound for the pairing coefficient of vorticity at the record radius $s^{11/20}$ is $O_\theta(s^{-p_\theta})$ with $p_\theta=\frac{49}{30}-\frac{8\theta}{15}>\frac{37}{30}$, whose time integral diverges like a power; the limiting exponents as $\theta\uparrow\frac34$ are $\frac{37}{30}$ for the rate and $\frac7{30}$ for its time integral (the endpoint $\theta=\frac34$ itself is excluded, and $c_\theta$ diverges there). At radii $\rho=s^d$ a fixed $\theta$ close enough to $\frac34$ gives an integrable upper bound exactly when $d<\frac{19}{60}$, that is far outside the record radius. These are upper bounds for the pairing coefficient, not its actual size, and they concern this particular history: on other admissible power-law histories the bound at the record radius can be integrable (for example $(a,b,c)=(\frac35,\frac{28}{25},\frac9{20})$, with $\|q\|_\infty\le Cs^{-82/75}$ and $\theta=\frac{149}{200}$, gives the exponent $\frac{4993}{5000}<1$). Moreover for $\theta>\frac12$ the weighted mass $\int|\eta|r^{2-4\theta}dx$ is not transport invariant: its transport term $(2-4\theta)\int|\eta|\frac{u^r}r r^{2-4\theta}dx$ grows under inflow, and its diffusion contribution is not sign-definite: with $\beta=2-4\theta\in(-1,0)$, for a smooth positive rapidly decaying $f$ one has $\int_{\mathbb R^3}r^\beta\Delta_5f\,dx=2\pi\beta(\beta-2)\iint r^{\beta-1}\big(f(r,z)-f(0,z)\big)\,dr\,dz$, in which the subtraction of the axis value is necessary (without it the integral diverges at the axis when $f(0,z)\ne0$), and for $|\eta|$ the Kato term enters in addition; the five-dimensional heat kernel $f=(1+4t)^{-5/2}e^{-(r^2+z^2)/(1+4t)}$ has $\int r^\beta f\,dx=C_\beta(1+4t)^{(\beta-2)/2}$, decreasing in $t$. (6) A weighted threshold. With $w(t)=e^{-3C_{\rm init}(t-s_0)}$, $L=\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}$, $\mathcal B_w(t)=\int_{s_0}^twL$ and $\Lambda_w(t)=\int_{s_0}^tw\Pi_A$, multiplying $Y'+\sqrt3L\le3\Pi_A+3C_{\rm init}Y$ (proof of (c)) by $w$ gives $(wY)'+\sqrt3wL\le3w\Pi_A$, hence $w(t)Y(t)+\sqrt3\mathcal B_w(t)\le Y(s_0)+3\Lambda_w(t)$. Since $w\ge e^{-3C_{\rm init}T_*}$, Lemma 7.26(b) shows that Case 3 is excluded by any bound $3\Lambda_w(t_n)\le(1-\delta)\sqrt3\,\mathcal B_w(t_n)+C$ with fixed $\delta>0$ and $C$ independent of $n$; (c) is the special case in which $\Lambda_w$ is estimated by (a) and (b). The bound on $\Lambda_w$ in this form is not weaker than a bound on the critical swirl dissipation. Lemma 7.22(a) with $k=3$ is the identity $Y'+\mathcal D=3\Pi$ with $\mathcal D=2X-3\int\Gamma^2r^{-5}dx$, and $\frac23X\le\mathcal D\le2X$, $\mathcal D\ge\sqrt3L$ (proofs of Lemmas 7.22(b) and 7.25). The same weighting gives $w(t)Y(t)+\int_{s_0}^tw\mathcal D\,ds\le Y(s_0)+3\Lambda_w(t)$, so the bound above implies $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}w(\mathcal D-\sqrt3L)\,ds+\delta\sqrt3\,\mathcal B_w(t_n)\le Y(s_0)+C$ and hence $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}X\,dt\le\frac32e^{3C_{\rm init}T_*}(1+\frac1\delta)(Y(s_0)+C)$; no bound on $Y$ is assumed. By Lemma 7.22(c) and Lemma 7.26(b) this bound on $\int X\,dt$ already excludes Case 3. Conversely, from $3\Lambda_w=\int_{s_0}^tw(Y'+\mathcal D-3\Pi_B)\,ds$, $|\Pi_B|\le C_{\rm init}Y$ and $\mathcal D\le2X$, bounds independent of $n$ on $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}X\,dt$ and on $\sup_{[s_0,t_n]}Y$ give $3\Lambda_w(t_n)\le(1+6C_{\rm init}T_*)\sup Y+2\int_{s_0}^{t_n}X\,dt$, which implies the bound above (with $\delta=1$). So a bound of this form on $\Lambda_w$, however it is obtained (for instance from size bounds on the delayed pairings of (7)), is equivalent to a bound on $\int X\,dt$ (the bound on $Y$ needed for the converse follows from the regularity statement at the end of this Remark). A pairing estimate can still be a way to prove it, but it must carry a critical input on the swirl: since $|K(t,s)|\le\frac12m(t)L(s)$ (Remark (7)) and $w(t)\le w(s)$ for $t\ge s$, Fubini gives $3\Lambda_w(t)\le\frac32\mathcal B_w(t)\int_{s_0}^tm\,dt'$, so the swirl-clock bound $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}m\,dt\le\frac2{\sqrt3}(1-\delta)$, $0<\delta\le1$, gives the hypothesis with $C=0$. This sufficient condition is more restrictive than the one obtained from (c): by (7.46.1), a clock below the logarithmic threshold already excludes Case 3. Finally, a bound on $\int_{s_0}^{T_*}X\,dt$ is itself a regularity criterion, given the criterion of Chen, Fang and Zhang quoted in Remark (3). Put $y=r^{-3/2}\Gamma=r^{1/2}q$. Expanding $\nabla\Gamma=r^{3/2}\nabla y+\frac32r^{1/2}y\,e_r$ gives $X=\|\nabla y\|_2^2+\frac94\|y/r\|_2^2$ and $\mathcal D=2\|\nabla y\|_2^2+\frac32\|y/r\|_2^2$. The cross term is a multiple of $\iint\partial_r(y^2)\,dr\,dz$, which vanishes because $\int y(r,z)^2dz=r\|q(r,\cdot)\|_{L^2_z}^2\to0$ as $r\to0$ (the trace bound in the proof of Lemma 7.25), with cutoff errors $O(R^{-2}Y)$ at infinity. Moreover $\|y\|_2^2=Y$ and $X<\infty$, so $y(t)\in H^1(\mathbb R^3)$. Since $|u^\theta|^3=|\Gamma|\,|y|^2\le G|y|^2$, the Sobolev inequality gives $\int\|u^\theta\|_{L^9}^3dt\le G\int\|y\|_{L^6}^2dt\le C_SG\int X\,dt$, so $u^\theta\in L^3_tL^9_x$ ($\frac23+\frac39=1$), with no bound on $Y$ used. Hence if the hypothesis of this Remark holds with fixed constants along some sequence $t_n\uparrow T_*$ (not necessarily Case 3 records), then $\int_{s_0}^{T_*}X\,dt<\infty$ and $T_*$ is not a singular time; the hypothesis itself is not proved. (7) Instantaneous decompression; the delayed pairing. Write $X=(y,z)\in\mathbb R^5$, $r=|y|$, $A=-\Delta_5$; for a smooth $O(4)$-invariant $f$ put $\phi_f=A^{-1}\partial_zf$, $\sigma_f=-\partial_z\phi_f$ (the value of $u^r/r$ for the meridional field whose $\eta$ is $\partial_zf$, since $u^r/r=-\partial_zA^{-1}\eta$) and $Q(f)=\frac1\pi\int\frac fr\sigma_f\,dX$. If $f$ has compact support, then$$Q(f)=\frac1\pi\int\frac{|\nabla\phi_f|^2}r\,dX+\frac1{2\pi}\int\frac{\phi_f^2}{r^3}\,dX\ge0:$$integrating by parts in $z$ and then in $X$, $\pi Q(f)=\int\frac{\phi_f}r(-\Delta_5\phi_f)\,dX=\int\frac{|\nabla\phi_f|^2}r\,dX-\frac12\int\phi_f^2\,\Delta_5\frac1r\,dX$ with $\Delta_5\frac1r=-\frac1{r^3}$ (the radial variable is four-dimensional); the boundary terms on $\{r=\epsilon\}$ are $O(\epsilon)$ because $\phi_f$ is smooth on $\mathbb R^5$, and those at infinity vanish because $\phi_f=O(|X|^{-4})$, $\nabla\phi_f=O(|X|^{-5})$. Writing $E(\phi_h)=\pi Q(h)$ for the right side times $\pi$, Cauchy-Schwarz in $L^2(r^{-1}dX)$ gives $E(\phi_h)\le\|h\|_{L^2(r^{-1}dX)}E(\phi_h)^{1/2}$ for compactly supported $h$, so $E(\phi_h)\le\|h\|_{L^2(r^{-1}dX)}^2$, and smooth cutoffs extend the identity to smooth $O(4)$-invariant $f\in L^1\cap L^\infty(\mathbb R^5)$ with $\int f^2r^{-1}dX<\infty$. These conditions hold for $f=q(s)^2$, $s<T_*$: $\int q^2dX=\pi\|u^\theta\|_{L^2(\mathbb R^3)}^2$ and $\int q^4r^{-1}dX=\pi\int q^4r\,dx\le\pi\|q\|_\infty^2\|q\|_{L^2(\mathbb R^3)}\|u^\theta\|_{L^2(\mathbb R^3)}$, with $\|q\|_{L^2(\mathbb R^3)}\le\|\nabla u\|_2$. Since $\Pi=-\frac1\pi\int\frac{u^r}r\frac{q^2}r\,dX$, with $P(t,s)$ the solution operator of the homogeneous equation $(\partial_t+b\cdot\nabla)h=\Delta_5h$ of Lemma 7.37 one has $\eta_A(t)=\int_{s_0}^tP(t,s)\partial_z(q^2)(s)\,ds$ and $\Pi_A(t)=-\int_{s_0}^tK(t,s)\,ds$, where $K(t,s)=\frac1\pi\int\frac{q(t)^2}r\big(-\partial_zA^{-1}P(t,s)\partial_z(q(s)^2)\big)dX$ satisfies $|K(t,s)|\le\frac12m(t)\|\partial_z(q(s)^2)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}$ (the bound of (b) and the $L^1$ contraction of $P$) and $K(s,s)=Q(q(s)^2)\ge0$: the $\eta$ generated by the swirl at one instant decompresses that same swirl. The diagonal identity gives no sign for $K(t,s)$, $t>s$. For independently chosen nonnegative profiles $f$, $g$ the static form $\frac1\pi\int\frac gr\sigma_f\,dX$ is negative when every displacement $(y,z)\in\mathbb R^5$ between their supports has $2|z|>|y|$, because away from the origin $-\partial_z^2G_5(y,z)=\frac{3(|y|^2-4z^2)}{8\pi^2(|y|^2+z^2)^{7/2}}$; this rules out positivity of the static form on arbitrary nonnegative profiles, but it does not produce a negative delayed pairing along a Navier-Stokes solution. The diagonal sign is special to $t=s$ even without transport or evolution of the swirl. In the model with a frozen profile $f\ge0$ and drift-free diffusion, $P(t,s)=e^{-(t-s)A}$, the pairing at delay $\tau>0$ is $K_\tau(f)=\frac1\pi\iint\frac{f(X)}{r_X}\kappa_\tau(X-Y)f(Y)\,dX\,dY$ with $\kappa_\tau=-\partial_z^2$ of the kernel of $A^{-1}e^{-\tau A}$, a bounded continuous function (its Fourier transform $\xi_z^2e^{-\tau|\xi|^2}/|\xi|^2$ is integrable) with $\kappa_\tau(0)=\frac15(4\pi)^{-5/2}\tau^{-5/2}$. Take $f=m\mu_\epsilon+\mu$, where $\mu_\epsilon$ is the normalized indicator of the ball of radius $\epsilon$ about the origin, $\mu\ge0$ is a fixed $O(4)$-invariant bump with $\int\mu\,r^{-1}dX<\infty$ and $b=(\kappa_\tau*\mu)(0)<0$ (for instance the normalized indicator of a ball of radius $\rho$ centred on the axis at height $d$ with $d\gg\rho+\sqrt\tau$, where $\kappa_\tau$ is exponentially close to $-\partial_z^2G_5<0$), and $m\ge0$. With $c=\frac{15\pi}{32}$ the mean of $|y|^{-1}$ over the unit ball of $\mathbb R^5$, continuity of $\kappa_\tau$ gives $\pi K_\tau(f)=m^2\frac c\epsilon(\kappa_\tau(0)+o(1))+m\big(\frac c\epsilon(b+o(1))+O(1)\big)+O(1)$ as $\epsilon\to0$, so the minimum over $m\ge0$ is $-\frac{c\,b^2}{4\pi\epsilon\,\kappa_\tau(0)}(1+o(1))\to-\infty$. A Monte Carlo evaluation of the four pairings at $\tau=1$, $d=3$, $\rho=\frac12$ gives negative minima for $\epsilon\le\frac1{100}$, growing like $1/\epsilon$, and an independent deterministic quadrature gives the minimum $-2.81\cdot10^{-4}$ at $\epsilon=\frac1{100}$. The mechanism is that a swirling region induces inflow on sufficiently distant near-axis regions above and below it, contributing to compression of a thin swirl core there. At a fixed delay $\tau>0$ the strain induced by the core's own source is bounded in magnitude by $m\kappa_\tau(0)$ (Fourier positivity gives $|\kappa_\tau|\le\kappa_\tau(0)$), while its weighted mass is $mc/\epsilon$; the minimization above makes the full delayed pairing negative. (At $\tau=0$ the self-pairing of the ball is $Q(\mu_\epsilon)=\frac{45}{256\pi^2}\epsilon^{-6}$, which is why the diagonal identity is not contradicted; the bounded-kernel expansion is not uniform as $\tau\downarrow0$.) Thus delayed pairings have no universal sign even in this model, which is a frozen-profile model and not a negative delayed pairing constructed along a Navier-Stokes solution. The diagonal identity alone cannot close the estimate: additional control of the accumulated negative contribution, or additional dynamical structure, is required. If $\Pi_A(t)>0$ (as Case 3 needs on average, Remark (6)), then $K(t,s)<0$ for a set of $s<t$ of positive measure; which effects of transport, diffusion and swirl evolution produce this is not determined here. Inside a record window the newly generated part is small: with $a=t_n-A_0/D_n$, $W=[a,t_n]$, $\eta_W$ the source-generated solution with zero data at $a$, $\sigma_W=-\partial_zA^{-1}\eta_W$ and $\Pi_W=-\int\sigma_Wq^2r\,dx$, (a) and (b) with $s_0$ replaced by $a$, and (e), give $\int_W|\Pi_W|\,dt\le\frac12\mathcal B(a,t_n)\int_Wm\,dt\le\frac12A_0G^{4/3}C_*^{2/3}\nu_n^{1/3}\mathcal B(a,t_n)$, a vanishing fraction of that window's source budget; the compression by older vorticity is not bounded by this. These statements are invariant under the Navier-Stokes scaling ($K_\lambda(t,s)=\lambda^4K(\lambda^2t,\lambda^2s)$ for $u_\lambda(x,t)=\lambda u(\lambda x,\lambda^2t)$); they give no decay in $t-s$ and no bound, small relative to the source budget, on the accumulated contribution of old sources. They locate the gap and exclude nothing.

### 4.8 What the proven restrictions allow (compatibility), and the location of the gap

#### 8.1 Summary of proven constraints along a Case 3 sequence
(i) $\nu_n=D_n/M_n^2\to0$ and $S_n/M_n\le(G^2C_*)^{1/3}\nu_n^{2/3}\to0$; (ii) $\alpha_n=r_nD_n/M_n\to\infty$, hence $r_nM_n=\alpha_n/\nu_n\to\infty$; (iii) $r_n\le C(C_*)E_0\nu_n^2$, equivalently $r_nM_n^4/D_n^2\le C(C_*)E_0$; (iv) $r_n^2M_n\to0$; (v) for every fixed $A$ and all $n\ge N(A)$: $D(t_n-A/D_n)\ge c(C_*)D_n$ and $M(t_n-A/D_n)\ge c'M_n$ (Corollaries 5.4-5.5 with Lemma 5.6), and in fact the instantaneous bounds $\|\nabla u(t)\|_\infty\ge c_UD_n/\sqrt2$, $\|u(t)\|_\infty\ge c_U^2M_n/(72C_*)$ hold for all $t\in[t_n-\delta r_n/M_n,t_n]$ (Lemma 7.1, Remark 7.4); (vi) summability: over the indices $n\ge N(\delta)$ of a subsequence with pairwise disjoint windows, $\sum_nc_D\delta r_n^2M_n\le E_0/2$; (vii) the solution is not of Type I (Corollary 7.5); (viii) the $L^1_tL^\infty_x$ source budget of Corollary 7.6; (ix) the critical norm $\|\eta(t_n)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}$ is at least $c_1/\nu_n$, and the $L^1_{t,x}$ swirl source budget $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}\ge c_1/(2\nu_n)$ holds for every fixed $s_0\in(0,T_*)$ (Corollary 7.9 under (L1$\eta$), Lemma 7.26 without it); (x) $r_n\alpha_n^2=r_n^3D_n^2/M_n^2\to0$, with dissipation $\ge c\,r_n^2M_n\max(1,\alpha_n\nu_n)$ on the long window (Corollaries 7.3, 7.11); (xi) the annular/axis dichotomy of Theorem 7.12: on a subsequence, either the record persists in a fixed annulus for a time $\asymp r_n^2$ and $r_n^5D_n^2\to0$, or a net fraction of the record $\eta$ comes from radii $o(r_n)$, which requires $\int\|u^r_+\|_\infty\ge(1-o(1))r_n$ and $\int\|(u^r/r)_+\|_\infty\to\infty$ over a horizon $o(r_n^2)$; (xii) the total absolute meridional circulation $\iint|\omega^\theta|\,dr\,dz$ on the disk $\mathcal D_n$ is $\ge\frac{3\kappa_0}{512C_N}D_nh$ at time $t_n-h$, $0<h\le\delta r_n/M_n$, and on the whole annulus $\mathcal A_\vartheta$ (unbounded in $z$) it is $\ge\frac{3\kappa_0\epsilon}{16C_NL^2(1+G)}\vartheta^6D_nr_n^2$ at time $t_n-T_\vartheta$ in alternative (I) (no signed or vertically localized version is proved), with the corresponding critical source budgets under (L1$\eta$) (Corollary 7.13); (xiii) in alternative (II), the hitting probability is $\gtrsim\max\{\vartheta_n,\alpha_n^{-1}\}$, the localized radial strain is bounded by the Biot-Savart quantities of Lemma 7.14, and $\sup_{W_n}\|\eta\|_{L^1}\gtrsim\Lambda_n^3\vartheta_n^{-7}r_n^{-4}D_n^{-2}$ (Corollary 7.15); (xiv) $\alpha_n\nu_n=r_nD_n^2/M_n^3=O(\log(r_nM_n))$, i.e. the long window lasts at most logarithmically many viscous core times (Corollary 7.17, from the velocity bound for killed averages of $\eta$, Lemma 7.16); (xv) alternative (I) of Theorem 7.12 requires $\alpha_n^2=O(\log(r_nM_n))$, and if $\alpha_n^2/\log(r_nM_n)\to\infty$ then $J_n(\vartheta)>a_n/4$ for every $\vartheta\in[\vartheta_n^\sharp,\frac14]$, so the axis alternative holds with $\vartheta_n=\vartheta_n^\sharp$, $\vartheta_n^\sharp=(4C_I\log(r_nM_n)/\alpha_n^2)^{1/5}$ (Theorem 7.18); (xvi) along subsequences with $\alpha_n\ge1+\log(r_nM_n)$, the full-space radial strain satisfies $\int_{t_n-\widehat T_n}^{t_n}\|(u^r/r)_+\|_\infty\ge\frac14\log(\alpha_n/L_n)-C$ on the horizon $\widehat T_n=r_n^2L_n^{1/2}\alpha_n^{-3/2}$ (Corollary 7.20, from the full-space five-dimensional representation, Lemma 7.19); (xvii) the logarithmic loss in the general estimate of Lemma 7.16 cannot be removed using only the structural properties listed in Proposition 7.21, even for finite-energy fields satisfying the instantaneous Biot-Savart relation (this does not show that the restriction of (xv) is optimal for Navier-Stokes solutions); (xviii) the inflow-weighted critical swirl integral $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\!\int(u^\theta)^2(u^r/r)_-r^{-1}dx\,ds$ is at least $c/\nu_n$ (Lemma 7.22 under (L1$\eta$), Lemma 7.26(c) without it), and the full-space inward strain over the history satisfies $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|(u^r/r)_-\|_\infty ds\ge\frac13\log(1/\nu_n)-C$ (Lemma 7.26(d)); (xix) for every Case 3 sequence the full-space outward strain satisfies (7.24.1), in particular $\int_{t_n-T_n^*}^{t_n}\|(u^r/r)_+\|_\infty\ge\frac13\log(1/\nu_n)-C\to\infty$ with $T_n^*=\ell_n^2L_n\nu_n^{-2/3}\to0$ (Corollary 7.24); (xx) the historical maximal swirl velocity satisfies $S(t_n)\nu_n\to\infty$, from the critical swirl inequality $Y(t)+\sqrt3B(t)\le Y(0)+\frac32\int_0^t\|u^\theta\|_\infty\|\nabla u\|_2^2ds$ (Lemma 7.25, and Lemma 7.26(c) without (L1$\eta$)); (xxi) the signed circulation moved by the swirl source across any plane $\{z=z_0\}$ during $[t_n-cr_n/M_n,t_n]$ is $o(1/\nu_n)$ (Lemma 7.26(e)); (xxii) $\|\eta(t_n)\|_{L^2}^2\ge c_2M_n^2/r_n$, so $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|u^\theta/r\|_{L^4}^4ds\ge2c_2M_n^2/r_n-2\|\eta(s_0)\|_{L^2}^2$, and the full-space inward strain satisfies $K_-(s_0,t_n)\ge\frac16\log(M_n^2/r_n)-C(s_0)$ and $\liminf_nK_-(s_0,t_n)/\log(D_n/r_n)\ge\frac14$ (Lemma 7.27); (xxiii) with $\psi_n$ the backward adjoint density of the record disk, $G\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|q\|_\infty V_{\psi_n}ds\ge c_3/\nu_n$, and within the long window either the adjoint-weighted circulation is inherited or $\sup V_{\psi_n}\gtrsim(\alpha_n\nu_n^{4/3})^{-1}\to\infty$ (Lemma 7.28); (xxiv) the adjoint weight has $2\int\|\nabla\psi_n\|_2^2\le\|\phi_n\|_2^2$, and on every non-inherited terminal interval $\int\!\int_{\{\partial_z\psi_n\ne0\}}q^4\ge c_4M_n^2/r_n$ (Lemma 7.29); for fixed $\vartheta$ and windows of length $\le\lambda r_n^2$ the source pairing from radii $\ge\vartheta r_n$ is $o(1/\nu_n)$, so the record circulation is either inherited at the beginning of the window or produced at radii $<\vartheta r_n$ (Lemma 7.30), and the same holds on windows of length $o(r_n^{7/2}D_nM_n^{-1/2})$; (xxv) the adjoint weight decays, $\|\psi_n(t_n-h)\|_2^2\le Cm_{\phi_n}^2h^{-3/2}$, so under the scalar condition (7.32.0) (for instance, with $\lambda_n=\lambda$ fixed, if $\liminf r_n^5D_n^2>0$, which excludes the persistence alternative of (xi); the converse fails) the annular inheritance is also $o(1/\nu_n)$ for $s_1$ in the earlier half $[t_n-\lambda_nr_n^2,t_n-\frac12\lambda_nr_n^2]$ of the window outside a set of measure $o(\lambda_nr_n^2)$, and there $\frac2\pi\vartheta^2e^{2K_+}(\mathcal E_n(s_1)+B_{\{r<\vartheta r_n\}}(s_1,t_n))\ge(2c_3-\epsilon)/\nu_n$, with $\mathcal E_n(s_1)$ the excess of $|\eta(s_1)|$ above $\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty$ at radii $<\vartheta r_n$ (Lemma 7.31, Corollary 7.32); (xxvi) $\|\psi_n(t_n-h)\|_\infty\le C\mu_nh^{-3/2}$, so inheritance of the record circulation at age $\lambda r_n^2$ costs an earlier source budget $\mathcal B(s_0,t_n-\lambda r_n^2)\ge c\lambda^{3/2}r_n^2D_n$; inheritance at a positive fraction of the window $[t_n-\lambda r_n^2,t_n-\frac12\lambda r_n^2]$ (that is, $|I(t_n-h)|\ge\epsilon/\nu_n$ on a set of $h$ of measure $\ge\delta\lambda r_n^2$, with $\lambda,\epsilon,\delta>0$ fixed) is impossible if $\liminf r_n^3D_n>0$ and forces $K_+\ge\log\alpha_n-\frac12\log(1+\log(r_nM_n))-C(\epsilon,\delta,\lambda)$ (Lemmas 7.33, 7.34); the adjoint-weighted swirl identity gives the necessary condition $F_n=\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\iint\psi_n(u^r/r)_-(u^\theta)^2\ge(\frac{2\sqrt2}3c_3-o(1))/\nu_n$, and, for fixed $\vartheta,\lambda,\epsilon$ under $\liminf r_n^5D_n^2>0$, a localized mixed inequality: for $s_1\in[t_n-\lambda r_n^2,t_n-\frac12\lambda r_n^2]$ outside a set of measure $o(r_n^2)$, $|\iint c\,\psi_nw(s_1)|+(J_c(s_1)+3F_c(s_1,t_n))/\sqrt2\ge(2c_3-\epsilon)/\nu_n$, in which the inherited near-axis circulation, the stored near-axis swirl $J_c$ and the localized inward-strain term $F_c$ all appear and none of them is shown to be negligible (Lemma 7.35(d)); (xxvii) the inward strain over the history satisfies $K_-(s_0,t_n)\ge\frac13\log(D_n/r_n)-C$, the net inward strain acting on the adjoint-weighted swirl is $\ge(\frac{2\sqrt2}3c_3-o(1))/\nu_n$, and the record circulation is produced, adjoint-weighted, only after $3K_-(s_0,\cdot)$ has reached $\log(D_n/r_n)-\omega_n$, for every $\omega_n\to\infty$ with $\omega_n\le\frac12\log(D_n/r_n)$ (the adjoint-weighted circulation present at that time being $o(1/\nu_n)$); and at every time $\|\eta(t)\|_\infty\le\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty+2^{-1/2}G^{1/2}\|q(s_0)\|_\infty^{3/2}e^{3K_-(s_0,t)}$ (Lemma 7.36); (xxviii) the strain clocks of (xxvii) and Corollary 7.24 are carried by azimuthal vorticity generated by the swirl source after any fixed $s_0$: the transported initial component has radial strain $\le C_{\rm init}$ up to $T_*$ (Lemma 7.37); (xxix) in alternative (I) at a fixed $\vartheta$: the logarithm of Theorem 7.18 can be refined to the logarithm of the vertical dispersion of the killed density, $s_n(\vartheta)\ge c(\vartheta)r_ne^{\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2/C_D}$ (Lemma 7.38); a time-averaged fraction $\gtrsim\vartheta$ of the killed density lies on $\{v_n\ge a_n/4\}$ at vertical distance $>r_ne^{\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2/(64C_D)}$ from any chosen centre (Lemma 7.39); its time-averaged occupation of $\ell_n^2|\nabla v_n|^2/a_n^2$ is $O(\vartheta^{-5}\alpha_n^{-2})$, and at every time the high set contains no square of side $>128\ell_n/(3\kappa_0\vartheta)$ (Lemma 7.40); and $r_n^3M_n^2\le C(\vartheta)E_0e^{-\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2/C}$ (Lemma 7.40, Remark (4)); and the source integral before $t_n-T_\vartheta/2$, from any fixed $s_0>0$, is at least $c(\vartheta)r_n^2D_ne^{c\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2}$, so that $r_n^2D_ne^{c\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2}=o(D_n^{2/3}M_n^{-1/3})$ and $K_-(s_0,t_n)\ge\frac13(\log(r_n^2D_n)+c\vartheta^5\alpha_n^2)-C$ (Lemma 7.40, Remark (5)); (xxx) the swirl clock over the history satisfies $\frac{\sqrt3}2\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2\ge\log(1/\nu_n)-C(s_0)$, while it is $O(A\nu_n^{1/3})$ on every window of length $A/D_n$ before $t_n$ (Lemma 7.46).

#### 8.2 Compatibility test (not a counterexample)
Sequence version: take $M_n\to\infty$ geometrically, $D_n=M_n^{19/10}$, $\alpha_n=M_n^{1/10}$, so $\nu_n=M_n^{-1/10}$, $\ell_n=M_n^{-9/10}$, $r_n=\alpha_n\ell_n=M_n^{-4/5}$. Then (iii): $r_n\nu_n^{-2}=M_n^{-3/5}\to0$; (iv): $r_n^2M_n=M_n^{-3/5}\to0$; (x): $r_n\alpha_n^2=M_n^{-3/5}\to0$; $r_nM_n=M_n^{1/5}\to\infty$; $r_n^5D_n^2=M_n^{-1/5}\to0$; (xiv): $\alpha_n\nu_n=1$; the sums in (vi) converge. (If a Case 3 sequence realized these scalar rates, Theorem 7.18 would exclude alternative (I) and force alternative (II), since $\alpha_n$ is a power of $M_n$.) (The choice $\alpha_n=M_n^{3/10}$ would give $r_n\alpha_n^2=1$, violating Corollary 7.11.) Time-history version (scalar functions of $s=T_*-t$): $M=s^{-3/4}$, $D=s^{-7/5}$, $r=s^{11/20}$, $H=D^2/M$. Then $\nu=s^{1/10}$, $\alpha=s^{-1/10}$, $rM^4/D^2=s^{7/20}$, $r^2M=s^{7/20}$, $r/M=s^{13/10}=o(s)$ (so $D$ and $M$ vary by a factor $1+o(1)$ over the long window, which is stronger than the positive comparability required for compatibility with Lemma 7.1), $D(t-A/D)/D\to1$ for fixed $A$, $Ds\to\infty$, and the budget of Corollary 7.6 holds because $\int_{t_0}^t H^{5/3}=\frac{12}{29}s^{-29/12}+O(1)\gg D/r=s^{-39/20}$. Also $r\alpha^2=s^{7/20}\to0$ (Corollary 7.11) and $\alpha\nu=s^0$, bounded (Corollary 7.17). In this test $r^5D^2=s^{-1/20}\to\infty$, so for a Case 3 sequence with these rates alternative (I) of Theorem 7.12 would be excluded and only the axis alternative (II) would remain. For $\vartheta=1/|\log s|$, the available bounds $\int M\,dt$ and $\int D\,dt$ exceed the required transport and strain sizes ($r$ and $\log(1/\vartheta)$) on the stated horizon; this comparison does not show that the actual positive radial velocity, outward strain or signed boundary contribution attains those sizes. With the same $M,D,H$ and $r=s^{5/8}$ instead, one gets $\alpha=s^{-1/40}$, $rM^4/D^2=s^{17/40}$, $r^2M=s^{1/2}$, $r\alpha^2=s^{23/40}$, $r^5D^2=s^{13/40}\to0$, $r/M=s^{11/8}$, $D/r=s^{-81/40}\ll s^{-29/12}$, $\alpha\nu=s^{3/40}$, so this history satisfies the listed $M,D,r,H$ exponent constraints of 8.1 (no source, inflow, $S$ or retention history is supplied) and also the conditions $r^5D^2\to0$, $r^2\lesssim s$ of alternative (I) in Theorem 7.12; but $\alpha^2=s^{-1/20}$ is not $O(\log(rM))$, so this second scalar history violates the logarithmic necessary condition of Theorem 7.18 for alternative (I); no realization of (II) is constructed. Exponent form: writing $M=s^{-a}$, $D=s^{-b}$, $r=s^c$, $H=D^2/M$, the constraints become linear: $2a>b$ ($\nu\to0$), $b>a+c$ ($\alpha\to\infty$), $c\ge4a-2b$ (E'), $2c>a$ (Corollary 7.3), $a+c\ge1$ (long-window persistence is compatible with the prescribed power history: $D(t-\delta r/M)/D(t)=(1+\delta s^{a+c-1})^{-b}$ must stay bounded below; this is also what Corollary 7.5 tests), $3c\le7b-5a-3$ (Corollary 7.6), $3c>2b-2a$ (Corollary 7.11), $c\ge2b-3a$ (Corollary 7.17), $c\ge b/2-a$, $3b\ge4a$ and, in (I), $4c\ge b$ (Corollary 7.13(c) against the scalar bound of Remark (2) after Corollary 7.9, under (L1$\eta$): the source budget $O(\sqrt{D_n})$ must dominate $\alpha_n$, $1/\nu_n$ and, in (I), $D_nr_n^2$; the last two inequalities are implied by the others, since (E') and $b>a+c$ give $3b>5a$, and $5c>2b$ gives $4c>b$), $a\ge\frac12$ (the classical lower bound $\|u(t)\|_\infty\ge c(T_*-t)^{-1/2}$), and for alternative (I) in addition $5c>2b$ and $2c\ge1$ (persistence of $\|\nabla u\|_\infty\gtrsim\vartheta D_n$ for a time $\asymp r_n^2$ requires $r_n^2\lesssim s_n$ when $D=s^{-b}$). An exact rational vertex enumeration (computer algebra, exact arithmetic) shows that the system without the conditions of (I) defines a nonempty convex region, containing the two time-history tests above, the point $(a,b,c)=(\frac34,\frac{57}{40},\frac35)$ into which the sequence version embeds ($b=\frac{19}{10}a$, $c=\frac45a$, $a=\frac34$), and for instance $(a,b,c)=(\frac85,\frac{14}5,1)$, where every inequality, written as above with integer coefficients, holds with slack at least $\frac15$, and that the self-similar point $(a,b,c)=(\frac12,1,\frac12)$ is a vertex of its closure; there $\nu$ and $\alpha$ are bounded, and the Case 3 boundary equalities $2a=b$ and $b=a+c$, the window condition, the budget of Corollary 7.6, $a\ge\frac12$ and Corollary 7.17 hold with equality. The list above does not use the summability (vi) for continuous histories. If $D=s^{-b}$ is strictly increasing, then $\|\nabla u(t)\|_\infty=D(t)$ at every $t$ (by continuity of $t\mapsto\|\nabla u(t)\|_\infty$ on $(0,T_*)$, where $u\in C((0,T_*);H^k)$ for every $k$, a supremum over $[0,t]$ that is not attained at $t$ stays constant for a while after $t$), so every time is a gradient record, and the supremum over $x$ is attained because $\nabla u(t)$ is continuous and tends to $0$ at infinity. If the scalar laws describe the record at every late time, with $r(t)$ the distance to the axis of a point $x_t$ where $|\nabla u(x_t,t)|=D(t)$, then by (A) $|\nabla u(t)|\ge D-\lambda H\ge D/2$ on the ball of radius $\lambda=M/(2C_*D)$ about $x_t$; since $\lambda/r=1/(2C_*\alpha)\to0$ and axisymmetry preserves $|\nabla u|$, the same holds on the solid torus swept by rotating this ball about the axis, of volume $2\pi^2r\lambda^2$, so $\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2\ge\pi^2rM^2/(8C_*^2)$. The energy inequality $\int_0^{T_*}\|\nabla u\|_2^2dt\le E_0/2$ then requires $\int_0s^{c-2a}ds<\infty$, that is $1+c-2a>0$, with strict inequality. (The same condition follows from (vi): the long windows $[t-\delta r/M,t]$ chosen backwards by $t_{j+1}=t_j-\delta r(t_j)/M(t_j)$ are disjoint, about $s^{1-a-c}/\delta$ of them lie in $[T_*-2s,T_*-s]$ by $a+c\ge1$, and each has dissipation at least $c_D\delta r^2M$, so each dyadic block contributes about $c_Ds^{1+c-2a}$.) On the region this is stronger than $2c>a$ (the difference of the two slacks is $1-a-c\le0$), and with $c<a$, which follows from $b>a+c$ and $2a>b$, it gives $a<1$ and $b<2a<2$, so the region becomes bounded. The classical time integrability of finite-energy solutions gives the same two conditions: $\int_{T_*/2}^{T_*}\|u\|_\infty dt<\infty$ and $\int_{T_*/2}^{T_*}\|\nabla u\|_\infty^{1/2}dt<\infty$ (Montgomery-Smith, arXiv math/0301207, first inequality of Corollary 1.4 with $q_1=6$, $q_2=\infty$, $n=0,1$ and $\Theta(\lambda)=\lambda$, whose right side is finite since $\int\|u\|_6^2dt\le C\int\|\nabla u\|_2^2dt<\infty$; the same bounds follow from the estimates $\int\|\nabla^ku\|_2^{2/(2k-1)}dt<\infty$ of Foias, Guillopé and Temam, Comm. Partial Differential Equations 6 (1981), 329-359, with $\|u\|_\infty\le C\|\nabla u\|_2^{1/2}\|\nabla^2u\|_2^{1/2}$, $\|\nabla u\|_\infty\le C\|\nabla u\|_2^{1/4}\|\nabla^3u\|_2^{3/4}$ and Hölder's inequality with exponents $(4,\frac43)$ and $(16,\frac{16}{15})$), that is $a<1$ and $b<2$ when the instantaneous norms $\|u(t)\|_\infty$ and $\|\nabla u(t)\|_\infty$ follow $s^{-a}$ and $s^{-b}$ up to constants. The two time-history tests above satisfy $1+c-2a>0$ (slack $\frac1{20}$ and $\frac18$), and so does the logarithmic example $(a,c)=(\frac35,\frac12)$ below (slack $\frac3{10}$); the points $(\frac85,\frac{14}5,1)$ and $(\frac65,\frac{11}5,\frac9{10})$ do not. With $1+c-2a>0$ added, the largest common slack is $\frac1{12}$ in the region without the conditions of (I), attained at $(\frac23,\frac76,\frac5{12})$, and $\frac1{16}$ with the two conditions of (I) from Theorem 7.12, attained at $(\frac34,\frac{11}8,\frac9{16})$ (exact maximization of the smallest slack, by enumeration of the vertices of the bounded polytope); the self-similar point $(\frac12,1,\frac12)$ is still a vertex of the closure. The swirl-clock condition $4b\ge2a+3$ of Remark (2) after Lemma 7.46, which also applies to such histories (by Lemma 1.5 the clock $\int\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2dt$ is at most a constant times $\int s^{-(4b-2a)/3}ds$, and it must diverge since $2a>b$), leaves both maxima and both maximizers unchanged (its slack there is $\frac13$ and $1$) and holds with equality at $(\frac12,1,\frac12)$; the list of scalar restrictions used here is not claimed to be complete. Since $(\frac12,1,\frac12)$ lies in the closure of the region, a further linear restriction can empty the region only if it fails or holds with equality at that point, which carries the exact self-similar scaling $M=s^{-1/2}$, $D=s^{-1}$, $r=s^{1/2}$, for which $\alpha=\nu=1$; restrictions with positive value there, such as $1+c-2a>0$ and (E') (value $\frac12$ each), cannot do so. Along a Case 3 sequence these conditions add no new necessary condition obtained from the long record windows: the sequence form of (vi) is already listed, and by 8.1(v) the two integrability bounds contribute at least $c\,\delta r_n$ and $c\,\delta D_n^{1/2}r_n/M_n$ on the long window, both of which tend to $0$ by (iii), since $D_n^{1/2}r_n/M_n\le C(C_*)E_0\nu_n^{5/2}$; over disjoint windows the sums are bounded by $\sum_nr_n^2M_n$ up to constants, since $r_nM_n\ge1$ and $D_n\le C_0M_n^2$ for large $n$, and so they are finite by (vi). Adding the two conditions of (I) from Theorem 7.12 to the listed system still leaves a nonempty region (for instance $(\frac65,\frac{11}5,\frac9{10})$, with slack at least $\frac1{10}$ in the system without $1+c-2a>0$, and with $1+c-2a>0$ also imposed, $(\frac34,\frac{11}8,\frac9{16})$, with slack $\frac1{16}$; if alternative (I) held with one fixed $\vartheta$ at every late record, packing the disjoint windows $[t-T_\vartheta,t]$, about $s^{1-2c}/\vartheta^3$ of them per dyadic block and each with $Q(T_\vartheta)\ge c\vartheta^{11}r^5D^2$, would also require $1+3c-2b>0$, which fails at $(\frac34,\frac{11}8,\frac9{16})$, and the largest common slack would drop to $\frac1{19}$, attained at $(\frac{13}{19},\frac{24}{19},\frac{10}{19})$), but Theorem 7.18 adds $a+c\ge b$ in (I), which contradicts $b>a+c$; in exponent form alternative (I) is empty. With logarithmic corrections it is not. Fix $G>0$ and $0<\vartheta\le1/4$, put $L=\log(1/s)$ and, on the boundary $b=a+c$, take $M=s^{-a}$, $r=s^c$, $D=s^{-(a+c)}(\kappa L)^{1/2}$, $H=D^2/M$, with a constant $\kappa>0$; then $\alpha=(\kappa L)^{1/2}\to\infty$ and, for $a>c$, $\log(rM)=(a-c)L$. Each listed comparison is now a power of $s$ times a power of $L$ (when the power of $s$ vanishes, the power of $L$ decides), and in Corollary 7.13(c) the three ratios to $\sqrt D$ are $(1/\nu)/\sqrt D=\kappa^{-3/4}s^{(3c-a)/2}L^{-3/4}$, $\alpha/\sqrt D=\kappa^{1/4}s^{(a+c)/2}L^{1/4}$ and $Dr^2/\sqrt D=\kappa^{1/4}s^{(3c-a)/2}L^{1/4}$. Claim: the comparisons listed above, together with Theorem 7.18 ($\kappa L\le C_I\vartheta^{-5}((a-c)L+O(1))$) and Remark (4) after Lemma 7.40 ($s^{3c-2a}\le C(\vartheta)E_0s^{\vartheta^5\kappa/C}$), hold for some fixed $\kappa>0$ exactly when $\frac12\le c<a<\frac32c$ (with the condition $1+c-2a>0$ above, which carries no logarithm here since $M$ and $r$ have none, the range becomes $\frac12\le c<a<\frac{1+c}2$, because $\frac{1+c}2\le\frac32c$ for $c\ge\frac12$; the swirl-clock condition $4b\ge2a+3$, here $2a+4c\ge3$, holds on this range, since $2a+4c>6c\ge3$). Indeed $\nu=\kappa^{1/2}s^{a-c}L^{1/2}\to0$ forces $a>c$ (at $a=c$ also $X=1$); persistence over $T_\vartheta\asymp r^2$ forces $r^2\lesssim s$, i.e. $c\ge\frac12$ (at $c=\frac12$, $r^2/s=1$ carries no logarithm and $D(t-T_\vartheta)/D(t)\to(1+\epsilon\vartheta^3/(1+G))^{-(a+c)}>0$); and $r^5D^2=\kappa s^{3c-2a}L\to0$ forces $a<\frac32c$. Conversely, these three inequalities give the required behaviour of every remaining listed comparison (several have power of $s$ equal to zero or negative, as required: $\alpha^2/\log X$ and, at $c=\frac12$, $r^2/s$ have power zero, and $X$ has a negative power; for instance $a+c>1$, so the window condition holds strictly, and the exponent $\frac23a+\frac43c-1$ of the ratio in Corollary 7.6 is positive), and every $\kappa$ with $0<\kappa<\min\{C_I\vartheta^{-5}(a-c),\,C\vartheta^{-5}(3c-2a)\}$ is admissible, the constants being those of Theorem 7.18 and Remark (4); no lower bound on $\kappa$ is needed. For instance $(a,c)=(\frac35,\frac12)$, $D=s^{-11/10}(\kappa L)^{1/2}$: $\nu=\kappa^{1/2}s^{1/10}L^{1/2}$, $r^5D^2=\kappa s^{3/10}L$, $r^2/s=1$, and $(D/r)/\int_{t_0}^tH^{5/3}\sim\frac53\kappa^{-7/6}s^{1/15}L^{-7/6}\to0$ (Corollary 7.6). The classification was also checked by exact arithmetic on a rational grid of $(a,c)$ with step $\frac1{40}$ by a computer enumeration; the grid is evidence, the argument just given is the proof. So the listed comparisons involving $M,D,r,H$ do not exclude alternative (I) in its logarithmic regime. These four functions assign no swirl, source, inflow, adjoint or retention history, so the comparisons of Section 8.1 that involve those quantities are not tested, and this is not a solution. The compatible scalar laws displayed here differ from Type I scaling, as Corollary 7.5 requires of every Case 3 history; they do not establish compatible Navier-Stokes histories. So, for every fixed $G>0$, the listed scalar comparisons involving $M,D,r,H$ are mutually consistent (for $G=0$ the compatibility test fails: Corollary 7.6 then contradicts the Case 3 assumptions, and under (L1$\eta$) so does Corollary 7.9). This is not a solution of NS and says nothing about whether such a solution exists.

#### 8.3 The gap
By Corollary 5.4, TARGET-3 is equivalent to the nonexistence of a Case 3 sequence, and by Lemma 7.1 the physical solution keeps vorticity of size $\gtrsim D_n$ in the meridional disk $\mathcal D_n$ of radius $3\delta r_n$ about the point $(r^0,z^0)$ of Lemma 7.1 (at distance $o(r_n)$ from the record point) for the whole window of physical length $\delta r_n/M_n$ (normalized length $\delta\alpha_n\to\infty$). The proofs above use two kinds of information.

(a) Local-in-time persistence, from transport at speed $\le M_n$, viscous smoothing, and the circulation bound (0.1) for the source. On $\{r\ge cr_n\}$ the accumulated contribution of the source over the long window, relative to $D_n/r_n$, is at most $\frac{r_n}{D_n}\cdot\frac{2GD_n}{c^3r_n^3}\cdot\frac{\delta r_n}{M_n}=\frac{2G\delta}{c^3r_nM_n}\to0$, and (0.1) only improves at larger radii. In fact (0.1) keeps the source small on $\{r\ge\vartheta r_n\}$ for all times $\ll\vartheta^3r_n^2$, which for fixed $\vartheta>0$ (more generally when $\vartheta_n^3r_nM_n\to\infty$) is much longer than the window. The window $\delta r_n/M_n$ is the time in which transport at speed $\le M_n$ can move material a distance comparable to $r_n$, and it is sharp for this kind of argument: a hypothesis $\int_{t_n-T}^{t_n}\|u(s)\|_\infty ds\le r_n/8$ with $T\le c\,r_n^2$, $c=c(G,\kappa_0)\le\frac1{48}$ small, still yields persistence of the vorticity on $[t_n-T,t_n]$. Indeed, in the proof of Corollary 7.11 use the disk of radius $3r_n/8$ about $x^0$ (inside $\{r_n/2\le r\le2r_n\}$ for large $n$); the drift displacement is at most $r_n/8+6T/r_n\le r_n/4$, so exit before $T$ requires $\sqrt2\sup|W|\ge r_n/8$, which has probability $\le4e^{-1/(512c)}$; the boundary and source errors relative to $D_n/r_n$ are at most $8\sqrt2e^{-1/(512c)}+16Gc$, which is $\le3\kappa_0/16$ for $c$ small, so the killed term stays $\ge a_n/2$. Hence $\|\nabla u(s)\|_\infty\ge cD_n$ and, by the interpolation of Remark 7.4, $\|u(s)\|_\infty\ge c'M_n$ on $[t_n-T,t_n]$, so the hypothesis forces $T\le r_n/(8c'M_n)$. On longer intervals the uncontrolled term is the contribution of $\eta$ entering through small radii, and Theorem 7.12 makes this precise: either the record persists in a fixed annulus for a time $\asymp r_n^2$ (then $r_n^5D_n^2\to0$), or a net fraction of it, in the stopped representation, enters through radii $o(r_n)$, which requires the integrated outward velocity and radial strain bounds of (II). By Theorem 7.18 the first alternative needs $\alpha_n^2=O(\log(r_nM_n))$ along the subsequence; if this fails, there is a further subsequence with $\alpha_n^2/\log(r_nM_n)\to\infty$ (an unbounded ratio need not tend to infinity along the whole sequence), and along it $J_n(\vartheta)>a_n/4$ for all $\vartheta\in[\vartheta_n^\sharp,\frac14]$, so the second alternative holds with $\vartheta_n=\vartheta_n^\sharp$. Neither alternative is excluded here, and $r_n/M_n$ is not an exact domain-of-dependence boundary for a parabolic equation.

(b) Global energy, dissipation and source budgets (Lemma 1.5, Corollaries 7.3, 7.6, 7.9, 7.11) and the smoothing dichotomy of Theorem 7.12.

The estimates proved here that constrain the solution at normalized backward times $|\tau|\gg\alpha_n$ are the restrictions listed in 8.1 (the integral budgets (vi), (viii), (ix), (x), the circulation and Biot-Savart restrictions (xii), (xiii), the dichotomy (xi) and its quantitative forms (xiv), (xv), and the items added after them) and the comparison on the horizons $T''_\vartheta$ of Theorem 7.18, Remark (4) (Theorem 1, item 37). None of them proves TARGET-3 or excludes either short-window alternative.

**The first missing implication** is TARGET-3 with the mandated order of limits,
$$\lim_{A\to\infty}\ \limsup_{n\to\infty}\ \frac{D(t_n-A/D_n)}{D_n}=0,$$
which by Corollary 5.4 is the whole problem. Two remarks fix its precise content.

(1) Decay at changing times $A=A_n\gg\alpha_n$ does not imply it. In the time-history example of 8.2, with $\sigma_n=T_*-t_n$ and $A_n=\sigma_n^{-1/2}$, one has $A_n/\alpha_n\to\infty$ and $D(t_n-A_n/D_n)/D_n=(1+\sigma_n^{-1/10})^{-7/5}\to0$, although $d(A)=1$ for every fixed $A$.

(2) A localized version must follow the backward characteristics. Depletion on a fixed normalized disk is not enough: the bounded smooth planar Euler flow $V(y_1,y_3,\tau)=(c,F(y_1-c\tau))$, with $c\neq0$ and $F'\neq0$ compactly supported, has conserved nonzero vorticity norm, while its vorticity vanishes on every fixed disk once $\tau$ is negative enough. A correct localized sufficient condition is: for some $A\ge0$,
$$\limsup_{n\to\infty}\ \sup_{|x-x_n|\le(R_0+A)\ell_n}\frac{|\omega(x,t_n-A/D_n)|}{D_n}<\kappa_0 .$$
Indeed, along a convergent subsequence this gives $|\zeta(\cdot,-A)|<\kappa_0$ on $\overline{B_{R_0+A}}$. The backward characteristics of Lemma 5.3 starting in $B_{R_0}$ stay in $\overline{B_{R_0+A}}$ up to time $-A$ (as $|V|\le1$), so $|\zeta(\cdot,0)|<\kappa_0$ on $B_{R_0}$. This contradicts $\sup_{|y|\le R_0}|\zeta(y,0)|\ge\kappa_0$, which is Lemma 5.6 passed to the limit by uniform convergence of $\nabla U_n$. By the same rigidity this condition, too, fails for every Case 3 sequence, so it is a localized reformulation, not an intermediate estimate.

(3) Pressure and temporal geometry do not supply it by themselves. Let $A=\nabla u$, $A_{ij}=\partial_ju_i$, $S=\frac12(A+A^T)$, $\omega$ the vorticity and $p$ the canonical pressure, $-\Delta p=\partial_iu_j\partial_ju_i$. The gradient equation $(\partial_t+u\cdot\nabla-\Delta)A=-A^2-\nabla^2p$ gives $(\partial_t+u\cdot\nabla-\Delta)\frac12|A|^2=-A:A^2-S:\nabla^2p-|\nabla A|^2$. At a point $(x_n,t_n)$, $t_n>0$, where the current spatial maximum of $|A|$ attains the historical supremum $D(t_n)$ (a first crossing is sufficient, not necessary), $\nabla|A|^2=0$, $\Delta|A|^2\le0$ and $\partial_t|A|^2\ge0$ (since $|A(x_n,t)|\le D(t_n)$ for $t\le t_n$), hence $0\le-A:A^2-S:\nabla^2p-|\nabla A|^2$ there. Writing $A=S+W$ with $Wv=\frac12\omega\times v$, $W^2=\frac14(\omega\otimes\omega-|\omega|^2I)$ and $\operatorname{tr}S=0$ give $-A:A^2=-\operatorname{tr}S^3+\frac14\omega\cdot S\omega$; this vanishes identically for planar fields (a trace-free $2\times2$ matrix $B$ has $B^2=-\det B\,I$, so $B:B^2=-\det B\operatorname{tr}B=0$). At a Case 3 record it is $O(D_n^3(\alpha_n^{-1}+\nu_n^{2/3}))$. In the cylindrical orthonormal basis at angle zero, the deviations from a trace-free planar meridional block are the entry $u^r/r=O(M_n/r_n)=O(D_n/\alpha_n)$ (which also corrects the trace) and the swirl entries $-u^\theta/r$, $\partial_ru^\theta$, $\partial_zu^\theta$. Conjugation by $Q=\operatorname{diag}(1,-1,1)$ fixes the meridional entries, reverses the swirl entries and leaves the polynomial $A:A^2$ invariant, so the swirl entries enter it quadratically (an algebraic invariance; no symmetry of the solution is assumed). The swirl entries are bounded by $C(S_nH_n)^{1/2}$: apply the one-dimensional interpolation used in Lemma 1.5(ii) to the Cartesian component $u_2(r,0,z)=u^\theta(r,z)$, odd in $r$, along radial and vertical lines, and use $u^\theta(0,z)=0$ for $u^\theta/r$; with Lemma 1.5(i),(iii) and (A), $S_nH_n\le CD_n^2\nu_n^{2/3}$. Hence $|A:A^2|\le C(D_n^2M_n/r_n+D_nS_nH_n)\le CD_n^3(\alpha_n^{-1}+\nu_n^{2/3})$. For the pressure, $\partial_a\partial_bp$ is a principal-value Calderon-Zygmund kernel $K_{ab}$ applied to $f=\partial_iu_j\partial_ju_i$ plus a multiple of $f$; with a smooth radial cutoff $\chi_R$ equal to $1$ on $|z|\le R$ and $0$ on $|z|\ge2R$, the inner part equals $\int\chi_RK_{ab}(z)(f(x-z)-f(x))\,dz$ by cancellation and is at most $C\|\nabla f\|_\infty R\le CDHR$; in the outer part one writes $f=\partial_i\partial_j(u_iu_j)$ and integrates by parts twice, and $\|\partial_i\partial_j((1-\chi_R)K_{ab})\|_{L^1}\le CR^{-2}$ gives at most $CM^2/R^2$ (a further cutoff at infinity, removed using $\|u\|_\infty<\infty$, justifies the integrations). With $R=M/D$: $\|\nabla^2p\|_\infty\le C(D^2+MH)\le C(C_*)D^2$. This gives no sign for $S:\nabla^2p$, so the record only forces $S:\nabla^2p\le-|\nabla A|^2+o(D_n^3)$, without contradiction. In time: on an interval $[t_A,T_*)$ on which (A) holds, the same maximum argument in upper Dini form gives $\frac{d^+}{dt}\|\nabla u\|_\infty\le C(\|\nabla u\|_\infty^2+\|\nabla^2p\|_\infty)$, and the historical supremum, locally Lipschitz, is either attained by the current norm or locally constant, so $D'\le KD^2$ almost everywhere on $[t_A,T_*)$ with $K=K(C_*)$. This integrates to $D(t-h)\ge D(t)/(1+KhD(t))$ for $[t-h,t]\subset[t_A,T_*)$, a backward lower bound; the logarithmic histories of 8.2 satisfy it eventually for every $K>0$ (there $D'/D^2=(a+c+1/(2L))/(sD)\to0$) and pass the listed scalar comparisons of alternative (I) while $D$ varies by a bounded factor over the window. Finally, a pressure-moment identity of one fixed sign does not hold for planar periodic flows: on $[0,2\pi]^2$ with $\langle f\rangle=(2\pi)^{-2}\int f$, $\langle p\rangle=0$, $\omega=\partial_xu_2-\partial_yu_1$ and viscosity one, $\frac d{dt}\langle p\omega^2\rangle$ is positive for the Taylor-Green flow (the rate is $-8\langle p\omega^2\rangle>0$) and negative at $t=0$ for $u=(\partial_y\psi,-\partial_x\psi)$, $\psi=A(\cos x+\cos2y+\cos(x+2y))$, $A=60$, where the rate is $\frac{A^4}{17}(14269-261A)<0$ (with the opposite convention $u=(-\partial_y\psi,\partial_x\psi)$ the $A^5$ term changes sign). This excludes a universally monotone behaviour of this particular moment over planar periodic NS flows; it does not exclude a weighted, localized or Case 3 specific pressure estimate, and the calculations of this remark show only that these particular estimates do not close the argument. These computations were rechecked here, the last rate by an independent spectral computation.

**Possible mechanisms to investigate (none proved here).** (0) Excluding each alternative of Theorem 7.12 separately: in (I), which by Theorem 7.18 is confined to $\alpha_n^2=O(\log(r_nM_n))$, an argument for this logarithmic regime, for instance removing the logarithm in Lemma 7.16 (which would make $\alpha_n$ bounded in (I), a contradiction); in (II), a depletion estimate for the net contribution $J_n(\vartheta_n)$ of $\eta$ entering from radii $o(r_n)$, for which the proof of Theorem 7.12 requires the integrated outward velocity and radial strain bounds displayed in (II) (these concern a weighted contribution of the stopped representation, not a single material trajectory). Lemma 7.16 bounds the killed part of the representation by the velocity, but not the inner-boundary term $J_n$. In (II), by Corollary 7.15 it would suffice to prove $\int_{W_n}\|\eta(s)\|_{L^{3,1}}ds=o(\log(1/\vartheta_n))$ on the horizon $W_n$ of length $\asymp\vartheta_n^3r_n^2$; no such bound is available; the scalar bound $\|u^r/r\|_\infty\lesssim D^{3/2}M^{-2/3}$ noted after Corollary 7.15 uses (L1$\eta$) and is weaker along the records than the elementary $\|u^r/r\|_\infty\le D$. (i) Quantitative depletion of $\eta$ along all backward characteristics of the record neighbourhood, with control of the source and diffusion terms on the whole backward history. (ii) An a priori bound $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}ds=o(M_n^2/D_n)$ for the critical $L^1_{t,x}$ norm of the swirl source, for one fixed $s_0$; by Lemma 7.26(b) this would suffice, with no decay assumption on the data. The bound for it derived here from energy, enstrophy and pointwise inequalities (Remark (2) after Corollary 7.9) is $\sqrt{E_0\|\omega_0\|_2^2+E_0^2D_n}$, of order $E_0\sqrt{D_n}$, much larger than $M_n^2/D_n$ because $D_n\gg M_n^{5/3}$. By Lemma 7.22 the swirl source is in turn bounded by the critical swirl energy $Y(0)$ plus the inflow-weighted integral $3\int_0^t\!\int(u^\theta)^2(u^r/r)_-r^{-1}dx\,ds$, so (ii) would follow from an upper bound $o(1/\nu_n)$ for that integral (the converse is not claimed); no such bound is proved. (iii) For alternative (I) in the logarithmic regime, Proposition 7.21 rules out a universal logarithm-free improvement of Lemma 7.16 based only on its structural properties, even for finite-energy fields satisfying the instantaneous Biot-Savart relation; an improvement for Navier-Stokes solutions must use dynamical restrictions (the vorticity evolution, the energy-dissipation law). No such improvement is proved here. By Lemmas 7.38-7.40, each of the following would exclude (I) at a fixed $\vartheta$: a bound $\log^+(s_n(\vartheta)/r_n)=o(\alpha_n^2)$ for the centred vertical spread of the killed density; tightness of its high-vorticity part at heights $k_n$ with $\log(k_n/r_n)=o(\alpha_n^2)$ (Remark (2) after Lemma 7.39); or a lower bound, by a fixed fraction of $a_n^2$, for its time-averaged occupation of $\ell_n^2|\nabla v_n|^2$ (Lemma 7.40(a); a lower bound for the unweighted occupation of $\{a_n/8<v_n<a_n/4\}$ is a different statement and would not suffice without a coercivity estimate, Remark (3) there). The soft bounds of Lemma 7.38(c) give none of these at leading logarithmic order; the energy form leaves $\log(1/\nu_n)$. For source-free passive data in the whole-space affine model, Proposition 7.41 gives $\alpha_n^2\le54\sqrt2(1+G)/(\epsilon c_a^2\vartheta^5)$ under the model dictionary; this is not a result for alternative (I). Proposition 7.42 rules out a Pe-independent bound for general whole-space passive drifts under the displayed amplitude and Hessian ratios, with $T/\ell^2\asymp\log\mathrm{Pe}/\log\log\mathrm{Pe}$; it does not show that the full logarithm is sharp. An exclusion of (I) along these lines needs structure of the actual evolution that these passive estimates do not capture; possible sources are the exact coupling $r\eta=\omega^\theta=\partial_zu^r-\partial_ru^z$ and its evolution, energy constraints, and the weighted annular geometry, and no sufficiency claim is made for any one of them. A corresponding planar model question is whether a smooth unforced solution of the two-dimensional Navier-Stokes equations on $\mathbb R^2\times[0,T]$ with $|u|\le M$, $|\nabla u|\le D$, $|\nabla^2u|\le H$ and $MH\le C_*D^2$ throughout, and $\omega(0,T)\ge c_0D$, must have $T\le C(c_0,C_*)\ell^2$ uniformly in $\mathrm{Pe}=M\ell$; the arguments here do not resolve it. For every fixed $P_0$, compactness and Theorem 5.1 of Koch, Nadirashvili, Seregin and Sverak (bounded ancient weak planar solutions are spatially constant) give $T\le C(c_0,C_*,P_0)\ell^2$ when $\mathrm{Pe}\le P_0$, with no constant uniform as $P_0\to\infty$; in the vanishing-viscosity limit, steady Euler shears with bounded profile prevent the same Liouville contradiction. A drift-independent partial answer needs neither the shape condition nor compactness. Assume additionally that $\omega(0)\in L^1(\mathbb R^2)$ (boundedness of $\omega$ follows from $D<\infty$). The vorticity solves $\partial_t\omega+u\cdot\nabla\omega=\Delta\omega$ with $\operatorname{div}u=0$; for this equation and for its backward adjoint, whose forward drift after reversing time on $[s,t]$ is $-u(t-\rho)$, the $L^1$ norm does not increase and $\|f(t)\|_2^2+2\int_s^t\|\nabla f\|_2^2=\|f(s)\|_2^2$ (on the whole plane both follow by testing against cutoffs, the error terms vanishing because $u$ and $f$ are bounded and $f(s)\in L^1$), so the planar Nash inequality $\|f\|_2^4\le C\|f\|_1^2\|\nabla f\|_2^2$ gives the bound $C(t-s)^{-1/2}$ from $L^1$ to $L^2$ for both, and duality with the composition law $U(t,s)=U(t,m)U(m,s)$ of the two-parameter evolution gives $\|\omega(T)\|_\infty\le\min\{\|\omega(0)\|_\infty,C\|\omega(0)\|_{L^1}/T\}$ with an absolute constant, whatever the drift. If $\omega(0,T)\ge c_0D$, then $D\le\omega(0,T)/c_0$ and $M\ge\|u(0)\|_\infty$, so $$\frac T{\ell^2}=\frac{TD^2}{M^2}\le\frac{T\|\omega(T)\|_\infty^2}{c_0^2\|u(0)\|_\infty^2}\le\frac{C\|\omega(0)\|_{L^1}\|\omega(0)\|_\infty}{c_0^2\|u(0)\|_\infty^2}.$$ Any family with $\|\omega(0)\|_{L^1}\|\omega(0)\|_\infty/\|u(0)\|_\infty^2$ bounded therefore satisfies the planar bound. This covers the Lamb-Oseen vortex (the ratio is an absolute constant) and $N$ one-signed vortex cores with disjoint supporting disks of radii in $[\rho,K\rho]$ and normalized profiles bounded by a fixed $F$ (the vorticity mass is $\sum_j|\Gamma_j|$, the maximal vorticity is at most $F\max_j|\Gamma_j|/\rho^2$, and Stokes' theorem on the boundary of the disk of the strongest core gives $\|u(0)\|_\infty\ge\max_j|\Gamma_j|/(2\pi K\rho)$, so the ratio is at most $4\pi^2FK^2N$, not uniform as $N\to\infty$). For data periodic in one direction with finite absolute vorticity mass per period the same method on the cylinder $(\mathbb R/L\mathbb Z)\times\mathbb R$, with the Nash inequality of the cylinder, gives $\|\omega(T)\|_\infty\le\min\{\|\omega(0)\|_\infty,CG(T^{-1}+L^{-1}T^{-1/2})\}$ with $G$ the vorticity mass per period, hence $T/\ell^2\le C(G\|\omega(0)\|_\infty+G^2/L^2)/(c_0^2\|u(0)\|_\infty^2)$ (treat $T\le L^2$ and $T\ge L^2$ separately). For the Kelvin-Stuart cat's-eye data $\psi_0=Ua\log(\cosh(y/a)+\varepsilon\cos(x/a))$, $0\le\varepsilon<1$, $u_0=(\partial_y\psi_0,-\partial_x\psi_0)$, one has $L=2\pi a$, $\omega_0=-\Delta\psi_0$ of one sign with $|\omega_0|=\frac Ua(1-\varepsilon^2)(\cosh(y/a)+\varepsilon\cos(x/a))^{-2}$, $\|\omega_0\|_\infty=\frac Ua\frac{1+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}$, $G=4\pi Ua$ (the jump $2U$ of the horizontal velocity times $L$), and $\|u_0\|_\infty\ge U(1-\varepsilon^2)^{-1/2}$ (evaluate $\partial_y\psi_0$ at $x=\pi a$, $\cosh(y/a)=1/\varepsilon$; for $\varepsilon=0$ use $|y|\to\infty$); so $G\|\omega_0\|_\infty/\|u_0\|_\infty^2\le4\pi(1+\varepsilon)^2\le16\pi$ and $G^2/(L^2\|u_0\|_\infty^2)\le4$, and $T/\ell^2\le C/c_0^2$ uniformly in $U,a,\varepsilon$. For bounded shears $u=(U(y,t),0)$ the vorticity solves the one-dimensional heat equation (the velocity does up to a function of $t$ fixed by the pressure), so $U_y(T)=(\partial_yG_T)*U(0)$ and $\|\omega(T)\|_\infty\le M\|\partial_yG_T\|_{L^1(\mathbb R)}=M/\sqrt{\pi T}$, since $\|\partial_yG_T\|_{L^1(\mathbb R)}=2G_T(0)=(\pi T)^{-1/2}$; hence $T/\ell^2=TD^2/M^2\le T\|\omega(T)\|_\infty^2/(c_0^2M^2)\le1/(\pi c_0^2)$; more generally, if $u\cdot\nabla\omega=0$ on the whole window, as for shears and for circular flows $u=v(r,t)e_\theta$, then $\omega$ solves the heat equation, $\omega(t)=\operatorname{curl}(G_t*u(0))$ with $G_t$ the heat kernel, $\|\omega(T)\|_\infty\le\|u(0)\|_\infty\|\nabla G_T\|_{L^1}=\frac{\sqrt\pi}2\|u(0)\|_\infty T^{-1/2}$, and so $T/\ell^2\le\pi/(4c_0^2)$; the same holds if $(u-b(t))\cdot\nabla\omega=0$ for a spatially constant $b(t)$ (the heat equation in a translating frame), and, by Duhamel's formula in that frame, $\sup_t\|(u-b)\cdot\nabla\omega\|_\infty\le\frac{c_0^3}{4\pi}D/\ell^2$ already forces $T<\pi\ell^2/c_0^2$ (at $h_0=\pi\ell^2/c_0^2$ the heat part of $\omega(0,T)$ is at most $c_0D/2$ and the source part at most $c_0D/4$); this condition asks the transport term to be smaller than its generic size $MD/\ell$ by the factor $c_0^3/(4\pi\mathrm{Pe})$, so it is a perturbative class; for the Taylor-Green cell $u=Ae^{-2k^2t}(\sin kx\cos ky,-\cos kx\sin ky)$ one has, with the Frobenius norm, $M=A$, $D=\sqrt2Ak$, $\|\omega(T)\|_\infty=2Ake^{-2k^2T}$, so $T/\ell^2\le\log(\sqrt2/c_0)$. A family violating a uniform persistence bound must therefore have unbounded $\|\omega(0)\|_{L^1}\|\omega(0)\|_\infty/\|u(0)\|_\infty^2$ on the plane, or unbounded $(G\|\omega(0)\|_\infty+G^2/L^2)/\|u(0)\|_\infty^2$ on the cylinder, or infinite absolute vorticity mass (per period); for instance arrays with an unbounded number of vortices. This does not determine the signs or the geometry of the vorticity, and no counterexample was found. The same duality bound, applied at an intermediate time to the excess over $c_0D/2$, gives a version that does not assume $\omega(s)\in L^1$ (integrability of the excess follows from boundedness of $E$): if for some $s\in[0,T)$ the set $E=\{\omega(\cdot,s)>c_0D/2\}$ is bounded and has (De Giorgi) perimeter at most $Q|E|^{1/2}$ with $Q<\infty$, then $T-s\le CQ^2\ell^2/c_0^2$ with the absolute constant $C$ of the $L^1$-$L^\infty$ bound above. Indeed, write $\omega(s)=\min\{\omega(s),c_0D/2\}+(\omega(s)-c_0D/2)_+$; the linear evolution from time $s$ to time $T$ preserves order and constants, so $\omega(0,T)\le c_0D/2+C\|(\omega(s)-c_0D/2)_+\|_{L^1}/(T-s)$, and the Gauss-Green formula for bounded sets of finite perimeter, applied to the Lipschitz field $(u_2,-u_1)$ whose divergence is $\omega$, gives $\int_E\omega(s)\le M\,\mathrm{Per}(E)\le MQ|E|^{1/2}$, so that $\|(\omega(s)-c_0D/2)_+\|_{L^1}\le MQ|E|^{1/2}-\frac{c_0D}2|E|\le M^2Q^2/(2c_0D)$; with $\omega(0,T)\ge c_0D$ this gives $T-s\le CM^2Q^2/(c_0^2D^2)$.
